| D230 | The allocator's cross-clone gap: re-check the floor on every claim, and read ORIGIN's ledger | - | PR [#305](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/305), branch `claude/llm-bets-moneyline-comparison-lmikrj-d230` | merged | **User request 2026-09-11** ("fix the cloud session allocator gap, it was supposed to be fixed"). It was: D226 made a brand-new database seed from DELIVERABLES.md's high-water mark instead of a hardcoded 203, which fixed a fresh clone reissuing D203. **Two holes remained, and both bit the very next cloud session.** (a) SEED-ONCE: the ledger is read only when the counter row is absent, so nothing ever teaches a separate clone's private counter about a number merged upstream mid-session. (b) A STALE LEDGER: the working tree's `DELIVERABLES.md` is as old as that clone's last pull. On 2026-09-11 a session seeded from a checkout topping out at D223 and claimed D224 - **merged to main 69 minutes earlier** (#296 at 02:20Z; the claim at 03:29Z) - then D225 the same way; both had to be renumbered to D228/D229 on rebase. **The fix is `syncCounterFloor`**, run before EVERY claim: raise the counter to `max(counter, local ledger + 1, origin ledger + 1)`, where the origin figure comes from `git show origin/main:DELIVERABLES.md` after a bounded, failure-tolerant `git fetch`. It RAISES ONLY, so on a machine whose shared counter is already ahead it is a no-op, and it can never recycle a number. The CLI reports the raise and names both ledgers; `--no-remote` / `BETSHEET_DELIVERABLE_NO_REMOTE=1` opt out, and **the tool never requires a network** - every remote failure path returns null, falls back to the local ledger, and SAYS the strongest check did not run rather than implying it passed. **A REAL BUG WAS FOUND IN THIS DELIVERABLE'S OWN FIRST DRAFT, by the test written for it.** The raise initially compared in JS (`if (floor > from) UPDATE ... SET next_number = floor`), which is racy: A syncs and reads 203, B reads 203 concurrently, A claims 601 and the counter moves to 602, then B's late write sets it BACK to 601 and the next claimer hits `UNIQUE constraint failed: deliverable_claims.number`. It reproduced at ~1 run in 5 under 25 concurrent processes - exactly the flake rate that gets dismissed as noise. Fixed by doing the comparison in SQL inside an immediate transaction: `SET next_number = MAX(next_number, ?)` cannot go backwards however stale the value reaching it. **VERIFIED**: `check-deliverable-numbers` extended from 57 to 81 assertions (24 added) - the seed-once hole, a ledger that SHRANK unable to lower the counter, `DEFAULT_NEXT_NUMBER` still a hard floor, `syncFloor:false` as a genuine opt-out, `remoteLedgerHighWaterMark` reading a REAL temp git repo's `origin/main` rather than its working tree, the incident reproduced exactly (local D223 + origin D240 -> D241, not D224), a repo with no origin returning null instead of throwing, the offline fallback still claiming, `BETSHEET_DELIVERABLE_NO_REMOTE=1`, a live read of this repo's own origin, and **25 concurrent processes racing the floor raise** - the test that caught the bug above. Green 20 runs in a row after the fix, having been 1-in-5 red before it. **Three negative controls run for real and restored**: a non-monotonic raise fails 10 of 10 runs; ignoring the remote fails the incident assertion; removing the sync entirely fails the seed-once assertion. **Proven live end to end**: against a deliberately stale ledger (rows above D223 stripped) the old path claims D224 and the new path claims D230, printing `Counter raised D224 -> D230 before claiming: local ledger D223, origin ledger D229`. The concurrency and CLI-argument tests now pin `BETSHEET_DELIVERABLE_LEDGER` and `BETSHEET_DELIVERABLE_NO_REMOTE` the way they already pinned the DB - without that they read whatever ledger and remote the machine happens to have, making their expectations a property of the checkout rather than of the allocator. **CLAUDE.md updated** - this is a workflow rule, and the honest residual is stated there: two clones claiming within the same minute still collide, and closing that needs a server-side atomic claim. **The D number was claimed with the allocator this time**, not by eye. |
| D229 | PT-1: the chart's post-time odds, stored - and beat-the-close | - | PR [#302](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/302), branch `claude/llm-bets-moneyline-comparison-lmikrj` | merged | **User request 2026-09-11** ("do the PT-1 backfill too"), PT-1 of `docs/requirements/post-time-odds-llm-comparison.md`. **The parser was never the gap.** `shared/chart-parser.js` has extracted `odds` and `favorite` for every finisher since it was written - the odds token is how `parseResultLine` locates the finish position, so the value was never optional to the parse - and `saveResults` dropped both because `race_results` had nowhere to put them. Migration 035 adds `post_time_odds REAL` and `favorite INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, two ordinary ALTERs, and the insert passes them through. **What it unlocks**: `shared/pick-scoring.js` gains `impliedProbabilities` (takeout-normalised - a raw board is overround, so against RAW implied probabilities every source on earth scores negative and the question is rigged), `marketBaseline` (the POST-TIME favorite, the baseline `favoriteBaseline`'s own docstring has wanted since D171 measured `entries.live_odds` empty), and `closeEdge` = `1{the primary pick won} - q`. **That last figure is the point of the whole deliverable**: every other rate on that object answers "did this source pick winners", which a source can do well simply by preferring short prices - and a short price is what you get paid least for. `closeEdge` asks whether the horses it backed won MORE often than the money said they would, which is the only shape of evidence that separates an edge from an opinion. Zero is the null hypothesis, not a floor, and the check asserts a market-matching source scores exactly 0. NULL and never 0 wherever no board was read, with its own `n` - an Apify-sourced day has a morning line but no closing price, and folding those in at 0 would report a market that was never read as a market that was wrong. Surfaced through `server/pick-scoring.js` (every per-race row carries its `market` block, so each aggregate is re-derivable by hand) and two new columns plus two per-race columns on `/sources`, reusing the existing `pl--pos`/`pl--neg` classes rather than inventing `.pos`/`.neg` - the D216 gotcha caught in the act, again. **VERIFIED**: new `scripts/check-post-time-odds.js`, 38 assertions, all green. **The one that matters most uses the real chart and a quantity Equibase states independently**: a winner's $2 win payout and its printed odds come from different fields and must agree, so `win_cents / 200 - 1` is cross-checked against the stored odds on all 10 races, to within breakage (0.06) - nothing else in this codebase could catch a column read off by one, which is precisely the D116 trap sitting in the parser next door. Plus: implied probabilities normalise to exactly 1 while the raw book is proven overround; backing the FAVORITE and losing costs more than backing a longshot and losing (the asymmetry that makes this an edge test rather than a hit-rate test); an unpriced race gives `priced:false` and a NULL edge; the aggregate's `closeEdge.n` is 1 of 2 races when only one carried a board; and the full chain chart -> DB -> `/api/pick-scoring` is re-derived BY HAND from the stored board. **A re-save is what a backfill IS**, so it is asserted directly: clearing the prices, re-uploading the same chart and confirming it refills them AND leaves all existing `graded_tickets` untouched. **A negative control was run for real and restored**: removing the takeout normalisation fails 5 assertions. Two vacuous passes were found and fixed during the run - `.every` over an empty result set is TRUE, and two assertions were satisfied by zero saved rows before each was given its own denominator. **Migration 035 applied to a COPY of the real 169-day corpus**: all 6,733 result rows intact and unpriced, 3,966 graded tickets intact, `integrity_check` ok, `foreign_key_check` 0 rows. 42 of 44 check scripts green; `check-equibase-otr` (needs Poppler) and `check-pl` fail on `main` too and are untouched. **NOT YET ANSWERED, and this is the honest state**: no stored day carries a price, because `result_charts` keeps only a digest and nothing archives chart text - so backfilling means re-uploading each day's chart through the ordinary results path. On the 2026-09-10 corpus that is 5 `equibase_pdf` days (Saratoga 09-06 and 09-07, Del Mar 09-07, Kentucky Downs 09-07, Horseshoe Indianapolis 09-10); the 3 Apify days can also be filled, but only by uploading a CHART for them, which replaces their Apify results wholesale and regrades the day. Same one-branch/one-row deviation recorded on D228. |
| D228 | Live odds capture: refresh a stored day's tote board, and keep every board | - | PR [#302](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/302), branch `claude/llm-bets-moneyline-comparison-lmikrj` | merged | **User request 2026-09-11** ("build the live odds capture"), following `docs/requirements/post-time-odds-llm-comparison.md`'s PT-4 - the phase that evaluation named as the one worth building regardless of how the odds-vs-ML thesis turns out, because it is a betting capability rather than a study. **The gap it closes is total, not partial**: `entries.live_odds` has existed since migration 024 and held 0 rows - D171 measured 0 of 13,710, and this deliverable re-measured 0 of 13,710 on the uploaded 2026-09-10 corpus. That emptiness is why `shared/tip-staking.js`'s ML-vs-live staking draft has been unrunnable since D171 and why `shared/pick-scoring.js` has to use the MORNING-LINE favorite as its market baseline. **Not a re-ingest, deliberately**: the entries path's only way to touch an existing day is `?replace=1`, which HARD-DELETES it and cascades away every card and ticket (the D204 gotcha, which cost 168 entries and 98 human tickets live), so this is an additive, price-only path. New `shared/live-odds.js` (PURE, browser-safe) is where additive is ENFORCED: `reconcileOddsCapture` emits nothing but `{raceNumber, programNumber, liveOdds, liveOddsDecimal}` and refuses, with a reason, on everything else. **Four deliberate refusals**, each pinned by a named assertion: the MORNING LINE is never overwritten (it is the other half of the comparison the capture exists to make); the ENTRY LIST is never added to or deleted from (a tote board is a price source, not a source of truth about who is in the race); the SCRATCHED flag is never changed (it drives invariant 1's refund rule, so folding in a late scratch would silently re-grade settled cards - drift is surfaced as a `scratch_drift` warning instead); and an unmatched race number is reported, never guessed. **Migration 034 stores a HISTORY, not a cell** - `odds_captures` + `odds_capture_entries` - because the question these captures serve is about MOVEMENT: a board at 30 minutes to post and the same board at 2 minutes are two facts, and writing the second over the first would destroy the measurement in the act of taking it. `entries.live_odds` keeps its meaning as the LATEST capture, so `effectiveOdds`, D117's staleness indicator and every future prompt read it exactly where they already do, with no join to learn. `race_days.odds_captured_at` moves FORWARD only - re-uploading an older page must not make a day read fresher than the board is. New `server/live-odds.js`: preview (writes nothing), confirm (re-parses the same markup server-side, so the browser never hands the server a price - D54/D63's rule), and a read-only GET listing every board on the day. Invariant 11 honoured via `recordAttempt`, including for a REFUSED upload, so a board that never came up is visible rather than absent. Invariant 6 untouched - nothing fetches; the capture is a file a person saved. New `LiveOddsModal.jsx` (`.modal-backdrop` with NO click handler, per house rule) wired to a "Capture live odds" button on `RaceDayView.jsx`; it uses only class names already in `client/src/styles.css` (`.notice`/`.notice--warn`/`.notice--error`/`.dim`/`.grid`), adding none, per the D216 global-CSS gotcha. **VERIFIED**: new `scripts/check-live-odds.js`, 60 assertions, all green - pure half covers every refusal (track and date mismatch, a board that never came up, a race or horse on only one side, scratch drift both directions, an unreadable price kept as TEXT with a null decimal rather than dropped, a D180 program-less scratch row ignored silently, degenerate inputs returning a refusal rather than throwing) plus D207's every-warning-has-a-message rule asserted on every warning path; server half boots the real server on a temp DB (port 8903) and proves preview writes nothing while still auditing, confirm writes prices and only prices, a SECOND capture APPENDS with the first board still readable at its own prices, `odds_captured_at` never rewinds, a refusal is 422 with nothing written, and a soft-deleted day 404s. **The most important assertion uses REAL data**: the 4.2MB Del Mar page is run through the whole path and must be REFUSED with `no_prices` - the exact mistake a person makes uploading a page before wagering opens, and the only real capture this project has. **The priced fixtures are SYNTHETIC and labelled as such in the check's own header**, because no real near-post capture exists anywhere in this project (`docs/requirements/equibase-entries-ingest.md` open question 2); the first real one is the thing to take next. **Migration 034 applied to a COPY of the real 169-day corpus**: both tables created, `integrity_check` ok, `foreign_key_check` 0 rows, and all of 169 race days / 1,526 races / 13,710 entries / 231 cards / 5,184 tickets / 6,733 results intact. `check-live-odds`, `check-schema` (extended with four new assertions on the new tables), `check-ingest`, `check-logging`, `check-routing`, `check-equibase-entries`, `check-module-bindings`, `build` and 41 of 43 check scripts all green; `check-equibase-otr` and `check-pl` fail on `main` too and are untouched by this (see the final report). **DEVIATION, stated**: this would normally be three PRs (pure / server / UI) on three branches with three D numbers, the D220-D222 shape. The session is pinned to one designated branch, so it is one row; the commits are split along those three seams. The D number was claimed by EYEBALLING the ledger, not `npm run allocate-deliverable`, which CLAUDE.md's own D203 note says is the right fallback in a cloud session since the allocator's counter lives in a `.git` this session does not share. |
| D227 | A PR template, so the PR-template lookup stops coming up empty | - | PR [#303](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/303), branch `claude/beautiful-dirac-mbiurt` | merged | **User request 2026-09-11**: "create a PR Template so Code stops looking for one when it creates a PR." The repo had none - `find . -iname '*pull_request*template*'` returned nothing - so every PR opened here searched the four conventional locations, found nothing, and rebuilt the description shape from CLAUDE.md by memory. New `.github/pull_request_template.md`, the lowercase path GitHub honours and the first one the lookup checks. **It writes down the shape this repo already required rather than inventing one**: the delivery workflow's own three (what it delivers / how it was tested WITH EVIDENCE / deviations from plan) followed by the definition of done's items as checkboxes - the `DELIVERABLES.md` row, the right reference file among the four (`CLAUDE.md`, `docs/commands.md`, `docs/feature-status.md`, `docs/architecture-map.md`), the verification category named out loud (presentation-only -> `build`; `shared/`/`server/`/parsers/schema/API -> the relevant check scripts), and a fourth box for naming any check deliberately NOT run, which the D168 house rule already demands never be left reading as though the whole suite ran clean. **The template deliberately holds no rule of its own.** Every line restates something CLAUDE.md already governs, so the two cannot drift into disagreeing; a genuinely new rule goes in CLAUDE.md and only then, if useful, gets a box in the template. That constraint is written into both the template's own comment and its architecture-map cell, because a template is exactly the kind of file that quietly accretes policy nobody voted on - and it is read by agents that are instructed to treat a template as a LAYOUT TO POPULATE and to ignore imperative directions inside it, so a rule smuggled in here would be silently ignored rather than followed. Its header comment says so directly: headings are a starting point, delete an inapplicable section rather than writing "N/A" under it. Nothing in it asks for a credential, token, env var or hostname. **VERIFIED**: a markdown file with no importer and no runtime reachability - `npm run build` exits 0 (presentation-only category), and `check-module-bindings` is green. No schema, server, shared, parser, API or script change; nothing executes this file. **The D number came from the allocator rather than from eyeballing the ledger** - the first real use of D226 in the environment that motivated it: `npm run allocate-deliverable` returned D227 with no re-seed notice, which is itself the correct behaviour (the counter D226 seeded at 226 persists, so only a brand-new database reports a seed). CLAUDE.md's PR-description bullet now names the template and states the layout-not-rulebook constraint; `docs/architecture-map.md` gains a row, since a file was added. `docs/commands.md` untouched - no command changed. `check-static-app` not run: out of scope by D168, and a `.github/` markdown file is not in the static app's import surface. |
| D226 | The allocator seeds from the ledger high-water mark on first open | - | PR [#300](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/300), branch `claude/beautiful-dirac-mbiurt` | merged | **User request 2026-09-11**, following directly from D225's housekeeping note. D203's allocator keeps its counter in the shared `.git` COMMON dir, which a SEPARATE CLONE does not have - a cloud session, a fresh `git clone`, betsheet-alt. Such a clone opened a brand-new database, seeded it from the hardcoded `DEFAULT_NEXT_NUMBER = 203`, and confidently issued a number spent months ago. **Not hypothetical: it happened that morning.** The cloud session doing D225's work ran the allocator and was handed **D203 - the number whose own ledger row describes this very tool** - and had to release it and fall back to eyeballing DELIVERABLES.md, which is the exact read-then-write race D203 was built to delete. The old advice (CLAUDE.md's own gotcha: "the eyeball-the-ledger fallback is still the right move there") therefore reintroduced the bug in the one environment that cannot see the counter. **`openAllocatorDb` now seeds from `ledgerHighWaterMark()`**, which reads DELIVERABLES.md and takes the max of its **ID COLUMN ONLY** (`| D225 | ...`; suffixed IDs like `D44-QA` and `D77-A` parse to their base number). The prose is deliberately never scanned - a Notes cell routinely cites a dozen other D numbers, and a row citing a FUTURE one ("superseded by D999") would poison the counter for every later claim. **The ledger is a floor-RAISER, never a floor-lowerer**: the seed is `max(high-water + 1, DEFAULT_NEXT_NUMBER)`, so a truncated, empty or unreadable ledger falls back to the old constant and can never hand out a number below it; seeding HIGH is safe (the never-recycle rule already accepts gaps), seeding LOW is the bug, so every ambiguity resolves upward. `DEFAULT_NEXT_NUMBER` is KEPT, now documented as a floor rather than a seed, because it encodes the one fact the ledger cannot: D202 was minted on a branch that was never merged and appears in no committed row. **The ledger is read ONLY when the counter row is genuinely absent**, so an existing database costs no file I/O and a later edit to DELIVERABLES.md can never move a counter that already exists - the ledger is a starting point, never an ongoing authority. New `onSeed` callback fires only when the calling process actually WON the creation race, and the CLI uses it to print the seed and its source to stderr; stdout stays exactly `D<number>`, so `NUM=$(npm run --silent allocate-deliverable ...)` is unaffected. `BETSHEET_DELIVERABLE_LEDGER` overrides the ledger path, mirroring `BETSHEET_DELIVERABLE_DB`. **VERIFIED**: `check-deliverable-numbers` gained 24 assertions. Every PRE-EXISTING case now pins `BETSHEET_DELIVERABLE_LEDGER` at a path that cannot exist, so its seed stays the constant it always was - a check that changes answer because an unrelated deliverable shipped is a check nobody trusts. New cases: ID-column parsing, suffixed IDs, CRLF, a no-rows file and a missing file both reading null rather than 0 or a throw; the floor cases (a ledger behind the floor, an unreadable one, and the exact `high-water + 1 === floor` boundary); a **LIVE assertion that the real DELIVERABLES.md still parses** (high-water >= 225), because a row-format change would silently reinstate the D203 bug while the allocator kept appearing to work; `onSeed` firing exactly once and NOT on reopen, with a reopen against a `D900` ledger proving the persisted counter wins; and 8 real processes racing to CREATE one database, asserting they agree on a single seed, get distinct contiguous numbers from it, and that exactly ONE of them reports seeding. **Four negative controls run for real and restored byte-identical** (md5-verified, by file copy rather than `git checkout` - D224's lesson): ignoring the ledger fails 9; dropping the floor clamp fails the stale-ledger case; and TWO loose-scan variants, which is where this deliverable found a bug in its OWN test - **the first version of the ID-column assertion PASSED under a deliberately loosened regex, verifying nothing**, because `.exec()` returns the first match on a line and the ID column is first, so the prose number was never reached. Fixed with a second fixture carrying D numbers on NON-ROW lines (a paragraph, a bullet); both loose variants now fail, each caught by the fixture built for it. **Proven live, end to end**: this session's own stale counter (sitting at 204 - another spent number) was backed up and deleted, and the identical command that returned D203 that morning returned `New allocator database seeded at D226, from the ledger high-water mark D225` - which is the number this row carries. `check-deliverable-numbers`, `check-module-bindings`, `build` all green. `check-static-app` not run - out of scope by D168, and nothing here is in the static app's import surface. No schema, server or API change. CLAUDE.md's D203 gotcha rewritten: run the allocator in a fresh clone too, and what has NOT changed - this is still not networked, so a number minted on an unmerged branch in a DIFFERENT clone is still yours to check by eye. |
| D225 | `check-pl` fix: set `BETSHEET_LLM_TEST_MODE` on the SPAWNED server so the LLM stub is honoured | - | PR [#298](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/298), branch `claude/beautiful-dirac-mbiurt` | merged | **A real defect in the check script, not in `server/pl.js`** - it reproduced on a clean `main` checkout and was not caused by any recent feature work. `scripts/check-pl.js` seeds the LLM_GENERATED bucket by posting `__stubResponse` to `/api/race-days/:id/llm-cards/preview`, but `server/llm-cards.js:537` only reads that field when `process.env.BETSHEET_LLM_TEST_MODE === '1'` - and this script's `spawn` env never set it. So the stub was silently IGNORED, the preview attempted a real Anthropic call, no LLM card was ever created, and the bucket was simply absent from the P/L response. **All four failures were one missing card**: `LLM_GENERATED bucket holds exactly the one Del Mar LLM card`, `the two producers on ONE day never pool into a single bucket`, `both cards side by side, both graded`, and `the compare really compares: the two producers differ on the race they share`. **The fix is the env var alone** - `BETSHEET_LLM_TEST_MODE: '1'`, plus `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: ''` as the second half of the same guard, exactly as `check-llm-cards.js:371`, `check-llm-input-capture.js:45`, `check-human-picks.js:105` and `check-equibase-otr.js:185` all already do; NOT ONE `check(...)` call was touched, so nothing was made to pass by weakening it (the assertions are strict and stayed strict: `b.cards === 1`, card A2's completeness identically `LLM_GENERATED`). The empty key matters beyond this box: without it the spawned server inherits the real `.env` key on a machine that has one, which is how `check-human-picks` once passed **by making a real, paid API call that happened to pick a winner** - its own comment records that, and this row is the same trap caught one file later. **The class is now fully swept**: `server/llm-cards.js:537` is the ONLY test-mode-gated stub in the entire codebase (`grep -rn 'TEST_MODE|__stub' server/ shared/` returns exactly that one line), and `check-pl.js` was the ONLY one of the five scripts posting `__stubResponse` without the flag - so no sibling is silently passing or failing the same way. **VERIFIED**: `npm run check-pl` reproduced all 4 failures before the change and exits 0 with all 24 assertions passing after it. The LLM bucket is genuinely exercised, not merely asserted into existence - a temporary probe (added, read, reverted) printed the live response: `LLM_GENERATED` with `cards: 1, tickets: 1, costCents: 4000, returnedCents: 5600, plCents: 1600` and `byModel: [{model: 'stub'}]`, the `stub` model label being positive proof the test-mode path ran rather than the network. Actual buckets are **HUMAN (2 cards) + LLM_GENERATED (1)**; there is no EQB_OTR card in this scenario - line 182's `['HUMAN','LLM_GENERATED','EQB_OTR']` is an allowlist of what MAY appear, not a list of what the check builds. `check-llm-cards`, `check-llm-input-capture` and `build` all green (the first two share the stub mechanism and were left untouched). `check-equibase-otr` fails here for an unrelated environmental reason - it needs Poppler's `pdftotext`, which CLAUDE.md documents as a Windows-machine dependency resolved via `BETSHEET_PDFTOTEXT` - and is out of scope. `check-static-app` not run: out of scope in this environment by the D168 house rule, and nothing here is in the static app's import surface (a check script is not imported by anything). No schema, server, shared, parser or API change - the diff is 12 lines in one check script, 10 of them comment. **Housekeeping note**: `npm run allocate-deliverable` handed out D203, a number long since taken, because this session is a FRESH CLOUD CLONE whose allocator counter (a SQLite file in the `.git` common dir) seeded from scratch and cannot see this machine's claims - the exact limitation D203's own row and the CLAUDE.md gotcha both name. Phase PS-1 of three. New `shared/pick-scoring.js`, pure and browser-safe, modelled on `shared/tip-scoring.js` (D170): `rolesFromTickets` reduces one race's tickets to the role shape the scope doc names (`winBacked`/`placeBacked`/`showBacked` from win/place/show tickets, `named` from every leg of every ticket, `primary` = the win ticket with the largest `stakeCents`, ties by lowest `sequence`); `rolesFromTipPicks` maps a `tip_picks` row (rank 1 -> winBacked + primary, ranks 1-3 -> named, NOTHING invented for place/show); `scorePickRace` scores roles against `race_results`/`result_scratches` directly - no grade set, no engine version, nothing stored - returning true/false/NULL per role where NULL means the source backed nothing live in that role (never a miss), a scratched primary scoring NULL rather than being promoted to the next win ticket (promotion would invent a rank), `namedTop3` against the chart's real top three, `unknownPicks` surfaced, and the MORNING-LINE favorite's own win/place/show on the same race from an optional `entries` list (a tie for lowest line is a hit if any tied horse got there, and is flagged; `live_odds` is empty corpus-wide per D171, so ML is the only honest control) plus the field size for the random baseline; `aggregatePickScores` gives every rate its OWN `{hits, n, rate}` because denominators genuinely differ per role (a source that never bets place has `place.n = 0`, rate NULL, not a 0% record), `meanWinBacked` to show what "any" buys over "primary", and the favorite/random baselines computed on that source's own scored races only; `bySource` groups by a caller-supplied `source` key and never pools (invariant 13's shape). **No money anywhere**: a stake is read once, to pick the primary. **VERIFIED**: new `scripts/check-pick-scoring.js`, pure, 52 hand-computed assertions - an OTR-shaped race, an LLM race with three win tickets where primary and any differ, a stake tie, a box-only race (every WPS role NULL, named full), scratches (primary NULL not promoted, a role whose only horse scratched NULL, every-named-scratched -> race null), refusals that never throw, an unknown program number surfaced, case/whitespace-insensitive program numbers, the favorite baseline skipping a scratched lowest line and handling a tie, field size from live entries with a finisher-count fallback, `rolesFromTipPicks` reproducing D170's `scoreTipRace` win/place/show AND `top3Overlap` on a real stored `tip_picks` row (order-independent), per-role denominators in the aggregate, NULL-everything at n=0, and `bySource` producing no combined figure. **Three negative controls run for real, each restored afterward**: primary = first-written instead of largest stake fails 2 (incl. the user-decision assertion by name); | D219 | Prune CLAUDE.md: Commands and Feature status move to `docs/`, and the house rule that keeps them there | - | PR [#288](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/288), branch `claude-md-prune` | merged | **User request**: move both sections out and leave a pointer, then write the rule down so it holds going forward. The D208 precedent (the architecture map) applied twice more, and the numbers are the argument: **CLAUDE.md was 186k and is now 67k** - `## Feature status` alone was 107k of it, `## Commands` another 15k. Both are now `docs/feature-status.md` and `docs/commands.md`, byte-for-byte, each behind a pointer paragraph in the section it vacated. **Nothing about upkeep changes, and both new files say so in their own headers**: a PR still updates its command line and its feature row in the same commit as the change. `Definition of done` #2 now names all FOUR files by what kind of change lands in each, instead of naming two and letting the other two go stale - which had already happened once, since README's document index still described the architecture map as living in CLAUDE.md eight deliverables after D208 moved it. **The HOUSE RULE, in Gotchas**: CLAUDE.md holds RULES - the invariants, the workflow, the house rules, the gotchas - because it is loaded in full into every session and its size is a tax paid whether or not the content is read; a reference table someone LOOKS UP (commands, per-file architecture, a feature ledger) goes under `docs/` with a pointer here. The stated evidence is that trimming did not work: four trims in five days (165k, 168k, 193k, 178k), each genuinely compressing real duplication, and the file was back to 186k two days after the last one - compression is a one-off, growth is continuous. The rule's operative sentence is the one for NEXT time: before adding a new section, ask whether it is a rule or a lookup. **Dogfooded**: this deliverable's own feature row went into `docs/feature-status.md`, not into CLAUDE.md. **Repaired in passing, since the text was being moved anyway**: two literal BEL (0x07) bytes D218 wrote into CLAUDE.md - the intended `src\win\async.c` had been mangled to `src\win<BEL>sync.c` because this machine's Bash heredoc strips one backslash level and Python then read the surviving `\a` as an escape. Both call sites now read correctly and the check below asserts no control byte survives anywhere. **Two stale cross-references fixed rather than left**: `DELIVERABLES.md`'s QA-suffix rule pointed at "CLAUDE.md's feature-status table", and CLAUDE.md's own copy of that rule said "the feature-status table below" - true until this PR, wrong after it. README's project-document index was rewritten to list the three `docs/` files beside CLAUDE.md and to say what earns a place in which. **VERIFIED** by a purpose-written script diffing the new tree against `origin/main`'s CLAUDE.md, 21 assertions, all passing: all **67** command lines and all **91** feature rows appear VERBATIM in the new files; none of either is left behind in CLAUDE.md; every `##` section still exists; the `## Invariants` section is byte-identical; every original Gotchas byte survives with the new house rule as the only addition; the BEL repair holds and no control byte remains in any of the three files; code fences balance in all three. `npm run build` green (docs-only change - no `shared/`, `server/`, parser, schema or API-contract file touched, which is the category `Definition of done` #3 scales to). **Flagged, NOT acted on**: `AGENTS.md` is a 149k single-commit copy of an older CLAUDE.md frozen at D187 (31 deliverables behind), referenced by nothing in the repo.
