| D218 | `die()` must not call `process.exit()` after a fetch - it aborted with a libuv assertion and returned 127 | - | PR [#286](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/286), branch `gh-api-die-exit` | merged | **User request** ("fix the libuv assertion on die()"), raised as cosmetic noise at the end of D217 and **found to be a real defect on inspection**: `scripts/gh-api.js`'s `die()` ended in `process.exit(code)`, and after a fetch to api.github.com that ABORTS on this machine - `Assertion failed: !(handle->flags & UV_HANDLE_CLOSING), file src\winsync.c` - so **the shell received 127, not the code the script chose**. 127 conventionally means "command not found", so a caller branching on `npm run gh`'s exit status could not distinguish a deliberate, carefully-worded refusal from a broken command: the message was right and the status was wrong. **Narrowed by experiment before writing any fix, and the negative results are the useful half**: deterministic 5 of 5 on node 24 / Windows; NOT reproducible with no fetch at all; NOT reproducible after a **localhost** fetch - which is why the ~20 check scripts that boot a temp server and exit 1 on failure were never affected, and why this never surfaced anywhere but here; NOT reproducible after https to example.com or registry.npmjs.org; and not caused by DNS, by TLS alone, by response compression, or by reading the body, each ruled out separately. The one measured difference is that GitHub's response carries no `connection` header where the others carry `connection: keep-alive`. **The diagnosis stops there deliberately** - it is a node/libuv bug rather than this codebase's, and the correlation is also the argument for the fix: stop calling `process.exit()` rather than try to predict when it is safe. **The fix**: `die()` sets `process.exitCode` and throws an `ExitSignal` sentinel; `uncaughtException`/`unhandledRejection` handlers ignore the sentinel (it has already reported itself) and report anything else **with its stack**, through `redact()` - this file's premise is that no byte escapes unredacted, error paths included, and a genuine bug needs the stack a usage error does not. Measured: `process.exitCode` exits with the right code and exits promptly (~0.5s; undici's pooled sockets do not hold the loop open), and cannot truncate a piped stdout write the way `process.exit()` can. The one surviving `process.exit(2)` on the usage branch went too - it ran before any fetch and was safe, but leaving one behind is how the next network-touching branch quietly inherits the bug. **VERIFIED live against GitHub, all six exit paths**: the merged-PR refusal that used to abort now exits **1** with its message and no assertion; `pr close` with no number exits 1; bare usage exits 2; an allowlist refusal exits 1; an HTTP 404 after a real fetch exits 1; a successful read exits 0. `check-gh-api` gained a D218 section - the source property that prevents the whole class (`process.exit(` appears nowhere outside comments), the handler shape, and the FATAL path exercised for real on a temp copy with a `TypeError` injected before the command dispatch (so no request is made and the real script is never modified), asserting exit 1, the error named, a stack present, and no abort. **Two negative controls**: restoring `process.exit(code)` fails 2 assertions; making the handler swallow every error fails 3, one of which proves the script would then exit **0** on a genuine bug - a worse failure than the one being fixed. `npm run build` green; nothing under `shared/`, `server/`, the parsers, the schema or an API contract is touched. |
| D217 | `npm run gh -- pr close <n>`, and the allowlist entry it needed | - | PR [#284](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/284), branch `gh-api-pr-close` | merged | **User request**: "close #281" - a stale duplicate ledger PR left over from D216, which `npm run gh` refused because closing was not on `scripts/gh-api.js`'s allowlist (reads, open, comment, merge). **Added rather than reached around**, on the script's own invitation ("Edit ALLOW in scripts/gh-api.js if that is genuinely wrong"). The two alternatives were both worse: a raw `curl` past a guard whose header says it is "the only real constraint here", or deleting the head branch so GitHub closes the PR as a SIDE EFFECT - which is precisely how PR #142 was lost during D104/D105 and is already written up in Gotchas. **The allowlist entry is deliberately narrower than the endpoint it opens.** `PATCH /repos/:o/:r/pulls/:n` also retargets a PR's base and rewrites its title and body, and an allowlist keyed on verb+path cannot express "this verb but only this field" - so the real guard is that the whole script contains exactly ONE PATCH call whose body is the literal `{ state: 'closed' }`, and the check asserts both. The regex is anchored with `$`, so PATCH still cannot reach `/merge`, a review, an issue, the repository root (settings, default branch) or a branch-protection rule. DELETE stays permitted nowhere, which is what makes "close a PR" structurally unable to become "delete a branch". `raw PATCH <path>` did become reachable and is a guaranteed no-op, because `raw` has no body flag and passes `body: undefined`. **One behaviour worth naming**: an already-MERGED PR is refused with its own message rather than PATCHed. GitHub answers 200 to `{state:'closed'}` on a merged PR and leaves it merged, so a blind PATCH would print a reassuring "closed" for something that did nothing. **VERIFIED**: new `scripts/check-gh-api.js` (`npm run check-gh-api`) - OFFLINE and CREDENTIAL-FREE by construction, which is the point: the allowlist is checked before any request is built, so what it permits is a pure property of the source and needs no token, no network and no real PR to assert. It re-derives the regexes by PARSING `gh-api.js` rather than restating them (the technique `check-track-codes.js` already uses on the track registry, and for the same reason - a hand-copied second rule passes while the real one drifts), covers 5 permitted and 10 refused verb/path pairs, the single-PATCH-body property, and spawns the real CLI for the usage-refusal paths. **Two negative controls**: dropping the anchoring `$` fails the two "PATCH cannot reach" assertions, and replacing the literal body with a variable fails the body assertion; both exit non-zero. The check also caught one of its own assertions written against the wrong source text. Then exercised for real against GitHub: `pr close 280` (merged) refused, `pr close 281` closed it, and a second `pr close 281` reported "already closed". `check-module-bindings` and `build` green. **Pre-existing, not introduced here**: `die()` on this Windows machine prints a libuv `UV_HANDLE_CLOSING` assertion after its message - visible on the earlier 405 from `pr merge` too, before this branch existed. |
| D216 | Highlight Baffert runners and the five-horse-field favorite in every entries table | - | PR [#280](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/280), branch `entry-flags-baffert-favorite` | merged | **User request**, and the second half of a thesis analysis the same session: the user asked whether the LLM Generator could implement "in a 5-horse race bet the favorite; if the trainer is B Baffert bet to win", then chose to have the two conditions SURFACED as a visual indicator and build the HUMAN tickets themselves. **The LLM Generator could not have done it, for a mechanical reason**: `server/llm-prompt.js` builds every ENTRIES line as `#<pgm> <name> - ML <odds>` (D178 trimmed it to that deliberately), so the model never sees a trainer at all. Beyond that, the thesis is a RULE, not a judgement - a paid, non-deterministic, un-versioned generator (`engine_version` is the literal `'llm'` on all 40 stored LLM cards, so invariant 14 never fires on a prompt change) is the wrong vehicle for something reproducible. **This deliverable deliberately produces no card, no ticket, no bucket and no migration.** It is a reading aid over entries that are already stored, which is why it needs none of invariants 13/14's machinery; the money decision stays with the person, in the existing HUMAN path. New pure, browser-safe `shared/entry-flags.js` (`isBaffertEntry`, `flagRaceEntries`, `FAVORITE_FIELD_SIZE`), rendered by a new shared `client/src/components/EntryFlagTags.jsx` in all FOUR entries tables (`ParsePreview.jsx` - the ingest preview, which is literally "when entries are created"; `RaceDayView.jsx`; the shared `EntriesTable.jsx`, which the static at-track builder also renders; and `CardView.jsx`, so the same horse never looks flagged on one screen and unflagged on another). **Two judgement calls, both measured against the real corpus first.** (1) The trainer test is a case-insensitive SUBSTRING, not an equality: the corpus holds three spellings of the same person - `Bob Baffert(J. Barnes)` (160 entries), `B Baffert` (9), `B. Baffert` (8), the parenthetical being the assistant trainer - so `trainer === 'B Baffert'`, the thesis as literally worded, would have reached 7 of the 163 runners in graded races and looked like a working feature. It is deliberately NOT a canonicalized trainer registry (`shared/track-codes.js`'s shape): that is the right answer for a STAKING rule that must be reproducible across sources, and overkill for a highlight. (2) The field size counts LIVE runners, so a six-horse race that scratches to five qualifies and a five-horse race that scratches to four stops - the consequence, that the flag can appear or vanish during the day, is deliberate, since five runners at post is what you are actually betting into. Co-favorites are ALL flagged rather than none, because a tie showing nothing is indistinguishable from "this race does not qualify" (rare: 5 of 1,526 stored races tie at the ML minimum, none of them a five-horse field). The morning line is read from the stored decimal where a caller has one and parsed from the printed string otherwise, which is required rather than defensive - `server/replay.js`'s `entriesPayload` carries the string only. **A real collision found in the browser, which no check script could have seen**: the row-highlight class was first named `row--flagged`, which `DistributionView.jsx` already uses for its outlier-day highlight - a RED tint meaning "something is wrong" - and whose rule, defined later in `styles.css`, won the cascade, so every Baffert runner and every five-horse favorite came up in the warning colour. Renamed `row--entry-flag`; `DistributionView` is untouched. **VERIFIED**: new `npm run check-entry-flags` (44 pure assertions - all three real spellings, case-insensitivity, the live-vs-entered field count in both directions, a scratched horse keeping BAFFERT but never taking FAV, co-favorites, an unpriced horse not blocking the favorite, all three real input shapes including the no-stored-decimal replay one, and the never-throws contract) with TWO negative controls: `/^B Baffert$/` fails 6 assertions and `FAVORITE_FIELD_SIZE = 6` fails 14, both exiting non-zero. The check also caught one of its OWN assertions written the wrong way round, which is recorded in the file. **Browser-verified on all four surfaces** in the agent's own `dev:preview` stack against a throwaway worktree-local database (never the real corpus), including the ingest preview driven with the REAL 4.2MB Del Mar 2026-09-06 Equibase fixture: 11 races parsed, 5 real Baffert runners flagged, and an independent DOM count confirming race 4 is the only race with exactly five live runners and the only one carrying a FAV tag - on which the Baffert horse (`Hughes (KY)`, 4/5) correctly carries BOTH. No console errors and `#root` intact on every surface. `check-entry-flags`, `check-module-bindings`, `check-routing`, `check-equibase-entries`, `check-ingest`, `check-schema` and `build` all green. **`check-static-app` NOT run** - out of scope in a worktree by house rule (D168), and the static app is out of scope entirely by the 2026-09-08 scope note. This is a GENUINE skip, not a provably-irrelevant one: `EntriesTable.jsx` is in the static app's import surface and this PR adds two imports to it, so the surface grows by `shared/entry-flags.js` and `EntryFlagTags.jsx` (both pure, no `node:` import, and `shared/betmath.js` was already reachable). Recorded, not treated as a blocker, per that decision. Worth noting the flags do work there: `scripts/build-static-payload.js` already ships `trainer`, `morning_line` and `morning_line_decimal` on every payload entry, so this is not a silent no-op on the phone. **Also measured, and reported to the user rather than built**: a rough offline backtest of the thesis itself over the 776 graded races on file - the five-horse-favorite rule is +10.4% ROI on n=20 (noise), the Baffert rule is -31.9% on n=163, and the same Baffert rule reads +7.1% if matched literally instead of by substring, which is the canonicalization point made in money. |
| D215 | LLM cards: a refused ticket LINE drops, the rest of the race is kept | - | PR [#279](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/279), branch `llm-partial-save` | merged | **User decision**, taken from D214's own offer: "treat blockers as warnings and throw them out." D214 measured the cost of the old behaviour - across the five reported Horseshoe Indianapolis races on 2026-09-10, 4-5 tickets parsed perfectly in every one and exactly ONE line blocked each race, so five paid generations produced nothing and ~22 good tickets were discarded. **The change is smaller than it sounds, and that is the point**: a blocking warning never meant "drop a parsed ticket." `parseColumnRow`, `parseTellerTicketString` and `buildTickets` each return WITHOUT pushing a ticket when they refuse a line, so by the time `persistLlmRace` ran, the bad ticket did not exist - the only thing the blanket 422 accomplished was throwing away the tickets that HAD parsed. Removing it is not "save a bad bet," it is "stop discarding the good ones," and no code that decides whether a line is legal changed at all. **Invariant 9 is satisfied, not bent**: the preview already renders `TicketsTable` from the same `parsed.tickets` the save writes, so the table a user confirms IS what Save stores, exactly as the invariant requires. The preview's per-warning label changed from `BLOCKING:` to `REFUSED, not saved:` and gained a line stating how many tickets a save would keep, because a warning that used to mean "this race cannot be saved" now means "this line will not be in it" and the old word would have been actively misleading. Save is gated on `tickets.length === 0` rather than on the presence of a refusal. **One case still refuses, deliberately (422)**: a race where EVERY line was refused. Saving it would write an empty race, which is indistinguishable from the model deciding no bet is worth making - a real, legitimate outcome the prompt explicitly asks for - and telling those two apart is exactly what the LLM_GENERATED bucket exists to measure (invariant 13). So "nothing usable, regenerate" stays a refusal rather than becoming a silent empty race. **Invariant 7 gets the record it requires**: a dropped line is a decision, so `persistLlmRace` now emits one `ticket_refused` trace event per refusal (correlation id, card, race, request, reason type, message) alongside the existing `ticket_added` events. Without it a card holding 2 of the 3 lines a model wrote would be an unexplained gap in the trace. The save response also returns `refused`, so the batch UI reports what was dropped rather than inferring it. **Scope: the LLM path ONLY - `server/human-cards.js` is untouched and still refuses.** Not an oversight. A human can fix the source text and re-parse, which is precisely the correction route invariant 9 mandates; silently dropping a line a person typed themselves would be the hand-editing that invariant forbids. A paid model response cannot be edited, which is what makes the LLM path different. **Batch flow**: "Regenerate all" no longer skips a race on a blocking warning - it saves and reports `saved - N ticket(s) saved, M line(s) refused: ...`. The results list is now keyed on a row HAVING a message rather than on its status not being 'saved', since a race can now be both saved and worth reporting. **VERIFIED**: a new section in `scripts/check-llm-cards.js` built on the real reported shape (a 4-horse trifecta box at a 50c base priced $6 instead of $12, beside a legal win bet and a legal exacta box) - the preview shows 2 tickets and 1 refusal, the save returns 201 with exactly `exacta_box,win` stored, `refused` comes back naming `below_minimum`, the card cost counts only the saved tickets ($10.00, not $16.00), the card reads back with 2 tickets, the decision-trace LOG carries exactly one `ticket_refused` and two `ticket_added` for that card (read from the log file the way `check-export.js` does, since there is no trace HTTP route), and an all-refused race still 422s and changes nothing. **With a negative control**: reverting `server/llm-cards.js` alone fails exactly 4 of them. The two PRE-EXISTING all-refused 422 tests (`unknown_program`, the D68 box regression) still pass unmodified - both are single-line responses where nothing survives, so the new rule refuses them identically. `check-llm-cards`, `check-human-picks`, `check-export`, `check-grading`, `check-replay`, `check-ingest`, `check-schema`, `check-module-bindings`, `check-routing`, `build` all green. **`check-pl` FAILS with 4 failures and they are NOT this branch's** - confirmed by reverting `server/llm-cards.js` alone and re-running, which reproduces the identical 4 on the untouched baseline. They are the same pre-existing failures D195 found and isolated the same way; the LLM_GENERATED bucket comes back absent entirely in that scenario. Re-flagged rather than quietly re-reported as known, because they have now been carried since D195 and they live in the same LLM save path this deliverable touches - worth its own deliverable. **`check-static-app` not run** - out of scope in a worktree (D168) and the static app is out of scope entirely by the standing 2026-09-08 decision; this one IS provably irrelevant, unlike D213/D214: `server/llm-cards.js` and `client/src/components/LlmCardModal.jsx` are not in the static app's seven-file import surface, and the static app ships no LLM code at all by construction. |
| D214 | The other printed-menu shape, a bet type carrying its base unit, and the column/teller routing trap between them | - | PR [#278](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/278), branch `menu-trailing-parenthetical` | merged | **User request** (finish the trailing-parenthetical form D213 explicitly left) **plus a user bug report** on Horseshoe Indianapolis 2026-09-10, races 1/5/6/8/9. Three fixes; the reports split cleanly into 'the parser was wrong' and 'the model was wrong', and saying which is which is most of this row. **(1) Trailing-parenthetical menus.** Saratoga names the bet first: `Exacta ($1), Quinella ($1), Trifecta (.50), Super (.10), Double ($1) 9 & 10 Pick 3 ($1) (9-11)`. D213 read none of it. A new `MENU_TRAILING` table plus `menuParenCents` reads it, applied LAST so the parenthetical wins a disagreement - which is exactly what the `Superfecta (10c min)` rule it REPLACES already did for one bet type, generalised rather than left as a special case. `menuParenCents` demands a money marker (`$`, a decimal point, or a cents word) and refuses a bare integer, the same rule and the same reason as D213's `MENU_AMOUNT`: nearly every parenthetical on a real menu is a race list (`(Races 1-2-3)`, `(9-11)`, `(4-7)`), so `Pick 4 (4)` is a race, not a $4 minimum. **A real pre-existing bug this found, not one anybody reported**: the replaced rule was `/Superfecta\s*\((\d+)c\s*min\)/`, which matches the exact spelling `(10c min)` and nothing else - so Del Mar's own `(10c min.)` (trailing period) and `(10-cent min)` both fell through to `$1 Superfecta` and **the home track's 10c superfecta minimum has read as $1 for the entire corpus**, refusing legal dime supers on 12 races. All three spellings now read 10c, pinned by a named assertion. **(2) `50 cent trifecta` as a bet type** (the reported race 9). `TELLER_LINE_RE`'s comment claims the grammars are 'provably disjoint' because no bet type starts with a digit - true of the BET_TYPES table, and the model wrote a type the table doesn't hold, putting the menu's base unit in front of it. The line matched the teller grammar and was then **SHREDDED on its own `/` separators into three fragments that each failed**, so the single reported warning was actually three. Fixed at both levels: `isColumnRow` routes any 3+-column pipe row to the column grammar whatever it leads with (a two-column oddity like `$2 EX BOX 1-2 | note` stays on the teller path, where it has always been), and `stripBaseUnitPrefix` reads `50 cent trifecta` as `trifecta` with a NON-blocking warning naming what it ignored. The prefix names the type only - the stake column is still what is bet, and it is still validated against the race's real menu, so a wrong prefix cannot buy a wrong-priced bet. **(3) Races 1/5/6/8 - the parser is right and the model is wrong.** All four wanted a 4-horse trifecta box; the base is 50c, so 24 combos cost $12.00; each had roughly $6-7 of its race bankroll left; each wrote `$6` in `<stake>` anyway, which is $0.25 a combo and unbuyable. Races 1 and 8 show the CORRECT arithmetic (`$0.50 x 24 combos = $12.00`) beside a `<stake>` of `$6` - the exact disagreement D162 already forbids. Races 5 and 6 are a genuinely new failure mode: the model annotated its own line as `invalid, see line below` and `invalid, priced below base) - dropped` **and emitted it anyway** - it decided against the ticket and then wrote it. One new prompt bullet says a line you have decided against must not be written at all (deleting it IS the action; there is no way to drop or flag a ticket from inside the block), and that an unaffordable ticket cannot be made affordable by shrinking `<stake>`. `PROMPT_TEMPLATE_VERSION` moves `fcfcd75bfd06` -> `d77fc1f078fa` on its own, as designed. **Stated plainly: this is the fifth prompt patch in the D145-D163 series and prompt patches in this series have recurred; it is a mitigation, not a guarantee.** **The measured argument for a structural fix, offered not taken**: in all five reported races 4-5 tickets parsed perfectly and exactly one line blocked the race, and `persistLlmRace` refuses the whole race (`nothing saved`) on any blocking warning, so the user paid for five generations and kept none of the ~22 good tickets in them. Letting a preview-confirmed save store the good tickets and report the refused one would end this class of report regardless of what the model writes, and it fits invariant 9 (the preview already shows exactly what Save would store). Not done here because it changes what money lands on a card, which is the user's call, not a bug fix. **VERIFIED, corpus-wide, the same method as D213**: the merged D213 parser was imported side by side with the new one and both run over all 488 distinct stored menus (1,471 races) - **18 menus change a minimum (34 races), every one correct**: 12 Del Mar races getting their real 10c superfecta back, and 22 Saratoga races reading `Quinella ($1)` 200->100, `Double ($1)` 200->100 and `Pick 3 ($1)` 50->100. No other track moves a cent, and no parenthetical race list was ever read as money. All 1,946 stored tickets on non-deleted days re-checked against the new minimums: no new sub-minimum ticket (the only 4 flagged are the same pre-existing Kentucky Downs `$5 Exacta` case D213 already recorded). Replaying the five reported races through the real `extractTicketBlock` -> `parseHumanPicksText` path: **race 9 goes from 3 blocking warnings to 0, with 5 tickets and one honest non-blocking warning**; races 1/5/6/8 stay blocked, correctly, on the one unbuyable line each. **Check coverage**: 12 new assertions in `scripts/check-human-picks.js` - Saratoga's whole menu, `Super (.10)`, race lists and bare integers refused in a parenthetical, Del Mar's three spellings, parenthetical-beats-leading precedence, D213's leading forms untouched, the un-shredded column row, the prefix read and announced, the stake still governing, an unknown type still refused, and a real teller line still splitting on `/`. **With a negative control**: reverting both source files fails exactly 7 assertions. **One PRE-EXISTING assertion was updated rather than left passing**: D213's bare-integer control asserted `pick3 === 50`, the fallback, because nothing read `Pick 3 ($1)` either - the trailing rule reads it now, so the expected value moved to $1 while the thing the control exists to prove is asserted directly and unchanged (`!== 1000`). `check-human-picks`, `check-llm-cards`, `check-equibase-entries`, `check-tip-staking`, `check-grading`, `check-equibase-otr`, `check-replay`, `check-ingest`, `check-schema`, `check-module-bindings`, `build` all green. `docs/prompts/llm-card-v1.md` updated with byte-identical text to the template, diff-verified. **`check-static-app` not run** - out of scope in a worktree (D168) and the static app is out of scope entirely by the standing 2026-09-08 user decision; as in D213 this is a real skip rather than a provably-irrelevant one, since `shared/betmath.js` and `shared/parsers/human-picks.js` are both in the static app's import surface. **Also confirmed here**: the user's earlier Woodbine 2026-09-10 report (all 8 races blocked at $0.20) is fixed by D213 - all 8 of that day's stored menus now parse to a 20c trifecta. It was reported against a server still running pre-D213 code. |
| D213 | Fix: a legal 20-cent trifecta box was refused - the printed wager menu's own minimum was unreadable | - | PR [#277](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/277), branch `claude/trifecta-box-minimum-price-66ba76` | merged | **User-reported**, as an LLM-generation failure: `BLOCKED Race 1: $0.20 per combo is below the $0.50 minimum for trifecta box.` **The model was right and the parser was wrong** - which is why this is the first fix in the D160/D161/D162/D163 box-arithmetic series that changes no prompt text at all. Traced to the stored request (`llm_card_requests` id 438, Woodbine race 1, 2026-09-10, template `fcfcd75bfd06`): the ticket read `trifecta box | #2,#4,#5,#6 | $4.80 | ... (4 x 3 x 2 = 24 combos; $0.20 x 24 combos = $4.80)`. The combo count is right (D160/D161), the arithmetic is shown (D161) and its product equals the `<stake>` column (D162) - every earlier fix in this series did its job. That race's own printed menu, stored verbatim on the row and rendered into the prompt, reads `Rolling Double / Exacta / 0.20 Trifecta / 0.20 Superfecta 0.20 Pick 3 (Races 1-2-3)/ $1 Swinger`: Woodbine genuinely sells a 20c trifecta, the prompt told the model to price per combination in the menu's base unit, and it did. **Root cause**: `shared/betmath.js`'s `MENU_PATTERNS` matched the amount as `(\$[\d.]+|\d+c)` - a `$`-led token or `Nc` and nothing else - so `0.20 Trifecta` matched no pattern, `parseWagerMenu` fell through to `BET.minimums.trifecta` (50c), and `human-picks.js`'s `wagerLimitsFor` refused a ticket the window sells. The menu amount is now one `MENU_AMOUNT` source shared by every pattern, covering `$1`/`$.50`, `50c`/`20¢`/`20 Cent`/`20 cents`/`20-cent`, and a **bare decimal** (`0.20`, `.50`); conversion delegates to `parseMoneyToken`, so the menu and the teller grammar read money through one function rather than a second hand-rolled `menuCents`. **A bare INTEGER is deliberately NOT an amount**, and the corpus is why: `Exacta ($1), Trifecta (.50), Super (.10), Double ($1) 9 & 10 Pick 3 ($1) (9-11)` is a real stored menu, and accepting bare integers would read that race pair as a $10 Pick 3. It is now a named negative control in the check. **Exactor/Triactor added in the same pass**, not as scope creep: they are the Canadian spellings of the exacta/trifecta and they are what Fort Erie and Assiniboia Downs print for the same 20c bet, so leaving them out would have fixed Woodbine and left the identical live bug standing at two other tracks in this corpus. **VERIFIED against the whole corpus, not a fixture**: the pre-change parser was reconstructed verbatim beside the new one and both were run over all 488 distinct stored `races.wager_menu` values (1,471 races). **34 menus change a minimum, covering 52 races, and every single one is a 20c change at Woodbine, Fort Erie or Assiniboia Downs** - real 20-cent tracks, zero false positives, no menu at any other track moves by a cent. A further 104 menus change only their `wagerMenuOffered` labels, in every case from 'the menu never named this bet' to naming it, with the minimum identical - which is precisely what that function's own docstring says it exists to report honestly. Also checked the other direction: every one of the 1,690 stored tickets on a non-deleted day was re-tested against its race's newly-parsed minimum, and the change creates no new sub-minimum ticket - the only 4 flagged are a pre-existing Kentucky Downs `$5 Exacta` case that the old parser read identically. **Check coverage**: a new pure section in `scripts/check-human-picks.js` - all four printed forms, the offered-labels assertion (needed because `.50 Trifecta` and `50 Cent Trifecta` parse to the same value as the fallback, so the minimum alone cannot distinguish 'read it' from 'never saw it'), the bare-integer negative control, the untouched `$N`/`Nc` forms, and the refused ticket itself parsing end to end into one 24-combo $4.80 box - plus the inverse, that the same ticket is still blocked on a menu whose trifecta really is 50c. **With a negative control**: reverting `shared/betmath.js` alone makes exactly 6 of the new assertions fail, one of them reproducing the reported message verbatim. `check-human-picks`, `check-equibase-entries`, `check-tip-staking`, `check-llm-cards`, `check-grading`, `check-equibase-otr`, `check-ingest`, `check-schema`, `check-module-bindings`, `build` all green. **`check-static-app` not run** - out of scope in a worktree by house rule (D168), and the static Pages app is out of scope entirely by the standing 2026-09-08 user decision recorded in CLAUDE.md. Unlike D212's skip this one is NOT provably irrelevant: `shared/betmath.js` is one of the seven files the static app imports from outside `static/`, and `TicketBuilder.jsx` calls `wagerLimitsFor` there, so the phone builder's live per-combo minimum moves with this change - in the same direction, toward the printed menu. Recorded as a real skip rather than dressed up as a clean sweep. **Noted, not fixed**: a trailing-parenthetical menu (`Trifecta (.50)`, `Super (.10)`) still parses no amount. It is harmless today because those values coincide with `BET.minimums` for exacta/trifecta/superfecta and the only ones it gets wrong (`daily_double`, `pick3`) are multi-race types the ticket parser refuses anyway; it was left out to keep the blast radius to the one shape that was actually blocking bets. |
| D212 | Fix: Del Mar's own track code was the one registry entry that carried no timezone | - | branch `dmr-code-alias` | merged | **User-reported**, with the diagnosis already correct on arrival: `shared/track-codes.js`'s Del Mar entry read `aliases: ['DELMARRACINGCOM']` while every other entry in the 39-track registry lists its own code (`KD` carries `'KD'`, `SAR` carries `'SAR'`, and so on). `canonicalizeTrack` compares its input against `display` and `aliases` and **nothing else** - it never consults `code` - so the bare string `'DMR'` fell through to the derived-code fallback and came back `{ code: 'DMR', display: 'DMR', recognized: false, timezone: null }`, where `'SAR'` and `'KD'` both resolved correctly with their zones. **Confirmed by running the real function over the registry parsed out of its own source**: 39 entries, exactly one whose own code does not round-trip. Fix is the one word the report proposed - `'DMR'` added to that entry's aliases - and no other entry needed it. **The alternative was considered and rejected**: teaching the lookup to match `t.code` implicitly would make the class structurally impossible rather than merely asserted, but it adds a second comparison rule to a function whose whole contract is "one spelling table, one comparison," against a registry where 38 of 39 entries already state the code explicitly and a tidiness check (`no alias merely repeats its own display name`) already polices that table's shape. The new assertion below is written on BEHAVIOUR, not on the shape of `aliases`, so it stays the correct check if that decision is ever revisited. **The report called the impact latent, and it is - but one step less latent than it looked.** Both entries parsers emit the display name as `.track` (verified against `tests/fixtures/equibase-entries/DMR090726USA-EQB.expected.json` and `tests/fixtures/equibase-apify/parseforge-dmr-2026-09-07.expected.json`, both `"Del Mar"`), so every real save resolves correctly and no stored row is wrong. But `shared/parsers/equibase-apify-parseforge.js:158` reads `const track = trackRows[0].trackName \|\| trackCode;` - **live code, not a hypothetical future parser** - so an Apify capture printing `trackCode` without `trackName` hands the save layer a bare code today. That fallback is correct for 38 of the 39 tracks *precisely because* each of those lists its own code; for Del Mar alone it would have stored the day as display `"DMR"`, `recognized: false`, and `server/race-calendar.js:42-50` would then route every one of that day's races into `unplaceable`, making the bulk of this corpus vanish from the D209-D211 calendar with no error anywhere. `scripts/pull-apify-results.js:105` (`canonicalizeTrack(rows[0].trackName ?? code)`) has the same shape. **VERIFIED**: `scripts/check-track-codes.js` gained a new section asserting the general form - every entry's own `code`, passed through the real `canonicalizeTrack`, must resolve to that entry's own code, display, `recognized: true` and `tz` - plus one named assertion pinning the specific case so a regression reads as itself rather than as one line in a list of 39. **With a negative control**: reverting the alias alone makes exactly those 2 assertions fail (`DMR -> {"code":"DMR","display":"DMR","recognized":false,"timezone":null}`) and the check exit non-zero, proving it catches the bug it was written for rather than passing vacuously. `npm run check-track-codes` and `npm run check-race-calendar` both green (the two the report named), and a full sweep of everything downstream of this module: `check-ingest` (the real server round-trip, which is where canonicalization actually runs), `check-schema`, `check-equibase-entries`, `check-equibase-apify-parseforge`, `check-module-bindings`, `build` - all green. **`check-static-app` not run** - out of scope in a worktree by house rule (D168) - and in this case provably not implicated either: the static app's seven-file import surface (and its transitive imports) does not reach `shared/track-codes.js` at all, re-derived rather than assumed. **Deliverable number**: `npm run allocate-deliverable` first returned D208, which collides with the real, merged D208 - the shared counter was four behind because the cloud sessions that shipped D208-D211 minted those numbers by hand (each of their own rows says so). Released that claim and advanced the counter honestly past D209/D210/D211, each recorded as released and naming the real ledger row it collides with, rather than leaving a counter that would hand the next agent a taken number; the real claim landed at D212. **Noted, not fixed**: PRs #264-#275 have merged since D211 with no ledger rows at all. |
| D211 | Race day calendar, phase C-3: the client matrix view | - | PR [#263](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/263), branch `claude/race-day-calendar-matrix-s3br3o` | merged | **User confirmation ("Yes")** to continue past D210, the third and final phase of `docs/requirements/race-day-calendar.md`. New `client/src/components/RaceDayCalendar.jsx`, wired via a new "Race calendar" button on `RaceDayList.jsx`, a `calendar` route in `routes.js`, and a `calendar` view block in `App.jsx`. Reads D210's `GET /api/calendar` through a new `getCalendar(date)` in `client/src/api.js` (a plain `fetch(...).then(asJson)`, matching every other read there). **The date input defaults to today's PACIFIC calendar date**, computed explicitly via `Intl.DateTimeFormat` against `America/Los_Angeles` rather than a bare `new Date()` - the same reasoning D210's own row already gives: a browser whose OS clock sits in a different zone would otherwise default to the wrong racing day right when it matters most (this was not a hypothetical during verification - the sandbox's own system clock read a full calendar day ahead of Pacific at the time, `2026-09-10` vs. the correctly-computed `2026-09-09`, which is exactly the failure mode this design choice exists to prevent). Column headers reuse `shared/race-calendar.js`'s exported `CALENDAR_START_HOUR`/`CALENDAR_COLUMNS` constants rather than re-deriving or hardcoding "10:00 AM" through "9:00 AM" a second time. Every populated cell reads `Race N - <postTimePacific>` STRAIGHT OFF the API response with no client-side time math at all, rendered as a clickable `.linkish` span (`onOpenDay(raceDayId)`) into that track's `/day/:id` - multiple races landing in one track's hour stack vertically in the same cell, exactly as the plan specified. The track name cell is ALSO clickable to the same day (a small, in-scope UX addition beyond the letter of the spec, which only required the cell itself to link). A footer line reports the total `unplaceable` count summed across every track whenever it's non-zero, never hiding a race the grid couldn't place. **New CSS**: `.grid--wide-scroll` (`overflow-x: auto` on the wrapper, `white-space: nowrap` on the table) - a 25-column table (Track + 24 hours) is wider than the 1440px shell at any realistic column width, so it scrolls on its own rather than stretching the whole page; this is the first table in the MAIN client (as opposed to the separate static Pages app, which already has its own such rule for a different reason, D157) that needed one. **VERIFIED**: `npm run build` and `npm run check-module-bindings` both green (a presentation-only diff otherwise - no schema, no server route, no shared-module change). **Browser-verified for real, not merely built** - this sandbox has no `chromium-cli`, so `playwright` was installed with `npm install --no-save playwright` (confirmed via `git status` that `package.json`/`package-lock.json` were untouched, then removed again with `npm uninstall --no-save playwright` after verification) and pointed at the pre-installed `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium` binary directly (the freshly-installed `playwright` package's own expected browser revision didn't match what was pre-installed, so `executablePath` was required - matches the CLAUDE.md environment note's own documented workaround). Seeded a throwaway SQLite database (never the human's `data/betsheet.sqlite`, never touched) with four race days for the real computed Pacific "today" - Del Mar (`America/Los_Angeles`, two races both in the 10am column), Kentucky Downs (`America/Chicago`, converting to the 11am column), Saratoga (`America/New_York`, converting to noon), and an unregistered "Some Brand New Fairgrounds" track - and drove the agent's own `dev:preview` stack (api 8795/vite 5185, never the human's ports) against it. The resulting screenshot confirmed every element of the design at once: Del Mar's two races stacked in one cell, the correct Pacific-converted times and PDT labels on every row, tracks ordered by earliest post with the unregistered track last (it placed nothing), and the footer correctly reading "2 races could not be placed" (Del Mar's own null-post-time race 3, plus the unregistered track's one race). Clicking the populated "Race 1 - 10:15 AM PDT" cell navigated the browser to the real `/day/1` Del Mar page, screenshotted and confirmed showing all 3 races including the unplaceable one (post "?"). **One console error was found and run to ground rather than dismissed**: a "Failed to load resource: 404" message on every load - isolated with a `response`/`console` listener to `GET /favicon.ico`, confirmed pre-existing (this dev app has never shipped a favicon) and unrelated to this change, not a regression from anything touched here. Preview server processes and the seeded throwaway database were torn down after verification; no artifact from this pass was committed. Full record: `docs/requirements/race-day-calendar.md`, now marked ALL THREE PHASES DELIVERED. |
| D210 | Race day calendar, phase C-2: read-only `GET /api/calendar` endpoint | - | PR [#263](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/263), branch `claude/race-day-calendar-matrix-s3br3o` | merged | **User confirmation ("Yes")** to continue past D209, the second phase of `docs/requirements/race-day-calendar.md`. New `server/race-calendar.js`, modeled directly on `server/distribution.js`'s shape - a small standalone read-only reporting router layered over existing tables, exactly like the distribution and P/L endpoints, with no writes of its own. `GET /api/calendar?date=YYYY-MM-DD` queries every `race_days` row for that date with `deleted_at IS NULL`, joined to its `races` (`number`, `post_time`), and for each race calls D209's `placeRacePacific(date, postTime, timezone)` - `timezone` coming from `canonicalizeTrack(track).timezone`, not a second lookup table. Response: `{date, tracks: [{raceDayId, track, trackCode, timezone, races: [{number, postTimePacific, hourBucket}], unplaceable: [race numbers]}]}`. **`unplaceable` is not an afterthought - it's the direct payoff of D209's `placeRacePacific` already returning `null` for exactly this case**: a race with no parseable post time, or an unregistered track carrying `timezone: null`, is routed into that list rather than silently vanishing from the response, matching invariant 11's standing preference for a visible gap over a silent one. Tracks are returned pre-sorted by their earliest post's Pacific column (ties broken by track name), so a caller rendering response order already reads "who's up soonest" without its own sort. `400` on a missing or malformed `date` query param - the one input-validation surface this endpoint has. Mounted in `server/index.js` alongside every other reporting router (`app.use('/api', calendarRouter)`, next to `distributionRouter`). **D number determined by eyeballing the ledger again, not the allocator - same mechanism as D209's row, worth noting rather than treating as a one-off**: `npm run allocate-deliverable` was called a second time in this session and, as expected given the fresh local counter has no memory of the real ledger, it returned `D204` - colliding with the real D204 already in this file. Released (`--release 204`) and D210 used instead (one past the real highest, D209), the identical cross-machine fallback CLAUDE.md's Gotchas documents. **VERIFIED**: `scripts/check-race-calendar.js` (already created for D209's pure module) gained a second section that boots the real server on a seeded temp SQLite database - three real tracks in three real, distinct IANA zones (Del Mar/`America/Los_Angeles`, Kentucky Downs/`America/Chicago`, Saratoga/`America/New_York`) all racing on 2026-09-12 (firmly mid-DST for every US zone, so offsets are fixed and hand-computable: EDT -4, CDT -5, PDT -7), with post times chosen to land in ascending, easy-to-verify Pacific columns - Del Mar's own 10:15 AM PDT at column 0, Kentucky Downs' 1:00 PM CDT converting to 11:00 AM PDT at column 1, Saratoga's 3:05 PM EDT converting to 12:05 PM PDT at column 2 - each hand-computed independently before being asserted, not copied from a first passing run. Also seeded: a race with a `NULL` `post_time` (correctly landing in `unplaceable`, not silently dropped), an entirely unregistered track ("Some Brand New Fairgrounds," a derived code with `timezone: null` - correctly carries zero placed races and its one race in `unplaceable`, never a guessed bucket), a soft-deleted day on the SAME date (correctly absent from the response entirely, invariant 12), and a genuinely different date (correctly absent from a query for the seeded date). Confirms the response's track ordering (Del Mar, Kentucky Downs, Saratoga, then the unregistered track last since it placed nothing), both `400` refusals (missing `date`, malformed `date`), and that a date with zero race days returns `{date, tracks: []}` rather than an error. 12 new assertions, all green alongside the 30 pure D209 assertions already in the same file. Full regression sweep green: `npm run build`, `check-module-bindings`, `check-schema`, `check-ingest` (the real server round-trip over HTTP, confirming the newly-mounted router changed nothing about existing routes). No schema change - the endpoint is read-only over tables that already exist. Phase C-3 (the client matrix view rendering this endpoint as tracks × hourly columns) remains specified but not scheduled. Full record: `docs/requirements/race-day-calendar.md`. |
| D209 | Race day calendar, phase C-1: per-track timezone data + pure hour-bucketing helper | - | PR [#263](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/263), branch `claude/race-day-calendar-matrix-s3br3o` | merged | **User request 2026-09-10**: a calendar view - tracks x 24 hourly columns - to get to a track's race day as close to post as possible; specified first in `docs/requirements/race-day-calendar.md` (not scheduled at write time), then the user clarified mid-plan that they are always in Pacific, so every displayed time must be Pacific - both the column a race lands in and the time printed in its cell, never a track-local mix, so a viewer is never doing the mental timezone math the feature exists to remove. This phase (C-1) builds the conversion that decision depends on; nothing yet reads or writes the database - C-2 (a read-only `/api/calendar` endpoint) and C-3 (the client matrix view) remain not scheduled. **`shared/track-codes.js`'s 39-entry registry gained a `tz` field**, each checked individually against the track's real city/county rather than assumed from its state or region - two corrections the individual check caught that a state-level assumption would have missed: Kentucky Downs (Franklin, Simpson County) and Atokad Downs (South Sioux City, NE) are both genuinely Central despite sitting in states usually thought of as Eastern-leaning, and Sandy Ridge Racing (Ashland, Boyd County, KY) is Eastern despite being in Kentucky, because Boyd County sits on the state's eastern edge. `canonicalizeTrack` now returns the zone as `timezone` - `null` for an unrecognized/derived-code track, since a derived code carries no real location to convert from and guessing one would be exactly the silent-wrong-hour failure this feature exists to prevent. **New pure `shared/race-calendar.js`**: `localWallClockToUtc(dateStr, timeStr, ianaZone)` converts a track's printed local post time into a real UTC instant via the standard DST-safe two-pass `Intl.DateTimeFormat` technique (format a UTC guess in the target zone, then correct by the resulting offset) - no new dependency, matching this codebase's existing hand-rolled-over-a-library preference (the same call `server/zip-read.js` made for D127); `hourBucket` reads the column 0-23 on the grid whose first column is 10:00 AM Pacific; `formatPacific` is the ONLY time string a viewer ever sees - always Pacific, with the correct seasonal PST/PDT label read from the platform rather than hardcoded; `placeRacePacific` is the one combined call a future `/api/calendar` endpoint needs, returning `null` (never a guessed bucket) for an unparseable post time or a track with no known zone. **D number determined by eyeballing the ledger, not the allocator, and this is worth recording rather than glossing over**: this session runs in a fresh ephemeral environment with no prior `.git` history, so its local deliverable-number counter had no memory of D203-D207 already being claimed elsewhere - `npm run allocate-deliverable` returned `D203`, which collides with the real D203 row already in this ledger ("Data-driven deliverable-number allocation"). That claim was released (`--release 203`, reason recorded in the tool's own log) and D209 used instead (one past the real highest, D207), exactly the cross-machine limitation CLAUDE.md's own Gotchas already documents ("a cloud session has its own `.git` and does not see this one's counter... the eyeball-the-ledger fallback is still the right move there"). **VERIFIED**: new `scripts/check-race-calendar.js`, 30 assertions - known winter/summer UTC offsets for two zones; the REAL 2026-03-08 US spring-forward boundary (two weeks either side of the actual transition date, not merely a same-track two-season pair, which correctly lands on the identical Pacific column either way since every US/Canada zone in this registry shifts DST on the same calendar - the two-season case is asserted too, to prove the printed PST/PDT label still flips even when the column doesn't); the hour-grid's boundary columns (10 AM -> 0, 9 AM -> 23, midnight -> 14); `formatPacific`'s seasonal label; `placeRacePacific`'s refusal on an unparseable time or a `null` zone; and every one of the registry's 9 distinct zones actually converting a sample post time, tying this module to `track-codes.js`'s data directly rather than trusting it in isolation. `scripts/check-track-codes.js` updated in the same pass: its regex-based registry-shape parser (it reads the SOURCE FILE rather than an export, deliberately, so it can assert the shape of every entry) widened to capture the new `tz` field, plus new assertions - every entry's `tz` is non-empty and a real zone `Intl.DateTimeFormat` accepts (an oracle that catches a typo'd zone name, not just a plausible-looking string), the existing round-trip check now also compares `timezone`, and the unrecognized/empty-track cases assert `timezone === null`. Full regression sweep green: `check-track-codes` (39/39 registry entries, all new assertions), `check-race-calendar` (new), `check-schema`, `check-module-bindings`, `check-ingest` (canonicalization end-to-end over a real server, confirming the additive field changed nothing about existing save behavior), `npm run build`. No schema or server change. `check-static-app` not run - out of scope in a worktree by house rule (D168); neither changed file is in the static app's import surface. |

Confirmed by running `parseApifyParseforgeDataset` against the real `parseforge-dmr-2026-09-07.json` fixture: all 15 warnings it produces (`implausible_weight` ×3, `no_wager_menu` ×11, `scratch_status_inferred` ×1) came back with no `message`. Not a data bug - `type`/`race`/`horse`/`weight`/etc. were always correct; only the prose was missing. **Why nothing caught this for three deliverables**: both parsers' check scripts (`check-equibase-apify-parseforge.js`, `check-equibase-apify-results.js`) assert on `type` and structured fields via hand-counted assertions against the real fixtures, but never asserted `.message` exists and never rendered through `ParsePreview` - the same blind spot D172's `check-module-bindings.js` was built to close for a different class of render-only bug. **Fix**: every warning site in both parsers (13 in `equibase-apify-parseforge.js`, 14 in `equibase-apify-results.js`) now carries a `message:` string, worded in the same style as the HTML parser's own messages (`Race N: ...`) - both goldens regenerated and diffed to confirm the ONLY change is the new `message` fields, nothing else moved. **Check-script assertion added, the thing that should have caught this**: both check scripts gained a `checkMessages(label, warnings)` helper run against every warning set the script already produces (every track/context path, and every malformed-input refusal), asserting `typeof w.message === 'string' && w.message.trim() !== ''` for every warning - so a future warning site missing a message fails the check immediately instead of shipping silently. **VERIFIED**: `check-equibase-apify-parseforge` and `check-equibase-apify-results` both green with the new message assertions and regenerated goldens; full regression sweep green: `check-apify-equibase-ingest`, `check-apify-equibase-client`, `check-equibase-apify-entries-preview`, `check-equibase-apify-results-pull`, `check-pull-apify-cli`, `check-module-bindings`, `build`. No schema or server change - `check-ingest`/`check-schema` not applicable (only `shared/parsers/*.js` and their check scripts/fixtures changed). Not browser-verified against a real live pull (that costs real money per invariant 9's Apify carve-out); verified instead by confirming `ParsePreview.jsx`'s exact render expression (`w.message`) is populated for every warning shape both parsers can produce, including every refusal path. `check-static-app` not run - out of scope in a worktree by house rule (D168); neither changed parser is in the static app's import surface. |

Client: `pullApifyResults(dayId, correlationId)` added to `api.js`; `ResultsPanel.jsx` gained a "Pull results from Apify" button beside "Upload chart PDF" and the same cost-warning notice text `NewRaceDay.jsx` already uses for the entries side. **VERIFIED**: new `scripts/check-equibase-apify-results-pull.js` mirrors `check-equibase-apify-entries-preview.js`'s two-part shape - `pullApifyResults()` unit-tested against a fake client (real actor id, single-track `tracks` array, `resultType: 'results'`, `includeWagers` defaulting true, the day's own date/track feeding the call, a real parse with scratch derivation exercised against a seeded `entriesByRace`, `apifyRunId` riding the return value), plus the real Express route over a real temp server seeded with the real D192/D193 Del Mar 2026-09-07 entries fixture (an unknown day 404s before any network call; the seeded real day with no `APIFY_TOKEN` fails inside `getApifyClient()` before any network I/O, with the real named error; a soft-deleted day refuses 410) - never a real, billed call. | D203 | Data-driven deliverable-number allocation | - | PR [#254](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/254), branch `claude/deliverable-number-allocation-6c23dd` | merged | **User request**: "Multiple agents repeatedly claim the same D number and are causing merge conflicts." The problem was confirmed real twice before writing any code: D200 was independently claimed by two concurrent sessions (see D201's own row - the loser renumbered on rebase), and a `git log --oneline --all` sweep across every worktree on this machine found a THIRD, still-unmerged branch (`apify-entries-live-pull-ui`) that had already minted `D202` locally, with no PR yet and no way for a new tool to have seen it coming except by looking. New `scripts/lib/deliverable-numbers.js` + `scripts/allocate-deliverable.js`: `npm run allocate-deliverable -- --title "..."` atomically claims the next D number from a SQLite counter (`better-sqlite3`, already a dependency) instead of a person or an agent reading DELIVERABLES.md/CLAUDE.md and incrementing the highest row by eye - the exact read-then-write race that produced the D200 collision. **Where the database lives is the load-bearing design choice**: `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`, confirmed against this machine's real state via `git worktree list` (one `.git` directory shared by the main checkout and all five active `.claude/worktrees/*` at the time of building this) - a file dropped there is visible to every concurrent worktree agent without any path needing to be agreed on in advance, and it is structurally never git-tracked, so the allocator's own database can never itself become the merge conflict it exists to prevent. `BETSHEET_DELIVERABLE_DB` overrides the path (mirrors `BETSHEET_DB`/`BETSHEET_LOG_DIR`'s existing convention) - used by the check script for isolation, and available if a separate clone (betsheet-alt) or future networked setup ever needs its own counter. **Numbers are never recycled**, even via `--release` (an annotation only, never freeing the number) - the same reasoning as invariant 12 (race-day ids never reused) and the D77-A/D77-B split-ID precedent: an abandoned claim may already be cited in a branch name or an open PR by the time anyone notices. **The tool's own first seed encodes the collision it was built to avoid**: `DEFAULT_NEXT_NUMBER = 203`, not 202, specifically because D202 was already spoken for by the time this was built, on a branch this tool has no way to see (it doesn't exist there yet). **Dogfooded for real**: this row's own ID, D203, was claimed by running the finished CLI against the actual shared database in `.git/`, not assigned by eye - confirmed the claim landed by re-listing it and by finding `.git/deliverable-numbers.sqlite` freshly created at the expected path. **VERIFIED**: new `scripts/check-deliverable-numbers.js`, every case against a throwaway `BETSHEET_DELIVERABLE_DB` file, never the real shared one - path resolution both with and without the override; a fresh database seeds to `DEFAULT_NEXT_NUMBER` exactly once and reopening it is a no-op; the counter survives across close/reopen; an empty title is refused rather than silently consuming a number; `--release` marks a claim released, is idempotent on a second call, and never lets the next claim reuse the released number; releasing an unknown number is refused; `setNextNumber`'s disaster-recovery escape hatch refuses to move the counter into an already-claimed number without an explicit force flag. **The load-bearing case**: 25 REAL child processes (`node scripts/allocate-deliverable.js`, not an in-process function call) spawned concurrently against ONE database, asserted to return 25 DISTINCT numbers forming one contiguous run from the seed with no gap and no repeat, and the persisted counter and ledger table both confirmed to have advanced by exactly 25 - the actual shape of the bug (independent OS processes racing a shared file, not threads sharing memory), not a weaker approximation of it. CLI argument handling covered separately: no-flags usage exits 2, a real claim's stdout is EXACTLY `D<number>` with nothing else (so it is safe to capture in a shell variable), `--list`/`--status`/`--release` all round-trip correctly, and releasing an unknown number exits 1 rather than exiting 0 on a no-op. All 31 assertions pass. `npm run build` green (no client/server code touched), `npm run check-module-bindings` green (new modules, correctly bound). No schema or server change - `check-ingest`/`check-schema` not applicable, this is tooling outside the app. `check-static-app` not run - out of scope in a worktree by house rule (D168); none of the new files are in the static app's import surface. **Not cross-machine**: a separate clone or a cloud session has its own `.git` and cannot see this counter - documented as a known limitation in both CLAUDE.md's architecture row and its Gotchas entry, not silently assumed away. |

This codebase's own `fetchEntries`/`fetchResults` (`server/apifyEquibase.js`) had never set `maxItems` at all on any call, live or otherwise - the actor's own default is too low for a big field and silently TRUNCATES rather than erroring, which is exactly the failure mode both of D204's live pulls hit (2 races, then 9, never 14, no error either time). Fixed identically to how `includeWagers` already overrides the actor's own too-low default (D197): both functions now default `maxItems: 10000` - the user's own confirmed-working value, and generously above the largest single-track single-day row count anywhere in this repo's fixtures (123) - overridable via an explicit `maxItems` in the call. **Confirmed cost-neutral for a normal card**: `shared/parsers/registry.js`'s `costModel` for this actor bills per `resultItem`/`racePage` actually PRODUCED ($0.007/$0.045 each), not per `maxItems` requested, so raising the cap only matters when the true row count would have exceeded it - it does not inflate the price of an ordinary card. No other file touched. `check-static-app` not applicable - this file is not in the static app's import surface. |
