| D191 | Parser comparison: `compare-parsers.js` + `shared/parsers/compare.js` (M-3) | - | [#239](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/239) / `multi-parser-entries-m3` | merged | **User request 2026-09-09**, M-3 of `docs/requirements/multi-parser-entries-ingest.md`, picked up the same day D190 unblocked it (a second real parser existing) - registering the parser and building the harness that compares against it were kept as two separate deliverables even though they landed on the same day. **No live fetching** - inherits M-2's decision (finding 9): `scripts/compare-parsers.js <date> <TRACK_CODE> --dir <dir> [--parsers id1,id2,...] [--write-report path.json]` locates one file per requested parser under `--dir` using a new `shared/parsers/registry.js` export, `EXTENSIONS_BY_SOURCE_KIND` (`{ html: /\.html?$/i, apify: /\.json$/i }`), and reports a parser UNAVAILABLE for the run - never fatal - when nothing under `--dir` produces the requested track/date through it. The pure diff, `shared/parsers/compare.js`'s `compareParsedDays(baseline, challenger, {baselineFieldsNotProvided, challengerFieldsNotProvided})`, is ALWAYS baseline-vs-challenger, never symmetric peer comparison, matching the scope's own stated rule that the registry's default parser is the trusted reference until a comparison process says otherwise. Horses are matched by `nameKey` (`shared/parsers/human-picks.js` - the one horse-name normalizer in this codebase), deliberately NOT by `programNumber`, since the field itself can be exactly the thing under test (a real parser bug could print the wrong program number, and matching on it first would hide that bug behind a "missing horse" report instead of surfacing it as a mismatch). Every field is classified from each side's `fieldsNotProvided`: neither side claims it -> skipped entirely; only the CHALLENGER lacks it -> `baselineOnlyFields` (the challenger's real gap); only the BASELINE lacks it -> `challengerOnlyFields` (the scope's own term, "additive value" - reported separately from mismatches on purpose, so a challenger with broader coverage but a few real formatting bugs doesn't read as worse than it is on net); both claim it -> diffed value-for-value, and a disagreement is a mismatch. The scope's two data-quality examples (getascraper's double/triple-space name artifact, a post-position sequence gap) are GENERALISED rather than reimplemented literally, since getascraper isn't a registered parser - both checks apply to any registered parser's output, not one vendor's alone. Every comparison actually performed appends one row to `data/parser_comparisons.jsonl` (JSON-lines, gitignored, append-only - added to `.gitignore` in the same PR) - mirroring M-2's own reasoning for `ingest_runs.jsonl`: the decision rule for ever proposing a new default (a minimum sample across many runs, zero critical regressions) needs a ledger that persists across invocations, which this script's real-work-free CLI process can provide but nothing inside it ever applies automatically - that reading is left to a person. **No real matched track/date pair exists between the two registered parsers' own fixtures** (the HTML set covers 2026-09-06/07/10, the Apify set covers 2026-09-09, zero overlap) - flagged explicitly rather than faked around: `shared/parsers/compare.js` is unit-tested with `scripts/check-compare-parsers.js` against hand-built synthetic parses engineered to exercise every code path in one pass (a race existing on only one side, a race-count mismatch, a real race-level field mismatch vs. a structural coverage gap correctly NOT flagged as one, an entry matched despite a scratched-status disagreement, a horse missing from each side, the type-coercion fix, both data-quality checks firing on only the correct side, and a baseline/challenger role-swap sanity check - 20 assertions, all independently reasoned through before being written, not copied from a first passing run), while the CLI's OWN file-discovery and honest-unavailability reporting is separately exercised against the two real fixture directories together. **Two real bugs were found and fixed DURING that verification, not shipped and discovered by a later comparison run** (findings 14 and 15 in the requirements doc): (1) the first draft passed the REQUESTED track/date as `context` to every parser during file discovery; `equibase-html`'s `context.track`/`context.date` are a FALLBACK used only when a page's own markup can't be read (`pageTrack = track ?? header...`), not a filter, so passing the request there made the parser report back whatever was asked for regardless of the file's real content - a request for Indianapolis "matched" a real Del Mar page. Fixed by passing only `context.trackCode` during discovery, which is harmless to `equibase-html` (it destructures a differently-named key and ignores the extra one) and is the only context key the Apify parser reads - and that parser only SELECTS among rows already present in a file, never invents one, so it carries no version of this risk. (2) Re-running the fixed CLI against the real DMR HTML fixture and a small hand-built Apify-shaped JSON file (built ONLY to exercise the "compared" code path end to end through the real CLI, since no real matched pair exists to build one from - never committed as a fixture) surfaced that `equibase-html` prints `postPosition`/`weight` as PRINTED STRINGS ("1", "124") while `equibase-apify-parseforge` gives numbers (`1`, `124`) - the identical real value, on the identical real horse (Broheim, race 1), reported as two separate "mismatches" by a strict `!==`. The SAME string/number gap had silently disabled the post-position-gap check on the `equibase-html` side entirely, since `Number.isInteger("1")` is `false` for every one of its entries - that check had never actually run on the baseline side at all until this fix. Both resolved by coercing through `Number` before comparing whenever both sides look numeric (`valuesEqual`, and the same coercion inside the gap check), re-verified end to end to confirm the two false mismatches on Broheim disappeared (entry-mismatch count on that real run dropped from 1 to 0) without masking any genuine disagreement - the check script's own synthetic case for a REAL weight difference (120 vs 121) still flags correctly. **VERIFIED**: `npm run check-compare-parsers` - all 20 synthetic assertions pass. Real CLI runs against the two real fixture directories together (a temp directory holding both the real DMR HTML file and the real IND/KD Apify JSON, never committed): requesting `DMR`/2026-09-06 correctly finds the HTML baseline and reports the Apify challenger `UNAVAILABLE`; requesting `IND`/2026-09-09 correctly reports the HTML baseline itself `UNAVAILABLE` (proving the context-hint fix - it no longer falsely matches the Del Mar file) and skips the challenger as a consequence, never fatally; an unknown `--parsers` id exits 2 naming the valid ones before any file is touched; a temporary hand-built Apify-shaped file for `DMR`/2026-09-06 (2 entries, one matching a real Broheim row, one fictional) exercised the full "compared" path, correctly reporting the race-count mismatch (1 vs 11), the fictional horse missing from the baseline, the 9 real Del Mar horses missing from the tiny challenger file, `postTime` as a baseline-only field, and - after the type-coercion fix - exactly 0 entry mismatches on the one genuinely matching horse. Ledger rows written and inspected directly from `data/parser_comparisons.jsonl` for every run; all test artifacts (the temp directory, the ledger, the report JSON) deleted afterward, none committed. `npm run check-equibase-entries` and `npm run check-equibase-apify-parseforge` both green (neither parser's own file was touched by this PR - only the new comparison module and script, plus the registry's new `EXTENSIONS_BY_SOURCE_KIND` export). `npm run check-module-bindings` green; `npm run build` green. No schema or `server/` change - `check-schema`/`check-ingest` do not apply. `check-static-app` not run - out of scope in a worktree by house rule (D168); none of the changed files are in the static app's import surface. M-4 (cost tracking) and M-5 (provenance column, still the explicit blocker on ever saving a card built from `equibase-apify-parseforge`) remain not scheduled. |
| D190 | Second registered parser: `equibase-apify-parseforge` | - | [#238](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/238) / `register-apify-parseforge-parser` | merged | **User request 2026-09-09**: "build a parser for this file," pointing at a real Apify dataset export the user supplied directly (`C:\Users\kevin\Downloads\dataset_equibase-scraper_2026-09-09_16-45-23-370.json`, copied into `tests/fixtures/equibase-apify/parseforge-ind-kd-2026-09-09.json` - never fetched, invariant 6 untouched). **Identity established, not assumed**: Apify's default dataset export filename is `dataset_<actor-name>_<timestamp>.json`, so `equibase-scraper` names the actor `parseforge/equibase-scraper`; corroborated by content, since the real rows carry scratches (`isScratched`), medication and claiming price together, matching the incoming scope's own claim ("only `parseforge` currently reports scratches, medication, and claiming price") - a partial, independent confirmation of one specific factual claim from an evaluation this repo otherwise has no record of (`docs/requirements/multi-parser-entries-ingest.md` finding 5). New `shared/parsers/equibase-apify-parseforge.js`, registered in `shared/parsers/registry.js` as `equibase-apify-parseforge` (`sourceKind: 'apify'`, `isDefault: false` - the HTML parser stays default regardless), unblocking M-3's own stated dependency ("needs >=2 registered parsers") without M-3 itself being picked up - registering a verified second parser and building the comparison harness that would use it are two separate deliverables. **Real, load-bearing finding this scope never anticipated**: the source's own `morningLineDecimal` field is European "decimal odds" (fraction + 1 - the real sample's "7/5" row carries `2.4`), while this codebase's `shared/betmath.js` `morningLineToDecimal('7/5')` returns `1.4`, the fractional ratio alone (D171's own ledger entry already establishes this as the codebase's convention). Trusting the source's field directly - the obvious, "it's already there" choice - would have silently double-counted the `+1` on every payout estimate and grade built from a card sourced this way; the parser always discards it and recomputes through the shared function instead. **The source spans multiple tracks in one file** (the real sample holds Horseshoe Indianapolis `IND` and Kentucky Downs `KD` together, unlike one Equibase HTML page which is always exactly one track) - `parse(rawInput, context)` accepts `context.trackCode` to select one, using the `context` parameter the registry's own architecture decision already reserved for exactly this kind of per-call hint; given more than one track and no `trackCode`, it refuses with a blocking warning naming every track present, never guessing. **`toPayload` throws unconditionally, by design** - `insertRaceDay`'s `entries_source` CHECK constraint (migration 024) admits only `program`/`ml_sheet`/`both`/`equibase_html`, with no value for an Apify-sourced day, and a value missing from the JS-level allowlist is silently coerced to `'program'` rather than refused (M-1's finding 8) - exactly the mislabeling invariant 13's bucket isolation exists to prevent. Adding a real value needs a schema-rebuild migration against `race_days`, the table CLAUDE.md itself names as the riskiest to rebuild (ten `ON DELETE CASCADE` children, needs verification against a `VACUUM INTO` copy of the real corpus) - explicitly out of scope for "build a parser," and M-5's job instead. Throwing turns a silent corruption risk into a certain, loud failure until that migration lands. Also caught, and flagged as non-blocking warnings, 3 genuine `weight: 1137` data-quality glitches present in the real source file (an implausible-weight guard, evidence-based rather than speculative - the exact three rows were hand-verified against the raw JSON before the check asserted them). `horseName` composes the state suffix (`"Sweet Scorecard (KY)"`) and `ageSex` composes to `"4/F"`, both matching the HTML parser's own stored conventions exactly, so downstream name-matching (`stripParens`/`nameKey`) behaves identically regardless of which parser produced an entry. `claimPrice` is stored "as printed" (a dollar string, e.g. `"$40,000"`), matching `entries.claim_price`'s own documented convention (migration 024), even though the source gives a clean number and no string-parsing was needed to get there (unlike the HTML adapter's claim-price aggregation). **VERIFIED**: new `scripts/check-equibase-apify-parseforge.js` (golden-diff plus independent hand-counted assertions against the raw JSON, mirroring `check-equibase-entries.js`'s own established shape) - a file spanning two tracks with no `trackCode` context refuses, naming both `IND` and `KD`; an unknown `trackCode` refuses distinctly; `IND` resolves to exactly 9 races (`1`-`9`) with the hand-counted entry/scratch pairs `7/0, 7/1, 6/1, 11/3, 7/1, 12/2, 10/1, 11/1, 10/2` (81 entries, 12 scratches total) and `KD` resolves to exactly 2 races (`14/2`, `5/0`, 19 entries, 2 scratches total) - both independently re-derived from the raw file with a one-off `node -e` pass before being hardcoded into the check, not copied from the parser's own output; the morning-line-decimal fix is asserted against `morningLineToDecimal` itself (not a hardcoded literal), after first asserting the fixture genuinely carries the `2.4` trap on its OWN raw row; the 3 weight glitches are matched exactly by race, horse and value; `toPayload` throws; malformed input (non-JSON text, a JSON object instead of an array, an empty array) each return a distinct blocking warning rather than an exception, the "never throws" contract every parser in this codebase holds. One arithmetic slip was caught and fixed DURING verification, not after: a first draft asserted "IND totals 95 entries (100 rows - 5 KD rows)," which is wrong on its face (KD alone accounts for 19 rows, not 5) - re-run with `--write-golden` after the fix confirmed 81, and the corrected assertion is what shipped. `npm run check-equibase-entries` green (the HTML parser is untouched); `npm run check-module-bindings` green; `npm run build` green. No schema or `server/` change, so `check-schema`/`check-ingest` do not apply - this deliverable is deliberately parser-only. `check-static-app` not run - out of scope in a worktree by house rule (D168); neither new file is in the static app's import surface. M-3 (comparison), M-4 (cost tracking) and M-5 (`entries_source`/provenance schema work, now the explicit blocker on ever saving a card from this parser) remain not scheduled. |
| D189 | Multi-track/day pull orchestration + `ingest_runs` ledger, no live fetch (M-2) | - | `multi-parser-entries-m2` | merged | **User request 2026-09-09**, M-2 of `docs/requirements/multi-parser-entries-ingest.md`. **The incoming scope's M-2, taken literally, asked for exactly what invariant 6 forbids**: resolving "which tracks are racing" from Equibase's own entries index page's "Today" column, then fetching each track automatically through whichever method its parser needs. CLAUDE.md's Gotchas state, verbatim, "Equibase blocks scripted fetching. Confirmed. Don't retry cleverly," and D113 deleted every fetcher/HTTP client/robots.txt checker in this codebase for that reason. **Asked the user before writing any code** (`AskUserQuestion`, three options: files-only / build real fetch code / lean on the `equibase-daily-entries` skill's browser automation) rather than deciding unilaterally either to silently build a scraper or to silently narrow the scope without saying so. Answer: no live fetching, files only. New `scripts/pull-race-day.js <date> --dir <dir> [--tracks CODE1,CODE2] [--parser id] [--per-track-parser CODE=id,...] [--write-report path.json]` - **`--dir` is not in the incoming scope at all; it exists purely because live fetching does not.** Every file under `--dir` is parsed (recursively, `.html`/`.htm` - the one sourceKind any registered parser has today) to learn its own track and date; a file whose parsed date doesn't match `<date>` is out of scope for that run, not an error - reported by name, never silently dropped. Omitting `--tracks` pulls every track the directory holds for that date, which is the closest this codebase can honestly get to "every track racing that day" without an index-page fetch. A track named in `--tracks` with no matching file still gets a ledger row, status `failed`, naming the gap - invariant 11 ("a failing source must be visible") applied to a missing FILE rather than a missing network response. `--per-track-parser CODE=id` resolves per track (falling back to `--parser`/the registry default), validated up front the same way M-1 validates `--parser`; a resolved parser whose `sourceKind` isn't `html` fails that ONE track (non-fatal to the run) since file discovery only scans HTML pages - there is exactly one registered parser today, so this path is exercised only by the error case, kept honest rather than faked. Re-parses a track's file with its assigned parser only when that parser differs from the discovery-pass default, which today never happens (only one parser exists) but keeps the logic correct once a second html-capable parser is registered. Writes to a brand-new throwaway temp-directory SQLite database - same SAFE-BY-CONSTRUCTION posture as `batch-import-equibase-entries.js`, never `data/betsheet.sqlite` - with a lightweight read-back check (day found by `track_code`+`date`, race count matches) to catch a broken run. **`ingest_runs` is `data/ingest_runs.jsonl` (JSON-lines, appended, added to `.gitignore`), not a database table** - the incoming scope offered either explicitly ("or a JSON-lines ledger... if a new table is overkill"), and here a table clearly would be: `pull-race-day.js` writes to a THROWAWAY per-run temp DB that is deleted at the end of every run, so a table inside it cannot be "the substrate M-3's comparison and M-4's cost tracking both read from" - that ledger has to persist ACROSS runs, on a completely different lifetime than the temp DB's. `runId` is one `newCorrelationId()` UUID per script invocation, shared by every track's row from that run, so a later comparison can group by run without re-deriving it from timestamps. **VERIFIED against the real 5-file fixture directory** (`tests/fixtures/equibase-entries`, which holds Del Mar 2026-09-06, Lethbridge 2026-09-06, Woodbine 2026-09-07, Thistledown 2026-09-10, and the KD index page with no track/date at all): no `--tracks` for `2026-09-06` pulled exactly DMR (11 races/113 entries) and LBG (6 races/34 entries), both `success`, correctly skipping the index page and the two other dates present in the SAME directory (named individually in the "out of scope" list, with the reason each was excluded); `--tracks DMR,BTP` for the same date pulled DMR `success` and reported BTP `failed` ("no file found for BTP..."), while correctly excluding LBG from the run entirely since it wasn't requested (confirmed absent from both the ledger rows and the summary count); an unknown `--parser bogus` and an unknown `--per-track-parser DMR=bogus` both exit 2 naming `equibase-html` as the only valid id, before any file is touched; a valid `--per-track-parser DMR=equibase-html` (the identical id, exercising the resolver's own code path since only one parser exists) still pulled DMR correctly; a malformed date (`not-a-date`) and an omitted `--dir` both refuse with a clear usage message rather than a stack trace; `data/ingest_runs.jsonl` accumulated 2, then 2, then 1 rows across three successive runs (5 total, append-only, never truncated), each line independently valid JSON with the expected `trackCode`/`status`/`parserId`/`droppedFields` fields. Every run exited 0 with 0 crashes and 0 read-back mismatches. `npm run check-module-bindings` green; `npm run build` green. **Neither `shared/parsers/registry.js` nor `server/ingest.js` was modified** (this script only calls their existing exports), so `check-equibase-entries`/`check-ingest`/`check-schema` are unaffected by construction and were not re-run. `check-static-app` not run - out of scope in a worktree by house rule (D168); `scripts/pull-race-day.js` is not in the static app's import surface. M-3 (comparison), M-4 (cost tracking) and M-5 (provenance column) remain not scheduled. |
| D188 | Entries parser registry + `--parser` flag on the batch import harness (M-1) | - | [#235](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/235) / `multi-parser-entries-m1` | merged | **User request 2026-09-09**, M-1 of `docs/requirements/multi-parser-entries-ingest.md` (filed [#234](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/234) the same day). One real entry, `equibase-html` (wraps `parseEquibaseEntriesHtml` unchanged), `isDefault: true`, `fieldsNotProvided: []` (the HTML page structurally provides every field `insertRaceDay` has a column for), `costModel: { type: 'free' }`. `getParser` throws naming every valid id on an unknown one rather than silently coercing to default - a typo silently ingesting through the wrong parser would be worse than a hard error, per the scope's own stated rule. `scripts/batch-import-equibase-entries.js` gained `--parser <id>` (default `DEFAULT_PARSER_ID`), refuses an unrecognized id with exit code 2, and now names the parser used in both the console summary and the `--write-report` JSON (`row.parser` per file, top-level `parser` field). **The adapter that shapes a parser's raw output into `insertRaceDay`'s payload MOVED from an inline function in the batch script into the registry entry's own `toPayload`** - unchanged byte-for-byte, just relocated - because a future parser (a JSON-native Apify actor, say) may need different or no shaping, while this one still needs to turn Equibase's formatted claim-price strings ("$50,000") into cents. The `UNMAPPED` field list (`race.purseCents`, `entry.effectiveOdds*`) stayed in the script: it names gaps in `insertRaceDay`'s SCHEMA, true for any parser, not a property of this one parser. **Deliberately does NOT register a second parser.** The scope doc's own finding 7 flagged that the accessibility-tree adapter's field-anchoring approach (horse-profile links, `Jockey:`/`Trainer:`/`Age:`/`Post Position:` labels) was never checked against a real captured sample - no fixture, no golden, nothing to verify against. Building it now to satisfy the scope's illustrative "once built" done-when would mean writing an adapter and a golden for a format nobody has confirmed, which is exactly the mistake `docs/requirements/equibase-entries-ingest.md` avoided by rejecting the print-to-PDF route only after measuring a real file. M-2 through M-5 (orchestration, comparison, cost tracking, provenance column) remain not scheduled. **VERIFIED**: `npm run batch-equibase -- tests/fixtures/equibase-entries --parser bogus-id` exits 2 with `Unknown parser id "bogus-id". Valid ids: equibase-html`; the real 5-file fixture batch (Del Mar, Woodbine, Thistledown's index page, a hyphenated-track card, a reduced-table card) run with no `--parser` flag and again with `--parser equibase-html` produced byte-identical `--write-report` JSON (diffed directly, zero lines differed) with `parser: "equibase-html"` at the top level and on every file row; both runs exited 0 with 0 crashes and 0 read-back mismatches, matching the script's pre-existing steady state. `npm run check-equibase-entries` green (the underlying parser file is untouched). `npm run check-module-bindings` green (code moved between modules - exactly the class that check exists to catch). `npm run build` green. The only skip reason is `not_found`, since there is no soft-delete state and no grading check for a card. Cascades (tickets/allocations/grades) already existed via `ON DELETE CASCADE`, unchanged. Client: `bulkDeleteCards` in `client/src/api.js`, and `CardsPanel.jsx` gained the checkbox column, select-all (with the same indeterminate-state handling), a two-step inline confirm naming the selected card numbers, and a result notice - all copied from `RaceDayList.jsx`'s established shape rather than inventing a new one. Checkbox clicks `stopPropagation` so they don't trigger the row's `onOpenCard`. **VERIFIED IN THE BROWSER**: seeded a fresh Del Mar day with three HUMAN cards via the real `human-cards` write path on the agent's own dev-preview stack (empty worktree DB, no prior data); selected two cards, confirmed the confirmation dialog named them correctly ("#3, #2") and warned it was permanent; clicked "Delete them"; the table dropped to the one remaining card and the notice read "Deleted 2 cards." Checkbox clicks did not navigate into a card. Test data cleaned up afterward via the app's own soft-delete route for the race day. `npm run build` green; `npm run check-module-bindings` green; `npm run check-pl` green (its own LLM-card scenario needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, absent by default in a fresh worktree - confirmed environmental by temporarily copying the primary checkout's gitignored `.env` in, rerunning green, then removing it again). `check-static-app` not run - out of scope in a worktree by house rule (D168); none of the changed files are in the static app's import surface. |
| D186 | CLAUDE.md pruned, fourth trim: 178k to 148k | 4 | [#231](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/231) / `prune-claude-md` | merged | **User request 2026-09-08.** CLAUDE.md is loaded in full every session and carries its own trim procedure; this is the fourth trim, following the procedure the file itself documents. At 177,578 bytes it was above both earlier trigger points (165k, 168k) and closing on the third (193k). **SNAPSHOT FIRST**: `docs/claude-md-pre-trim-2026-09-08.md` is a verbatim copy, which is what licenses cutting at all - nothing is lost, only relocated. **TWO CUTS, both following precedent rather than inventing one.** (1) The 71 settled pre-D130 feature rows were collapsed into SEVEN era rows - Foundations D01-D28, Provenance and sources D34-D52, Human cards and Replay, LLM cards, Equibase OTR, Dev harness, and the pivot's rebuild D110-D129 - exactly the shape the existing "Retired by the 2026-09-05 pivot" row already had. (2) The twelve fattest architecture cells and seventeen fattest feature rows were rewritten to the file's own stated rule: a cell says what a file OWNS and the invariants a reader must not break, not the history of how it got that way. **GOTCHAS WERE LEFT ENTIRELY ALONE**, on purpose and against the temptation of 27k sitting there: every entry exists because something broke in a way nothing else catches, and the three prior trims touched feature rows and architecture cells only. Invariants, Commands, Delivery workflow, Definition of done and Findings are byte-identical too - asserted, not assumed. **THE PRUNE FOUND A REAL DOC DEFECT**: the `server/tip-picks.js` cell still described a `POST .../tip-picks/preview` route that called the model, an image archive it re-read on confirm, and a `BETSHEET_TIP_TEST_MODE` stub - **all deleted by D177**, whose feature row recorded the removal while the architecture cell kept describing the removed surface. Verified against the code (the router has exactly GET / POST-manual / PATCH / DELETE) and rewritten. That is the argument for pruning on a schedule rather than only when the file gets large: a cell nobody rereads is a cell that can go quietly false. VERIFIED: every section still present; both tables structurally intact (no malformed rows, three columns throughout); the six untouched sections byte-identical to the snapshot; and an automated audit of all 87 removed-or-rewritten rows for rule-shaped sentences ("never", "must", "HOUSE RULE", "invariant N") with each survivor confirmed still present elsewhere in the file - `ONE card per day per bucket`, `Dirt ... never inferred from absence`, the reset's unknown-table sweep, `warned, never blocked`, `invariant 9 literally true`, `llmInputs null never []` and the whole-dollar floor all still have a home. | D185 | Tip-sheet ranks are three toggle buttons, not a dropdown | 4 | [#230](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/230) / `tip-rank-buttons` | merged | **User request 2026-09-08**: replace the per-cell rank `<select>` with small 1/2/3 buttons, clicking to toggle a rank on or off, same exclusion logic. Done exactly as asked. **THE LOGIC IS UNTOUCHED, DELIBERATELY** - `setRank` already both cleared a rank on a falsy value and MOVED it off whichever horse held it in that column, so the buttons call the existing function rather than growing a second copy of the exclusion rule: on-button click passes `''`, any other passes the rank. The server still validates independently through the same `validateTipPicks` a model's output passes, so the UI is a convenience over the rule, never the rule itself. **WHY IT IS BETTER, beyond the click count**: a `<select>` hides the current value inside a control that has to be opened to be trusted, so reading a whole column meant opening nine dropdowns; three lit buttons make the entire race legible at a glance, which is the point of the grid. The lit state is plain `.btn--primary` - "chosen" looks the same here as everywhere else in the app rather than acquiring a private vocabulary - and carries `aria-pressed`, so the control reports the same state to a screen reader that the fill reports to the eye. One small CSS rule (`.rankpick`) sizes the buttons down from `.btn--sm` so three fit a narrow column without stretching the table, which the D157 house rule cares about. **VERIFIED IN THE BROWSER on real day 268**: the dialog rendered **0 `<select>` elements** and 9 button groups; clicking rank 1 on row 0 lit it (1 pick); clicking rank 1 on row 2 **MOVED** it - row 0 went dark, row 2 lit, the count stayed 1, which is the exclusion rule intact; clicking row 2's lit 1 again cleared it (0 picks, 0 pressed). With a second column added, row 0 was set to trackmaster **1** and numberfire **2** simultaneously - the cross-sheet case D176 exists for - and the columns proved independent. Saving persisted exactly that (`trackmaster r1: 1->#1, 2->#2` / `numberfire r1: 1->#3, 2->#1`), and REOPENING the dialog lit the saved ranks back correctly, so the seeding path survives too. `#root` held one child throughout, no new console error. **The database was left exactly as found** - 0 `tip_picks`, 11 notes and 10 cards on day 268, re-checked against SQLite. Presentation-only (one component, one CSS rule), so the gate is `npm run build`, green. `check-static-app` not run - out of scope in a worktree by house rule (D168); this file is not in the static app's import surface. |
| D184 | Analyst notes move into the race card; race-specific input is a HOUSE RULE | 4 | [#228](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/228) / `notes-into-race-ui` | merged | **User request 2026-09-08**, three parts. **(1)** Removed the staking panel's explanatory paragraph ("Enter a race's picks in its own Tip sheets panel above. Staking is day-level because..."). The reasoning it carried is not lost - it lives in the component's own header comment and in this ledger - but it was telling the user about an implementation constraint they did not ask about. **(2)** Per-race analyst notes are now edited IN the race panel (`RaceNotesEditor.jsx`), directly under that race's entries, exactly as D182 did for tip sheets; "Enter Analyst Notes" became "Day Analyst Notes" and now writes the WHOLE-DAY note (race 0) alone. **(3)** The generalisation was written down as a HOUSE RULE in Gotchas: *a race-specific input or edit belongs in the Race UI component, as tightly coupled to it as the code allows.* **WHAT THE RULE BUYS, concretely**: entering a note used to mean opening a dialog, finding the right race among twelve, and typing into a box with the entries no longer on screen - the one thing you were reasoning about was the one thing you could not see. **THE RULE IS ABOUT AUTHORING, NOT READING**, and that distinction is load-bearing here: the same note still renders READ-ONLY on the card sheet via `RaceNotes.jsx`, because a card is a record of what was decided rather than a place to change it. `RaceNotes` and `RaceNotesEditor` are deliberately **two components rather than one with an `editable` flag** - the read-only case has no day id, no save path and no dirty state, and giving it those to serve one caller is how a record surface quietly becomes an editor. A day-level surface survives only for what is genuinely day-level: the whole-day note, and tip-sheet staking (which splits the bankroll across every race with picks). NO SCHEMA, NO ENDPOINT, NO SERVER CHANGE - `llm_notes` has been keyed by `(race_day_id, race_number)` since D92, so this is purely where the same row is typed. Two things fell out: the modal dropped its now-dead `getRaceDay` call (it no longer lists races, so it no longer needs the day), and the source datalist is rendered ONCE per page rather than per race, since the per-race inputs reference it by id. Care taken in the editor: an unsaved local edit always beats an arriving refetch, and the prop is adopted during RENDER rather than in an effect (D155's rule, which exists because the effect version silently ate saved text once); a failed save KEEPS the text on screen and `dirty` set, so the next blur retries rather than the edit vanishing into a dismissed error; and `postResult` is carried down so the not-blind warning appears where a note is actually typed. **VERIFIED IN THE BROWSER on real day 268, which carries 11 genuine analyst notes** - and that mattered: the first save attempt was made against race 3, which holds a real 2,568-character note. It did NOT save, because React maps `onBlur` to `focusout` and a synthetic `blur` never reached the handler; the page was reloaded immediately to discard the unsaved edit before it could flush over real data, and testing moved to race 11, the one race with no note. **Not a code defect - a verification hazard, recorded because the near-miss is the lesson.** Then: 12 editable panels rendered with one datalist for the page; existing notes prefilled correctly (race 3 showing 2,568 chars, source `llm`); typing into race 11 and blurring saved through the real endpoint (`sourceLabel: 'own'`), the summary flipped from "— none" live, and the note survived a reload; race 3 was byte-identically untouched throughout. The day dialog opened with exactly ONE textarea and ZERO race sections. The card sheet at `/card/181` rendered 12 notes panels and **0 textareas** - read-only preserved. `#root` held one child throughout, no new console error. **The database was left exactly as found**: 11 notes on races 1-10 and 12, race 3 still 2,568 chars, re-checked directly against SQLite. Presentation-only (four client components), so the gate is `npm run build`, green, plus `check-module-bindings`, green. `check-static-app` not run - out of scope in a worktree by house rule (D168); none of these files is in the static app's import surface. |
| D183 | One "Stake all tip sheets into cards" button, not one per source | 4 | [#226](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/226) / `stake-all-tip-sheets` | merged | **User request 2026-09-08**, immediately after D182: drop the per-source stake buttons and show a single "Stake all tip sheets into cards". Straightforward, with one thing that had to be got right. **"ALL" IS A CONVENIENCE, NEVER A MERGE.** The obvious reading of one button is one card set, and that reading would break invariant 13 outright: whether TrackMaster agrees with NumberFire is the thing being measured, so a card that cannot say which sheet picked it answers nothing. Each source is therefore still previewed and saved through its OWN existing per-source call, and still gets its OWN three cards carrying its OWN `tip_source_label` — the button collapses N clicks into one and changes nothing else. The panel says so on screen ("Each source is staked separately and keeps its own cards — they are never pooled") rather than leaving it to be inferred, and the preview renders one labelled block per source instead of one combined plan. **NO TOTAL IS SHOWN, deliberately** — not across variants and not across sources. The three variants are mutually exclusive ways to bet the same picks, and adding them up is exactly the D175 bug that reported ~$598 wagered against a $200 bankroll; a "grand total" on this screen would reintroduce it in a new place. **PARTIAL FAILURE IS REPORTED, NOT INFERRED**: sources are saved sequentially, a failing source does not stop the others (each save is its own server-side transaction), and every source's outcome is listed by name. That is safe precisely because D174 made a re-stake UPDATE rather than duplicate, so the remedy is always "fix it and press the button again". Preview is equally sequential, so an error names the source it came from and the on-screen order never shuffles. No server change: the two endpoints, the planner, the validator, scoring and P/L are untouched. **VERIFIED IN THE BROWSER on real day 268 with TWO sources**, which is the only case where any of this is testable: entering trackmaster and numberfire picks on one race (the same horse deliberately ranked differently in each, the D176 case) produced exactly one button reading "2 sources: numberfire, trackmaster"; preview rendered two separate labelled blocks with two tables and no combined figure; saving took Betting cards 10 → 16, which is **3 cards × 2 sources**, and SQLite confirmed six rows with three `tip_source_label = 'numberfire'` and three `'trackmaster'` — separate, not pooled. Adding race 2's picks for one source and pressing the button again showed "updates #205…#210" with the update warning, the button relabelled "Update cards for every source", trackmaster's ticket counts growing 2→3 and 3→6 while numberfire's held — and saving left the count at **16, not 22**, so the update path survives the change. Per-source outcomes rendered ("numberfire: cards saved / trackmaster: cards saved"), the preview cleared, `CardsPanel` refreshed with no page reload (D173's wire) and stayed at exactly ONE panel (D181). `#root` held one child throughout with no new console error. **The database was left exactly as found** — 0 `tip_picks`, 0 TIPSHEET cards, 10 cards on day 268, re-checked directly against SQLite. Presentation-only (one client component), so the gate is `npm run build`, green, plus `check-module-bindings`, green. `check-static-app` not run — out of scope in a worktree by house rule (D168), and this file is not in the static app's import surface. |
| D182 | Tip sheets moved into the race UI they describe | 4 | [#224](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/224) / `tipsheets-into-race-ui` | merged | **User request 2026-09-08**: "tip sheets are tightly coupled to race, like analysts notes, and should be managed in the race ui. Each race will have its own tipsheets card." Before this, a race's picks were entered from a button on the race and then read back in a day-level list where every row had to re-state which race it belonged to - the same split D164 had already closed for analyst notes. **WHAT MOVED**: `RaceTipPicks.jsx`, one panel per race, rendered inside that race's `<details>` immediately after `RaceNotes.jsx` and styled by the same rule (one CSS declaration now covers `.race-notes` and `.race-tips`, because they are the same affordance and letting them drift would say they were different kinds of thing). It owns the picks table, the score line, D169's correction editor and delete, and opens D176's entry dialog - so a race's whole tipsheet story is in one place. Several sheets can rank one race (the two real screenshots this project extracted both did), so each source gets its own `.tip-source` block. **WHAT DID NOT MOVE, AND WHY**: staking. `planTipCards` sizes every ticket from `perRaceBankrollCents(bankroll, racesWithPicks.length)` - **race 1's ticket price depends on how many OTHER races have picks**, and D174 made a re-stake recompute the card whole for exactly that reason. A per-race "stake this race" button would have to invent a denominator, and would quietly mean something different from what a TIPSHEET card has meant since D171. It stays day-level, renamed `TipStakingPanel.jsx` to say what it now does, and its copy states the constraint on screen rather than leaving the user to infer it ("adding a race re-prices the ones already there"), with a per-source count of races with picks. **A DUPLICATE FETCH REMOVED ON THE WAY**: `RaceDayView` and `TipPicksPanel` both called `listTipPicks(dayId)` for the same rows. The day now loads rows AND scores once and passes them down to every race panel and to the staking panel, so twelve races cost one request rather than twelve, and no second copy can disagree about which sources exist. That also let the staking panel's `key={tips-N}` remount go: it is prop-driven now, which is one fewer counter of exactly the kind that collided in D181. Scoring is fetched as a SEPARATE supplementary read - a day with no results must still show its picks perfectly. **VERIFIED IN THE BROWSER on the real day 268**, end to end, because a pure UI move is not provable any other way: entering picks from race 1's own panel wrote one row and left the other eleven races reading "Tip sheets — none"; the panel then showed the source, the picks, and a real score line ("Top pick #1 off the board · caught 0 of the top 3 · winner was #8"); adding race 2 moved the staking panel's counter from "1 race with picks" to "2 races" with no reload, proving both surfaces read the same rows; staking previewed three variants and saved, taking Betting cards from 10 to 13 **with no page reload** (D173's `onSaved` wire survives the relocation) and still exactly ONE cards panel (D181 holds); Delete from inside a race panel removed the row and dropped the staking counter back, live. `#root` held one child throughout and no new console error was emitted at any point. **The database was left exactly as found** - 0 `tip_picks` rows and 10 cards on day 268, re-checked directly against SQLite after cleanup. Presentation-only (three client components, one CSS rule; no `shared/`, `server/`, parser, schema or API change - the endpoints, the validator, staking, scoring and P/L are untouched), so per CLAUDE.md's definition of done the gate is `npm run build`, green, plus `check-module-bindings` (green) because this moves exports between modules, which is the D172 class. `check-static-app` not run - out of scope in a worktree by house rule (D168), and none of these three files is in the static app's seven-file import surface. |
| D181 | Fix: the /day page grew extra "Betting cards" panels on any re-render | 4 | [#222](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/222) / `fix-delete-day-duplicate-cards` | merged | **Bug report 2026-09-08**: clicking "Delete race day" displayed multiple betting-card panels. **Delete was not the cause and the confirm dialog was innocent** - it is simply the most visible state change on that page. THE DEFECT: `RaceDayView.jsx` rendered `<CardsPanel key={cardsVersion}>` and `<TipPicksPanel key={tipVersion}>` as SIBLINGS in one children list, and both counters initialise to `0`. React reconciles a static children list by key when keys are present, so from the very first render the two panels claimed the same key. This is not a cosmetic warning: React's own text says "Non-unique keys may cause children to be duplicated and/or omitted", and duplication is exactly what happened. MEASURED IN THE BROWSER on `/day/268` before the fix: **2 panels on a hard reload, 4 after clicking Delete, 5 after Cancel** - the DOM accumulating a panel per state change rather than reconciling, with `rootChildren: 1` and zero application errors, which is why nothing else surfaced it. ARCHAEOLOGY: `key={cardsVersion}` is D142's (PR #184), `key={tipVersion}` is D176's (PR #216) - **neither is wrong alone**; the collision was created by adding the second one beside the first, and a bare numeric key is only safe while nothing next to it uses one, which is not a property a later edit can be expected to check. THE FIX: namespace both keys (`cards-${cardsVersion}` / `tips-${tipVersion}`), which restores identity without touching the D142/D173 remount-to-reload pattern either panel depends on. A comment at the site says why, so the next sibling counter does not reintroduce it. VERIFIED IN THE BROWSER, same page and same gestures: **1 panel on load, 1 after Delete, 1 after Cancel**, the confirm dialog still correct ("12 races, 125 entries... 10 cards with 237 tickets"), and the React key warning count frozen at its pre-fix total - no new warning emitted after the change. Presentation-only (a JSX `key` attribute; no `shared/`, `server/`, parser, schema or API change), so per CLAUDE.md's definition of done the verification is `npm run build`, green. `check-static-app` not run - out of scope in a worktree by house rule (D168), and `RaceDayView.jsx` is not in the static app's seven-file import surface, so it provably cannot move that bundle. |
| D180 | A program-less scratch is stored NULL, not the literal 'SCR' | 4 | [#220](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/220) / `fix-scr-program-numbers` | merged | **User request 2026-09-08**, from a finding I raised in D178 - and the investigation corrected the finding on two counts before fixing anything. **(1) IT IS NOT A PARSER DEFECT.** `shared/parsers/equibase-entries.js` reads a scratch row correctly and returns `programNumber: null`. The placeholder was introduced downstream by `program_number TEXT NOT NULL`, which forced `insertRaceDay` to substitute something. **The number is genuinely absent from the page**, verified in the saved Equibase capture rather than assumed: a scratch row is `<tr class="scratch"><td colspan="2"><b> SCR</b></td><td>King of Clubs (KY)</td>`, the program-number and post-position cells REPLACED by a spanning marker. So it was never recoverable, and any attempt to infer it from the gaps in the numbering would have been a guess. Note there are TWO kinds of scratched row - the other keeps its number and carries a scratch reason (153 such rows are stored, untouched). **(2) IT HAD CORRUPTED NOTHING, and D178's row said otherwise.** That row claimed the placeholder 'already corrupted two LLM generations' and quoted a 0.1% program-number error rate. Both were artefacts of my own measurement: the regex scanned the WHOLE ticket line, including the `<rationale>` column, and the `#3`/`#8` references it caught were the model writing prose - 'the scratch of #8 removes his main pace rival' - which it had learned from the analyst notes. `resolveToken` only ever reads the `<selections>` column. Re-measured against that column alone: **0 of 3,156 references were wrong, across all 257 stored responses**. The real selection error rate is zero. D178's ledger row has been corrected in place; its conclusion (do not strip program numbers) is strengthened, not weakened. THE FIX: migration 032 rebuilds `entries` with a NULLABLE `program_number` and backfills the 150 placeholder rows, every one of which is `scratched = 1` (checked, so the backfill cannot blank a horse that has a real number). NULL is what the page actually says. It also **dissolves** D122's `SCR` / `SCR-2` / `SCR-3` suffixes rather than preserving them: those existed because the placeholders collided on `UNIQUE(race_id, program_number)` and broke 10 of 91 real days outright, and SQLite's UNIQUE permits ANY NUMBER of NULLs - so two scratches in one race now need no suffix, no counter, and no dependence on the order rows happen to be parsed in, which `SCR-2` always did. VERIFIED AGAINST A `VACUUM INTO` COPY OF THE REAL CORPUS before any code changed: 13,710 entries and 1,526 races preserved, 303 scratched unchanged, 150 SCR to NULL with 13,560 real numbers untouched, 76 referencing `consensus_picks` rows intact, 9,660 distinct horse names intact, `foreign_key_check` clean, `idx_entries_race` restored (migration 030's lesson - check the live index list, do not trust the previous migration), and two NULLs proven to coexist in one race. **A REAL HOLE FOUND AND CLOSED ON THE WAY**: `resolveToken`'s name-only branch returned `entry.program_number` without checking it, so a ticket naming a scratched, numberless horse would have carried a NULL selection - and the scratch guard downstream keys on the NUMBER (`scratched.has(pgm)`), so a null would have slipped straight past it. That was the one path by which a scratched horse could still have been bet. It now refuses with a blocking warning. Three other sites that keyed maps on `String(program_number)` would have produced the literal string 'null' as a key and are now null-skipping. VERIFIED: `check-ingest`'s 'stored visibly as SCR' assertion was INVERTED to assert NULL, and a new case proves D122's collision is gone - two program-less scratches in one race saving with no suffix and neither renamed. That case is placed LAST inside the run on purpose: it CREATES a race day, and several assertions above count the days on file - running it earlier broke three of them, a test changing the state its neighbours measure. Full gate green: check-ingest, check-schema, check-human-picks, check-llm-cards, check-tip-picks, check-tip-staking, check-tip-scoring, check-equibase-entries, check-entries-zip, check-grading, check-replay, check-export, check-pl, check-module-bindings, check-charts, npm run build. |
| D179 | Tipsheet and OTR picks as a baseline LLM input | 4 | [#219](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/219) / `llm-baseline-inputs` | merged | **User request 2026-09-08**, scoped first and committed as `docs/requirements/llm-baseline-inputs.md` before any code. The LLM generator now sees what the day's OTHER sources already think - the tip sheets (D176) and Equibase's Off to the Races (D71) - as a labelled baseline, beside the optional analyst notes it already read. **THE ARCHAEOLOGY FOUND THE WORK HALF-DONE IN HISTORY.** D112 deleted a `CONSENSUS` block from `buildLlmRaceUserPrompt` (`git show 2543e52 -- server/llm-prompt.js`) that already implemented every decision this scope reached: labelled per source, both sources with no precedence, an absence path, and OTR rendered in its own vocabulary rather than forced into ranks. D74 had left the reason attached to it - OTR prints a show pick, a win pick and unranked box MENTIONS, never a ranked 3rd pick, so a generic ranked line would misrepresent a box mention as a '3rd' pick it never claimed. That argument is better than any written for this scope and is what settled the tickets-not-ranks question. **IT ALSO FOUND THE ORIGINAL SCOPE TOO HEAVY.** The first pass proposed capture columns and a `baseline_present` latch so a card stayed reproducible. Checking `llm_card_requests` showed `prompt_text` already stores the ENTIRE user prompt verbatim, so a block rendered there is captured for free: **no migration, no new columns, no latch**. The heavier design was defending a corpus that is, by standing instruction, a sandbox. DECISIONS, all Kevin's: OTR as **tickets**; **both** sources when both exist, no precedence; **labelled** per source. Two more were settled by checking rather than asking - OTR data is read from the `both` VARIANT (verified as the only one carrying all four printed tickets; `some-reward` and `higher-reward` each hold half) and from its NEWEST card, because OTR ingest is APPEND-ONLY (D71) and joining every match would render the same sheet two or three times over. `BASELINE_CLAUSES` appends to the system prompt ONLY when a baseline exists - the same conditional shape `ANALYST_NOTES_CLAUSES` uses, for the same reason (an LLM card has no version axis). **An absent baseline renders no block at all** - no header, no 'none on file' line - so a race without one produces the byte-identical prompt it always did, and `buildSystemPrompt({})` is still `SYSTEM_PROMPT`. The clause that matters is the instruction NOT to reproduce a baseline source's tickets: these sources are cheap to copy, and a card that copies the sheet is worth nothing the sheet is not already worth. Source labels are free text a person typed (D167 lets an unknown app keep its own slug) and pass through the same `sanitizeSourceLabel` a note's label does. VERIFIED: nine new `check-llm-cards` assertions - a labelled line per sheet, both sources present, OTR carrying tickets and NO rank vocabulary, the bred suffix stripped (D125), a hostile label unable to forge the ticket markers, absence rendering nothing, an omitted baseline behaving identically to an empty one, the clauses appending only when a baseline exists, and byte-identity holding with neither block. A `check-equibase-otr` assertion was SPLIT rather than deleted: D112's 'the CONSENSUS section is still gone' is kept as-is, and a new D179 assertion proves the sheet IS named under BASELINE PICKS - so neither can drift back into the other, and that pair also proves the real sheet reaches a stored prompt through the actual route. Separately verified end to end that the block reaches `llm_card_requests.prompt_text` and the clauses reach `system_prompt_text`. Full gate green: check-llm-cards, check-llm-input-capture, check-tip-picks, check-tip-staking, check-tip-scoring, check-equibase-otr, check-human-picks, check-pl, check-schema, check-export, check-module-bindings, npm run build. **OUT OF SCOPE BY INSTRUCTION, and recorded so it is not mistaken for an oversight**: any measurement of whether the baseline improves results or grades - no P/L breakdown, no findings document, no pre-registration. **THE CONSEQUENCE, WRITTEN DOWN DELIBERATELY**: `EQB_OTR`, `TIPSHEET` and `LLM_GENERATED` were independent opinions that could be compared. Now that the LLM reads the other two, its cards are DERIVATIVE of them and that comparison quietly stops measuring what it measured before. Fine in a sandbox, not fine in a corpus - this sentence exists so the distinction is not rediscovered the hard way. |
| D178 | Three dead inputs removed from the LLM prompt | 4 | [#218](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/218) / `llm-prompt-trim-dead-fields` | merged | **Arrived as a different proposal 2026-09-08**: strip program NUMBERS from the LLM input to stop the model thrashing about reconciling horses, tell it not to return numbers, and cut cost. Measured against the corpus before answering, and **the premise did not hold**. THE EVIDENCE: across **257 real LLM responses** carrying **3,190 program-number references**, exactly **4 pointed at a horse that did not exist - 0.1%, in 2 of 257 responses**. Both failures then INVERTED on inspection: they are the same race, where two scratched horses are stored with `program_number` of `SCR` and `SCR-2` instead of `3` and `8`. The model named **#3 and #8 - the correct numbers** - and the parser rejected them because the stored data had lost them. The model was not thrashing; the entries parser was wrong. Cost was measured too: stripping every `#N` from the largest stored prompt saves 167 chars (~42 tokens), **2.5% of the user prompt**, on the cheaper half of the request. AND REMOVAL WOULD HAVE COST SOMETHING REAL. `resolveToken` already accepts a bare horse name, so it would have worked - but a numbered selection lets the parser cross-check number against name and emit a NON-BLOCKING `name_mismatch`; a name-only token that fails to match raises `unknown_program`, which is **blocking**, so a name variant would lose the whole ticket where a number carries it. It would also move reconciliation from the model, which has the full entry list in front of it, into `nameKey`, which has one string and must survive bred-country suffixes and curly apostrophes. Numbers must reappear regardless - `tickets.selections`, grading and the teller grammar (`$10 W 5`) are all keyed on them. WHAT WAS ACTUALLY DEAD, and is what this removes: the system prompt's opening promised the model *program handicapper rankings*, *the program's own Bottom Line analysis*, and *the consensus picks already gathered from external sources*. **Consensus was deleted by D112** - the prompt had been describing an input that could not exist for over sixty deliverables. Program rank and BEST BET came from the Del Mar program ingestion that D113 deleted: **0 of 574 entries on an ACTIVE race day carry either**. The Bottom Line came from the same ingestion: **0 of 49 races on an active day carry one**, so its block could never render. Removed for CORRECTNESS first. A system prompt that promises inputs which never arrive is describing a different task than the one being asked, and no amount of downstream care fixes that. The token saving is real but small and was NOT the reason: 159 characters, about 40 tokens, **1.9% of the system prompt** - because the rest of it is load-bearing rules (D145, D146, D148, D160-D163) that each fixed a live bug and must not be cut. Anyone reading this later should not expect a cost win from it. This is an un-versioned prompt-comparability boundary, the usual kind for LLM cards (no version axis, `engine_version` is the literal `'llm'`). Kevin's standing instruction that the existing corpus and P/L are a SANDBOX - no backfill, no backwards compatibility - is what makes that acceptable here rather than a blocker. `buildSystemPrompt({hasNotes:false}) === SYSTEM_PROMPT` still holds untouched, and `PROMPT_TEMPLATE_VERSION` moved on its own, being a hash of SYSTEM_PROMPT. VERIFIED: two `check-llm-cards` assertions were **INVERTED rather than deleted** - `carries every entry with scratch/best-bet/rank annotations` and `carries the Bottom Line text` became `does NOT carry program rank or BEST BET` and `does NOT carry a Bottom Line block`, so re-adding a dead field has to be a deliberate act. The fixture still PASSES `bottomLineText` deliberately, which makes the assertion prove the builder ignores it rather than that the test stopped supplying it. `docs/prompts/llm-card-v1.md` updated in the same commit, as CLAUDE.md requires. Full gate green: check-llm-cards, check-llm-input-capture, check-human-picks, check-replay, check-export, check-pl, check-schema, check-module-bindings, npm run build. **A REAL ISSUE FOUND ON THE WAY, NOT FIXED HERE**: 150 entries across 15 race days carry `SCR` or `SCR-2` as their `program_number`. **CORRECTED BY D180 - two claims in this row were wrong.** (1) It is NOT an entries-parser defect: the parser reads those rows correctly and returns `programNumber: null`; the placeholder came from `program_number` being NOT NULL, and the number is genuinely absent from the page (Equibase replaces the cell with a `colspan="2"` SCR marker). (2) It did NOT corrupt two LLM generations. The `#3`/`#8` references counted here came from the ticket line's `<rationale>` PROSE, which `resolveToken` never reads. Re-measured against the SELECTIONS column alone - the only one that becomes a bet - **0 of 3,156 references were wrong, across the whole corpus**. The 0.1% figure quoted above is an artefact of scanning the whole line; the real selection error rate is zero, which strengthens this row's conclusion rather than weakening it. |
| D177 | The tip-sheet screenshot path removed | 4 | [#217](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/217) / `remove-tipsheet-extraction` | merged | **User decision 2026-09-08**, taken once D176's typed entry existed: extraction was tedious and cost an API call per race, for data that is three horses and three ranks. Deliberately a SEPARATE PR from D176 so the new path proved out first and the old one stayed available as a fallback in between - the repo's rule that a deliverable is independently reviewable and revertable, and the safer ordering when one thing replaces another. REMOVED: `server/tip-extraction.js` entirely (the vision call, the JSON-only prompt, the fenced-response recovery, the magic-byte format sniffing), the `POST .../tip-picks/preview` and parseToken `POST .../tip-picks` routes, the image + response archive and `BETSHEET_TIP_ARCHIVE_DIR`, the `BETSHEET_TIP_TEST_MODE` escape hatch, the upload UI in `TipPicksPanel`, the two client API calls, the gitignore rule for an archive nothing writes, and the sample screenshots. **KEPT, and the distinction is the point**: `insertTipPicks` and `tipPicksForDay` were never part of the vision half - they are the ONE writer and the ONE reader - so they moved into `server/tip-picks.js` rather than dying with the module around them. The `raw_extraction` / `model` / `image_sha256` COLUMNS survive too: rows extracted before this carry real values there, dropping a column needs a table rebuild, and a shipped migration is immutable regardless. They are simply NULL on everything typed since, which makes provenance readable without a flag anyone maintains. **NOTHING DOWNSTREAM CHANGED.** Scoring (D170), staking (D171), P/L (D175), the shared validator and the odds normalizer were all untouched by this removal, and their checks passed unmodified. That is the evidence the layering was right in the first place - extraction was one INPUT to a pipeline, not the pipeline. Two smaller honesty repairs on the way out: the vision content-block support in `server/anthropic-client.js` is kept (three lines, general, costs nothing) but its comment no longer claims a caller that no longer exists; and the Gotcha about newer models rejecting a pinned `temperature` now records that its motivating caller was removed while the trap itself remains. VERIFIED: `scripts/check-tip-extraction.js` became `scripts/check-tip-picks.js` (`npm run check-tip-picks`), losing its two vision phases - a stubbed extract->persist round trip and one that ran real screenshots through a paid model - and keeping everything they were really guarding, because none of it was about the model: the `tip_picks` shape and refusals, every odds conversion re-checked through `morningLineToDecimal`, the blocking-vs-advisory split, the correction path, and the factory reset naming the table. The correction assertions were REWIRED onto a manually-entered row rather than deleted, since that behaviour always acted on a stored row and never on a preview. 75 assertions, all green. Full gate green: check-tip-picks, check-tip-staking, check-tip-scoring, check-pl, check-schema, check-ingest, check-grading, check-module-bindings, check-llm-cards, check-export, npm run build. A repo-wide grep for every removed symbol and env var returns nothing, and the one stale comment it did find was fixed rather than left. |
| D176 | Manual tip-pick entry: type the sheet instead of photographing it | 4 | [#216](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/216) / `tipsheet-manual-entry` | merged | **User request 2026-09-08**: screenshot extraction is tedious and costs an API call per race, when a tip sheet is three horses and three ranks - faster to type than to photograph. **THE GRID SHAPE CHANGED FROM THE ONE DESCRIBED, ON THE EVIDENCE OF THE USER'S OWN DATA.** The request was a row per horse with two dropdowns, tipsheet and rank. That cannot express the data this feature exists for: a horse routinely appears in SEVERAL sheets at different ranks, and in the two real screenshots this project extracted, two of the three horses did - Karazest was TrackMaster 1 and NumberFire 2, Union Roar was NumberFire 1 and TrackMaster 2. With one tipsheet dropdown per row a horse belongs to exactly one sheet and that race is unenterable. Raised with the evidence; Kevin's call was one rank COLUMN per tipsheet, horse rows crossing them, which also enters every sheet for a race in one pass. THE DELIVERABLE: `POST /race-days/:id/tip-picks/manual` and `client/src/components/TipPicksEntryModal.jsx`, reached from an `Enter tipsheet picks` button on each race in `RaceDayView`. **The payload is IDENTICAL to extraction's** - the same `validateTipPicks` a model's output passes, the same `insertTipPicks` writer, the same rows - so D170 scoring and D171 staking cannot tell a typed sheet from a photographed one, and neither can a findings query later. `raw_extraction` / `model` / `image_sha256` stay NULL, which is the honest record (there was no model and no image) and makes provenance readable without anyone setting a flag. Details that are decisions rather than defaults: horse NAMES come from the day's own entries, never the client's copy; **no odds are captured**, because the morning line already sits on `entries` where staking reads it and a tip sheet's printed price is a different number that copying the ML would invent; assigning a rank MOVES it off whatever horse held it in that column, so a sheet cannot rank two horses first; every column is submitted on save INCLUDING empty ones, because an empty sheet is how a column is cleared and omitting it would leave stale picks; and the dialog opens seeded from the race's existing rows, so it edits rather than starting blank. **SPLIT DELIBERATELY FROM THE REMOVAL.** Kevin also chose to delete the screenshot path entirely. That is D177, a separate PR, so the new entry path proves out while the old one is still there as a fallback - the repo's own rule that a deliverable is independently reviewable and revertable, and the safer ordering when one thing replaces another. VERIFIED: `check-tip-extraction` gained the manual round trip - one row per sheet, source normalized the same way, **the same horse ranked differently in two sheets** (the case the original grid could not express), names taken from the day, no odds invented, audit columns honestly empty, a typed row shaped exactly like an extracted one, a duplicated rank refused 422 by the SAME validator, a horse not in the race refused 422, and an empty sheet clearing one source while leaving the others. Full gate green: check-tip-extraction, check-tip-staking, check-tip-scoring, check-pl, check-schema, check-ingest, check-module-bindings, npm run build. **BROWSER-VERIFIED end to end** on the real corpus copy: opened the dialog from race 1, added a second tipsheet column, ranked the same two horses differently in each sheet, saved - both rows stored with the overlap intact (Torre Eiffel TM 1 / NF 2, Holding Power TM 2 / NF 1), the panel refreshed to `On file (2)` without a reload (the D173 wire, remembered this time), and reopening the dialog pre-filled every rank in the right column. Layout measured rather than eyeballed after a screenshot looked cropped: at 1280x900 the modal is 880px, does not overflow, shows all nine horses and keeps Save reachable without scrolling - the crop was the 560px preview pane, not the component. Zero console errors, React root intact throughout. All test rows removed from the corpus copy afterwards. |
| D175 | TIPSHEET P/L counted three mutually-exclusive variants as one spend; P/L on the day view | 4 | [#215](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/215) / `tip-pl-variant-and-day-view` | merged | **Found while answering a user question**, not from a bug report: asked to weigh merging the three TIPSHEET variant cards into one, the first thing checked was whether P/L already treated them as one thing. It did - and wrongly. `server/pl.js` sums every card in a bucket (`b.costCents += row.costCents`), so three variants of one source on one race reported ~$598 wagered against a $200 bankroll, an ROI-on-bankroll of -299%. Distributions never had this problem: D20 has always selected ONE card per day per bucket. The rule simply never reached P/L. THE FIX: the TIPSHEET bucket total counts exactly ONE variant per (day, source). **The counted variant is FIXED** (`TIP_HEADLINE_VARIANT` = `win-only`, declared once in `shared/tip-staking.js`) and that is the load-bearing part - selecting the best-performing variant per day would be cherry-picking and would inflate every reported figure. The honest reading of the total is now "if you had bet the top pick to win, every time". A day staked with only one structure still counts once rather than being dropped, by falling back to the earliest variant present. The other two ride along as `byVariant`, rendered beside the total with an explicit note that adding them up would report money no bankroll could have staked - a breakdown, never a second pool, exactly the shape `byModel` (D76) and `byNotes` (D94) already use. SECOND HALF, same PR because it answers the same question - what did this card do: the day view's Betting cards table gained a **P/L column** for graded cards. Read from `graded_tickets_latest`, the same view `/api/pl` reports from, via correlated subqueries rather than another JOIN (joining grades beside the existing LEFT JOIN on tickets would have risked changing what `COUNT(t.id)` counts). The two screens are asserted to agree to the cent. **An ungraded card shows a dash, never $0** - not-graded-yet and broke-even are different facts, the same distinction `shared/tip-scoring.js` makes by returning null rather than zero. `plMoney`/`plClass` were moved into `client/src/api.js`, beside `modelLabel`/`blindnessLabel` and for the documented reason those live there, so `PLView` and `CardsPanel` render a P/L figure through ONE definition. The first draft used two invented class names (`pl-up`/`pl-down`) that exist nowhere in `styles.css` - caught by auditing every class against the stylesheet, the same mistake D169 made and the reason that audit is now habit. VERIFIED: `check-tip-staking` gained the assertions that matter - the total counts one variant not three (compared against SQL scoped to GRADED cards only, since that is the population `/api/pl` reports), it names which variant it counted, `byVariant` reports all three with exactly one flagged as counted, the breakdown sums to MORE than the total (proving it is not folded in), the total counts one CARD, a graded card carries `pl_cents` on the day view, an ungraded one reports null rather than 0, and the day view agrees with `/api/pl` to the cent. A first draft of those assertions FAILED for a good reason and was corrected rather than loosened: it compared P/L against every stored card while `/api/pl` only ever reports graded ones - two different populations. Full gate green: check-tip-staking, check-pl, check-distribution, check-tip-scoring, check-tip-extraction, check-schema, check-ingest, check-grading, check-export, check-module-bindings, check-llm-cards, check-human-picks, npm run build. **BROWSER-VERIFIED on real graded data** (Saratoga 2026-09-06, real results): the TIPSHEET bucket now reads $200 wagered on a $200 bankroll where it would have read $598; the by-variant table shows win-only -$200 (-100%), across-the-board -$144.87 (-73.2%), exacta-box -$200 (-100%) side by side - which is exactly the comparison the variants exist for, and would have been invisible in a merged total; and the day view's new P/L column renders real figures for TIPSHEET and LLM_GENERATED cards alike. Zero console errors. All repro data removed from the corpus copy afterwards. |
| D174 | One tipsheet card per source, growing as races are added | 4 | [#214](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/214) / `tipsheet-one-card-per-source` | merged | **User request 2026-09-08**: as tipsheet cards are added for each race, they need to be combined so there is one card for all races per tipsheet. D171 staked APPEND-ONLY, which was defensible in isolation and wrong for how a tip sheet is actually used - the picks arrive race by race, so staking after each one minted three MORE cards and a day accumulated a dozen near-duplicates instead of one card per way of betting one source. THE FIX IS THE PRECEDENT ALREADY IN THE CODEBASE: `server/human-cards.js` and `server/llm-cards.js` both accumulate races onto ONE card rather than multiplying cards. Staking now does the same - one card per (race day, source, variant), found again via `cards.tip_source_label` (migration 031, an ADDITIVE column, no rebuild; same shape and purpose as `cards.llm_model` from D76). The migration BACKFILLS the cards D171 already wrote, deriving the source from `cards.name` - safe only because D171 built that string in exactly one place and one format (source, em dash, label); a name not matching that shape is left NULL rather than guessed at, and a NULL simply means the next stake starts a fresh card. **A RE-STAKE REPLACES EVERY TICKET RATHER THAN APPENDING THE NEW RACE'S, AND THAT IS REQUIRED, NOT CONVENIENT.** The per-race budget is bankroll divided by races-with-picks (D163's whole-dollar floor), so adding a fourth race changes the stake on races 1-3 as well. Appending would leave three races priced for a three-race card sitting beside one priced for four, on the same card, summing past the bankroll. Verified live: a two-race card at $100/race became a three-race card at $66/race on the next stake, total $198 = 3 x $66. **Re-staking a GRADED card discards its grades** - `graded_tickets` cascades from `tickets`, exactly as the LLM regeneration path already behaves - and the card regrades. Warned, never blocked, which is the call D149 made for LLM regeneration on a graded card and D140 for locking onto a graded human card. Preview now returns `willUpdate` / `willRegrade` / `existingCardId` per variant so the UI says Update three cards instead of Save, names the card being updated, and shows an explicit warning before a reported P/L figure moves. **Pre-existing duplicates are handled by a script, not silently.** `npm run dedupe-tip-cards` (dry run by default, the house convention) folds each duplicated group to ONE, keeping the NEWEST because that is the card a later stake will reuse - keeping any other would leave the app writing to a card the script had decided was not the survivor. It **REFUSES to delete a graded card**, reporting it instead: a graded card is evidence, its P/L is in reports and its grades join into exports, so it is a person's decision rather than a tidy-up. Tested against seeded duplicates including a graded one: kept the newest, deleted the ungraded duplicate, refused the graded. VERIFIED: `check-tip-staking` gained the behavioural core - re-staking REUSES the same three card ids rather than appending, reports `reused`, a third race re-prices the WHOLE card (asserted as a strict decrease in the per-race budget), the card then covers all three races, a DIFFERENT source still gets its own three cards, migration 031's backfill statement exercised verbatim (including that a non-matching name stays NULL), and a graded card warned, re-staked, its stale grades gone, then regraded cleanly. Full gate green: check-tip-staking, check-tip-scoring, check-tip-extraction, check-schema, check-ingest, check-pl, check-grading, check-module-bindings, check-llm-cards, npm run build. **BROWSER-VERIFIED end to end on the real corpus copy**: staked two races (button read Save three cards, 3 cards created), added a third race's picks, re-staked - button now read Update three cards, the update notice rendered, the card count did NOT move, and the three cards each covered races 1-2-3 re-priced to $66/race. Under D171 that second stake would have produced six cards. Zero console errors, React root intact. A stale D166 assertion was updated rather than deleted: cards gained no tip_* column is now carries exactly ONE tip_* column, tip_source_label, so a third one would still have to be deliberate. All repro data removed from the corpus copy afterwards. |
| D173 | Fix: staked tipsheet cards needed a page reload to appear | 4 | [#213](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/213) / `fix-tip-cards-refresh` | merged | **Live bug report 2026-09-08**, and a repeat of a defect this codebase had already fixed once. `RaceDayView.jsx` remounts `CardsPanel` by bumping a `cardsVersion` counter passed as its `key`, because `CardsPanel` self-fetches on mount and cannot know a SIBLING wrote cards. D142 added that wire for the Equibase OTR panel. D171 then added a second sibling that writes cards - the tip-staking panel - and never gave it the `onSaved` prop, so its three cards landed in the database and appeared in the UI only after a manual reload. **The stale comment is part of the story and was fixed too.** The code above `cardsVersion` read "Bumped when a sibling of CardsPanel (currently just the Equibase OTR upload) writes cards" - accurate when written, and precisely the sentence that makes a second sibling easy to forget. It now names both and says plainly that any future card-writing sibling needs the same wire. VERIFIED IN A BROWSER, both directions, on the real corpus copy: tip picks seeded onto three races of SAR 2026-09-06 (day 268) using real non-scratched program numbers, then the whole Stake -> preview -> Save flow driven through the actual UI. On the shipped code the Betting cards table stayed at 10 rows while `GET /race-days/268/cards` returned 13 - the bug, measured rather than described. With the fix the table went 10 -> 13 with no reload, matching the server exactly, zero console errors, React root intact. **An incidental confirmation worth recording**: the staked cards show D171's longshot rule firing on real data - race 1's win-only card split into `$33 W 1` + `$33 P 1` because #1 was **8/1**, while races 2 and 3 (4/1 and 5/1) got win only. 8/1 is exactly the horse the supplied draft's `LONGSHOT_THRESHOLD_DECIMAL = 9` would have skipped, so the D171 correction is now demonstrated against a real morning line rather than only a unit case. All three variants totalled $198.00 = 3 races x $66, the whole-dollar per-race floor from D163 behaving correctly. Client-only wiring change; per the Definition of Done that is the presentation category, so `npm run build` is the bar - green, plus `check-module-bindings`. All repro data (3 cards, 3 tip rows) was removed from the worktree's corpus copy afterwards, verified back at 10 cards. |
| D172 | Fix: "Generate Card from LLM" blanked the whole page; a guard so the class cannot recur | 4 | [#211](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/211) / `fix-analyst-notes-blank-page` | merged | **Live bug report 2026-09-08**: clicking "Generate Card from LLM" on `/day/269` went to a blank page. **My own regression, from D167.** That PR moved the notes vocabulary into `shared/source-labels.js` and left `export { NOTE_SOURCE_LABELS as SOURCE_SUGGESTIONS } from '@shared/source-labels.js'` behind in `AnalystNotesEditor.jsx` - which re-exports the name WITHOUT creating a local binding, while the file still wrote `SOURCE_SUGGESTIONS.map(...)` in `NoteSourceDatalist`. That component renders only inside the LLM modal, which is why the day page itself looked fine and only the button was fatal. DIAGNOSED AND PROVED, not guessed: reproduced in a browser on day 268 with the exact console stack - `ReferenceError: SOURCE_SUGGESTIONS is not defined` at `NoteSourceDatalist` -> `LlmCardModal` -> `CardsPanel` -> `RaceDayView` - with `#root` going from 1 child to 0 and body text to 0 characters. Then the single file was reverted to `origin/main` to confirm causation, and restored to confirm the fix: modal opens, root intact, 17,713 characters of body text, zero errors on a fresh load, and the datalist rendering all four values (`program`, `public-handicapper`, `llm`, `own`) - which also proves the shared vocabulary now genuinely reaches the UI, the thing D167 claimed. Fix is one line of substance: import the name, then export it. **WHY NOTHING CAUGHT IT, AND THE GUARD THAT NOW DOES.** esbuild compiles a bare unresolved identifier as a global (it could be `window.foo`), so `npm run build` was green; and every check script in this repo is server-side and never renders a component body, so none of them execute the line.
