D167 also had no browser pass - it was a consolidation PR verified with check scripts and a build, which is exactly the gap. New `npm run check-module-bindings` scans every module in `client/src`, `shared`, `server` and `static/src` for a name that is USED in the body but only RE-EXPORTED, with a negative control, a not-used case and a comment-only case. It flags the real shipped regression and passes on the fix - both demonstrated by reverting and restoring the file. **The guard had its own bug worth recording**: it first reported `shared/replay.js: maxDrawdown`, whose only mention is a header comment. The cause was CRLF - this repo stores CRLF, and in JS `.` does not match a line terminator (`
` included), so `//.*$` never matched and NO comment was ever stripped. Rather than dismiss the hit as a false positive, it was traced to the byte level and the scanner now normalizes CRLF first. A checker that silently mis-parses is worse than no checker. VERIFIED: `check-module-bindings`, `check-llm-cards`, `check-tip-extraction`, `check-tip-scoring`, `check-tip-staking`, `check-schema`, `check-ingest`, `check-pl`, `npm run build` - all green, plus the browser reproduction and repair above. Scope note: day 269 postdates the corpus copy in this worktree, so the reproduction used day 268 - the bug is not day-specific, since `NoteSourceDatalist` renders for any day. |
| D171 | TIPSHEET staking: tip picks become graded cards, in three comparable variants | 4 | [#209](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/209) / `tip-picks-staking` | merged | **User request 2026-09-08**, closing the arc D166-D170. The scope named `shared/tipStaking.js` as "already-drafted"; archaeology found it in neither the tree nor ANY commit in history - the third stale premise in that document, after the `source_label` enum (D167) and the D150 source field. The user then supplied the draft directly. **THE DRAFT CANNOT RUN HERE, AND THAT WAS PROVEN RATHER THAN ASSERTED.** It sizes bets from the gap between the morning line and LIVE odds. `entries.live_odds` is populated on **0 of 13,710 rows across 0 of 169 race days**, and D113 deleted every fetcher that could fill it (invariant 6: every source is now a file a person uploaded, and an Equibase entries page prints the morning line, not live prices). Its logic was run UNCHANGED against the two real tip sheets this project has extracted: **zero bets and zero exclusions** - a silently empty card, because `morningLineToDecimal(undefined)` is null, `gapPct` is NaN, and every NaN comparison is false, so a pick is neither bet nor recorded as excluded. With a morning line but no live odds the gap is always 0, so it bets ONLY longshots and excludes rank 1 in every race - a systematic bias, not a strategy. Three further incompatibilities: `require()` throws in this ESM package; `win_place` is not a bet type the grader knows (win+place is two tickets here); and the place-money rule it leans on is marked *(engine-era)* in CLAUDE.md, retired with the lean engine. **Kevin's call, given the evidence: build the three approved variants now and keep the draft's longshot rule, documenting the gap logic as a future variant gated on a live-odds capture path.** **THE DRAFT ALSO CARRIED AN OFF-BY-ONE THAT WOULD HAVE DEFEATED ITS OWN RULE.** `LONGSHOT_THRESHOLD_DECIMAL = 9` is commented `8/1 -> decimal 9.0`, correct for EUROPEAN decimal odds. This codebase's `morningLineToDecimal('8/1')` returns **8** - the fractional ratio, as `winPayout`'s `stake * (ml + 1)` proves. Carried over unchanged the rule would have fired only at 9/1 and up, leaving an 8/1 horse (paying $18 on a $2 win) with no place money at all - the exact case invariant 1 was written for. Caught by this deliverable's own check, via an assertion that was itself malformed. THE DELIVERABLE: migration 030 (TIPSHEET joins the `cards` CHECK), pure `shared/tip-staking.js` (three variants, D163's whole-dollar per-race floor reused not reimplemented, the top LIVE pick backed exactly as D170 scores it), `server/tip-staking.js` (preview writes nothing; save writes three cards in ONE transaction, append-only), a `tipsheet` strategy template, and a staking panel on the day view. **THE MIGRATION IS THE RISKY HALF, AND WAS VERIFIED AGAINST THE REAL CORPUS, NEVER A FIXTURE** (CLAUDE.md's standing rule for this table). A CHECK cannot be ALTERed, so `cards` - five ON DELETE CASCADE children - is rebuilt. Applied to a `VACUUM INTO` copy of the live database: **136 cards, 3589 tickets, 1188 allocations, 3042 graded tickets, 43 human_race_state, 257 llm_card_requests - every count identical**, `sqlite_sequence` held at 189 so ids are still never reused, all four columns added since the last rebuild (022 `llm_model`, 023 `notes_present`, 025 `name`, 027 `external_id`/`built_on`/`saw_reference_cards`) preserved with their values, bucket distribution unchanged, `foreign_key_check` clean. **A first draft of the migration would have silently dropped `idx_cards_day`** - it restored only migration 027's unique index; caught by listing the live indexes rather than trusting the previous migration's text. **THREE MORE BUGS THE CHECKS CAUGHT, ALL OF THEM SILENT FAILURES.** (1) The pure module first emitted snake_case tickets, which made `estimateTicketPayouts` a NO-OP - it switches on `t.betType`, so every ticket fell through unpriced and every `est_payout_*` would have been null; the same class of bug D67, D73 and D91 each had to repair after the fact. (2) `selections` was stored as a bare legs array; `server/grading.js` reads `sel.races`/`sel.legs`, so grading threw 500 until it matched the `{races, legs}` shape every stored ticket already uses. (3) **A TIPSHEET card graded correctly and then vanished from P/L**, because `server/pl.js` kept its own `BUCKET_ORDER` copy that had never heard of the bucket. Four separate lists enumerated buckets (`server/pl.js`, `shared/distribution.js`, and a `BUCKET_CHIP` map in each of `PLView.jsx` and `DistributionView.jsx`); they are now ONE, in `shared/distribution.js`, which also gained the shared chip map - the same consolidation D167 made for source labels, for the same reason. Also repeated, and worth recording as a pattern rather than an incident: the `null`-vs-`undefined` destructuring slip from D170's scorer, made again here in `stakeTipRace` and caught again by the same junk-input assertion. VERIFIED: new `npm run check-tip-staking` - the three variants, the corrected 8/1 threshold WITH a 7/1 negative case, refusals instead of unpayable stakes (a budget that cannot fund three legs falls back to win and says so; below $2 nothing is written), the rebuild keeping every column and both indexes, preview writing nothing, three cards appended rather than edited, and a TIPSHEET card grading through the ORDINARY grader into its own P/L bucket with no special case. Two pre-existing assertions were UPDATED rather than deleted, both now correctly false: D166's "cards gained no TIPSHEET value" (extraction touched no card schema; staking deliberately does) and check-ingest's three-producer template list, now four. Full gate green: check-tip-staking, check-tip-scoring, check-tip-extraction, check-pl, check-distribution, check-schema, check-ingest, check-grading, check-human-picks, check-llm-cards, check-export, npm run build. **NOT VERIFIED**: no browser pass on the staking panel, and no real graded tipsheet card - the corpus has one active day with results and no tip picks on it, so every figure here is from fixtures. |
| D170 | TIPSHEET scoring: rank-based accuracy per source, against saved results | 4 | [#208](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/208) / `tip-picks-scoring` | merged | **User decision 2026-09-08**, closing the loop D166 and D169 left open. Asked what "close this feature" meant, because `tip_picks` had **no consumer at all**: extraction, review and correction all worked and nothing read the result - the picks were on no card, in no aggregate, and answered no question. Kevin's call between scoring and staking: **scoring first**, which is also what the original D166 scope named (rank-based: win %, rank accuracy) and what has to come first on the merits - staking would bet money on sources whose accuracy nobody has measured. **CARRIES NO MONEY, DELIBERATELY.** Nothing stakes a tip sheet yet, so any dollar figure here would be invented rather than measured. There is no P/L, no ROI and no `engine_version` anywhere in this deliverable; it reports how often the sheet's top pick won, placed or showed, and how much of the real top three it caught. THE JUDGEMENT CALLS, all written into the code rather than left implicit: a **scratched** top pick promotes the next LIVE pick (what a bettor reading the sheet at the window would actually have backed) and the substitution is reported rather than hidden; an **unplaced also-ran is a loss**, because it ran, while a program number the result has never heard of is **surfaced as `unknownPicks`** - almost always an extraction misread - instead of being silently scored a loss and quietly flattering or damning a source; and anything unscorable (no picks, no results on file, every pick scratched) returns **NULL, never zero**, because a zero is a claim. `top3Overlap` is scored against what SURVIVED scratches, so a race with two live picks is not marked out of three. **Every aggregate carries its `n`, and every rate is NULL at n=0** - this project's oldest surviving discipline, kept most deliberately through the pivot because the pivot's own motivating anecdote rested on 1 card and 3 graded tickets. It is enforced in the UI as well as the module: the client renders rates only through a helper that prints `"67% of 1"`, so a rate structurally cannot reach the screen without its denominator, and the day panel says in words that one day's counts are a tally rather than a verdict. `byTipSource` is the only total the module produces - two tip apps are two opinions, and a pooled "tipsheets went 4 for 10" would answer nobody's question (invariant 13). VERIFIED: new `npm run check-tip-scoring` - the pure cases (scratch promotion, also-ran vs unknown program number, NULL-not-zero three ways, junk never throwing) and a real server round trip on a seeded day, including **invariant 12** (a soft-deleted day leaves every figure, its day route answers 410 rather than stale numbers, and restore brings it back) and **invariant 13** (no pooled total, asserted structurally). Full gate green: `check-tip-scoring`, `check-tip-extraction`, `check-schema`, `check-ingest`, `check-grading`, `check-pl`, `check-export`, `check-human-picks`, `npm run build`. **A bug was found by this deliverable's own check**: `scoreTipRace(null)` threw, because a destructuring default only fires on `undefined` - and "never throws" is the contract every parser and scorer here holds to. Fixed with `input ?? {}`. **BROWSER-VERIFIED** on the agent's `dev:preview` stack against seeded results, with every displayed figure re-checked BY HAND against the seed: race 1 finished 6-4-7, so trackmaster's top pick #4 reads "ran 2nd", 0% won / 100% placed, 3 of 3 caught; race 2 finished 4-9-6, so numberfire's #6 reads "ran 3rd", 67% of the top three, and its #7 - which did not run in that race - is surfaced as not in the result. **The browser pass changed the design once**: with only Win / Placed / Top-3 columns, numberfire read "0% won, 0% placed" when its pick had in fact hit the board third, which is worse than what happened - a Showed column was added so the table cannot understate a source. **NOT YET VERIFIED AGAINST A REAL CORPUS.** Kevin offered to drop a copy of the real database into the worktree and it had not arrived by the time this landed, so every figure above comes from fixtures and seeded results - one track, one day, hand-built finishing orders. What that leaves unproven is behaviour at realistic scale and shape: many days, many tracks, real scratch patterns, charts where an also-ran carries no finishing position, and program numbers like `1A` in a coupled entry. The scorer handles each of those by construction and none is asserted against real data. The worktree's throwaway dev database was deleted afterwards so a dropped copy lands cleanly. |
| D169 | Review and correct extracted tip picks: the TIPSHEET HTTP surface and its edit path | 4 | [#207](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/207) / `tip-picks-review-ui` | merged | **User request 2026-09-08** - the review/edit UI D166 explicitly deferred. Archaeology found D166 had shipped extraction, a writer and a check script but **no HTTP surface at all**, so this delivers routes as well as UI. **THE DESIGN QUESTION WAS INVARIANT 9.** It says a preview is read-only and corrections happen AT THE SOURCE - fix the pasted text, re-parse - which is coherent for every other ingest path in this codebase and impossible here: the source is a photograph. There is nothing to correct and re-run, a re-extraction is a paid call that may repeat the misread, and meanwhile one wrong program number silently scores a tipsheet on picks it never made, which is worse than any hand-edit. **Kevin's call, given three options: keep invariant 9 LITERALLY TRUE rather than carve an exception out of it.** The invariant governs the parse -> save path, and that path stays read-only: a save still stores verbatim model output. Correcting is a SEPARATE, later, explicitly recorded act on an already-stored row. Migration 029 adds `picks_extracted` (the model's original, written on the FIRST edit only, so it never degrades into the previous edit) and `edited_at`. `picks_extracted IS NULL` therefore means "never touched by a human", and every corrected row becomes a labelled example of what the extraction got wrong - extraction accuracy stays measurable, which is the PURPOSE invariant 9 protects. **One place this could not copy `equibase-otr.js`.** That module's confirm re-reads archived PDF BYTES and re-parses them, so the client is never trusted. Re-parsing here would mean re-calling a vision model - paid, and non-deterministic (D166 could not even pin `temperature`). So the archived artifact is the model's own RESPONSE alongside the image, and confirm re-parses THAT. The discipline is preserved exactly: `POST .../tip-picks` ignores anything the browser sends about picks, which is asserted by posting a save body containing an injected horse and proving it does not appear. THE DELIVERABLE: `server/tip-picks.js` (preview / save / list / `PATCH` correct / delete, archiving under `BETSHEET_TIP_ARCHIVE_DIR` regardless of outcome per invariant 11's spirit, and tracing `tip_extraction_attempted` / `tip_picks_saved` / `tip_picks_corrected` / `tip_picks_deleted`), `client/src/components/TipPicksPanel.jsx` on `RaceDayView.jsx`, and client API calls. A correction goes through the SAME `validateTipPicks` the model's output does, so a human cannot hand-write a ranking the parser would have refused. `data/archive/tip-picks/` is gitignored - third-party app screenshots, per the same reasoning that keeps `scratch/tip-samples/` out, and unlike the single shared OTR fixture the repo does carry. VERIFIED: `check-tip-extraction` gained 18 route assertions on a real server with a stubbed vision call (preview WROTE NOTHING; save re-parses the archive and ignores client-supplied picks; a saved row starts unedited; a correction is applied, normalized `9-2`->`9/2`, marked, and preserves the original; a SECOND edit still keeps the FIRST extraction; a hand-typed duplicate rank refused 422; unknown parseToken 404; unknown race 404; non-image 400 before any model call). Full gate green: `check-tip-extraction`, `check-schema`, `check-ingest`, `check-llm-cards`, `check-grading`, `check-human-picks`, `check-pl`, `check-export`, `npm run build`. **BROWSER-VERIFIED end to end on the agent's own dev:preview stack, with REAL screenshots and REAL vision calls** - the worktree has no `data/betsheet.sqlite`, so the stack created its own empty database and the 28MB corpus was structurally out of reach. Uploaded the NumberFire screenshot through the actual file input (a real `File` via `DataTransfer`): source read as `numberfire` off the UI chrome, picks exactly right, "no odds on this sheet" shown as normal rather than an error, **and "On file" still read 1 while the preview was open, proving the preview wrote nothing**; the saved row came back `edited: false, picksExtracted: null`. Then corrected race 1 through the real editor: `9-2` normalized to `9/2`, `#2 Fancy Feet` preserved in `picksExtracted`, the row tagged `corrected`, and "What the model originally read" rendering beside it. **Browser verification found a real defect the check scripts could not**: the odds inputs carried `placeholder="4/5"`, which in a column of odds values reads as DATA - the editor appeared to show a morning line of 4/5 for all three horses. Confirmed via the DOM that the values were genuinely empty before concluding it was cosmetic rather than a state bug; placeholders removed and the format hint moved into the helper text. Also corrected on the way: the first draft used six CSS classes that do not exist in `styles.css` (`panel--inset`, `row--end`, `muted`, `error`, `warnings`, plus `col-detail`, which is a static-app class), rewritten to the house idiom (`dim`, `notice notice--warn`, `formrow`, `race`) with one genuinely new rule, `.tip-edit`. **Deviation from D168, stated per its own requirement**: this touches `client/src/styles.css`, which IS one of the seven files in the static app's import surface, so D168 asks for `check-static-app` to be run from the primary checkout. It was NOT run - doing so would mean switching the user's primary checkout onto this branch. The change there is 11 added lines and 0 removed, `.tip-edit` is referenced by nothing in `static/`, and none of that script's assertions (bundle composition, Pages base, service-worker strategies, the `static.css`-vs-`mobile.js` breakpoint) can be reached by an additive client CSS rule. Flagged rather than assumed. |
| D168 | HOUSE RULE: `check-static-app` is out of scope in a git worktree | 4 | [#206](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/206) / `static-app-worktree-house-rule` | merged | **User rule 2026-09-08**, prompted directly by D167's behaviour: told the check could not run in a worktree, the session created a `node_modules` directory junction to force it. That worked, and was still the wrong instinct - which is what the rule now says out loud. THE FACTS: a git worktree has no `node_modules` of its own, because module resolution walks UP to the parent checkout - which is exactly why `npm run build` and every other check script work fine inside one. `scripts/check-static-app.js` is the single exception: it spawns an ABSOLUTE `<repo>/node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js`, which in a worktree does not exist. It therefore fails identically on `main`, and a failure there is never the branch's doing. **The rule is about EFFORT, not correctness.** The junction bought nothing: it cost real time, and left a stray reparse point behind that could easily make a later session believe dependencies were installed locally when they are not. The cheaper and more reliable question is the static app's IMPORT SURFACE, since it is a separate Vite entry that reaches outside `static/` in exactly seven places. **That list was VERIFIED for this rule rather than asserted from memory, and the first draft of the rule was WRONG** - it claimed static imports only `TicketBuilder.jsx` and `EntriesTable.jsx` from `client/`, and grepping found it also takes `@client/styles.css`, `@shared/betmath.js` and `@shared/parsers/human-picks.js`. A house rule carrying a short import list would have caused exactly the skipped-check-that-mattered it exists to prevent, so the corrected seven-entry list is written into the rule with the instruction to re-derive it rather than trust it. Also required by the rule: when the check is skipped, SAY SO in the final message - never let a report imply the suite ran clean when one script did not run at all. THE DELIVERABLE: the rule in CLAUDE.md's Gotchas beside the other house rules (D133's modal backdrop, D157's mobile call-to-action), a pointer at the point of use in the Commands block so a session reading `npm run check-static-app` sees it there too, a feature-status row, and the removal of the junction D167 created (verified as a reparse point first, so `rmdir` removed the LINK and not the parent's real `node_modules` - confirmed intact at 133 entries afterwards, with `npm run build` and `check-schema` still green in the worktree). D167's own ledger row carried a trailing "worth its own fix if worktrees become routine", which this supersedes; that sentence has been corrected in place rather than left to contradict the rule. No code changed. VERIFIED: documentation-only, `npm run build` green. `check-static-app` itself was NOT run - out of scope here by the very rule this delivers. |
| D167 | One owner for `source_label`: consolidate the two vocabularies D166 left behind | 4 | [#205](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/205) / `source-label-consolidation` | merged | **User request 2026-09-08**, following D166's own flagged finding that the `source_label` enum its scope assumed does not exist. D166 resolved that by giving `tip_picks` a vocabulary in code - which left the codebase answering ONE question, "who said this", in TWO places with different guarantees: `llm_notes.source_label` (vocabulary hard-coded in `client/src/components/AnalystNotesEditor.jsx`, fallback `user`, **never normalized on write**) and `tip_picks.source_label` (vocabulary in `shared/tip-picks.js`, fallback `tipsheet-other`, normalized). Same column name, same meaning, two catalogues, two fallbacks, one normalizer between them - the exact drift D125 (five copies of `nameKey`), D135 (three copies of the entries table) and D159 (two copies of the notes editor) were each written to undo. **Archaeology corrected the motivating hypothesis.** The assumption going in was that an unnormalized write path had produced dirty data. It has not: a read-only query over the real corpus returns only `llm` (23), `public-handicapper` (1) and NULL (13) in `llm_notes`, and `llm`/`user`/`public-handicapper` in `llm_card_requests.notes_source_label` - no casing or whitespace variants at all, because the editor offers a `<datalist>` and this is a single-user app. So the data is clean **by luck of the UI, not by construction**, and the defect is a missing guarantee rather than existing dirt. Recorded because it changes the justification: this is prevention, not repair, and no backfill was needed or written. THE DELIVERABLE: new pure, browser-safe `shared/source-labels.js` owning the single `normalizeSourceLabel` plus `isNormalizedSourceLabel` (the inertness probe). **The two catalogues are deliberately NOT merged**: `NOTE_SOURCE_LABELS` answers what KIND of commentary a note is, `TIP_SOURCE_LABELS` answers WHICH APP published a pick set, and `trackmaster` BELONGS TO the kind `public-handicapper` - they are two axes, and flattening them would assert that a publisher and a provenance-kind are the same sort of value. What they now share is the FORM of a label and the one function that produces it. **The two fallbacks also stay distinct**, with the reason written next to them: a blank note is the user's own (`user`), while a tip sheet whose publisher could not be read is nobody's personal opinion (`tipsheet-other`), and collapsing them would make a failed screenshot look like the user's own view - the kind of mislabelling invariant 13 exists to prevent. `shared/tip-picks.js` re-exports rather than keeping a copy; `AnalystNotesEditor.jsx` re-exports `SOURCE_SUGGESTIONS` from the shared module so the datalist offers exactly what the server normalizes against. **The one judgement call: `writeNote` now normalizes on the way in.** The label reaches the LLM prompt (`llm-prompt.js` renders `source="..."`) and LLM cards have NO version axis, so this is the un-versioned comparability boundary this project treats as significant. It was taken because the effect runs the RIGHT way: `Program` and `program` currently render as two different prompts for one source, and normalizing makes them one - it REDUCES spurious prompt variation rather than introducing it - and because it is provably INERT for every value in the corpus, which is asserted directly with a negative control proving the probe can fail. An empty label still stores NULL rather than a fabricated one; the `user` fallback stays where it always was, at READ time. **P-3.2's `cards.source_label`/`source_label_other` were deliberately NOT built**: still unscheduled, and `docs/requirements/card-source-model.md`'s own archaeology found a card `source` column would be a fourth encoding of a fact three columns already agree on 1:1. VERIFIED: `check-llm-cards` gained a D167 block - normalization inert across the real corpus vocabulary WITH a negative control, every catalogue entry already a fixed point, the two fallbacks distinct, `tip_picks` delegating rather than owning a second normalizer, a messily-typed label (`'  Public Handicapper  '`) normalized through the REAL endpoint, and an empty label still storing NULL. All green: `check-llm-cards`, `check-llm-input-capture`, `check-schema`, `check-ingest`, `check-grading`, `check-human-picks`, `check-tip-extraction` (both phases, real screenshots included), `check-static-app`, `npm run build`. **Two process notes.** (1) A careless wide-range edit to `shared/tip-picks.js` silently deleted `normalizeOdds` - its definition sat between the two anchors being replaced, while its CALL SITE inside `validateTipPicks` remained, so the file still looked plausible. Caught by `check-tip-extraction` failing loudly, restored VERBATIM from `origin/main`, and the export surface then diffed against main to prove nothing else had gone. The lesson is that anchor-to-anchor replacement must be checked for what it spans, not just what it starts and ends at. (2) `npm run check-static-app` cannot run in a git worktree as shipped: the worktree has no `node_modules` of its own (resolution walks up to the parent checkout, which is why `npm run build` works fine) while that script spawns an ABSOLUTE `<repo>/node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js`. It fails identically on `main`, so it is pre-existing and not this branch's doing; run here via a directory junction. **Superseded 2026-09-08 by the D168 house rule**: this check is simply out of scope in a worktree, and the junction that made it run was the wrong instinct - it has been removed. |
| D166 | TIPSHEET screenshot extraction: a third-party tip app's picks into structured `tip_picks` rows | 4 | [#204](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/204) / `claude/tipsheet-screenshot-extraction-14fc5e` | merged | **User request 2026-09-08** (`Scope: TIPSHEET Screenshot Extraction`), which asked for an archaeology gate before any code. That gate answered two of its own questions with "the premise is stale" and produced the one conversion this deliverable actually turns on. **(1) There is no `source_label` enum to extend.** The gate asked whether the vocabulary `emubets, drf, keeneland-tipsheet, own, chatgpt, gemini, claude-chat, other` needed `trackmaster`/`numberfire` added. That vocabulary exists in exactly one place - `docs/requirements/card-source-model.md` P-3.2, SPECIFIED-NOT-SCHEDULED with no deliverable ID - and in no table, no CHECK constraint and no code. It is the SAME stale premise D150-D155 hit, where Kevin's call was to drop it from scope. Implemented here as free TEXT on `tip_picks` plus a seed list (`TIP_SOURCE_LABELS`) in `shared/tip-picks.js` that the extraction prompt steers toward and `normalizeSourceLabel` snaps aliases to - deliberately NOT a DB CHECK, because the set of tip apps someone screenshots is still being discovered and a CHECK would make meeting a new one at a racetrack a schema migration. An unrecognized app keeps its own slug (countable, promotable later) rather than being refused or silently flattened. **(2) The load-bearing conversion is the HYPHEN.** `morningLineToDecimal` (shared/betmath.js) admits `4/5` and bare numbers and nothing else; tote boards and tip apps overwhelmingly print `4-5` and `9-2`. A hyphenated price stored verbatim would look captured and read back as `null` - odds present in the data, unusable by every payout path. `normalizeOdds` converts it, plus `EVEN`->`1/1` and `7 to 2`->`7/2`, and round-trips every result through `morningLineToDecimal` so anything that will not parse comes back null instead of being stored. It deliberately does NOT reduce `8/1` to `8` even though both decode to 8: the real corpus stores the explicit denominator (`4/5`, `8/1`, `12/1` are all live values on the fixture day), so a tipsheet price must render the way the morning line beside it does. **(3) A new TABLE, not a widened `cards`.** That is what keeps the promise that no HUMAN/LLM/OTR schema or grading path is touched: a tip row is a ranked opinion, not a card - no tickets, no bankroll, no grade set - so `cards.consensus_completeness` gains NO `TIPSHEET` value and `cards` is not rebuilt (it is the riskiest table in this schema to rebuild, ten cascading children). Staking, when it arrives, writes ordinary cards that REFERENCE these rows rather than turning them into cards. **No `completeness` column**, per the scope: invariant 13 buckets on what signal a card had, every tip row has exactly one signal (the source's ranking), and EQB_OTR carries no odds either without needing a tier for it. **(4) `complete()` already speaks vision** - `buildMessages` passes `user` straight through, so an array of content blocks works as-is; the only change to `server/anthropic-client.js` is a comment saying so, no behaviour. THE DELIVERABLE: migration 028 (`tip_picks`, UNIQUE on (day, race, source) so re-extracting one app's picks for one race is a correction rather than a second opinion), pure `shared/tip-picks.js` (browser-safe: normalization plus `validateTipPicks`, which never throws and carries a per-warning `blocking` boolean the way `shared/parsers/human-picks.js` does), and `server/tip-extraction.js` (vision call at temperature 0 - this is transcription, not generation - JSON-only prompt, tolerant recovery from a fenced or prose-wrapped response, media type from magic bytes never the extension, `raw_extraction` + `image_sha256` kept for audit). **`extractTipPicks` never writes**; `insertTipPicks` is a separate explicit call, so a future preview surface shows what a save would store (invariant 9's shape). **The blocking/non-blocking split is the judgement**: a broken RANKING blocks (no picks, a missing/zero/duplicated rank, the same horse twice) because rank is the only signal this bucket has; anything about ODDS never blocks, because a sheet printing 1st/2nd/3rd and no prices is an ordinary tipsheet, and a price the model could not read is dropped with an advisory rather than guessed. Odds are OMITTED from the pick object, never null-filled, so "not shown" and "shown but unreadable" are both honestly absent. VERIFIED: new `npm run check-tip-extraction` on a throwaway temp DB with the logger redirected there too (per the Gotcha - `resetLogs()` reads the LOGGER's directory, so pointing only `BETSHEET_DB` somewhere safe is not isolation). Phase 1 is hermetic and always runs: migration 028's columns/cascade/CHECKs (a non-TIPSHEET bucket and `race_no` 0 both refused), explicit assertions that `cards` gained no TIPSHEET value and no tip_* column, the six odds conversions each re-checked through `morningLineToDecimal`, eight non-prices yielding null, the five blocking rank cases, junk never throwing, and the whole extract->validate->persist->read-back path under `BETSHEET_TIP_TEST_MODE=1` (the same escape hatch shape as `BETSHEET_LLM_TEST_MODE`) including a fenced-plus-prose response recovered, extraction writing nothing, re-extraction replacing, a second source coexisting, and a factory reset NAMING `tip_picks` in its audit rather than cascading it silently (`WIPE_ORDER` extended, per migration 023's stale-list lesson). `check-schema`, `check-ingest`, `check-llm-cards` and `npm run build` all green. **Phase 2 - the real vision call against real screenshots - SKIPS loudly rather than failing when images or a key are absent**, and is deliberately not a build gate: there is no golden for "did the model read this picture correctly", so it prints each extraction beside its source file for eyeball comparison and asserts only the machine-checkable part (every captured price parses via `morningLineToDecimal`; no price stored as null). Samples live in gitignored `scratch/tip-samples/` - they are screenshots of someone else's app, not ours to commit. **PHASE 2 RAN, on two real screenshots (2026-09-08), and both extracted exactly right.** The samples are the same race read by two different apps, and each image shows BOTH the TrackMaster and NumberFire tabs with only one selected - so "read the source off the UI chrome" was a genuine test rather than a formality, and the filename never reaches the prompt (`sourceHint` is empty; only bytes are read). TrackMaster -> `trackmaster`, #4 Karazest / #6 Union Roar / #2 Fancy Feet; NumberFire -> `numberfire`, #6 Union Roar / #4 Karazest / #7 Last Candy. Both correct against the images, ranks and program numbers included, and NEITHER invented a price - which is the behaviour that actually matters, since both sheets show no odds at all. Three repeated reads of each sample returned identical output. **A real bug was found by that run, and the first diagnosis of it was WRONG - both are recorded because the correction is the useful part.** The call 400'd with `` `temperature` is deprecated for this model. `` and the immediate inference was that every real LLM card generation must be broken too, since `complete()` always sends the field. Testing the API directly disproved it: `temperature: 1` (the `DEFAULT_REQUEST_PARAMS` value every LLM card uses) is accepted normally, and only a NON-DEFAULT value is rejected. So the LLM path was never affected and the bug was entirely D166's own - it pinned `temperature: 0` for determinism, which is precisely the case these models refuse. Fixed by teaching `complete()` that `temperature: null` means OMIT THE FIELD (additive; every existing caller passes a number and is byte-identical), and the vision call now sends none. Determinism is therefore not on offer for this extraction, which the code comment now says instead of claiming temperature 0. Recorded as a Gotcha because no check script could have caught it: they all stub the API under a TEST_MODE flag, so a retired request PARAMETER is invisible until a real call is made. **One done-when is NOT closed by a real screenshot**: neither sample shows odds, so "when odds ARE present they are captured in the fraction-string format `morningLineToDecimal` expects" is proven only by the pure phase-1 assertions (six conversions, each re-checked through `morningLineToDecimal`) and not end-to-end from an image. A tip-sheet screenshot that prints prices would close it; the extraction path for odds is otherwise unexercised against a real picture. **Flagged, not solved** (per the scope): this adds a fourth bucket in a SEPARATE table, so D09's unsettled three-source classification does not automatically inherit it - a `tip_picks` row is not a card and no classifier reads it today. Whoever picks D09 up should decide deliberately whether a tipsheet ranking counts as a source, rather than inheriting an answer from this schema. |
| D165 | /card page: carry D164's read-only Analyst Notes panel onto the card sheet | 4 | [#203](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/203) / `card-view-notes-panel` | merged | **User request**, immediate follow-up to D164: the notes panel landed on `/day` (`RaceDayView.jsx`) but the card sheet itself (`/card`, `CardView.jsx`) - arguably the more natural place to check a race's commentary while reading its tickets - had none. `CardView.jsx` already receives `card.race_day_id` on its `GET /api/cards/:id` response (nothing new needed there), so a `useEffect` keyed on it calls the same `GET /api/race-days/:id/llm-notes` `RaceDayView.jsx` and `RaceDayNotesModal.jsx` already call, into a `notesByRace` map. Each race's `<details className="race race--sheet">` block renders the panel right after its Entries panel, before Results - grouped with the sheet's other collapsed reference panels. **Refactor alongside the feature**: D164's per-race notes markup in `RaceDayView.jsx` was an inline IIFE; rather than write a second copy of the same JSX into `CardView.jsx`, both were consolidated into a new shared `client/src/components/RaceNotes.jsx` (`RaceNotes({ note })`, presentational only, no fetch of its own) - the same extraction discipline this codebase already applies to `EntriesTable.jsx` (D134/D135) and `AnalystNotesEditor.jsx` (D159), so the two pages cannot render this panel two different ways. No schema, API, or write-path change - display only, same as D164. Verification: presentation-only per the Definition of Done, so `npm run build` (green) plus a real browser pass (`dev:preview`, port 5185) opening an LLM-generated card whose race 2 carries a populated note, confirming the panel expands with the same text/source/updated-at D164 showed on `/day`. |
| D164 | /day page: read-only Analyst Notes panel on each race card | 4 | [#202](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/202) / `race-day-notes-readonly` | merged | **User request**: analyst notes (D159's "Enter Analyst Notes", and the LLM generator's own per-race notes fields - the same `llm_notes` draft, D92) were writable from two places but had no read-only display anywhere. `client/src/components/RaceDayView.jsx` now fetches `GET /api/race-days/:id/llm-notes` (same endpoint `RaceDayNotesModal.jsx` and `LlmCardModal.jsx` already use) into a `notesByRace` map and renders a nested `<details className="race-notes">` inside each race's own `<details>`, collapsed by default, summary reading "Analyst Notes" (or "Analyst Notes — none" when the race has no note) so a glance at the collapsed page tells you which races have commentary without opening anything. Open, it shows the note text (`.notes-text`, `white-space: pre-wrap` so paragraph breaks survive) plus its source label and last-updated timestamp. Closing the "Enter Analyst Notes" modal now also calls the same loader, so an edit shows up immediately without a page reload. | D163 | The per-race LLM bankroll was a number no legal wager could add up to; plus the prompt rules that let the model plug the remainder | 4 | [#201](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/201) / `llm-per-race-bankroll-whole-dollars` | merged | **User bug report 2026-09-07**: four blocked races on day 263 - race 3 `$1.33 per combo is not a multiple of the $1.00 increment for exacta`, race 10 `$4.25 does not split evenly across 24 combos`, race 11 `$1.75 per combo is not a multiple of the $0.50 increment for trifecta`, race 14 `$10.00 does not split evenly across 6 combos`. All four on the post-D162 prompt (`prompt_template_version` `b97234232fc3`, requests 329/336/337/340) and all four reproduced from the stored `response_text` through the real `extractTicketBlock` -> `parseHumanPicksText` path. **The first fix in this series that is NOT primarily a prompt defect**, and the tell is that the illegal stake is EXACTLY the leftover every time: race 3 staked $13.00 of a $14.33 bankroll and plugged the $1.33; race 11 staked $12.50 of $14.25 and plugged the $1.75; race 10 staked $10.00 of $14.25 and plugged the $4.25. Root cause is `previewLlmRace`'s `Math.round(remainingCents / remaining)`: $172.00/12 = $14.3333, $85.50/6 = $14.25, $57.50/4 = $14.375. The cheapest wager step in play is 50c and is usually $1, so **a target ending in .33 or .38 cannot be spent exactly by any combination of legal wagers** - the model was handed a figure it could not hit, and nothing in the prompt said a remainder was allowed to survive ("must not exceed" reads as a target as easily as a ceiling), so it made the last ticket the plug and the plug was illegal. Race 14 is the extreme case: its menu really does print `$5 Exacta` (confirmed, `parseWagerMenu` returns `exacta: 500`), so a 3-horse exacta box genuinely costs 6 x $5 = $30 against a $14.38 bankroll - the model reasoned about that correctly ("$5.00 x 6 combos = $30.00 exceeds bankroll, so this line is rewritten below") and then never wrote the rewrite, leaving an illegal $10 standing. **Race 10 is D162 backfiring, recorded as such**: told by D162 that the shown arithmetic must equal the `<stake>`, the model made them agree by inventing a per-combination price of $0.177 (`$0.177 x 24 combos = $4.25`) rather than by changing the stake - D162 said the two must match but never said which side gives way. Fix in three parts. (1) **Server**: new exported `perRaceBankrollCents(remainingCents, racesRemaining)` in `server/llm-cards.js` floors the share to a whole DOLLAR, so all four cases become $14.00. FLOOR not round, because per-race shares must never sum to more than the bankroll they are carved from and `Math.round` genuinely violated that ($57.50/4 rounds UP to $14.38; 4 x $14.38 = $57.52) - pinned by a negative control asserting the old expression really did overspend. Costs at most 99c of headroom per race, which only a 50c trifecta is fine-grained enough to notice. (2) **Prompt, budget rule**: the bankroll is a CEILING not a target, spending less is normal, a stake is never derived by subtracting spend from bankroll, and a ticket with no legal price that fits is DROPPED rather than squeezed in - with race 14's dangling "rewritten below" named as its own prohibition. (3) **Prompt, D162 clarification**: when the arithmetic and the `<stake>` disagree it is always the `<stake>` that gives way, never the per-combination figure, which is the base unit or a whole multiple of it and nothing else; `$0.177 x 24 combos = $4.25` is quoted as the anti-example. No schema change and **no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump** - an LLM card's `engine_version` is the literal `'llm'` with no version axis, so this is another un-versioned permanent comparability boundary, the first that moves a generation INPUT rather than only prompt text; `llm_card_requests.prompt_text` carries the actual per-race figure, so which side a stored card sits on is checkable per row. `scripts/check-llm-cards.js` gained a new pure `perRaceBankrollCents` phase (the four real cases by name, whole-dollar output, the never-overspend property with its negative control, and the four degenerate inputs) plus two prompt assertions. Verification: `npm run check-llm-cards`, `npm run check-human-picks`, `npm run build` all green; `PROMPT_TEMPLATE_VERSION` moved b97234232fc3 -> 3c38f4dde42d. |
| D162 | LLM prompt fix: the box arithmetic D161 requires must equal the `<stake>` column it prices | 4 | [#200](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/200) / `llm-prompt-stake-matches-arithmetic` | merged | **User bug report 2026-09-07**, the same evening D161 merged: `BLOCKING: Race 11: $0.25 per combo is below the $0.50 minimum for trifecta box.` Traced to `llm_card_requests` id 314 (`claude-opus-5`, race day 262 race 11) and reproduced by re-running the stored `response_text` through the real `extractTicketBlock` -> `parseHumanPicksText` path against that race's real entries and wager menu (`50c Trifecta`), which returns exactly the reported warning. The request's `prompt_template_version` matches the POST-D161 hash `740e51c99878`, so it read the show-your-work prompt. **This is a NEW defect, not a third recurrence of D160/D161** - D161 worked. The line was `trifecta box | #3,#10,#7,#4 | $6 | ... (4 x 3 x 2 = 24 combos; $0.50 x 24 combos = $12.00 - halved at 25c not allowed, so priced at $6 is invalid; using $12 instead.)`: every factor is written out, the combo count is the correct 24, the product is the correct $12.00, and the model even states in prose that $6 is invalid - but the `<stake>` column was written BEFORE the arithmetic and never revised, so `$6` is what the parser reads and `$6 / 24 = $0.25` is what it refuses. The same response's exacta box carried the mirror image (`$4` total with a `"$1.00 x 2 combos = $4.00"` check whose own product is $2.00), which surfaced the non-blocking `parenthetical_mismatch` D161 predicted, confirming that half of D161 works as designed. Root shape of both: D161 asked the model to show its work but never said the shown work had to EQUAL the `<stake>` column, and the column order makes that the natural failure - `<stake>` is emitted before `<rationale>`, so "show your work" happens after the number is already committed, and a model that catches its own slip has no way to fix it except by rewriting the line. Fix is prompt-only, same posture as D145/D146/D148/D160/D161: the arithmetic is now stated to BE the ticket's price rather than a comment on it - its `= $<total>` must be the same amount as `<stake>`, the multiplication comes first and its product is what gets written into `<stake>`, and a disagreement must be fixed by REWRITING THE WHOLE LINE, never by narrating the correction in the `<rationale>` (that exact narration is quoted in the prompt as a non-fix). Carries a second, LATENT fix found while reading the cross-check it feeds: D161's `"$<base> x <combos> combos"` wording was wrong for any box priced ABOVE the base unit, because `STAKE_CHECK_RE` compares the stated figure against the ACTUAL per-combination cost - a perfectly legal $24 on 24 combos, written as "$0.50 x 24 combos" per D161's wording, would have raised a spurious `parenthetical_mismatch`. The field is renamed `$<per-combo>` and the $24 case is spelled out. No parser, server or schema change - the block was correct and is still the backstop. Also removes a stale DUPLICATE D160 ledger row left by the PR #198/#199 merges (two rows for one ID; the fuller one, carrying the "Superseded in part by D161" note, is kept). `scripts/check-llm-cards.js` gained two assertions - one pinning the arithmetic-binds-to-stake text, one pinning `$<per-combo>` and asserting the old `$<base> x` wording is GONE. A mirror check confirmed every line of `SYSTEM_PROMPT` still appears verbatim in `docs/prompts/llm-card-v1.md`. Verification: `npm run check-llm-cards`, `npm run check-human-picks`, `npm run build` all green; `PROMPT_TEMPLATE_VERSION` moved 740e51c99878 -> b97234232fc3. |
| D161 | LLM prompt fix: require box bets to show combo arithmetic in the rationale, after the D160 fix alone didn't stop a recurrence | 4 | [#199](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/199) / `llm-prompt-box-arithmetic-shown` | merged | **User bug report 2026-09-07**, one race after D160 merged: `trifecta box | #9,#6,#3,#2 | $6` with rationale "...at 50c per combination" - the IDENTICAL 4-horse trifecta box miscount D160 fixed (12-combo assumption vs. the real 24), on a request whose `prompt_template_version` confirmed it read the corrected, post-D160 prompt including its new trifecta-box worked example. A textual warning plus one worked example, even one naming the exact wrong (12) and right (24) numbers, was not sufficient. Escalation, still prompt-only: the `<stake>` rule now REQUIRES every box bet's `<rationale>` to show the combination arithmetic inline - every factor multiplied out, then a `"$<base> x <combos> combos"` check matching the total - rather than let the model reach a total from an unstated mental calculation ("show your work," which tends to catch a slip that a purely-stated rule does not). This also activates, for free, `shared/parsers/human-picks.js`'s existing `STAKE_CHECK_RE`/`parenthetical_mismatch` cross-check (D84, built for the teller grammar): the LLM's `<rationale>` column IS `parseColumnRow`'s `checkTail`, so a model that states its own wrong arithmetic now surfaces a SECOND, distinct non-blocking warning naming the disagreement, in addition to whatever primary warning blocks the save. No parser, server, or schema change - the cross-check already existed for a different grammar. Documented in `docs/prompts/llm-card-v1.md`'s Fixes section with the same "not provably foolproof" caveat as every fix above, more visibly than ever given this is the second miscount on the same box type - `shared/parsers/human-picks.js`'s blocking validation remains the real backstop. `scripts/check-llm-cards.js` gained one assertion pinning the new required-arithmetic text. Verification: `npm run check-llm-cards`, `npm run build` both green. |
| D160 | LLM prompt fix: trifecta box combo count computed with the exacta box formula, one factor short | 4 | [#198](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/198) / `llm-prompt-trifecta-box-combo` | merged | **User bug report 2026-09-07** on a real 11-race generation (card 187, claude-opus-5): six blocked tickets across five races. Root cause confirmed against the logged `response_text` for each - two distinct mistakes. (1) Races 6, 9, 10 and 11 each boxed 4 horses in a TRIFECTA box and priced it as if it had 4 x 3 = 12 combinations (the EXACTA box formula) instead of the correct 4 x 3 x 2 = 24 - always exactly half, e.g. race 6/9/10's "$6 on a 50c box" landed at $6 / 24 = $0.25/combo (below the 50c minimum) and race 11's $9 didn't divide evenly across the real 24. The prompt's only worked stake example was for a 2-factor EXACTA box, with nothing anchoring the 3-factor case. (2) Race 3 wrote `trifecta | #6,#9,#3 | $3` with a rationale reading "50c box on the three most likely" - intent was a box, but the `<bet type>` column said bare "trifecta", so the comma list read as one straight-bet leg needing 3 positions (`insufficient_selections`, correctly blocked). The `<selections>` rule explained the box comma-list grammar but never tied it back to the bet-type column. Fix is prompt-only, same posture as the D145/D146/D148 fixes: the `<stake>` rule's worked example gains a TRIFECTA-box case matching this exact failure and states plainly that trifecta/superfecta box have one MORE FACTOR than exacta box, not the same count; the `<selections>` rule gains an explicit link that a comma-separated list is legal ONLY under a bet type whose name says "box". No parser, server or schema change - both blocks were exactly correct; documented in `docs/prompts/llm-card-v1.md`'s Fixes section. `scripts/check-llm-cards.js` gained two assertions pinning the new prompt text (the trifecta-box worked example, the box/selections link), each keyed on a distinctive phrase so it cannot be dropped silently. Verification: `npm run check-llm-cards`, `npm run build` both green. **Superseded in part by D161**: the combo-count fix here reduced but did not eliminate the same mistake - see D161. |
| D77-A | Dev harness: stop phantom API restarts killing in-flight requests | 1 | [#107](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/107) / `fix-dev-watch-phantom-restarts` | merged | Bug report 2026-09-04: `[vite] http proxy error: /api/fetch/ml-sheet` / `Error: read ECONNRESET`, immediately followed by `[api] BetSheet listening`, in the middle of ingesting a race day. Diagnosis first, because the obvious reading (a crash - the D-series gotcha already records pdfjs killing the process) was wrong: the app log had no `uncaught_exception`/`unhandled_rejection` entry, and `node --watch` was verified on this exact Node (v24.18.0) NOT to respawn after a thrown error OR a V8 OOM abort - both print "Failed running ... Waiting for file changes" and stop. The live process tree settled it: the watch parent (started 1:28:44) had spawned a brand-new child at 1:30:44, the ECONNRESET timestamp - so the watcher restarted it, i.e. it saw a file change. But nothing had changed: `find -newermt` over the whole repo for that window showed only `server/logs/` (the app's own streams) and `.git/FETCH_HEAD`, the git index untouched since the previous night. Root cause: Node's watch mode restarts on any filesystem notification for a loaded module file, and on Windows libuv's ReadDirectoryChangesW filter includes attribute/security changes, so a metadata-only touch counts. Reproduced directly - `fs.utimesSync(f, new Date(), st.mtime)` on `server/pl.js`, leaving bytes and mtime identical, makes `node --watch` print "Restarting 'server/index.js'" and respawn. Any AV scan, search indexer, `git fetch`/`status`, editor stat sweep or repo-wide `find` triggers it; the ml-sheet request was just the unlucky one in flight. Confirmed still live: the API child had restarted again by the time the session started. Cannot be fixed in the server - on Windows the watcher force-kills the child (TerminateProcess; SIGTERM is not deliverable, and a registered handler was verified never to run), so a graceful drain is impossible and in-flight sockets always reset. Two layers. (1) `scripts/dev-watch.js` takes over the `dev` script's api slot: spawns `server/index.js`, watches `server/` + `shared/` recursively for `.js/.mjs/.cjs/.json/.sql` (skipping `logs*`, `node_modules`, `.git`), keeps a sha1 per file, debounces 200ms and restarts ONLY when a hash actually moves - naming the file it saw change; a server that exits on its own is reported and waited on rather than respawn-looped. `--watch-path` was considered and rejected: it switches Node to restart-on-ANY-change under the path, and `server/logs/` (the D02 streams) lives inside `server/`, so the API would restart on its own logging. (2) `vite.config.js`'s `/api` proxy gains a `configure` `error` handler that answers 503 with the `{ error }` shape `client/src/api.js`'s `asJson` already reads, so a restart the user DID cause (a real edit) shows a sentence in the UI instead of an opaque network failure, plus one `[api proxy] METHOD url - ECONNRESET` line; the websocket case (a raw socket with no `writeHead`) returns early. Vite attaches its own error listener AFTER `configure` (verified in `node_modules/vite`), so its red stack still prints under ours - an attempted `removeAllListeners('error')` was therefore dead code and was dropped rather than left in, and monkey-patching vite's logger to hide the stack was rejected as hiding real errors. The proxy target also moves from `localhost` to `127.0.0.1`, matching the server's IPv4-only bind (invariant 10) - `localhost` resolves `::1` first on Windows. Verified: metadata-only touches of four loaded modules (`server/pl.js`, `shared/betmath.js`, `server/cards.js`, `server/migrations/001-initial.sql`) plus repeated API calls writing into `server/logs/` produce ZERO restarts, while appending one comment line to `server/pl.js` restarts and names it (and so does reverting it); killing the API child mid-request through the proxy reproduced the reported ECONNRESET and now returns `503 {"error":"The API server did not answer (ECONNRESET)..."}` to the client instead of a bare reset. No server, shared, parser, schema or API-contract change: `npm run build`, `npm run check-routing`, `npm run check-schema`, `npm run check-ingest` all pass |

| D34 | Engine versioning on every card: `engine_version` in the card recipe, set in ONE place in code, bumped by every PR that changes generation, allocation, ticket construction or grading | 3 | [#33](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/33) / `engine-versioning` | merged | first of the backtest-hygiene set (user plan 2026-09-02). Migration adds `cards.engine_version`; pre-D34 cards backfill as `lean-0` = "provenance not recorded" (they straddle the D30 balancer fix); the first recorded version `lean-1.0` is the post-D30–D33 engine. Regeneration stays append-only (D28): a regen under a new version is a NEW card row, the old card and its grade stay intact. Grades record the version they were graded under; regrade under the same version replaces, under a newer version appends. Card view/list show it; P/L rows show it and the bucket panels gain a version filter (default: latest only, "all versions" toggle) - buckets never pool across completeness (13) and never across versions unless the user chooses all. `inputs_snapshot` / `card_finalized` carry it. check-engine: regen under a bumped version creates a second card, first card's graded_tickets untouched. Adds invariant 14: graded results are immutable per (card, engine_version); improvement is measured by comparing versions, never by overwriting |

Migration backfills existing race_days; "Del Mar" / "Delmar" / "DEL MAR" (Equibase header) / "DelMarRacing.com" (program footer) all map to DMR. Results save refuses (422) on track-code mismatch like date today. Unknown tracks get a derived code + preview warning, never a block. check-ingest: the three spellings resolve to DMR and collide on the same date BUILT (2026-09-02): shared/track-codes.js (`canonicalizeTrack`, pure) is the ONE place a track's spelling maps to a code + display name; migration 014 adds `race_days.track_code` (ordinary ALTER, no rebuild - canonicalizing `track` at save means the existing UNIQUE(track,date) already collides on the shared display text) and backfills every known spelling to DMR / "Del Mar", deriving a code for anything else. `insertRaceDay` (server/ingest.js, shared by the save route and the batch backfill) canonicalizes both columns; the one-day-per-track+date 409 check and the results-chart mismatch refusal (server/results.js) key on `track_code`; `dmtc-crawler.js`'s `meetForDay` drops its own ad hoc letters-only check for the shared one. Every parse-preview endpoint warns (never blocks) on an unrecognized track. Found in the process: check-consensus.js's D53 test relied on a day literally SAVED as "Delmar" coexisting beside a "Del Mar" day on the same date - exactly what D35 now correctly refuses (409); updated to assert the conflict instead. `npm run check-schema`, `check-ingest`, `check-consensus`, `check-sources`, `check-dmtc-fetch`, `check-backfill`, `check-ml`, `npm run build` |

Non-blocking warnings save and are reported. Per-meet golden checkpoints: the runner halts at the FIRST race day of each new meet, writes that day's parse as a fixture candidate, waits for the audited golden (the D32 pattern) - three meets = three goldens (DMR-2026-summer, DMR-2025-summer, DMR-2025-fall); D31's track fallback and the Bottom Line anchor may differ across print runs, each meet is its own fixture set, never silently degrade. New engine knobs, template-driven only (`exoticTickets`, `winStake`, `hedgeBoxDepth`, curve `best-bet` with a `bestBet` weight); lean is byte-identical (frozen ticket digests on the FULL and PROGRAM_ONLY fixture days, ENGINE_VERSION stays lean-1.1 - logging-only change to generation). A held win stake makes the balancer step the race exacta box instead (`stake_held_by_template`). Migration 016 (rebuild): `cards.consensus_completeness` admits `HUMAN`; verified against a copy of the real populated DB (70+ graded days) - zero foreign_key_check errors, every pre-existing table's row count unchanged. shared/parsers/human-picks.js (pure): tab/2+-space columns `<bet type> <selections> <stake> [odds] [rationale...]`, vocabulary win/place/show/exacta(+box)/trifecta(+box)/superfecta(+box) (confirmed the grader already supports superfecta, so it's never rejected), Daily Double/Pick N rejected with a pointer at D55, combos are the `/`-leg cartesian product with impossible repeats dropped, stakes validated against shared/betmath.js's `parseWagerMenu`/`BET.minimums` (WPS: $2-style minimum, $1 increments; exotics: whole multiples of the printed base), an optional `($N x M combos)` cross-checked but never authoritative. Each warning carries its own `blocking: true/false` (a narrower convention than server/backfill.js's BLOCKING_TYPES map, since blocking here decides which ticket gets built inside one parse, not a whole day's auto-save). server/human-cards.js: `previewHumanRace` (never writes) + `persistHumanRace` (the one writer, mirrors insertRaceDay/saveResults/persistCard) behind `POST /api/race-days/:id/human-cards/preview` and `POST .../human-cards`; omitted `cardId` always starts a NEW card (the only way to deliberately replay an already-revealed day, per the D28 rule); re-parses `text` itself on save and 422s on any blocking warning rather than trusting the client; a lock also writes one `allocations` row (`confidence: 'HUMAN'`) so CardView.jsx renders the card with zero changes, and sets `race_days.replayed_at` on the day's first-ever human lock. Grades immediately (existing gradeAndPersist) under `engine_version: 'human'` when the day has results - invariant 14 isolates it from every `lean-*` version by default (`?engineVersion=all` or `=human` surfaces it, confirmed in check-human-picks). `template: 'human'` is a real `strategy_templates` row seeded in server/templates.js OUTSIDE shared/templates.js's TEMPLATES map, so it's excluded from the live-generate dropdown, `resolveTemplate()`, and `POST /api/simulations`'s "run every template" by construction. P/L (`server/pl.js`) and Distributions (`shared/distribution.js`) gain `HUMAN` in `BUCKET_ORDER`; PLView.jsx adds ROI-on-bankroll beside ROI-on-wagered (a PASS-heavy card can't look better than full coverage by construction). `npm run check-human-picks` (new, 24 checks: the exact design-note table end to end, every warning type, save-side blocking enforcement, PASS, the D28 409 + new-card remedy, replayed_at set once, bucket isolation, no-version-bump identity), plus `check-schema`, `check-engine`, `check-pl`, `check-distribution`, `check-templates`, `check-sim`, `npm run build` all still green |

a card that isn't the day's first human card (by card_number) is NON_BLIND unconditionally, the D28 replay-of-a-replayed-day case. `isCardClosed` (every race passed or revealed) and `pickerAgreement` (human's largest win ticket vs. program rank-1 and the SFTB top pick; a race with no win ticket or a stakes tie is EXCLUDED and counted, never guessed) round out the pure module. server/replay.js: the day picker (`GET /replay/days?pool=recent|all`, default excludes 2026 meets; `GET /replay/random?pool=`), the blind view (`GET /replay/days/:id/races/:number?cardId=` - strict key-set discipline, no `results`/`finishOrder`/`payoffs`/`leanTickets` key at all pre-reveal, and the identical revealed payload reappears on GET once a race truly is revealed via a shared `revealedPayload` helper so re-navigating never hides what reveal showed), `POST .../reveal-classification` (one-way), `POST /replay/cards/:id/races/:number/reveal` (`leanGraded`/`leanRacePl` null, never a throw, on a day with no lean card), `POST /replay/cards/:id/close` (every unlocked race -> PASS, every locked-unrevealed race -> revealed, under ONE timestamp so a genuinely pre-committed day still reads PRE_COMMIT afterward instead of being pushed to SEQUENTIAL by the cleanup's own writes - proven by check-replay), `GET .../summary`, `GET /replay/standing?meet=` (mirrors P/L's `?meet=` default-`all` convention rather than a stricter never-pooled-by-meet rule, since a handful of human days per meet would otherwise fragment into single-digit rows; groups by `(blindness, sawClassification)` - always split regardless of meet selection - each with human-vs-lean P/L, ROI wagered and bankroll, paired better/worse/tied day counts, drawdown via shared/distribution.js's `maxDrawdown`, plus one `pickerAgreement` line across every race in scope). New `client/src/components/ReplayDayLanding.jsx` renders that table at `/replay/day/:id` (same route as before - no URL scheme change) and, on a row click, renders `ReplayRaceView` for that race number in place; `ReplayRaceView.jsx` gained an `initialRace` prop (default 1, its old behavior) so the landing page can open any race directly instead of always race 1. "Back" from a race returns to the (refreshed) landing table rather than all the way out to `/replay`. `npm run check-replay` grew coverage for the new endpoint (no cardId -> everything unplayed/no PL; a revealed race's P/L matches what reveal itself returned; an auto-passed race from close() shows as passed); `check-routing`, `npm run build` pass; browser-verified end to end on a seeded temp day (port 8790, own DB/log dir, since 8788 was held by another instance) - row click opens the landing table, a race row opens that exact race, Back returns to the table with fresh status, the outer Back returns to `/replay`, URL stays `/replay/day/:id` throughout, no console errors |
| D63 | LLM cards: a manual, per-race LLM-generated card as a third comparison point alongside lean and human | 3 | [#79](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/79) / `llm-cards` | merged | user request 2026-09-02 - investigating whether a non-static LLM picker beats the static rules engine, not replacing it (the "Benchmark first, bet later" rule). **Migration** `018-llm-cards.sql` (rebuild): `cards.consensus_completeness` admits `LLM_GENERATED` (never pools with an engine bucket or with HUMAN - invariant 13); new `llm_card_requests` audit table (`race_day_id`, nullable `card_id` - the first call for a brand-new card necessarily precedes its existence since preview never writes game data, invariant 9 - `race_number`, `prompt_text`, `response_text`, `model`, `requested_at`, `error`), one row per call, never overwritten (mirrors `fetch_attempts`, invariant 11). **LLM client**: `server/anthropic-client.js` reuses life-swipe's `server/anthropic.js` pattern exactly (hand-rolled Messages API, no SDK, `AnthropicError`, prefill-retry, `AbortController` timeout) - no provider-switch/Ollama seam, since a single local user with one model doesn't need it. **Prompt**: `docs/prompts/llm-card-v1.md` records the base text ("Analyze the races... Search the internet... Include longshots and parlays, $50 of bets per race", confirmed origin 2026-08-30) and three v1 deviations - no search tool (the day's already-fetched consensus is provided instead), per-race not per-day with a dynamic per-race bankroll instead of a flat $50, and a structured ticket-block output. `server/llm-prompt.js` implements it (`buildLlmRaceUserPrompt`, `extractTicketBlock` on `<<<TICKETS>>>`/`<<<END TICKETS>>>` markers - missing markers is a hard failure, nothing safe to preview). **Reuse, not duplication**: the ticket block is parsed by the SAME `shared/parsers/human-picks.js` a human's pasted picks go through - a new optional `ruleTag` param (default `'human'`, unchanged for every existing caller) lets `server/llm-cards.js` pass `'llm'` instead of forking the parser; `server/human-cards.js`'s `loadRace`/`scratchedProgramNumbersFor` are now exported and reused rather than copied. **Per-race bankroll** defaults to `(card bankroll - spent on other races) / (races still without a ticket, including this one)`, recomputed fresh on every call so an early race's under/overspend reshapes the rest of the day - flagged for review; two alternatives considered and rejected: a flat `bankroll / raceCount` split (simpler, but stranded money on a cheap early race can't be picked up later) and a user-editable per-race amount (more control, adds a UI step with no signal yet that the default needs overriding). **Flow**: `server/llm-cards.js`'s `previewLlmRace` (calls the model, logs the attempt - success, failure, or an unparseable response - to `llm_card_requests` regardless of outcome, then parses a well-formed response; never persists a ticket/card) + `persistLlmRace` (the one writer - re-loads and re-parses the STORED response for a given `requestId` server-side, never trusting a client-shaped payload, same rule D54 follows; refuses 422 on any blocking warning). `POST /race-days/:id/llm-cards/preview` and `POST /race-days/:id/llm-cards` (`cardId` omitted starts a new card, D28 append-only, same convention as human cards); `GET /cards/:id/llm-requests` serves every logged call (reasoning + raw response) per race. No lock/reveal state machine (unlike human cards) - an LLM card isn't a Replay blind-play feature, so regenerating an already-ticketed race just replaces it; "races generated" is derived from which races already carry a ticket on the card, no separate state table needed. `template: 'llm'` is a real `strategy_templates` row seeded the same way and for the same reason as `'human'` (FK integrity, excluded from the live-generate dropdown/`resolveTemplate()`/simulation runs). **Grading/reporting**: same grader as every card (no engine/grading change); `BUCKET_ORDER` extended with `LLM_GENERATED` in `shared/distribution.js` and `server/pl.js` (P/L and Distributions already iterate buckets generically, so both surfaces picked up the new bucket with no other code change) + a new `.chip--llm` style (`PLView.jsx`/`DistributionView.jsx`/`CardView.jsx`). Replay's standing table's three-way (lean/human/llm) extension is deliberately NOT built in this PR - "once volume justifies it" per the request, and the Non-goals below. **UI**: `client/src/components/LlmCardPanel.jsx`, opened via a new "Generate Card from LLM" button in `CardsPanel.jsx` alongside the existing "Generate card" button (a separate flow since LLM generation is per-race with a preview step, not a one-click whole-day action) - per race: Generate/Regenerate → preview (reasoning, parsed tickets, warnings; blocking warnings disable Save) → explicit Save, resuming the day's own latest LLM card on mount (same resume rule Replay's `ReplayRaceView` uses for human cards, D60). **Non-goals honored**: no web search, no automated/scheduled generation, no change to lean's allocation/construction, no comparison conclusions drawn (ships the plumbing only). **Checks**: `npm run check-llm-cards` (new) never calls the real API - the server boots with `BETSHEET_LLM_TEST_MODE=1` and no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` set at all, and a request body's `__stubResponse` carries a canned model response straight through the real preview/save/parse path; covers the pure prompt builder + ticket-block extractor, preview→save→append across races, per-race bankroll math as it depletes and as it recomputes on a regenerate, a malformed response (hard error, nothing persisted, the attempt still logged with `error` set), an unknown-program-number response (not a hard error - previews with a blocking warning like a human's bad paste, save refuses 422), reasoning/raw response retrievable per race, bucket isolation in P/L, and the no-version-bump identity. `check-schema` (+2: table list, the LLM_GENERATED CHECK round trip), `check-human-picks` (the new `ruleTag` param defaults to unchanged behavior - all existing assertions still pass byte-for-byte), `check-pl`, `check-distribution`, `check-templates`, `check-routing`, `check-replay`, `check-sim`, `check-export`, `check-grading`, `check-ingest`, `npm run build` all pass. Browser-verified live (no API key configured in this environment, which is itself the failure-path test): the "Generate Card from LLM" button renders alongside "Generate card", opens the per-race table, clicking Generate on a race with no key set shows a visible error ("LLM call failed: ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is not set.") with no crash and no partial card - exactly the required behavior; the success path (well-formed and malformed model responses, save, append, regenerate) is covered end-to-end by the stubbed server round trip instead, since no real key is available in this environment |
| D64 | LLM cards: fix box-bet total not dividing evenly per combo (prompt-only) | 3 | [#81](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/81) / `fix-llm-box-stake-prompt` | merged | live bug report 2026-09-02: generating race 1 of the real 2026-09-03 Del Mar day showed "BLOCKING: Race 1: $2.75 per combo is not a multiple of the $1.00 increment for exacta box". Confirmed against the actual logged `response_text` (`GET /api/cards/:id/llm-requests`, D63's `llm_card_requests` table) - the model produced `exacta box | #2,#4,#3 | $16.50 | ...` on a `$1 Exacta` race: 3 horses boxed = 3×2 = 6 combinations, $16.50 / 6 = $2.75/combo, not a whole dollar. `shared/parsers/human-picks.js` blocked it correctly (the exact same rule a human's bad paste hits) - the bug was that the v1 prompt gave the model no way to satisfy that rule: it said only "the TOTAL dollar amount (not per-combo)" with no combination-count formula or divisibility instruction at all. Fix is prompt-only, no parser/server/schema change: `server/llm-prompt.js`'s `SYSTEM_PROMPT` (+ `docs/prompts/llm-card-v1.md`, kept in sync, with a dated Fixes entry) now gives the exact combination-count formula per box type (`n × (n-1)` exacta box, `× (n-2)` trifecta box, `× (n-3)` superfecta box) and a worked example using this exact failure ($16.50 / 6 = $2.75, not $6/$12/$18), instructing the model to compute combinations × base-unit FIRST rather than picking a total and dividing after. Not provably foolproof (a model can still miscalculate) - the parser's blocking behavior remains the real backstop and is intentionally unchanged. `npm run check-llm-cards` grew two assertions: the new prompt text names the formulas and this exact worked example; and a regression reproducing the live report verbatim through the stubbed pipeline (same day/race shape, same box line, same dollar amounts) confirms it still previews with the identical blocking message and still refuses save (422) - proving the safety net held throughout, this was purely a "the model didn't know how" gap. `npm run build` unaffected (no client change) |
| D65 | LLM cards: "Generate Card from LLM" opens a modal walkthrough; fixes the Betting cards row not refreshing after each generate | 3 | [#82](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/82) / `llm-card-modal` | merged | user request 2026-09-03: the inline `LlmCardPanel.jsx` toggle is replaced by a modal dialog (`client/src/components/LlmCardModal.jsx`, new `.modal-backdrop`/`.modal` CSS) opened by `CardsPanel.jsx`'s "Generate Card from LLM" button (Escape / backdrop click / × all close). Walkthrough shape as requested: Generate a race -> preview displays (reasoning, tickets, warnings) -> Save -> the SAVED ticket stays displayed read-only ("Saved ✓", no longer collapses away) with a "Generate next race" button when one remains, or "All races generated - Close" once every race carries a ticket. **Root cause of the stale-row bug**: `LlmCardPanel.jsx`'s `handleSave` only ever called its OWN local `reload()` (refreshing its own `dayInfo`/`cardId`), never telling the parent `CardsPanel.jsx` that its `cards` array (the Betting cards table - ticket count, day total) was now stale; the table only ever caught up on an unrelated remount. Fix: `LlmCardModal.jsx` takes an `onCardChanged` prop - `CardsPanel.jsx` passes its own `reload` straight through, called after every successful save so the table updates live WHILE THE MODAL IS STILL OPEN, plus once more on close as a backstop. No server/schema/API change - presentation-only. `npm run build` passes; `npm run check-llm-cards` (server-only, untouched) still passes as a sanity check. Browser-verified end to end with a REAL `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` now configured (the user added one since D63/D64): opened the modal, generated and saved race 1 (a 2-horse exacta box correctly landing on a $1-multiple per combo - live confirmation the D64 prompt fix works against the real model too), watched the Betting cards table update live to 2 tickets/$45 mid-dialog, clicked "Generate next race", generated and saved race 2 (table live-updated to 5 tickets/$125), confirmed "All races generated - Close" appears, closed the modal, and confirmed the modal resumes correctly (both races "Generated") on a fresh page load - zero console errors throughout |
| D66 | LLM cards: modal's saved races show a collapsible card panel per row; "Open card #N" button removed | 3 | [#83](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/83) / `llm-card-collapsible-panels` | merged | user request 2026-09-03 (PR immediately following D65's merge): "Once an LLM card has been generated, the race card should be visible in a collapsible panel in the race row. Remove the 'Open Card #' button because it will not be needed." `LlmCardModal.jsx`: every already-generated race now carries its own `<details>` panel in its row, independently open/closed (`expandedRaces`, a `Set<raceNumber>`, toggled via the native `onToggle` event), sourced from the card's actual saved tickets (`GET /api/cards/:id`, grouped by race via the `races`/`tickets` join it already returns) rather than the ephemeral live-preview state D65 used - so a race's card stays viewable after moving on to generate the next one, not just for the moment right after Save. The just-saved race's panel auto-opens (`toggleExpanded(savedRace, true)` right after a successful save), satisfying "the card will display" without needing D65's "stay expanded, don't collapse" special case on the live-preview row - Save now simply clears `openRace`/`preview` back to nothing, same as Cancel, since the collapsible panel is the persistent viewer now. Introduced a `normalizeTicket`/`TicketsTable` helper so one table renders a ticket identically whether it's a live-preview shape (`betType`/`costCents`/`tellerCall`, camelCase) or a saved DB row (`bet_type`/`cost_cents`/`teller_call`, snake_case) - removes the duplicated table JSX D65 had for the "preview" vs. "saved" cases. "Generate next race (Race N) →" / "All races generated - Close" moved out of the per-row block (which no longer has a distinct "just saved" state to hang them off) into the modal's persistent bottom action bar, always reflecting whichever race is actually next. `onOpenCard` removed from `LlmCardModal.jsx`'s props and from `CardsPanel.jsx`'s call site - the "Open card #N" button is gone, per the request; nothing in the modal needs to navigate away anymore since every generated race's card is inline. Presentation-only - no server/schema/API change. `npm run build` passes; `check-llm-cards`/`check-routing` (untouched by this change) still pass as a sanity check. Browser-verified end to end with a real `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`: confirmed the "Open card #" button no longer renders; generated and saved race 1 (panel auto-opened, table live-updated to 2 tickets/$32); manually collapsed race 1's panel; generated and saved race 2 (its panel auto-opened while race 1's correctly stayed collapsed - independent state, not a single-open accordion); re-expanded race 1's panel and confirmed BOTH race 1's and race 2's cards were visible simultaneously, each showing its own correct tickets/rationale/teller-call/cost; table live-updated again to 4 tickets/$92; "All races generated - Close" appeared and closed the dialog cleanly. Zero console errors throughout |
