| D67 | LLM cards: fix the "If it hits" column showing empty on the card sheet | 3 | [#84](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/84) / `fix-llm-card-est-payout` | merged | live bug report 2026-09-03: `CardView.jsx`'s "If it hits" column read `t.est_payout_min_cents`/`est_payout_max_cents`, which `shared/parsers/human-picks.js` always sets `null` on every ticket it produces - by design for a HUMAN'S pasted picks (D54: a human already knows what they bet, no estimate needed), but D63 reuses that exact parser for LLM tickets too, so the same null carried straight through and the column read empty for every LLM-generated card. **Root cause confirmed, not assumed**: traced the parser's ticket construction (`estMinCents: null, estMaxCents: null, estIsRange: false` hardcoded) and the INSERT statement in `server/llm-cards.js` that persists those nulls verbatim. **Fix, scoped to LLM cards only** (human cards intentionally keep the empty column - a person's own bet doesn't need software to estimate its payout for them): new `estimatePayouts(tickets, entries)` in `server/llm-cards.js`, called in `persistLlmRace` right after the blocking-warning check, reuses the SAME validated formulas `shared/card-engine.js` uses for the lean engine's own cards (`shared/betmath.js`'s `winPayout` exact-at-ML for win, `placeEstimate`/`exactaEstimate`/`trifectaBoxEstimate` banded estimates for place/exacta/exacta-box/trifecta-box), keyed off the day's own `morning_line_decimal` per entry. Exacta box picks the two SHORTEST-priced (most-favored) horses in the box for its estimate, matching how `trifectaBoxEstimate` already picks the 3 shortest for a trifecta box. **Deliberately NOT covered**: show, straight trifecta, superfecta, superfecta box - no validated formula exists anywhere in this codebase for them (the engine itself never constructs those bet types, so there's nothing calibrated against real payouts to reuse), and inventing new unvalidated multipliers alongside `betmath.js`'s carefully-tuned constants (`exactaFactor`/`trifectaFactor`/`rangeSpread`, calibrated against real Del Mar payouts per the engine's own goldens) would be guessing with money math - those bet types keep the honest "—" rather than a fabricated number. `npm run check-llm-cards` grew two regressions: a win ticket's estimate is exact and matches `winPayout` computed independently in the test; an exacta box's estimate correctly uses the two shortest-priced horses in the box (not the order they were pasted in), matching `exactaEstimate` computed independently. `check-human-picks` (unmodified - confirms human cards are unaffected), `check-schema`, `npm run build` all pass. Browser-verified end to end with a real `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`: generated and saved a 3-ticket LLM race (win/place/exacta box), opened the actual card view, and confirmed "If it hits" now reads $280 (exact) for the win, $60.00–$90.00 (est.) for the place, $62.56–$129.94 (est.) for the exacta box - all matching hand-computed expected values from the same formulas, zero console errors. **Same-branch addendum**: user request 2026-09-03, from a screenshot of an LLM card's footer - move the responsible-gambling line ("Entertainment wagering...") to the very bottom of the sheet, after every race/multi-race section, instead of alongside the failure-mode warnings in the top footer. Clarified via AskUserQuestion first, since the failure-mode-warnings box is a documented requirement (REQUIREMENTS.md: "Known-failure-mode warnings encoded on the sheet") and both elements render on every card type - confirmed scope is relocate-only (not remove), the responsible line only (failure-mode warnings box stays put), applying to every card type (lean/human/LLM alike), not LLM-only. `client/src/components/CardView.jsx`: `<p className="responsible">{RESPONSIBLE_LINE}</p>` moved from the top `card-footer` div to just before the closing `</section>`, after the `card.allocations.map(...)` race sections and the multi-race tickets block. Presentation-only. `npm run build` passes; browser-verified on a lean card (no API key needed) - failure-mode warnings still render right after "Sources used" at the top, the responsible line now renders at the very bottom below the race table, zero console errors |
| D68 | LLM cards: regression test proving consensus picks reach the model prompt | 3 | [#88](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/88) / `test-llm-consensus-in-prompt` | merged | user follow-up 2026-09-03, after a real bug surfaced in review: `shared/classification.js`'s `buildConsensusTable` returns the table ARRAY directly, not `{table: [...]}`. `server/llm-cards.js` (D63) called it as `buildConsensusTable(entries, picks).table`, so `consensusTable` was silently `undefined` on every call and every LLM prompt read "No external consensus on file for this race" even on days with real, already-fetched consensus picks - fixed independently (not by this PR) via a `qa`-branch commit that dropped the stray `.table`. This PR is the missing regression coverage: `npm run check-llm-cards` grew a new block that seeds a REAL consensus pick through the actual manual-paste API (`POST .../consensus/manual-preview` then `POST .../consensus/manual`, "Race 1: 1, 2" -> top #1, second #2) rather than constructing a table object by hand, then inspects the ACTUAL `prompt_text` logged to `llm_card_requests` (not just the parsed reply) to confirm it names the real source and pick and does NOT contain the "no consensus" fallback line. **Verified the test actually catches the bug it's guarding**: temporarily reintroduced `.table` in `server/llm-cards.js`, reran the suite, confirmed the new check fails and prints the broken prompt (still showing the fallback line) as evidence, then reverted - "if a test has never failed, it has not been tested." Caught two bugs in the test itself along the way before it was trustworthy: `jpost()` returns the raw unparsed `Response` (needed an extra `.json()`), and `storePicks` counts individual ranked picks, not races (a top + second pick is 2, not 1). Test-only change - no product code touched. `npm run check-llm-cards` passes (all checks, including the 3 new ones) |
| D69 | At The Races racecard PDF upload as a consensus source | 3 | [#89](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/89) / `atr-pdf` | merged | replaces the eight-paste-per-day ATR workflow with one same-day "print to PDF" of `attheraces.com/racecards/<track>/<date>` covering every race. **Spec named nonexistent precedent, substituted the real one**: the request asked to reuse `shared/parsers/program-pdf.js`/`chart-pdf.js` and a `pdftotext -layout` convention - neither exists (confirmed: zero `pdftotext` matches repo-wide via grep; `Read` on the actual attached PDF failed with "pdftoppm is not installed", confirming poppler-utils is absent from this environment). Substituted the codebase's real equivalent instead: `server/pdf-text.js`'s `extractPdfLines` (pdfjs-dist line-reconstruction), the SAME function `server/ingest.js`'s `/parse/results-pdf` already feeds into the chart parser so a downloaded PDF and a pasted chart share one parser - identical spirit, real tooling. Verified against the user's actual attached Sep 3 2026 Del Mar PDF BEFORE writing the parser: ran `extractPdfLines` on it directly, confirmed the extracted text matches every one of the spec's worked examples exactly (all 8 races' Top Tip/Watch out for pairs). New pure parser `shared/parsers/atr-pdf.js` (`parseAtrPdfText`, mirrors `shared/picks-parser.js`'s never-throws/warnings contract): splits on the authoritative `Race N - ` header (never inferred from Top Tip's own context), extracts `Top Tip: NAME (pgm)` / `Watch out for: NAME (pgm)` per block, warns non-blocking `{type: 'missing_verdict', race}` on a block missing the pair - the day proceeds with whatever races DID extract (invariant 3). v1 does not extract the Verdict paragraph's optional third horse, matching the same limitation already accepted for the pasted Verdict format. Server wiring lives in `server/consensus.js` beside the manual-paste routes it mirrors (not a new file): `POST /race-days/:id/consensus/atr-pdf-preview` (raw `application/pdf` body via `express.raw`, same pattern as `server/ingest.js`'s PDF routes) and `POST /race-days/:id/consensus/atr-pdf` (confirm) - same preview-writes-nothing/confirm-persists contract as manual paste (invariant 9). A small adapter (`atrPicksToRaces`) maps the parser's `{topPick, watchFor}` shape into `shared/picks-parser.js`'s existing `{race, picks: [{programNumber, horseName, pickType}]}` shape so it feeds the SAME `resolvePicks`/`storePicks` - no new consensus schema. Fetch-audit row uses `outcome: 'manual_upload'`: this value already existed in the `fetch_attempts` CHECK constraint (migration 001) and was already read-side filtered as a "real source used" signal in `server/cards.js`, `ConsensusPanel.jsx`, and `server/trace-export.js` - but grepping for actual write-sites found none anywhere in the codebase before this PR, meaning the schema anticipated exactly this feature and this is its first real write. `ConsensusPanel.jsx` gets a sibling upload panel next to manual paste (file input, source name, Preview -> read-only table -> Confirm & save), same UI shape reused verbatim. Fixture strategy: committed the real PDF at `tests/fixtures/atr-pdf/delmar-2026-09-03.pdf` (1MB) rather than a trimmed synthetic - it's the SAME real Del Mar 2026-09-03 card already seeded by `tests/fixtures/entries/dmtc-2026-09-03.txt` for other checks, so program numbers resolve against real entries instead of a hand-built stand-in. `npm run check-atr-pdf` (new, 14 checks): all 8 real races extract with the exact {name, programNumber} pairs the spec's grep output named; a synthetic block missing Top Tip warns `missing_verdict` for that race only, others unaffected; synthetic running-header/footer noise between blocks never bleeds a pick into the wrong race; full server round trip (upload -> preview resolves against real entries, writes nothing -> confirm stores 16 `consensus_picks` rows, 8 races x top+watch -> `fetch_attempts` row with `outcome='manual_upload'`, correct `picks_extracted`) against a freshly seeded real race day. `check-schema`, `check-consensus` (regression, unaffected), `npm run build` all pass. Browser-verified end to end in a real Chrome tab (not just the check script): uploaded the actual committed PDF through the real file input, clicked Preview and got all 8 races with zero warnings and zero unmatched picks (every program number resolved to a real entry - e.g. race 1 top #4 Bit's Tiger Magic, watch #2 Tahini), clicked Confirm, watched the Consensus panel populate live with all 8 races' picks and every race's classification flip from CHAOS to SPLIT with 1 external source, and "Fetch audit - 1 attempt" appear |
| D70 | LLM cards: "Regenerate All Races" button in the modal | 3 | [#90](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/90) / `llm-regenerate-all` | merged | user request. `LlmCardModal.jsx` gets a "Regenerate All Races" button that runs the existing per-race generate-then-save operation (`previewLlmCard` -> `lockLlmCard`, exactly what `handleGenerate`/`handleSave` already do for one race) across every race on the day in race-number order, auto-saving each without the manual "Save this race" click. Refreshes `ticketsByRace` live after each race completes rather than batching the update to the end, so the modal's per-race panels populate one at a time as the user watches. A race whose preview response carries a BLOCKING warning is left untouched (its previous ticket, if any, is not replaced) and the run moves to the next race rather than stopping - one bad race never blocks the rest, the same shape as invariant 3's "missing consensus never blocks generation" and D69's `missing_verdict` handling; a thrown/network error on one race is caught the same non-fatal way. A results summary ("Regenerate all: N of M races saved" plus a list of any blocked/failed races and why) renders once the run finishes. `cardId` and `correlationId` are threaded through the loop as local variables rather than read back from React state (state setters don't apply synchronously mid-loop), so the first race's save can create the card and every later race in the same run correctly reuses it instead of racing to create duplicates. Presentation/orchestration-only - no server or schema change, no new API endpoint; reuses the exact calls the existing single-race flow already makes. `npm run build` passes (no other check script covers client components in this repo - the established pattern for presentation-only LLM-modal changes, per D65/D66, is build + real-key browser verification). Browser-verified end to end with a real `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`: opened the modal on the real 8-race Del Mar 2026-09-03 day with no prior LLM card, clicked "Regenerate All Races", watched the progress line advance through all 8 races ("Regenerating race N (N of 8)…") with each race's reasoning/ticket panel populating live as it completed, saw the final summary read "Regenerate all: 8 of 8 races saved", confirmed the day's Betting cards table showed the new card (31 tickets, $198 of the $200 bankroll spent), and the modal footer correctly flipped to "All races generated - Close" |
| D71 | Equibase "Off to the Races" PDF as a picker | 3 | [#92](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/92) / `equibase-otr` | merged | new picker source, not a consensus source: Equibase's free at-track sheet prints its own tickets for four wager types across two "reward" tiers; this PR ingests them VERBATIM (no re-sizing, no interpretation) into a new `EQB_OTR` completeness bucket, graded and P/L-compared exactly like HUMAN (D54) and LLM_GENERATED (D63). D07 stands unmodified - no fetcher; the PDF is a manual download (`equibase.com/EntriesPlus2/downloadOffToRaces.cfm`), uploaded and archived (`fetch_attempts`-style audit trail not needed since this never touches `consensus_picks` - a dedicated `equibase_otr_ingested` trace event serves the same "every attempt visible" purpose, invariant 11). Writes nothing to `consensus_picks`: that mapping (top=showPick, second=winPick, box members unranked) is deliberately deferred to a separate later PR gated on a D09 three-source classification decision, recorded here so the follow-up doesn't have to re-derive it. Extraction method (`shared/parsers/equibase-otr.js`, pure) was verified byte-for-byte against the real 2026-09-03 Del Mar file BEFORE any parser code was written (same discipline D69 followed): `pdftotext -layout` interleaves the two side-by-side tiers and wraps exacta-box lines across them; plain `pdftotext` scrambles race-block reading order; the working method is `pdftotext -tsv`, word coordinates grouped into lines by `(page, round(top))` and split into tiers by x-coordinate at a verified boundary (`OTR_COLUMN_BOUNDARY = 360` - the right tier's "$2" tokens sit at x=365, the left tier never exceeds ~345). A `Race N:` header line's centered trivia sentence ("#1 Visually is considered the long shot...") is read only for its race number, never mined for picks (proven on the real R1/R4 trivia text in `check-equibase-otr.js`). All 8 real races matched the spec's hand-computed golden exactly on first verification. `server/equibase-otr.js`: `POST /race-days/:id/equibase-otr` (raw `application/pdf` body - the user's spec said "multipart", which doesn't exist anywhere in this codebase; substituted the real established convention every other PDF upload here uses, same deviation D69 made for the same reason) archives the upload under `data/archive/equibase-otr/<TRK>/<date>.pdf` plus a per-track `manifest.json` keyed by date (sha256/uploaded_at/bytes, the D41 manifest pattern; the archive root is overridable via `BETSHEET_OTR_ARCHIVE_DIR` so check scripts and the QA/-alt instance never write into the real committed archive tree - the same isolation convention `server/dmtc-crawler.js`'s `rawDir` parameter already established, and a mistake this PR made and caught during its own check-script development), then parses and returns a preview that writes nothing (invariant 9) with a `parseToken` equal to the archived file's own sha256. `POST .../equibase-otr/confirm {parseToken}` re-reads the ARCHIVED bytes (never trusts a client-shaped payload - the same rule D54/D69 already follow), verifies the hash still matches what's on disk (guards against a newer upload for the same day silently replacing the archive between preview and confirm), re-parses fresh, and persists THREE cards in one transaction - `some-reward` ($14/race: $2 show + $1-per-combo 4-horse exacta box = $12), `higher-reward` ($14/race: $2 win + $2-per-combo 3-horse exacta box = $12), `both` (both tiers, $28/race) - append-only with consecutive `card_number`s (a re-upload adds three MORE cards, never replaces), `template`/`engine_version` = `'equibase-otr'` (new `strategy_templates` row seeded outside `shared/templates.js`'s TEMPLATES map, same reasoning as `'human'`/`'llm'`), `consensus_completeness` = `'EQB_OTR'` (migration 020, CHECK-rebuild on `cards`, same shape as 016/018), `bankroll_cents` = the card's own ticket total (there's no bankroll to allocate against - the sheet dictates spend - so ROI on bankroll equals ROI on wagered by construction for this bucket, shown rather than hidden in the P/L view per the spec). One `allocations` row per race per card (confidence `EQB_OTR`, rule `equibase_otr`) for the CardView rendering requirement. An unknown program number (checked against the day's real entries when supplied) is `blocking` for that ONE printed ticket only - never its tier-mate, never the whole race - the identical per-ticket severity contract `shared/parsers/human-picks.js` established for D54; every other structural anomaly (box3≠box4[:3], the box's first/second horse not matching the show/win pick) is a non-blocking `otr_structure_warning`, the race still ingests with whatever parsed. The day's own `track`/`date` are checked against the sheet's printed header and the whole upload is refused (422, D07's mismatch rule) on a mismatch; the header's track and date print on two SEPARATE lines in the real file (not the one combined line the spec described) - read independently, verified against the real fixture before writing the check. `pdftotext -tsv` is a Poppler-only flag: this dev machine's PATH `pdftotext` resolves to xpdf 4.06, which accepts `-tsv` as an unrecognized-but-silently-ignored-in-effect flag and produces plain text instead of erroring - found live by running the real fixture through it and getting zero races. `resolvePdftotextCommand()` locates the winget-installed Poppler binary automatically (confirmed present at `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WinGet\Packages\oschwartz10612.Poppler_*\`), `BETSHEET_PDFTOTEXT` overrides explicitly, and every call sanity-checks the TSV header line so a wrong binary throws a clear, actionable error instead of silently mis-parsing. No client UI component in this PR - the user's spec described only the parser/server/reporting surface (unlike D54/D69, which named UI files explicitly) - upload/preview/confirm is exercised via the API directly in `check-equibase-otr.js`; a dedicated upload panel is a natural, small follow-up if wanted (**landed as D72**, along with a batch CLI and an extraction fix the single-file verification here had missed - see that row). `npm run check-equibase-otr` (new, 37 checks): golden parse matches on all 8 real races with zero blocking warnings (non-blocking `name_mismatch` warnings on foreign-bred name suffixes like "Certitude (FR)" vs. the sheet's plain "Certitude" are correct, expected behavior, not a defect); every structural invariant holds; the R1/R4 trivia sentences contribute no picks; a synthetic wrapped 4th box horse on a continuation line still yields box4 of length 4; a synthetic box3≠box4[:3] block raises the non-blocking warning and still ingests; the real upload→preview→confirm round trip produces exactly $112/$112/$224 across the three variants with the correct ticket counts (16/16/32); re-upload is append-only (6 cards total); an unknown program number blocks only its own ticket, proven against the real entries with program #4 removed; the D07 track-mismatch and date-mismatch refusals both fire with the correct message and persist nothing; P/L bucket isolation against the day's own lean/HUMAN/LLM_GENERATED cards (each carries exactly its own card count, no bleed either direction); archive + manifest are written correctly and a from-archive re-parse reproduces the identical golden; `shared/card-engine.js`'s `ENGINE_VERSION` is unchanged and a live lean card still generates normally on the same day. `check-schema`, `check-pl`, `check-distribution`, `check-human-picks`, `check-templates`, `check-llm-cards`, `check-replay`, `check-routing`, `npm run build` all pass unmodified except for their bucket-list/BUCKET_CHIP additions. |
| D72 | Equibase OTR follow-up: upload panel, batch CLI, extraction fix | 3 | [#95](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/95) / `equibase-otr-ui` | merged | follow-up to D71 (three parts, one PR): the upload panel D71 deferred, a batch ingest CLI, and an extraction bug found by running the D71 method over all 16 archived files instead of the one D71 was verified against. **Extraction fix** (`shared/parsers/equibase-otr.js`): running the parser over all 16 files (145 races claimed in the request; the real count is 143) found race 9 corrupted on every 10-race day (08-15/16/29/30) - box4 came back with 5 numbers, box3 correct. Root cause confirmed against the real files: race 10's trivia sentence sometimes prints on its OWN y-line, ABOVE the "Race 10:" header line rather than sharing it (two lines one point apart in `top`), so it is never recognized as a header and its own leading program-number reference is absorbed into race 9's still-open column text. Two changes, matching the request: (a) a trivia-line guard independent of the header - initially anchored on "starts with `#\d+`" per the request's literal wording, but the real corpus has a SECOND trivia template ("The #N horse, Name, has/is...") that doesn't start with `#N` - broadened to "contains a `#\d+` reference, none of the three fixed ticket-line phrases (`to Show on`/`to Win on`/`Exacta box on`), and a sentence verb"; the request's "no `$`" discriminator also had to be dropped - a real trivia sentence cites a horse's career earnings ("#6 Maaz has earned $90,283...") and DOES carry a `$`; (b) box lists now parse BY their printed grammar (`#a, #b, #c and #d` - collect through the first `#` after "and", flag anything left over as non-blocking `otr_trailing_tokens` `{race, tokens}` rather than absorbing it) instead of "every `#` up to the next `$`". Found and fixed a real bug in the grammar helper itself while verifying against the real corpus: `String.match()` with a global-flagged RegExp returns an array with no `.index` property, so the original `andIdx + firstAfterAnd.index` was `NaN`, `slice(0, NaN)` silently returns `''`, and EVERY box came back empty with every token reported as trailing (caught immediately - all 16 files went from 0 races to 0 warnings only after adding a dedicated non-global twin regex for the single-match lookup). All 16 files now parse with zero warnings of any kind; committed all 16 real archived PDFs as fixtures (`tests/fixtures/equibase-otr/DMR-<date>.pdf` + `.expected.json`, matching the precedent of `tests/fixtures/programs/`'s committed 12MB program PDFs) rather than trimmed synthetics, so the golden proves the real files, not a stand-in. **Batch CLI** (`server/equibase-otr.js`'s `batchIngestEquibaseOtr`, `scripts/ingest-otr.js`, `npm run ingest-otr -- [dir]`, default `data/archive/equibase-otr/DMR`): walks the folder, reads each file's own printed track/date header (never trusts the filename), matches to an existing race day (skip with a listed reason if none), and runs the SAME archive -> parse -> confirm path the upload endpoint takes. Policy A (D43's rule): zero blocking warnings -> auto-confirm; any blocking warning (an unknown program number) -> left UNCONFIRMED ("queued", printed with why) for review through the upload panel - there is no separate review-queue table for this bucket (unlike D43's `backfill_queue`), since OTR has no whole-day-blocking condition to persist against; "queued" here means simply not yet confirmed. Idempotent by sha256, tracked in a new `confirmed_sha256` field on the existing per-track `manifest.json` (no schema change) - a file already confirmed for its date is skipped without re-parsing, and a re-run never appends duplicate cards. Summary line: files seen / ingested / queued / skipped, cards written. **Upload panel** (`client/src/components/EquibaseOtrPanel.jsx`, wired into `RaceDayView.jsx`): styled to match `ConsensusPanel.jsx`'s At The Races PDF upload (D69) exactly - `<details>`/file input/Preview/read-only warnings table/Confirm - since that is the closest actually-existing precedent in this codebase for a verbatim-picker PDF upload on a stored day; the request's literal file references (RaceDayView.jsx "next to the ML sheet / program / chart uploads") don't describe an existing pattern (ML sheet and program uploads happen during initial day creation, not on a stored day's view; the only PDF-upload controls actually present ON a stored day's view are ResultsPanel's chart upload and ConsensusPanel's ATR upload) - substituted the real precedent, same class of deviation D69/D71 already established for this codebase. Shows tickets per variant, warnings, totals, and the header track/date read back from the response (the D07 mismatch check itself runs server-side and surfaces as the request's own error message if it fails); an append-only notice appears when the day already has equibase-otr cards. Two new `api.js` wrappers (`equibaseOtrPreview`/`equibaseOtrConfirm`) matching the ATR pair's shape exactly. **Checks**: `npm run check-equibase-otr` grew from 37 to 74 checks - golden + structural-invariant assertions on all 16 real files (with the four previously-broken race-9s explicitly re-verified at length 4, race 10 confirmed unaffected), a synthetic wrapped-4th-box-horse-on-a-continuation-line regression (still length 4), a synthetic box3≠box4[:3] regression (still non-blocking), a NEW synthetic stray-5th-`#`-after-the-box's-close case (`otr_trailing_tokens`, box4 still length 4, the token never silently absorbed), the full batch CLI run as an actual subprocess (16 seen/16 ingested/0 queued/48 cards, verified by direct DB count) plus a second run proving idempotency (0 ingested/16 skipped/48 cards unchanged) - run as a real subprocess rather than an in-process function call because `server/equibase-otr.js` reads `BETSHEET_OTR_ARCHIVE_DIR` once at module load and this check script's own process had already imported that module earlier for `runPdftotextTsv`, so only a subprocess with its own environment keeps the batch run off the real committed archive (a mistake this PR made and caught while building the batch check itself - the archive briefly picked up two files from manual browser verification, found via `git status` before committing and removed). All pre-existing D71 checks (37) unaffected. `check-schema`, `check-pl`, `check-distribution`, `check-human-picks`, `check-templates`, `check-llm-cards`, `check-replay`, `check-routing`, `check-ingest`, `check-consensus`, `npm run build` all pass. Browser-verified the panel end to end against a real isolated server instance (port 8790, its own DB/log dir, `8788` left untouched per the known stale-instance gotcha): the in-app Browser pane's file input can't be driven by a native OS file-chooser dialog, so the file was assigned via a `DataTransfer`-constructed `File` and a dispatched `change` event (fetched from a temporarily-served copy in the gitignored `dist/`, removed afterward) - preview rendered the exact expected picks/warnings/totals, confirm persisted 3 cards (visible immediately in the Betting cards table), a second upload+confirm correctly showed the append-only notice and added 3 more (6 total, verified via the API), and uploading the same file to a different-dated day rendered the exact D07 mismatch message in `.notice--error`. No engine-version bump; `shared/card-engine.js`'s `ENGINE_VERSION` unchanged and a live lean card still generates fine. |
| D73 | Equibase OTR cards: fix "If it hits" showing "—" for every ticket | 3 | [#98](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/98) / `fix-otr-card-est-payout` | merged | bug report, same day as D72 merged. Root cause: `buildRaceTickets` (`server/equibase-otr.js`) constructs every ticket with `estMinCents: null, estMaxCents: null, estIsRange: false`, a convention copied from `shared/parsers/human-picks.js` where it's correct - a human already knows their own bet, no estimate needed - but wrong here: an equibase-otr ticket is Equibase's own printed suggestion, not the user's pick, and should be reviewable like the engine's or an LLM's own cards. This is the identical gap D67 fixed for LLM cards, for the identical reason. Fix: new `estimateOtrTicketPayouts(tickets, entries)` in `server/equibase-otr.js`, called inside `buildRaceTickets` before it returns, reusing `shared/betmath.js`'s SAME validated formulas D67 already used - `winPayout` exact at the horse's own morning line for `win` tickets, `exactaEstimate` (a banded range) off the two shortest-priced horses in the box for `exacta_box` tickets (both box4 and box3). `show` tickets are deliberately left without an estimate - no validated formula exists anywhere in this codebase for a show bet (the engine itself never produces one, and D67 already declined to invent unvalidated multipliers alongside `betmath.js`'s tuned constants for the same reason). `entries` (the day's real entries, already loaded per race for program-number validation) is threaded through `buildRaceTickets`'s existing call site in `parseAgainstDay` - no new query. `npm run check-equibase-otr` grew from 74 to 79 checks: every win ticket on the real 2026-09-03 day carries an exact, non-range estimate; every exacta box ticket carries a range with max > min; every show ticket stays null; one hand-computed regression (race 1's win ticket, #5 Certitude at a 3/1 morning line, $2 stake → `winPayout(200, 3.0)` = exactly $8.00, matching the standard $2-win-at-3-1 payout by hand). `check-schema`, `check-pl`, `check-llm-cards` (regression - confirms the LLM estimate path is untouched), `npm run build` all pass. Browser-verified live against a freshly isolated server instance (this time with `BETSHEET_OTR_ARCHIVE_DIR` set from the very first command, unlike the mid-D72 mistake): uploaded the real fixture PDF via direct API calls, confirmed three cards, opened the "both" card's sheet in the browser, and read back real figures for every Win ($8/$12/$26 across races 1-3, matching hand math) and Exacta Box ($3.25–$6.76 (est.) etc.) ticket, with every Show ticket still correctly reading "—". |
| D74 | Equibase OTR as a consensus source (D09 three-source rule) | 3 | [#103](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/103) / `otr-consensus` | merged | makes the sheet (D71/D72) a third, independent consensus source alongside SFTB and ATR - feeds D09 classification, contrarian flags and the LLM generator - reusing the archive D71/D72 already parse, D07 still stands (no fetcher). **Mapping** (recorded in the D71 row, implemented exactly): pick_type `top` = showPick, `second` = winPick, the other two box4 members = the NEW pick_type `also` (migration 021 rebuild - CHECK constraints can't be ALTERed - never `third`/`fourth`, the sheet ranks nothing beyond show/win). Source row `name: 'Equibase Off to the Races'`, `kind: 'algorithmic'` (the spec said `'algo'`; `sources.kind`'s CHECK constraint only admits `'algorithmic'` - the same real-vs-described substitution D69/D71 already established for this codebase). Each row's `note` carries the tier (`show-tier` / `win-tier` / `box-only`) so the consensus table and LLM prompt can show what the pick actually was. Written through `server/consensus.js`'s ordinary `resolvePicks`/`storePicks`/`classifyAndPersist` (newly exported `storePicks`), not a parallel implementation, in the SAME transaction as the three EQB_OTR cards (`server/equibase-otr.js`'s new `writeOtrConsensus`) - a card and its consensus rows are never out of step. Refresh-on-reupload is free: `storePicks` already deletes-then-inserts per (source, race), so a re-upload replaces the source's rows, never duplicates. **The three-source classification rule** (`shared/classification.js`, migration 021 also adds `races.classification_agreement`): UNANIMOUS keeps its exact pre-D74 semantics (every external source with a top pick agrees AND there are >= 2 of them - 3/3 with three sources). CHAOS is redefined from "3+ distinct top picks" to "no two sources (external or program) share a top pick" - the plurality vote group has exactly one supporter - because those two readings are IDENTICAL for every input reachable with at most 2 external + 1 program source (the whole world before today), so nothing behavioral changes for any existing day, but they diverge once a 3rd external source exists: 4 total contributing sources can produce 3 distinct picks where a PAIR still agrees, which the old unconditional rule would have mislabeled CHAOS. SPLIT is "everything else" and now stores `agreement` (the plurality group's size, e.g. 2-of-3) on every classification regardless of the call, so a majority view exists without reclassifying anything. New `CLASSIFY_UNANIMOUS` config (`'all'` default | `'majority'`, exported constant + a per-call override for comparison): `'majority'` promotes a SPLIT race to UNANIMOUS when >= 2 of >= 3 external sources agree on the plurality pick AND the program (if it named one) doesn't contradict it - off by default, flipping it is an `ENGINE_VERSION`-bumping engine change (invariant 14), never a side effect of a source landing. **Source-count cap verified absent**: read `classifyRace`/`classifyDay` end to end looking for the described "silently drops a 3rd source" cap - none exists; `votes`/`external` already aggregate every contributing source regardless of count. `npm run check-classification` grew 11 cases proving this (including the exact regression shape above: program + 3 externals, 3 distinct picks, one pair agreeing -> SPLIT with `agreement: 2`, never CHAOS). **Contrarian flags**: `algo_fades_favorite` gains a partial-order variant for a "box" algorithmic source (publishes a set of horses with no rank at all, unlike SFTB's ranked `fullOrder` note) - fires when the public favorite is absent from the box entirely. Generic by source-name-agnostic design (keyed on whether a source's `top` pick's note parses as JSON with `fullOrder`, not on the literal name "Equibase Off to the Races"), so any future box-only source gets it for free. **Bug found and fixed before merge**: the first version checked `hasFullOrder` per PICK ROW instead of per SOURCE, so a source with a ranked `top` pick that ALSO writes `second`/`third` rows (SFTB does) leaked those rows into a phantom box and double-flagged the same fade under both rules - caught by `check-sources`' existing SFTB fixture failing; fixed by computing `fullOrderSources` as a first pass and excluding the whole source, not just its `top` row. `corroborated_longshot` needed no code change - it already counts any source's pick generically by `program_number`, so an OTR win pick or box mention corroborates exactly like any other source's. **LLM generator** (`server/llm-prompt.js`'s `buildLlmRaceUserPrompt`): the OTR source is excluded from the generic per-source `top {{top}}, 2nd {{second}}, 3rd {{third}}` loop (translating an unranked box mention into "3rd" would misrepresent a rank the sheet never claimed) and gets its own sentence - "Equibase Off to the Races (the free at-track sheet, algorithmic): show pick #N Name; win pick #N Name (higher-reward tier); 4-horse exacta box #a, #b, #c, #d." - included only when the day has OTR rows, omitted (never "none") when it doesn't. A prompt CONTENT change from data, not a template change, so v1's prompt file keeps its version (`docs/prompts/llm-card-v1.md` notes it). **Batch backfill**: `npm run ingest-otr -- --consensus-only [dir]` (new flag, `server/equibase-otr.js`'s `writeOtrConsensusOnly` + `batchIngestEquibaseOtr`'s `consensusOnly` option) writes the source's consensus rows for a day that ALREADY has EQB_OTR cards from an earlier run, without creating any new cards - lets the 16 archived days (D71/D72's corpus) gain the third source without an append-only pile of duplicate pickers; a file not yet confirmed is left alone in this mode. Regenerating those 16 days' lean cards under the resulting PARTIAL completeness (up from PROGRAM_ONLY, since `completeness` in `shared/card-engine.js` already reads `externalSourceCount` off the fresh classification with zero changes needed there) is a deliberate separate manual step, not run by this PR - flagged n=16 wherever read. **Second bug found and fixed before merge**: `scripts/check-engine.js`'s "big remainder" synthetic fixture used THREE distinct-picking sources (A/B/C) plus a program pick that happened to agree with source A - under the pre-D74 rule this was miscoded CHAOS anyway (the bug this PR fixes), so the fixture's four-CHAOS-races precondition was accidentally exploiting the very bug being corrected; fixed by dropping `program_rank` from the fixture's entries (no Program analysis source contributed, restoring a genuine 3-way no-agreement CHAOS) rather than weakening the new classification rule - the fixture was testing engine remainder-distribution behavior, not classification, and never needed a 4th source in the first place. **Findings**: `docs/findings/lean-1.1-program-only.md` gains a short addendum (section 8) pre-registering this: the 70-day backfilled corpus carries zero consensus picks (PROGRAM_ONLY by construction), so none of its numbers change; the one live day this landed on (Del Mar 2026-09-03, card #4) classifies under the fixed rule going forward and is not retroactively reclassified for any earlier stored card or grade. `npm run check-equibase-otr` grew from 79 to 94 checks (exact per-race mapping on all 8 real races against the golden; re-upload replaces not duplicates; the real favorite/box4 fade rule proven on all 8 real races' actual morning lines; the LLM prompt's OTR block present/absent correctly; `--consensus-only` on the real 16-file batch, idempotent). `check-schema` (migration 021), `check-classification`, `check-consensus`, `check-llm-cards`, `check-human-picks`, `check-pl`, `check-templates`, `check-distribution`, `check-replay`, `check-sim`, `check-routing`, `check-sources`, `check-engine`, `check-atr-pdf`, `check-backfill`, `check-ml`, `check-dmtc-fetch`, `check-dmtc-results`, `check-ingest`, `check-parsers`, `check-program`, `check-charts`, `check-grading`, `check-logging`, `check-export`, `npm run build` all pass unmodified (full sweep, 26 scripts). No `ENGINE_VERSION` bump; `CLASSIFY_UNANIMOUS` stays `'all'`. |
| D75 | LLM cards: "Start a New Card" + a model picker | 3 | [#104](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/104) / `llm-model-picker` | merged | user request, following a user observation: `LlmCardModal.jsx` always resumed the day's LATEST `template: 'llm'` card, and every Generate/Regenerate/Regenerate-All call overwrote race tickets on that same card in place - there was no UI path to start a fresh one, even though the API already supported it (omit `cardId`, D28 append-only, same convention every other card type follows). New "Start a New Card" button (disabled until the resumed card has at least one generated race, so it's never a no-op) clears `cardId` and every per-race UI state (`ticketsByRace`, `expandedRaces`, `lastSavedRace`, preview/error state) locally - no DB write happens until the next Save, at which point `persistLlmRace`'s existing `!cardId` branch mints a genuinely new card. The previously-resumed card is left completely untouched, still visible in the Betting cards table for comparison. Alongside it, a Model `<select>` lets the user pick which Claude model answers the NEXT generation: `server/anthropic-client.js` gains `SELECTABLE_MODELS` (Opus 5 / Sonnet 5 / Haiku 4.5 / Fable 5.1 - the one place this list is defined) and `complete()`'s `model` was promoted from a hardcoded module constant (`MODEL`, still the server-configured default) to a real parameter, threaded through `previewLlmRace`'s new `model` option and the `POST .../llm-cards/preview` route's new `model` body field - an unknown id is refused 400 (`Unknown model "...". Choose one of: ...`) before the request ever reaches the Anthropic API, never silently sent through or silently swapped for the default. New `GET /api/llm-models` (`{models, default}`) is the one source the modal's picker reads, so the client never carries a second copy of the list that could drift from the server's. `npm run check-llm-cards` grew 4 checks: the endpoint's shape; an unknown model rejected 400; and - since this check server deliberately carries no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (so the stub escape hatch never has to prove anything about a real call) and a plain (non-`__stubResponse`) preview request therefore takes the REAL code path up to the point of calling `complete()` - a check that the REQUESTED (non-default) model is recorded on the `llm_card_requests` row even though the call itself then fails with "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is not set", proving the selection survives all the way to the logged attempt without a real key or the stub path ever touching the `model` parameter. `check-schema`, `check-routing`, `check-consensus`, `check-human-picks`, `check-equibase-otr`, `check-pl`, `check-templates`, `npm run build` all pass (regression, unaffected - none of these touch LLM cards or the Anthropic client). Browser-verified live against the real running dev instance with a real `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`: opened the modal on a stored day (Del Mar 2026-08-30) that already had a 1-ticket `llm` card from prior testing, confirmed the picker listed all four models with Sonnet 5 pre-selected as the server default, switched to Haiku 4.5, generated race 1 (a real API call - "Calling the model…" then real reasoning text and two win tickets), saved it (card now 4 tickets total under the RESUMED card), clicked "Start a New Card" and confirmed every race reverted to "Not generated" with "Start a New Card" itself correctly disabled again (nothing generated yet on the new card), then closed the modal and confirmed via the Betting cards table that the original `llm` row was still exactly as it was (4 tickets, untouched) - no console errors throughout. |
| D76 | LLM cards: identify + display the generating model everywhere, compare by model in P/L | 3 | [#106](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/106) / `llm-model-identity` | merged | user request following D75. Considered the user's own suggested shortcut - fold the model into `engine_version`'s name (`llm-claude-sonnet-5`) - and substituted a separate `cards.llm_model` column (migration 022) after tracing `server/pl.js`: `?engineVersion=` is ONE GLOBAL filter across every completeness bucket, defaulting to whichever engine_version the most-recently-graded card ANYWHERE carries; multiplying `engine_version`'s LLM values would have made the default P/L view silently hide whole buckets until the user picked "all versions" - which then POOLS every model into one blended number, the opposite of the stated goal. `engine_version` stays the constant `'llm'` (unaffected); `llm_model` carries the real model, set once by `persistLlmRace` at card creation from `llm_card_requests.model` (recorded per-request since D63), never overwritten - mirrors `engine_version`'s own frozen-at-creation convention (invariant 14's spirit, extended). Enforced, not just displayed: saving a race whose request used a DIFFERENT model than the card's locked one is refused 409 ("Start a new card to compare a different model") - a mixed-model card's P/L can't be attributed to either model cleanly. `LlmCardModal.jsx` is the primary guard (the picker locks to the resumed card's `llm_model`, disabled, with a note, the moment a card exists); the server 409 is defense in depth. Displayed everywhere a card already showed its engine version - `CardsPanel.jsx`, `CardView.jsx`, `PLView.jsx` - via a shared `modelLabel()`/`MODEL_LABEL` lookup in `client/src/api.js` (no network round trip per row); a legacy card predating the column shows plain `llm` with no model suffix and leaves the picker unlocked. The actual comparison feature: `GET /api/pl`'s LLM_GENERATED bucket entry gains `byModel` - cards/tickets/wagered/returned/P&L/ROI per model (never a second pool, the same selected rows grouped instead of summed once), labeled `'unknown'` for a legacy card; `PLView.jsx` renders it as an always-open sub-table under the bucket panels. `npm run check-llm-cards` grew 9 checks: `llm_model` on the card at creation, echoed on `GET /race-days/:id/cards`; the 409 mismatch refusal (manufactured by writing directly to `llm_card_requests.model`, since stub mode always records `'stub'` and a real off-stub call needs a key this environment doesn't have) plus its "nothing changed" follow-up; the same request corrected back saving normally; a two-card two-model P/L scenario proving the split, isolation, and that the rows sum exactly to the bucket total. `check-schema`, `check-pl`, `check-routing`, `check-consensus`, `check-human-picks`, `check-equibase-otr`, `check-templates`, `check-export`, `check-replay`, `check-sim`, `npm run build` all pass (regression, unaffected). Browser-verified live against the real running dev instance: two pre-D76 cards showed an unlocked picker (`llm_model` null); "Start a New Card" + Haiku 4.5 + a real generate-and-save immediately locked the picker with "This card was generated with Haiku 4.5..."; the Betting cards table showed "llm (Haiku 4.5)" for the new card and plain "llm" for the legacy two; the card sheet header read "engine `llm` · model `Haiku 4.5`"; the P/L page's new by-model table correctly split the bucket (Haiku 4.5: 1 card/3 tickets/-$50; unknown: 2 cards/47 tickets/-$78) - no console errors |
| D77-B | Bottom Line: strip the handicapper byline block from parsed and stored analysis | 3 | [#108](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/108) / `strip-bottom-line-byline` | merged | user request 2026-09-04: strip the handicapper column's byline block from all Bottom Line data. `server/program-parser.js`'s `parseAnalysis` splits the analysis pages into per-race chunks on the "RACE <ordinal>" markers, so the byline block that prints ONCE at the foot of the section's last page - "Del Mar Bottom Line By Brad Free", the author bio, the page numbers, then a repeated "Del Mar Bottom Line" masthead - was absorbed into whichever race paragraph happened to run last and read as if the handicapper had written it about that race. 416 chars, byte-identical on every one of the 72 affected stored rows and on both program goldens; it reached the card view, the Replay blind view and every LLM prompt built off `races.bottom_line`. Fix is a new pure exported `stripBylineBlock`, applied to each chunk before the existing 80-char stray-mention gate: a chunk ends at the next race marker, so nothing following the byline inside one chunk can be another race's analysis - the strip runs from the byline to the end of the chunk, which takes the page numbers and masthead repeat with it regardless of what page the section ended on (more robust than pinning the literal string, whose page numbers vary by program). The masthead's track words must be Title case - the same grammar `trackFromBottomLine` already reads - because a first draft accepting any capitalized word ate two words of the ALL-CAPS best-bet line printed immediately before it ("BEST BET: RACE 4, RUN WITH LIBERTY" -> "...RACE 4, RUN"), caught by inspecting the regenerated golden. Deliberately NOT stripped: the sponsor-ad blocks that trail some paragraphs behind a byline-less "Del Mar Bottom Line" masthead, and the "SUNDAY, AUGUST 30, 2026 BEST BET: RACE 4, ..." line - both furniture, neither in the reported scope; flagged to the user rather than silently widened. Four goldens regenerated from the real fixtures (both program PDFs, plus the DMR-2025-summer and DMR-2026-summer backfill meet goldens - one line changed in each program golden, two in each backfill golden since the document carries both `program.analysis` and `merged.analysis`); DMR-2025-fall is a foreign Breeders' Cup program with no analysis, unaffected. `scripts/strip-bottom-line-byline.js` (new, one-off, idempotent, dry-run by default, `npm run strip-bottom-line-byline`) applies the SAME exported function to already-stored rows rather than a second copy of the pattern, so the DB and a fresh parse can never diverge; deleted days are included (invariant 12 keeps them out of aggregates but their rows stay readable and restorable). Run against the real corpus 2026-09-04: 72 races across 72 days, one race per day, 416 chars each, zero rows left matching the byline; DB backed up first via `VACUUM INTO` to `data/betsheet.sqlite.pre-d77-backup` (a plain file copy would have missed the 4MB WAL). The betsheet-alt instance's DB cleaned the same way (2 races). `npm run check-program` grew 6 checks: no analysis paragraph on EITHER program fixture carries a byline, all 10 + 11 race paragraphs survive the strip, and four pure units - the bio/page-numbers/masthead tail removed, the ALL-CAPS best-bet line before it left intact, a clean paragraph returned unchanged, an ad-block masthead with no byline NOT stripped. `check-backfill` (which re-verifies every committed meet golden against a fresh parse of the artifacts beside it), `check-ingest`, `check-engine`, `check-llm-cards`, `check-schema`, `check-pl`, `npm run build` all pass. No schema change and no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump - this changes the TEXT the parser hands back, not generation, allocation, ticket construction or grading behavior (invariant 14) |
| D78 | Dev harness: isolate the betsheet-alt instance's Equibase OTR archive | 1 | [#109](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/109) / `gitignore-alt-otr-archive` | merged | Found while explaining the betsheet-alt instance 2026-09-04. Alt isolated its port, DB and log dir but NOT the OTR archive: `BETSHEET_OTR_ARCHIVE_DIR` already existed in `server/equibase-otr.js` and was simply unset in `C:/repos/.claude/launch.json`, so both instances shared `data/archive/equibase-otr/`. Not merely shared storage - a confirm writes `confirmed_sha256` into the per-track `manifest.json`, and that is the exact key `batchIngestEquibaseOtr` reads to decide a file is already ingested, so a day confirmed on alt would be SKIPPED by a later `npm run ingest-otr` on main whose DB never received the cards, and the run would report it as already handled - the same shape as the shared-log-dir bug already recorded in CLAUDE.md's Gotchas. Latent, not triggered: main's manifest held zero `confirmed_sha256` entries. Fix in two halves - the launch config (outside this repo, so it carries no commit) now points alt at `data/archive-alt/equibase-otr`, and this PR is the one-line `.gitignore` entry that keeps alt's uploaded PDFs from being swept into a commit, mirroring the `server/logs-alt/` entry above it. Verified with `git check-ignore -v` against a real file at the exact path the server writes to (`server/equibase-otr.js` mkdirs the dir on first write, so it needs no pre-creation). No code, schema or API contract touched, so no check scripts apply |
| D79 | Containerized QA/pre-prod instance, side by side with native dev | 1 | — | planned | Investigated and designed 2026-09-04, parked unbuilt. GOAL: A/B testing - Docker is pre-prod/QA, native is dev, both running at once with no DB or port conflict. DECISIONS TAKEN: one image that builds the client and serves it (no dev/hot-reload container, no check-script runner, no batch-CLI container); Docker Desktop on this machine (engine 29.7.2, linux/amd64, WSL2 backend, `docker-desktop` the only distro, repo on NTFS); ISOLATION rather than a shared DB - QA owns its DB, logs and OTR archive outright; QA publishes `127.0.0.1:8890` (clear of dev's 8788, betsheet-alt's 8902, the check-script band 8899-8920 that every `scripts/check-*.js` binds into, and life-swipe's 8787/5173-4; verified 8890 appears nowhere in the repo); the QA DB is a NAMED VOLUME seeded once from a `VACUUM INTO` snapshot of dev's; `./data` bind-mounted READ-ONLY so QA reads the 932MB raw archive without duplicating or diverging from it. FINDINGS WORTH NOT RE-DERIVING: (1) Poppler's version is a PARSE-CORRECTNESS dependency, not packaging - Debian bookworm ships poppler-utils 22.12 while `shared/parsers/equibase-otr.js` was authored and goldened against 25.07 and is TSV-geometry-sensitive, so the base image must be trixie (25.x), pinned BY DIGEST, with the 16 archived OTR sheets diffed container-vs-native before shipping; a passing smoke test is not enough. (2) `/api/health` ALREADY EXISTS (`server/index.js:72`) and is the repo's own readiness probe (`scripts/check-ingest.js:39`) - reuse it, don't invent a healthcheck. (3) `better-sqlite3` imposes NO base-image constraint - 13.0.3 ships every prebuild (glibc and musl, x64 and arm64) in one tarball with no install script and picks the target at runtime, so no build toolchain is needed and Alpine would work too; Poppler alone decides the base. (4) Read-only `data/` is safe, TRACED not assumed: the only server-side reader of `BETSHEET_RAW_DIR` is `server/ingest.js:326` (`/results/from-archive`), pure reads; every rawDir write lives in `dmtc-crawler.js`'s crawl/probe, reachable only from `scripts/dmtc-fetch.js` and `dmtc-probe.js`; `backfillRouter`'s confirm/reject touch only the DB, and the `docs/backfill` + `tests/fixtures/backfill` writes (`server/backfill.js:431,563`) are runner-only, so neither directory needs to be in the image. (5) `BETSHEET_OTR_ARCHIVE_DIR` MUST be repointed at a writable volume - `archiveOtrPdf` writes on the request path (the preview IS the parse, and confirm re-reads the archived bytes by sha256) and `ARCHIVE_DIR` is a module-scope const, so there is exactly one archive root per process; consequence, QA's OTR manifest and its sha256 idempotency become per-instance and a re-seed overwrites QA-only uploads by filename. (6) Volume ownership: `mkdir` the volume paths and `chown node:node` them in the image BEFORE `USER node` - Docker copies ownership into a named volume only from a path that exists in the image, otherwise it is created root:root and uid 1000 cannot write; seed via `docker compose run` as `node`, never `docker cp` (root-owned file in a node-owned volume -> `SQLITE_READONLY`, a confusing failure two steps from its cause). (7) Why the DB is a volume and not a bind mount: WAL needs an mmapped `-shm` and POSIX advisory locks, neither dependable over WSL2's 9p bridge to NTFS, and Windows `LockFileEx` and Linux `fcntl` locks do not interoperate across it at all - so nothing at the storage layer would stop two simultaneous writers; `data/*.sqlite` staying on the read-only mount also makes a mis-set `BETSHEET_DB` fail loudly at boot instead of silently writing into dev's corpus. (8) Smaller: `init: true` (Node registers no SIGTERM handler and PID 1 otherwise waits the full 10s grace period, which matters because the re-seed ritual stops the container every time); `.dockerignore` as an ALLOW-list, since the build context is 1GB+ and this repo accumulates untracked trees (`data/raw` 932MB, `data/archive`, `server/logs*`, `tests/fixtures` 63MB) that a deny-list would start leaking; `server/logs-qa/` must be added to `.gitignore` (`*.log` does not match `.jsonl`); a plain Compose healthcheck does NOT restart an unhealthy container, and `server/index.js`'s `uncaughtException` handlers mean the realistic failure is wedged-but-alive, so the healthcheck is diagnostic, not self-healing. CARRIES WITH IT: `server/index.js` gains `BETSHEET_HOST` (default `127.0.0.1`, native behavior byte-identical, including the console banner); INVARIANT 10 reworded from "the server binds 127.0.0.1" to "reachable only from the local machine", since the container binds `0.0.0.0` inside its own namespace behind a loopback-published port; CLAUDE.md's "Two instances, two log dirs" gotcha becomes THREE (dev `server/logs` :8788, betsheet-alt `server/logs-alt` :8902, QA `server/logs-qa` :8890) and the Ports gotcha gains 8890 + the 8899-8920 check band; `.env.example`'s "The server always binds 127.0.0.1" line and README's matching paragraph both become false and must change. OPEN WHEN PICKED UP: whether QA shares dev's `.env` or gets its own (a separate one would let you A/B `BETSHEET_LLM_MODEL`, arguably the most interesting thing a QA instance could test now that D75/D76 landed model selection); whether to ship `scripts/` in the image (~300KB, enables `docker compose exec app npm run ingest-otr`, outside the stated scope; the check scripts would still need `tests/fixtures`) |
| D80 | Dev harness: refuse to start when a stale dev stack owns the ports | 1 | [#112](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/112) / `dev-port-preflight` | merged | Reported 2026-09-04 as "why does this keep happening?" - a fresh `npm run dev` printed `Port 8788 is already in use` / `[dev-watch] server exited (code 1)` while vite cheerfully reported itself running on 5176. Diagnosed from the live process tree rather than guessed: TWO `npm run dev` stacks were up, started an hour apart. The older one still held 8788 and 5175 AND was running the pre-D77-A `node --watch server/index.js` command, whose API child had been respawned an hour after its parent started - D77-A's phantom-restart bug still live inside an already-running process, so the port was continuously re-taken and could not be waited out. It had orphaned because `concurrently -k` only kills siblings when concurrently itself exits, so a closed terminal or an ended session leaves the whole tree running. The damaging part was silent: vite's default port fallback moved the NEW front end to 5176 while the API hard-failed, so a new client proxied `/api` to the OLD stack's server with no error anywhere - the shape of "my server changes aren't taking effect". THREE FIXES: (1) `vite.config.js` gains `strictPort: true`, so vite refuses a taken port instead of walking to the next one - loud beats convenient, and it removes the mismatched-halves state entirely. (2) `scripts/dev-preflight.js` runs BEFORE `concurrently` in the `dev` script and refuses to start when either port is held, printing the holding process, its TREE ROOT, and the exact command to free it; the placement is load-bearing - a check inside the api half was written and tested first and LOST THE RACE, because vite binds in ~50ms and `concurrently -k` SIGTERMed the diagnosing half mid-message, leaving the least useful line on screen. (3) `npm run dev:clean` (`scripts/dev-clean.js`) finds stale stacks BY PORT (api, vite, and vite's fallback range), groups them by tree root, stops each tree with `taskkill /T`, then re-probes rather than claiming success; dry run by default, `--yes` kills, matching the convention of `reset` / `backfill-bottom-line` / `strip-bottom-line-byline`. `scripts/dev-ports.js` holds the shared logic and `scripts/dev-watch.js` keeps its own preflight as a backstop for direct invocation. THE ROOT-NOT-LEAF DISTINCTION IS THE WHOLE POINT: the process holding the port is a supervised child, so killing it alone makes its supervisor respawn it; `holderTree` walks the chain up to the topmost ancestor still recognisable as a dev stack and stops at the first shell or non-dev ancestor, so a terminal is never named as a kill target (verified: the walk passed through node/cmd and stopped before powershell.exe / Code.exe / explorer.exe). TWO REAL BUGS found by testing the tooling against live orphans, both fixed: `probePort` bound only 127.0.0.1 and therefore reported a vite-held port FREE (vite binds ::1 on Windows while the API binds 127.0.0.1 per invariant 10 - both families are probed now), and the PowerShell lookup came back as `{"value":[...],"Count":n}` instead of an array because Windows PowerShell 5.1 has no `ConvertTo-Json -AsArray` and the usual `,$out` array-wrap serializes that way (both shapes normalized; `$pid` was also avoided throughout as a PowerShell automatic variable that would have walked the wrong tree). VERIFIED end to end against real stacks, not mocks: the preflight on a live stack names leaf PID, root PID and both ports; the escape hatch it prints (`BETSHEET_PORT=8790 BETSHEET_VITE_PORT=5177 npm run dev`) was actually run and does bring up a clean second stack beside the first; `dev:clean --yes` stopped a genuinely supervised stack and re-probed all seven ports free; a normal `npm run dev` on free ports is unchanged (api 8788, vite 5175, no fallback). `npm run build`, `check-routing`, `check-schema`, `check-ingest` all pass. No server, shared, parser, schema or API-contract change - dev tooling plus one vite dev-server option. NOTE: this row was written as D78 and renumbered to D80 when it merged with main - PR #111 had meanwhile taken D78 for PR #109 and D79 is the Docker backlog row. The ledger briefly carried TWO D77 rows (the merged dev-harness deliverable and the merged Bottom Line byline strip); they are now D77-A and D77-B, per the suffix rule at the top of this file |
| D81 | Card sheet: per-race results panel, and the D77 duplicate split into D77-A / D77-B | 2 | [#113](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/113) / `card-race-results` | merged | user request 2026-09-04: for every race on `/card/:id` that has results, show the race results below the entries in a collapsible panel. `server/cards.js`'s `GET /api/cards/:id` gains a `results` object (`finishers` / `exotics` / `scratches`, read from `race_results` / `exotic_payoffs` / `result_scratches` for the card's race day). Nested under one key rather than flattened alongside the existing top-level `scratches`, which is a DIFFERENT set of horses - the footer's are program-time scratches off `entries.scratched`, known before the race, while these come off the result chart; merging them under one name is how a sheet would start claiming a horse was scratched at a time it wasn't. Extending the card document rather than having the client call `GET /race-days/:id/results` separately keeps the sheet one fetch with one loading state, matching how races, entries, Bottom Line text, sources and scratches already ride along. `CardView.jsx` groups the flat rows by race number with the same four-line pattern `ResultsPanel.jsx` already uses, and renders `<details className="race-results">` directly below the Entries panel - keyed off the grouped map, so a race with no finisher gets no panel at all rather than an empty one. Collapsed by default, like the Entries and Bottom Line panels beside it (the sheet is read for its tickets; eleven expanded result tables would bury them), with a summary that is useful closed: "Results (8 finishers) · won by #6 Be a Goldfish". The panel shows the FULL finish order, not just the WPS placings the day view lists - what a graded sheet gets read for afterwards is why a ticket missed, which usually means finding a horse that ran 4th or 6th - plus the exotic payoffs line and the chart's scratches. A price renders only where that pool actually paid; an unhit or absent pool is a dash, never a zero. `npm run check-grading` grew 5 checks: the card document carries the three arrays and they agree row-for-row with `GET /race-days/:id/results`; the chart's scratches stay OUT of the footer's program-time scratch list (asserted as genuinely different sets, not just both present); every race with a finisher has a position-1 row and monotonic finish positions, so a panel can always render; and - the empty case, asserted at the one moment it really occurs, before results are saved - the card carries an EMPTY results set rather than a missing key the client would have to guard. `npm run build`, `check-grading`, `check-engine`, `check-routing`, `check-pl`, `check-export` all pass. Browser-verified against two real cards on different meets (card #87, Del Mar 2025-09-07, and card #51, 2026-08-22): 11 panels each, one per race, correct winner in every summary, and an expanded race 1 showing all 8 finishers with $27.80/$9.60/$6.00 on the winner, place-and-show only on the runner-up, show only on third, dashes for 4th-8th, the four exotic payoffs and the chart scratch #9 Coldasice - no console errors. LEDGER: the two rows that both carried D77 are now D77-A (PR #107, dev harness) and D77-B (PR #108, byline strip), in merge order, updated everywhere the ID was cited - both ledger rows, four CLAUDE.md entries (the two feature rows, the strip-bottom-line-byline and dev-watch architecture rows, the `stripBylineBlock` mention in the program-parser row, the Commands comment, the dev-watch gotcha) and two code comments (`scripts/check-program.js`, `scripts/strip-bottom-line-byline.js`). A merged row's ID is already cited in commits and PR descriptions, so both were suffixed rather than one renumbered; the rule is now written at the top of this file beside the `-QA` rule so the next collision is not re-litigated |
| D82 | Remove Sports from the Basement as a source | 1 | [#115](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/115) / `remove-sftb` | merged | User decision 2026-09-04: SFTB will not be used as a source of anything, done in the most cost-effective way. A `git revert` was asked for and REJECTED ON EVIDENCE: the fetcher landed in `adb8e8b` (D08c) 278 commits back, and its only other commit `2edd6f2` (D53) also carries shipped migration 013 (`013-fetch-attempts-discovery.sql`) plus generic consensus-framework work - the `not_published` outcome, the fetch-audit discovery columns, ConsensusPanel's URL/Discovery columns, and check-schema/check-consensus coverage. Reverting it would delete a SHIPPED migration, which CLAUDE.md's own gotcha forbids outright, and would take four unrelated features with it; six check suites also now build their real-day goldens on the fixture that PR introduced. REMOVED: `server/fetchers/sftb.js`, its `registerFetcher` call (the registry comment updated - only the ML sheet is fetched now; ATR and Equibase OTR are manual uploads, D08a/D08b were closed manual-only), the page and sitemap fixtures it parsed (`sftb-delmar-2026-08-30.html`, the whole 2026-09-03 fixture pair, both sitemap XMLs), and `scripts/check-sources.js` with its npm script - that file existed ONLY for SFTB, since every other source has its own check script (check-ml, check-atr-pdf, check-equibase-otr). Its one source-agnostic test - `not_published` counting as neither success nor failure for backoff purposes - moved into `check-consensus.js`, which is where the framework's own rules are verified, with the SFTB-specific sitemap constant replaced by a literal stub URL. GENERALIZED: `shared/replay.js`'s `pickerAgreement` took `sftbTopPgm` and reported `matchesSftbTop`/`sftbComparable`; it now takes `externalTopPgm` and reports `matchesExternalTop`/`externalComparable`, and `server/replay.js` sources that pick from whichever external source the race actually has (`s.kind != 'program'` so the program is not counted twice against itself, `ORDER BY s.id LIMIT 1` so a multi-source race answers the same way every run) instead of matching one source by name. `ReplayStanding.jsx` reads "the top external-source pick". STORED DATA (`npm run remove-sftb-picks`, new, dry run by default): deleted the 24 `consensus_picks` rows so nothing can consume SFTB as signal again, disabled the source row, and re-classified the affected day through the SAME `classifyAndPersist` the fetch runner uses. That re-classification was load-bearing, not cosmetic: on 2026-09-03 races 2 and 4 flipped CHAOS to SPLIT (CHAOS requires two externals that disagree) and every race's source count went 2 to 1, so leaving the stored calls would have kept SFTB-derived classifications on the rows under a different name. The 4 `fetch_attempts` rows are KEPT and the source row disabled rather than deleted - invariant 11 exists precisely so a source's fetch history stays visible, and the decision-trace log files are never rewritten. DB backed up first via `VACUUM INTO` to `data/betsheet.sqlite.pre-d82-backup`. DELIBERATELY KEPT (user decision): `tests/fixtures/sources/sftb-delmar-2026-08-30.expected.json`, the recorded pick set that check-engine / check-grading / check-pl / check-sim / check-export / check-dmtc-results use as a generic second consensus source. It is frozen JSON that is never fetched, so it is not "a source" in any runtime sense, and replacing it would force golden regeneration across five suites - expensive, and it would risk blessing a regression behind a regenerated golden. `docs/findings/lean-1.1-program-only.md` keeps its SFTB mentions for the same reason a findings file is never rewritten after the fact, and the historical D08c/D53/D35/D74 rows keep theirs, now cross-referenced to this row. VERIFIED: `check-consensus` (incl. the moved backoff test), `check-replay`, `check-classification`, `check-engine`, `check-grading`, `check-pl`, `check-sim`, `check-export`, `check-schema`, `check-equibase-otr`, `check-llm-cards`, `check-ingest`, `npm run build` all pass. No schema change and no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump - removing a source changes the inputs available, not generation, allocation, ticket construction or grading behaviour (invariant 14) |
| D83 | Scratch environment: `betsheet-alt` becomes a separate clone, documented in the README | 1 | [#118](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/118), `alt-environment-docs` | merged | Follow-up to the D82 session: the user asked for a place to run `npm run reset -- --yes` without risking the main corpus. Established first, by reading `server/reset.js` and `logging.js`'s `resetLogs`, that a factory reset touches EXACTLY two paths - the DB at `BETSHEET_DB` (every row in 19 tables, VACUUM, race-day id sequence restarted) and the three log streams in `BETSHEET_LOG_DIR` (active + rotated) - and never `data/raw`, `data/archive`, `tests/fixtures`, `docs/backfill` or `data/meets`. So safety is entirely a question of where two env vars point. The old `betsheet-alt` launch config ran THIS checkout with `BETSHEET_DB` / `BETSHEET_LOG_DIR` / `BETSHEET_OTR_ARCHIVE_DIR` redirected by absolute path, which is safe only PROCEDURALLY: one missing or mistyped override and the reset lands on the real 74-day corpus. Replaced with a separate clone at `C:\repos\betsheet-alt`, where every path default resolves inside that clone and the main checkout is unreachable whatever you forget - structural isolation, and the only overrides left are ports, where a mistake fails loudly instead of destroying data. Launch config repointed: `BETSHEET_PORT=8798` / `BETSHEET_VITE_PORT=5178` (deliberately outside 8899-8920, the check-script band that the OLD alt port 8902 sat inside, and clear of 8890 which D79 reserves for the QA container) plus `BETSHEET_RAW_DIR` pointed at the main archive - a deliberate share, safe because the archive is read-only on every request path (sole reader `POST /race-days/:id/results/from-archive`; all writes are in the dmtc-fetch/dmtc-probe CLIs) and a reset never touches it. README gains a prominent "The scratch environment" section: the warning, the clone/install commands, the reset blast-radius table, the port rules, the `VACUUM INTO` seeding recipe (a plain copy misses the WAL) and why `--ignore-scripts` is needed - `better-sqlite3` ships prebuilts for every platform and declares `gypfile: false` but ALSO ships `binding.gyp`, so npm attempts a source build, fails without a VC++ toolset and rolls the entire install back; found live doing exactly this install. A pointer sits in the Run section so it is read before someone clicks Danger zone. CLAUDE.md's "Two instances, two log dirs" gotcha rewritten for the new shape, keeping the original lesson (a shared log dir once let either side's reset wipe the other's decision traces - exports came back `traceStatus=missing`). VERIFIED end-to-end, not asserted: cloned, installed, `npm run build` and `check-schema` pass in the clone, booted it, then ran `npm run reset -- --yes` INSIDE it and confirmed the main checkout still had 74 race_days / 96 cards with a byte-identical `data/betsheet.sqlite` and `app.jsonl`, and all 73 archived days intact, while the clone's own DB went to 0 race_days. FOLLOW-UP, same branch: the README's own by-hand launch line was `set BETSHEET_PORT=8798&& set BETSHEET_VITE_PORT=5178&& npm run dev` inside a ```bash fence - cmd.exe syntax in a Git Bash block. In bash `set X=Y` assigns positional parameters and exports nothing, so both vars stayed unset, `scripts/dev-preflight.js` checked the DEFAULT ports and refused, naming the MAIN app's stack as the process in the way; the user hit exactly this. Rather than only correcting the syntax, the ports now come from the clone's own `.env`: `import 'dotenv/config'` added to `scripts/dev-preflight.js`, `scripts/dev-watch.js`, `scripts/dev-clean.js` and `vite.config.js`, which each read `BETSHEET_PORT` / `BETSHEET_VITE_PORT` but never loaded `.env` (only `server/index.js` did - so a `.env` port would have moved the server while the preflight, the watcher and vite's `/api` proxy all still pointed at 8788/5175, which is worse than not supporting it). `.env` is gitignored, so each clone carries its own ports and `npm run dev` is the whole command in both checkouts. README's run section rewritten accordingly, with the per-shell prefix forms (bash `VAR=x cmd`, PowerShell `$env:VAR=x`) for a one-off override and an explicit note that `set` is cmd.exe only; the `BETSHEET_RAW_DIR` share is shown as an `.env` line too. Verified in the alt clone: `npm run dev` with no prefix started api 8798 + vite 5178, `/api/race-days` answered 200 both directly and through the proxy, the clone's own (empty) DB was in use, and main's 8788 stack was untouched throughout. SECOND FOLLOW-UP, prompted by the user asking whether `npm run dev` now works in both shells and can never conflict. It does (verified in Git Bash and PowerShell, both start api 8798 + vite 5178 from `.env` alone), but the honest answer was "one case remained": `npm run dev:clean` sweeps vite's fallback range as well as the configured ports, so from the MAIN checkout it scans 5176-5180 - which contains the scratch clone's 5178. Confirmed live, not theorised: with both stacks up, main's dry run listed alt's tree (`C:\repos\betsheet-alt\node_modules\...\vite.js`) as a kill target, and `--yes` would have stopped a stack that was never in main's way, since `strictPort` means main only ever wants 5175. Fix in `scripts/dev-clean.js`: a fallback port is swept only when the holding process tree is provably this checkout's - every member of a dev stack names its own directory in its command line, and the repo root is matched WITH its trailing separator, because `C:\repos\betsheet` is a string prefix of `C:\repos\betsheet-alt` and only the separator distinguishes them. Each clone's own two configured ports are still swept whoever holds them (you cannot start without those), an unidentifiable holder on a fallback is not treated as proof, and the final re-probe/exit code now covers only the ports actually targeted so a port left to another checkout cannot report a failed clean. Proven live with both stacks running: the predicate separates the two checkouts on all four ports; a forced overlap (`BETSHEET_VITE_PORT=5174`, making main's 5175 a fallback) prints "port 5175: in use by another checkout, left alone" while still targeting alt's own 8798 + 5178; and `--yes` from alt stopped only alt's tree, with main's 8788 and 5175 still answering 200 afterwards. NOTE: the guard lives in this branch, so until it merges, a `dev:clean -- --yes` run from the main checkout will still stop the scratch instance. No app code changed - documentation, one launch config outside the repo, a new checkout, four dev-only `dotenv/config` imports and the dev-clean ownership guard |
| D84 | Teller-call grammar: emit it and parse it, as exact inverses | 3 | [#117](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/117) / `teller-grammar` | merged | user request 2026-09-04. First of four (D84 grammar, D85 reformat stored strings, D86 inline ticket builder, D87 day-level builder modal). **Why**: human bets were typed as `Bet type | selections | stake | [odds] | [rationale]`, a grammar shaped by the parser rather than by how a bet is placed - the type in prose, the stake as a ticket TOTAL rather than the per-combo base a teller quotes, and no race number anywhere. **Emit**: `shared/betmath.js`'s `tellerCall` becomes one code path producing `[Races a-b ]<money> <TYPE> <pos1> [WITH <pos2> ...]` - `$10 W 5`, `$2 EX BOX 2-4-5`, `$1 TRI 5 WITH 2-4 WITH 2-4`. The per-type `switch` it replaces had NO case for straight trifecta or superfecta, so both fell to a `default` arm emitting `/`-joined legs and a raw `superfecta_box` underscore - a latent bug the unified path removes. Race prefix ONLY on multi-race tickets (they span races and `CardView.jsx` renders them in a table with no race column), so a single-race ticket's stored string is byte-identical to what D86's builder will compose. `moneyToken`/`parseMoneyToken` are a new explicit inverse pair (canonical out `$25`/`$0.50`/`$0.10`; tolerant in `50c`, `50-cent`, `$.50`, `.50`), left separate from `dollars()`, which keeps its prose spellings for engine warning text and `EquibaseOtrPanel.jsx`. **Parse**: `shared/parsers/human-picks.js` gains the teller grammar, decided PER LINE before any splitting - `/` means "next ticket" in one grammar and "next position" in the other, and the two are provably disjoint since teller leads with money and no bet-type word starts with `$`/`.`/a digit. The ` / ` split is a paren-depth scan, not a regex, so a rationale containing " / " is never cut in half. Odds and rationale ride in an optional trailing parenthetical (`$10 W 5 (9/2 big overlay)`). Both grammars converge on one `buildTickets` tail, so the warning vocabulary and ticket shape are identical either way. **The column grammar stays** (decision 2026-09-04): `server/llm-cards.js` parses a model's ticket block with it and spreadsheet paste carries tabs, so `server/llm-prompt.js`, `docs/prompts/llm-card-v1.md` and every `check-llm-cards` fixture are untouched. **Two money bugs found and fixed**: (1) a straight exotic with the wrong number of positions - `$1 EX 5`, one keystroke away in the new grammar - used to build a ticket `shared/grading.js` rejected at `combination shape mismatch` AFTER taking the money; now blocking `insufficient_selections` / new `too_many_positions`, verified against every existing fixture as breaking none. (2) a multi-horse win bet (`Win | 2,5 | $20`) charged for two combinations while the grader read only `legs[0][0]` and settled ONE horse; now split into k real tickets at the stated stake with a non-blocking `wps_split`, total preserved - the only parser change that alters the shape of the tickets table. **Quinella deliberately excluded** from the vocabulary despite every real Del Mar menu selling one and both chart parsers reading its payoffs: grading it is internally inconsistent (`straight.quinella = 2` counts combinations with the ordered formula while the hit test at grading.js:141 matches an unordered set, so one leg shape overcharges and the other can never hit). `abbrForBetType`'s doc comment records why;
