fixing it is its own deliverable. **Exports** `wagerLimitsFor` + new `comboCountFor` so D86's builder shows the same minimums and combination counts the server validates with, never a second copy - which makes browser-safety an enforced constraint on the file, noted in its header. **No `ENGINE_VERSION` bump**: a display string and the human/LLM text parser, not generation, allocation, ticket construction or grading behaviour (invariant 14); human cards carry `engine_version: 'human'` regardless, and `check-human-picks`'s no-version-bump block still asserts `lean-1.1`. **Verification**: `check-human-picks` grew a PURE teller phase that runs before the server boots (so a grammar break reports as one) - the user's worked example verbatim -> 8 tickets, exact per-ticket costs, $50.00 for the race; an EXECUTABLE inverse proof that every ticket from BOTH grammars re-parses from its own `tellerCall` to an identical ticket; money-token round trips incl. `$0.10`; synonyms/case; the parenthetical incl. one containing " / "; the structural and split rules; and both grammars in one paste. `check-engine`'s teller assertion moved from a shape regex (a proxy that had already rotted) to re-derivation against the formatter. `check-replay`'s pinned `'Race 1, $25 win, 1'` -> `'$25 W 1'`. VERIFIED: `check-human-picks`, `check-engine`, `check-replay`, `check-schema`, `check-grading`, `check-templates`, `check-pl`, `check-llm-cards`, `check-equibase-otr`, `check-sim`, `check-export`, `check-distribution`, `npm run build` all pass |
| D85 | Reformat every stored `teller_call` into the D84 grammar | 3 | [#119](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/119) / `reformat-teller-calls` | merged | second of four (D84 grammar, D85 this, D86 inline ticket builder, D87 day-level builder modal). D84 changed what `tellerCall` EMITS; every ticket saved before it still carried the old string, so the sheet would have shown two grammars side by side indefinitely and any future "parse a stored teller call" work would have been ambiguous. `scripts/reformat-teller-calls.js` re-derives the column from each ticket's own `bet_type` / `selections` / `stake_cents` - every input is already in the row, so it needs no source files, no API calls and no day join, and therefore covers the HUMAN, LLM_GENERATED and EQB_OTR cards uniformly. Follows `scripts/strip-bottom-line-byline.js` (D77-B) exactly: idempotent, dry run by default, `--yes` writes, deleted days included (their rows stay readable and restorable, and a restored day must not come back wearing the old grammar). A row whose `selections` JSON cannot be read is reported and left alone, never guessed - zero occurred. **A factory reset was offered and rejected on evidence** (user decision 2026-09-04): it would have produced uniform strings too, by re-backfilling from the archive, but the live corpus holds what no source file can rebuild - 4 HUMAN cards with 22 `human_race_state` rows and 13 reveals (invariant 15 DERIVES blindness from those timestamps, so they are the primary record of the blind-play experiment), 9 LLM cards across 4 models with 86 `llm_card_requests` (regenerating means paying for nondeterministic answers, and D76's model comparison would restart at zero), 48 `consensus_picks` carrying the only PARTIAL and FULL cards in the corpus (backfilled days are PROGRAM_ONLY by construction, since no consensus fetcher runs on an archived day, and live-day picks for past dates are not re-fetchable), the 50 simulation runs `docs/findings/lean-1.1-program-only.md` cites by ID (reset restarts the sequences, dangling every citation), and every decision-trace log file (factory reset is invariant 12's one exception that deletes logs alongside records). Recorded here so the trade is not re-litigated. One trap noted even though we did not reset: a reset does NOT clear `data/archive/equibase-otr/*/manifest.json`, whose `confirmed_sha256` entries are the idempotency key `batchIngestEquibaseOtr` reads, so `npm run ingest-otr` would skip all 16 days against an empty DB. **Real-corpus run 2026-09-04**: DB backed up first with `VACUUM INTO` (`data/betsheet.sqlite.pre-d85-backup`, so the 4MB WAL was captured; verified complete at 2321 tickets / 74 race_days / 96 cards / 2321 old-format strings BEFORE any write), then 2321 tickets across 74 days reformatted, 0 unreadable, and a second run reporting 0 to change. **Verified** by diffing live against the backup on every `tickets` column EXCEPT `teller_call` - all 2321 rows byte-identical - plus unchanged row counts for graded_tickets (2321), allocations (820), cards (96), race_results (5870), exotic_payoffs (4720) and human_race_state (22), and identical graded returned/P&L totals ($1,927,185 / -$10,015). Then all 2321 stored strings were re-parsed through `parseHumanPicksText` and every one returned the identical ticket - D84's inverse property proven over the real corpus rather than fixtures. The run also corrected two latent formatter bugs that were sitting in stored text: `Race 1, 10-cent superfecta_box, 7,9,3,2` (raw underscore, comma-joined box) and `Race 8, $1 trifecta, 8 / 3 / 5` (slash-joined positions), both from the pre-D84 `default` switch arm that had no case for straight trifecta or superfecta. `check-grading`, `check-pl`, `check-replay`, `check-export`, `check-human-picks` all pass; browser-verified card #8 (Del Mar 2026-09-04, PARTIAL, graded) rendering `$14 W 4`, `$5 EX BOX 4-5`, `$1 EX 4 WITH 2` in the Say-to-the-teller column. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump - a display column only |
| D86 | Ticket builder, inline in the Replay race view | 3 | [#120](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/120) / `ticket-builder` | merged | third of four (D84 grammar, D85 reformat, D86 this, D87 day-level builder modal). Replaces `ReplayRaceView.jsx`'s bare paste box with `client/src/components/TicketBuilder.jsx`. **Shape**: a CONTROLLED TEXT PRODUCER - it owns no server state and posts nothing, it composes the D84 teller grammar and hands the string up, so `handlePreview`/`handleLock` needed no logic change and invariant 9 holds unchanged (the server still re-parses the text and refuses blocking warnings independently). Every number on screen comes from the SAME code the server validates with - `tellerCall`/`parseWagerMenu`/`wagerMenuOffered` plus the `wagerLimitsFor`/`comboCountFor` D84 exported for exactly this - imported via the `@shared` vite alias, so builder and preview cannot disagree about a cost or a minimum. **UI**: one draft row per ticket; a straight exotic gets N independently multi-select horse strips headed 1st/2nd/3rd/4th (that array IS the `WITH` structure - no second representation), a box or WPS gets one; ALL/clear per strip give the classic wheel; a derivation line names the arithmetic people get wrong at the window (`2 combos (4 - 2 impossible repeats) x $1 = $2`); the composed call renders in a `.teller` span as you click, so the grammar is taught rather than hidden. All six single-race families are offered unconditionally - `parseWagerMenu` cannot answer whether a bet is SOLD (it always returns every key with `BET.minimums` fallbacks) and `races.wager_menu` is frequently null, so gating on it would silently HIDE bet types that are in fact available; `wagerMenuOffered` only labels a minimum "(assumed - not on this race's menu)". Not bidirectional in v1 (starts empty, never parses text back into rows), which is why the "Type it instead" textarea escape hatch stays - the column grammar and spreadsheet paste are still supported. No payout estimates: the human path deliberately stores `estMinCents: null` and a client-only figure would put a number on screen that is not in the database. **Two things beyond the plan.** (1) **A real bug found by browser-verifying, not by the check scripts**: the blind view's `entries[].scratched` carried only `entries.scratched`, while `shared/parsers/human-picks.js` blocks on program-time scratches UNION the CHART's - and Replay is only ever played on a day that HAS results, so every chart scratch was invisible to the client. The builder offered #4 on Del Mar 2026-09-04 race 1 and the server refused it at Preview with no way for the user to have known. The blind payload now sends the same set the parser enforces, via `human-cards.js`'s own exported `scratchedProgramNumbersFor` rather than a second copy, keeping the program-time flag as `scratchedOnProgram`. This adds nothing withheld: a scratch is known at the window, and it is not finish order or a payoff. (2) **The edit affordance is a rule, not a warning**: re-locking re-stamps `picks_locked_at` (`server/human-cards.js`'s upsert) and `computeBlindness` compares max-lock vs min-reveal, so editing a locked race after ANY race on the card was revealed silently flips it PRE_COMMIT -> SEQUENTIAL and re-buckets it in `/replay/standing`. Invariant 15 says blindness is DERIVED from the timestamps and never hand-set, so a UI click must not be able to degrade it: once anything is revealed the affordance is simply absent and the D28 remedy is named instead; before any reveal it is offered with a note that the card stays Pre-commit. That needed one new field, `summary.anyRevealed` (`buildSummary`), which leaks only that a reveal happened. **Verification**: `check-replay` +4 (`anyRevealed` false before and true after the first reveal, plus the summary still carrying no `finishOrder`/`payoffs`/`classification` keys); `check-human-picks`, `check-llm-cards`, `check-routing`, `check-pl`, `npm run build` all pass. Browser-verified against the real running instance on Del Mar 2026-09-04 race 1: built `$10 W 5` and `$2 EX BOX 2-3-5` by clicking chips, derivation lines read `1 combo x $10 = $10` and `6 combos x $2 = $12`, a trifecta `5 WITH 2-4 WITH 2-4` correctly read `2 combos (4 - 2 impossible repeats) x $1 = $2`, the composed text matched the derivation byte-for-byte, and the server preview returned ZERO warnings with an identical $22 race total; after the scratch fix #4 rendered disabled and struck through; the dark theme re-themed entirely through the existing semantic tokens (new CSS defines no colour of its own). Lock was deliberately NOT clicked - it would have written a real human card to the live corpus. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D87 | Day-level ticket builder modal | 3 | [#121](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/121) / `day-builder-modal` | merged | last of four (D84 grammar, D85 reformat, D86 inline builder, D87 this). "Build tickets for the day" on the Replay day landing opens `client/src/components/ReplayDayBuilderModal.jsx`: one card per race, each mounting the same `TicketBuilder.jsx` the race view uses, with per-race Preview / Lock / PASS and a footer "Lock all previewed races". **Why it matters**: locking several races BEFORE revealing anything is precisely the shape `shared/replay.js`'s `computeBlindness` reads as PRE_COMMIT (max lock vs min reveal), and until now the only way to play was one race at a time through the race view. **Structure** is `LlmCardModal.jsx`'s throughout, deliberately: same modal chrome (Escape / backdrop click / ×), same per-race cards - now on generic `.race-grid` / `.race-card` / `.race-card__header` aliases added to the existing `.llm-race-*` rules so a non-LLM component is not wearing LLM class names (purely additive; `LlmCardModal` untouched) - and the same sequential save loop threading a LOCAL cardId, because the first save is what mints the card and React state does not apply mid-loop. A race whose preview is missing or blocking is skipped and the run continues, the same "one bad race never stops the rest" rule `handleRegenerateAll` follows. The modal constructs no ticket: the builder produces TEXT and the modal posts it to D54's own endpoints, so the server still re-parses independently (invariant 9). **Server**: the landing endpoint `GET /replay/days/:id/races` gains `wagerMenu`, `entries` and a locked race's own `tickets`, with the entries projection extracted into ONE `entriesPayload` helper the blind view now shares so the two can never drift. Chosen over N per-race calls to the blind endpoint, which would each also recompute a consensus table and, per revealed race, a full `revealedPayload` regrade the modal never uses. Nothing withheld is added - all of it is pre-race data the blind view already exposed in full, and `scratched` remains D86's real set (program-time UNION chart). A locked race renders read-only with no Build button - editing lives in the race view, which owns D86's blindness rule - and a day with any revealed race shows a notice that it is no longer a blind pre-commit. **Two ordering traps hit and recorded**: the shared-helper refactor had to rewrite the blind view BEFORE inserting the helper, since the helper's own body is a second match for the same two lines (the first attempt replaced the helper's body with a recursive call to itself); and the batch-lock check block had to be placed AFTER the standing section, because its own closed card otherwise broke standing's "exactly the 3 closed cards" assertion. **Verification**: `check-replay` +10 - the landing carrying entries/wagerMenu, its entries agreeing byte-for-byte with the blind view for the same race, an unrevealed row still carrying null PL and no `finishOrder`/`payoffs`/`classification`, locked rows carrying tickets and unplayed rows carrying none, plus a batch-lock block proving every race in a batch lands on ONE card (the first save mints it), nothing is revealed during the batch, both races report their tickets on the landing, and a fully batch-locked day closes as PRE_COMMIT. `check-human-picks`, `check-llm-cards`, `check-routing`, `check-pl`, `check-sim`, `npm run build` all pass. Browser-verified against the real running instance: on Del Mar 2026-09-04 the modal opened all 8 races, two builders opened independently and each showed its own race's entries with correct scratch state (#4 disabled in race 1, all 9 live in race 2), previewing both moved the footer to "2 races previewed and ready · $16"; on the already-played 2026-08-30 all 10 races rendered read-only with their real D85-reformatted teller calls (`$10 W 6`, `$5 W 1`, `$2 EX BOX 1-6`, …), a PASSED race showed as passed, the "no longer a blind pre-commit" notice fired, "Lock all previewed races" was correctly disabled with nothing staged, and Escape closed the dialog. "Lock all" was deliberately NOT clicked against the live corpus - it would write real human cards; the batch semantics are covered by the new check-replay block instead. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D88 | Multi-race human tickets (Daily Double / Pick N) in the ticket builder | 3 | — | planned | out of scope for D84-D87 by decision 2026-09-04; recorded here with its cost analysis so it is not re-derived later. `shared/grading.js:91-106` ALREADY grades `daily_double` and `/^pick\d$/` off the settling race's payoff row, and `tickets.race_id` is already nullable-means-multi-race with `selections` carrying `{races, legs}` - the grader and the ticket table need nothing. The cost is elsewhere. **(1)** `allocations.race_id` is `NOT NULL` - the one hard schema blocker; making it nullable is a SQLite table rebuild (`-- betsheet:schema-rebuild`, follow `016-human-completeness.sql`), and note `UNIQUE (card_id, race_id)` is permissive with NULLs in SQLite so several NULL-race allocations could coexist: decide deliberately. Avoidable by charging a multi-race ticket's cost to its first leg's allocation, at the price of a misleading per-race allocated figure. **(2)** Four `race_id = ?` queries mishandle NULL: `server/replay.js:134` (reveal grading) and `:149` (allocation lookup) make the ticket invisible on every reveal screen, `server/human-cards.js:79-83` INNER JOINs `races` so it drops out of the running-cost preview, and `server/human-cards.js:168`'s re-lock DELETE never matches it - so **re-locking any leg duplicates the ticket**. **(3)** `human_race_state` is keyed `(card_id, race_number)`, so "which race does a two-leg ticket lock?" has no defined answer; locking both legs is natural but makes the ticket deletable and re-lockable from two places. **(4)** Pick-N leg spans are not machine-readable: `races.wager_menu` is free text and "50c Late Pick 5" does not say which races it covers - only "$3 Turf Pick 3 (R4-6-8)" ever states them, in a parenthetical. Needs new parsing, a structured column, or user-entered leg races. **(5)** `parseHumanPicksText` is single-race by construction (one `race`, one `entries` array, `raceNumbers = [race]`), so it needs an `entriesByRace`-shaped signature. D84 ships the two forward-compatible hooks: the `Races a-b ` teller prefix that `tellerCall` emits and the parser reads, and the blocking `multi_race_unsupported` rejection that names this row |
| D89 | Fix quinella grading, then admit it to the ticket vocabulary | 2 | — | planned | found while building D84's teller grammar and deliberately left out of it: `QU` was in the planned vocabulary table and was dropped once the grader turned out to be broken, since advertising a bet type that cannot be graded correctly is a trap. **The bug**: `shared/grading.js` puts quinella on the ORDERED-exotic path for combination counting (`straight = { exacta: 2, trifecta: 3, superfecta: 4, quinella: 2 }`, so `combosTotal = legs.reduce((a, l) => a * l.length, 1)`) while matching the result as an UNORDERED set (`if (betType === 'quinella' || betType in boxed)` uses `new Set(legs[0] ?? legs.flat())`). Those two disagree, and neither leg shape works. Reproduced against the real grader on 2026-09-04 with a $2-base quinella paying $18.60 on 2-4: **(a)** legs `[['2','4']]` (the set in one leg, how a box is represented) grades `win` returning $1860 - correct outcome, but `combosTotal` is 2 so the ticket is charged $4 when a 2-horse quinella is ONE combination costing $2, and the refund fraction is computed against the wrong denominator; **(b)** legs `[['2'],['4']]` (two positional legs, correct $2 cost) grades **`loss`, returning $0 on a winning ticket** - `legs[0]` is the one-element set `{2}`, so `parts.every((part) => partMatches(part, set))` can never be true for a two-horse combination. That second case is the severe one: a cashed ticket reported as a total loss. Note `legs[0] ?? legs.flat()` is also dead code - `legs[0]` is truthy whenever `legs` is non-empty. **The real formula**: a quinella of n horses is C(n,2) = n(n-1)/2 combinations, which is neither the ordered `perms(n,2)` a box uses nor the leg product a straight exotic uses. It happens to agree with the current code at n=3 only (3 either way), which is why a casual test could miss it; n=2 overcharges 2x and n=4 undercharges (4 counted vs 6 real). **Why it is worth fixing**: quinella is otherwise supported end to end - `shared/chart-parser.js` and `shared/dmtc-results-parser.js` both map the chart's quinella payoff rows, `parseWagerMenu` reads its minimum, and every real Del Mar wager menu in `tests/fixtures/entries/` prints "$2 Quinella", so a human at the window can bet one today and has no way to record it. **Scope**: (1) pick the canonical leg shape (recommend one leg holding the set, matching how box types are already represented) and make counting, cost, the refund fraction and the hit test all agree on C(n,2); (2) hand-computed cases in `scripts/check-grading.js` - a 2-horse quinella that hits, one that misses, a 3-horse (3 combinations), a 4-horse (6, the case that catches the current undercount), and a scratched-selection refund; (3) only then add `quinella` to `BET_TYPES` in `shared/parsers/human-picks.js`, `QU` to `BET_ABBR` in `shared/betmath.js` (whose `abbrForBetType` doc comment currently records why it is absent - update it), a case in `scripts/check-human-picks.js`, and the type to `TicketBuilder.jsx`'s `BET_TYPES` list. `shared/card-engine.js` never constructs a quinella, so check whether any engine output changes before deciding on an `ENGINE_VERSION` bump (invariant 14) - on current evidence none does, and no stored ticket in the corpus is a quinella, so this looks like a grader fix with no version consequence |
| D90 | Fix: closing the day-level ticket builder blanked the whole app | 3 | `fix-builder-modal-close` | merged | bug report 2026-09-05, a D87 regression. `ReplayDayBuilderModal.jsx` had `useEffect(reload, [dayId, cardId])` where `reload` is an expression-bodied arrow returning a promise. React stores an effect's return value as its cleanup and invokes it on unmount, so closing the modal threw `TypeError: destroy is not a function` in the commit phase and React unmounted the ROOT - the user saw a blank screen rather than an error notice. Reproduced in the browser before changing anything (`#root.children.length` went 1 -> 0 with two `destroy is not a function` errors); one-line fix to `useEffect(() => { reload(); }, ...)`, the shape `CardsPanel.jsx` and `ConsensusPanel.jsx` already used. The other three `useEffect(<named fn>, deps)` call sites (`LlmCardModal.jsx` x2, `ReplayRaceView.jsx`) were audited and were SAFE - block bodies returning undefined - but were made uniform anyway, since shortening any of those bodies to an expression would silently reintroduce the crash. **Why D87's own browser verification missed it**: it asserted `!document.querySelector('.modal-backdrop')` after Escape and called that "Escape closed the dialog" - an assertion that is equally true when React has torn the whole app down, so it could not distinguish success from this exact failure. The re-check now asserts the page still renders. Verified on the real instance across all four close paths (footer Close, Escape, backdrop click, x) plus reopen (8 race cards) and a full smoke of the two components whose call sites changed - `LlmCardModal` open/close and `ReplayRaceView` in/out of a race - every one leaving `#root` populated with zero console errors. `npm run build` passes. No server, schema or API change; nothing for the check scripts to cover, since they never render React - the guard is the new Gotcha |
| D91 | One shared "If it hits" estimator; human tickets get one | 3 | [#124](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/124) / `shared-payout-estimator` | merged | first of three behind the Replay day-landing final card (D91 estimator, D92 backfill, D93 display). The display work needs human tickets to carry a payout estimate, and they never have: `shared/parsers/human-picks.js` writes `estMinCents`/`estMaxCents`/`estIsRange` null by D54's decision ("a human already knows their own bet"), so the sheet's If-it-hits column would have been a full column of dashes. **User decision 2026-09-05 reverses D54** - with the column on screen, an empty one reads as broken rather than deliberate. Rather than add a THIRD copy of the estimator, the two that already existed are collapsed into one: `server/llm-cards.js`'s `estimatePayouts` (D67) and `server/equibase-otr.js`'s `estimateOtrTicketPayouts` (D73, a strict subset with byte-identical branches) are deleted in favour of `shared/betmath.js`'s new `estimateTicketPayouts(tickets, mlOf)`. It takes an `mlOf(pgm)` LOOKUP rather than DB entry rows, because `program_number`/`morning_line_decimal` are DB vocabulary and betmath is browser-safe with no imports - the shape boundary is now each caller's one-liner, which also lets D92's per-race SQL feed it directly. A ticket whose type has no validated formula (show, straight trifecta, superfecta, superfecta box) or whose selection has no morning line is returned UNCHANGED BY IDENTITY, never a guessed multiplier. `server/human-cards.js` runs it in `previewHumanRace` AND in `persistHumanRace` after the blocking check, mirroring `persistLlmRace`'s ordering exactly so the preview is byte-for-byte what Save stores (invariant 9). **`shared/card-engine.js` is deliberately NOT unified**: its estimates are computed mid-construction with context the dispatcher lacks (straight exactas priced off the LONGEST-priced under, the rebalancer rewriting est after a stake moves), so folding them in would change every stored lean estimate and require an `ENGINE_VERSION` bump (invariant 14) - the reason is recorded in the new function's doc comment so it isn't later tidied away. **Byte-identity for the two refactored paths is proven, not argued**: every existing numeric pin in `check-llm-cards` and `check-equibase-otr` (exact cents for win / exacta / exacta box) stays green, and two new assertions close the gaps - every persisted OTR ticket's `bet_type` is show/win/exacta_box (turning "the extra branches are unreachable for OTR" into a test) and a `place` band is pinned numerically in check-llm-cards, the one branch no other suite covered. `check-human-picks` gains a PURE phase before the server boots: win exact, place/exacta/trifecta-box bands, `exacta_box` asserted with the box pasted in DESCENDING price order so "it happened to take legs[0][0..1]" cannot pass by accident, and IDENTITY assertions (`out[0] === in[0]`) for show / straight trifecta / superfecta / superfecta box / a missing morning line - which are what stop a future "helpful" multiplier being invented. Then invariant 9 mechanically: a saved human win ticket on the 9/2 fixture horse carries `est_payout_min_cents === 13750` exact, an exacta box carries a range, a show ticket stays null, and a preview's `estMinCents` equals the saved row's. Six CLAUDE.md rows that recorded the old "by design" null (betmath, human-picks, human-cards, llm-cards, equibase-otr, TicketBuilder) are corrected in this PR - left alone they would have been actively misleading. VERIFIED: `check-human-picks`, `check-llm-cards`, `check-equibase-otr`, `check-engine` (lean goldens byte-identical, `ENGINE_VERSION` unchanged), `check-templates`, `check-replay`, `check-pl`, `check-schema`, `npm run build` all pass. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump - `est_payout_*` has exactly two readers, the card sheet and `server/trace-export.js`, so no P/L, ROI, distribution or standing figure can move |
| D92 | Per-race analyst notes for the LLM card generator: schema, prompt, logging | 3 | [#125](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/125) / `llm-analyst-notes` | merged | user scope doc 2026-09-05. First of three (D92 server/prompt/schema, D93 modal UI, D94 reporting + findings). Lets the user paste unstructured handicapper commentary per race, feeds it to the model as an ADVISORY, UNTRUSTED input, and snapshots it verbatim into the generation log so its effect can be measured later. **The thing the scope doc missed, and the most important line in the PR**: adding the five rules to `SYSTEM_PROMPT` would have changed the prompt for EVERY LLM card, notes or not - and LLM cards have no version axis (`engine_version` is the literal `'llm'` for all of them, so invariant 14's bump rule never reaches them), so every future notes-FREE card would have become incomparable to the existing corpus and the whole notes-vs-no-notes comparison would have been invalid from day one. Fixed by making the clauses CONDITIONAL (`buildSystemPrompt({hasNotes})`), with `buildSystemPrompt({hasNotes:false}) === SYSTEM_PROMPT` asserted. **Three scope-doc assumptions did not hold and were corrected with the user**: (1) "review queue under invariant-9 policy A" - policy A and `backfill_queue` are the D43 BATCH BACKFILL's machinery with zero connection to LLM cards; the real mechanism is preview-warns / save-refuses-422. (2) template `llm-notes` when any race has notes - unimplementable: `strategy_template_id` is set only in `persistLlmRace`'s card-creation branch and never updated, and both that route's `template !== 'llm'` guard and the modal's card-discovery filter would reject such a card; replaced by `cards.notes_present`, exactly as D76 used `cards.llm_model` rather than multiplying `engine_version`. (3) blindness derived from `notes_entered_at` vs post time - no blindness concept exists for any non-human card, and `races.post_time` is a bare "2:00PM" with no date or timezone; the timestamp is RECORDED now and the derivation deferred, which is invariant 15's own shape. **Drafts are keyed by `(race_day_id, race_number)`, not `card_id`** (0 = the day note): notes belong to a race, so the same commentary feeds a Sonnet card and an Opus card, and the first preview of a new card has no card id anyway. `llm_notes` is the only mutable table in the LLM subsystem - a scratchpad; the immutable record is the per-call snapshot, where `notes_*_text` is what the human typed while `notes_hash`/`notes_char_count` cover the composed, sanitized, truncated payload the model actually saw. `cards.notes_present` LATCHES rather than freezes (no 409, unlike `llm_model`) because a user will realistically have notes for 3 of 8 races - so it means "at least one race used notes", stated in the migration comment, the CLAUDE.md row and the findings doc. `notes_conflict` is NON-BLOCKING by user decision: handicapper prose constantly names horses from other races ("beat Chrome last out"), so blocking would refuse most real notes; the warnings are pushed AFTER the parse so notes structurally cannot refuse a save. Marker neutralization happens at the INPUT boundary rather than by making `extractTicketBlock`'s `indexOf` cleverer - `persistLlmRace` re-parses STORED responses, so a scan change would be retroactive and could make an already-approved request parse differently. Batch guard is a POSITIVE `interactive` flag only the preview route passes (fail-closed), refusing 409 rather than silently dropping notes, with a `check-backfill` tripwire. **Verification**: `check-llm-cards` grew ~40 checks across a pure phase (the byte-identity assertion, one per rule keyed on a distinctive phrase plus the same phrases in `docs/prompts/llm-card-v1.md`, sanitizer, `trailingText`, report parsing incl. the documented "a report before the ticket block is never parsed" rule) and a server phase (draft round trip, snapshot immutability across a draft edit, notes reaching the ACTUAL logged `prompt_text`, the prompt-injection fixture, non-blocking conflict + Save 201, the latch with no 409, `notes_post_result`, the batch 409). **The injection test's limits are printed in its own output**: it proves the PLUMBING is injection-resistant, because notes only ever enter as prompt text and never as a parameter, so bankroll arithmetic and stake validation ignore note content by construction; it proves NOTHING about whether a real model obeys the rules - the stub is not a model, and that is H2 in the findings doc. `check-schema` +3, `check-backfill` +1. VERIFIED: `check-llm-cards`, `check-schema`, `check-backfill`, `check-human-picks`, `check-pl`, `check-equibase-otr`, `check-engine`, `npm run build` all pass. No `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D93 | Analyst notes: the modal UI | 3 | [#126](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/126) / `llm-notes-ui` | merged | second of three (D92 server/prompt/schema, D93 this, D94 reporting + findings). One collapsible notes editor per race, mounted after `EntriesTable` and before the preview block, plus one for the whole day above the race grid; each is a textarea, a `source` datalist seeded program / public-handicapper / llm / own (free text still accepted, so an unexpected source is never blocked while the four canonical values stay one click away for the findings doc's H3), and the codebase's FIRST character counter - guidance, not a gate, since over-cap text is truncated visibly by the server and flagged in the preview rather than refused. **Notes live in their own `notesByRace` Map loaded on dayId**, never merged into `ticketsByRace`: `refreshTickets` rebuilds that one wholesale from the server on every card change and would clobber an in-progress note. Its loader is `useEffect(() => { loadNotes(); }, [dayId])` - a BLOCK body, never `useEffect(loadNotes, deps)`, which is exactly the shape that unmounted the whole app in D90. Local edits sit in a ref and flush on blur AND explicitly at the top of both `handleGenerate` and `handleRegenerateAll`, so a note pasted and Generated in one motion cannot be lost. **`handleStartNewCard` deliberately does NOT clear notes** (its nine hand-reset pieces of state stay nine, with a comment saying why) - notes belong to the DAY, not the card, which is the whole point of the day+race key: a new card on the same day should see the same commentary. Two small server additions the UI needed: `GET .../llm-notes` now also returns `postResult`, so the amber "results already recorded, these are not blind" banner is right on load rather than only after the first save; and `GET /cards/:id/llm-requests` exposes `notesPresent`/`notesEnteredAt`, which is what lets the `notes edited since generation` badge be correct after a reload instead of relying on remembered local state. **Verification**: no check script renders React, so this is `npm run build` plus browser verification against the real instance on Del Mar 2026-09-04 - all 8 race cards render a notes section, the datalist offers the four labels, the counter reads 48 / 4000 while typing, the amber banner fires on a day with results, a note typed and blurred persists (confirmed against `GET /llm-notes`) and the `notes` badge appears, day-level and race-level notes plus the source label all survive a FULL PAGE RELOAD, and - per D90 - closing the modal leaves `#root` populated with zero console errors. Test notes were cleaned from the live day afterwards. One case verified by inspection rather than click: the explicit pre-generate flush is belt-and-braces, since clicking Generate already moves focus and fires the blur flush; exercising it live would have made a real paid API call. `check-llm-cards` unaffected. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D94 | Analyst notes: the reporting split and the pre-registered findings doc | 3 | [#127](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/127) / `llm-notes-reporting` | merged | last of three (D92 server/prompt/schema, D93 modal UI, D94 this). **Must merge before the first live notes-augmented card** - the findings entry is a PRE-REGISTRATION, and it is worthless written afterwards. `GET /api/pl`'s LLM_GENERATED bucket gains `byNotes`, following D76's `byModel` precisely: the SAME selected rows grouped by `cards.notes_present`, never a second pool, asserted to sum to the bucket total on cards/cost/returned/P&L. Ordered notes-first rather than by P/L - with only ever two rows a deterministic label order reads better than a sort (a one-line deviation from byModel, noted here). Rendered in `PLView.jsx` only when BOTH sides exist: a one-sided split is not a comparison and a lone number invites being read as a result. `notesPresent` is coerced to a real boolean on every card row (SQLite hands back 0/1) and echoed so a reader can cut it finer; `PLView`/`CardsPanel`/`CardView` each show a `notes` tag beside the D76 model label. **Deliberately NOT cross-tabbed model x notes** - with a handful of cards each cell is a pool of one. `docs/findings/llm-analyst-notes-v1.md` states H1 (do notes change the ticket set at all, on DELIBERATELY paired generations - the only hypothesis a small corpus can answer, and if it comes back "no difference" the feature is a no-op worth reverting), H2 (does the model obey the five rules, measured against TICKETS not against the model's own self-reported `influence`, since a model writing "used" every time is narrating rather than behaving - and this is where injection resistance is actually measured, because CI's stub is not a model), and H3 (P/L, with a pre-registered floor of 20 cards a side across two meets before any claim, and an explicit note that notes are not randomised so an observational difference is uninterpretable). It also records the corpus-hygiene rules: batch runs cannot carry notes, `notes_post_result = 1` days are not blind and are excluded, the day note is repeated into every race's prompt so tallies must dedupe via `notes_card_text`, and `prompt_text` must never reach the export or publish path because notes are someone else's copyrighted prose. The file heads with the PROMPT version alongside engine and bucket, because LLM cards have no version axis - the gap D92's conditional clauses close for this feature, and which a future prompt change must close again. VERIFIED: `check-llm-cards` +4 (both sides present, deterministic order, rows sum to the bucket, every card row echoes the flag), `check-pl`, `check-schema`, `check-sim`, `check-distribution`, `npm run build` all pass. No schema change (D92 added the column), no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D95 | Replay day landing: the final card, once the day is closed | 3 | [#129](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/129) / `replay-final-card` | merged | user request 2026-09-05, and the original screenshot ask behind D91 - the day landing showed a race-by-race P/L table and nothing else, so the card you actually played was only reachable one race at a time. **ID note**: D91's own row plans this as "D93 display" with a "D92 backfill" before it; those numbers went to the analyst-notes feature in between, so the display is D95 and the backfill is the next free ID. **The sheet is `CardView` itself**, mounted with a new `embedded` prop that drops only its pagehead - the title line and the Export / Grade / Delete / Back row. Writing a second sheet was rejected: the per-race tables, the subtotal rows, the day total and the grade join are all money presentation, and a second copy is free to drift from the real card page. Delete in particular has no business firing from a page whose subject is a race day. **The gate is `summary.closed`, not "the day has results"** - a Replay day ALWAYS has results (the picker only lists days that do), so "after results are generated" would render the sheet for a card sitting part-played, handing back the finish order for a race the player is still blind on; `buildSummary`'s human totals also sum every graded ticket on the card regardless of reveal state (card 102 on day 27 is the live example: 2 races locked, 0 revealed, 7 tickets). It is the same bit and the same reason D60 gates ReplayRaceView's own graded tables on. A part-played card gets a dim line saying so instead; a day with no human card gets nothing. Above the sheet, a closed-day banner repeats the D60 numbers (blindness chip, human wagered/PL/ROI-both-ways/hits, lean if the day has one) so the two views never disagree, and names how many earlier playthroughs the day carries - a replayed day mints a new card (D28), and the landing shows the LATEST, the same resume rule ReplayRaceView follows (D60). `BLINDNESS_LABEL` moved to `client/src/api.js` beside `modelLabel` (D76's precedent for a display label kept off the network): ReplayRaceView and ReplayStanding each already carried a private copy and this would have been the third. Presentation-only - no server, schema or API change, and no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump. Browser-verified against the real corpus on all four states: day 27 (2026-08-30, card #3 closed) renders 7 race sections with Result/P&L columns and race-1 subtotal +$8.60 matching the landing row exactly, footer "Graded: returned $712.40 on $200 wagered · +$512.40"; day 24 (2026-08-27, card #2, Pre-commit) renders its one played race, the other 7 correctly absent because a PASS writes no allocation; day 28 (part-played) renders NO sheet and no outcome anywhere in the DOM; a day with no human card renders nothing. `/card/:id` keeps its full chrome (Export/Regrade/Delete/Back), the embedded copy has none. Per D90, `#root` still has children after the day-builder modal opens and closes, with zero console errors, and the race view round trip restores the landing intact. Dark theme re-themes through the existing tokens - no new CSS at all. **KNOWN GAP, not introduced here**: every one of the 27 tickets on the two closed cards predates D91, so "If it hits" reads "—" on the whole sheet. | D96 | Retroactive "If it hits" estimates on tickets saved before their writer had one | 3 | `backfill-payout-estimates` | merged | closes the gap D95 shipped with and named. D91 made `shared/betmath.js`'s `estimateTicketPayouts` the ONE "If it hits" dispatcher and wired it into the human path (D67 and D73 had done the LLM and OTR ones), but only for tickets saved from then on - so D95's Replay final card rendered a dash down its whole estimate column on the only two days that can show it. `scripts/backfill-payout-estimates.js` (new, one-off, idempotent, dry run by default) re-runs the SAME dispatcher over stored rows, following `scripts/reformat-teller-calls.js` (D85) and `scripts/strip-bottom-line-byline.js` (D77-B) exactly. **Three safety properties, each load-bearing.** (1) Scope is `engine_version IN ('human','llm','equibase-otr')` - the three writers that actually call the dispatcher. Engine cards are excluded ON PURPOSE, not incidentally: `shared/card-engine.js` computes its estimates mid-construction with context the dispatcher does not have (place-rule ML, straight exactas priced off the LONGEST-priced under, the rebalancer rewriting est after a stake moves), so re-deriving one would CHANGE a stored lean number - an engine change requiring an `ENGINE_VERSION` bump (invariant 14). CLAUDE.md's betmath row already forbids unifying the two; this is the same rule applied to stored data. (2) The UPDATE carries its own `AND est_payout_min_cents IS NULL`, so it can only ever fill, never move a number anyone has read. (3) A bet type with no validated formula anywhere in this codebase - show, straight trifecta, superfecta, superfecta box, none of which the engine produces either - or a selection with no morning line comes back from the dispatcher UNCHANGED BY IDENTITY: the row stays NULL and is reported as `declined`, never guessed. **On today's corpus 42 of the 76 NULL tickets are exactly that, and they are correct rather than a miss** - 16 EQB_OTR show, 13 LLM straight trifecta, 6 LLM show, 6 LLM superfecta box, 1 LLM superfecta. The fillable 34 are precisely the human tickets the D91 plan named (21 win, 13 exacta box). A future validated show formula would pick the rest up on a re-run with no code change here. REAL-CORPUS RUN 2026-09-05: DB backed up first via `VACUUM INTO` (`data/betsheet.sqlite.pre-d96-backup`, so the WAL was captured and verified complete at 2321 tickets / 96 cards / 2321 graded / 820 allocations / 76 NULL before any write), 34 filled across 2 days, second run reports 0 to fill. VERIFIED by diffing live against the backup: **0 of 2321 ticket rows differ on ANY of the 13 non-estimate columns** (id, card_id, race_id, sequence, bet_type, selections, stake_cents, cost_cents, teller_call, rationale, rule_tags, rationale_text, odds_at_bet), exactly 34 estimates changed, **0 pre-existing estimates overwritten, 0 lean-engine tickets touched**, every other table's row count unchanged, and graded returned/P&L totals byte-identical at $1,927,185 / -$10,015 (the same figures D85 pinned). Two estimates hand-computed independently against the entries and matched to the cent: ticket 1831 win #5 at ML 5/2 -> `winPayout(8000, 2.5)` = $280.00 exact; ticket 1833 exacta box 5-7 at 5/2 and 3/1 -> `exactaEstimate(3500, 2.5, 3)` = 3500 x 3.5 x 4 x 0.55 = 26950, banded +/-35% = $175.18-$363.83. `check-human-picks`, `check-grading`, `check-pl`, `check-replay`, `check-llm-cards`, `check-equibase-otr`, `check-export`, `check-schema`, `npm run build` all pass. Browser-verified the D95 sheet afterwards: day 24 race 1 reads $280 / $200 / $175.18-$363.83 (est.) against a real WIN $240 return, and all 24 tickets on day 27's card carry an estimate with zero dashes remaining. No new check script, the same call D85 made - the script's own logic is one call to an already-covered pure function, and the real-corpus diff is the evidence. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D97 | Dev harness: the agent's preview stack stops taking the human's ports | 3 | [#130](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/130) / `dev-preview-ports` | merged | reported 2026-09-05 as "this CONTINUES to be a problem" after `npm run dev` was refused by a process the user had never started. Diagnosed from the live preview registry, not guessed: the holder was the session's OWN browser-verification server (preview `ac6f5777`, started 19:11:46Z = 12:11:46 local, matching the reported root PID 27812 at 12:11:47 to the second). Root cause is structural rather than a stale orphan: `.claude/launch.json` ran `npm run dev`, so **every agent browser verification took 8788 and 5175 - the exact ports the human's own `npm run dev` wants**. D80's preflight can only report that collision after the fact, and it names a process the person never started, which is why it read as a recurring mystery. The fix is D83's reasoning applied to ports instead of paths: make the isolation STRUCTURAL rather than something the agent has to remember. `scripts/dev-preview.js` (new) sets `BETSHEET_PORT=8795` / `BETSHEET_VITE_PORT=5185` and spawns the ordinary `dev` script rather than re-implementing it, so the preflight, the watcher and vite stay defined in one place and simply read the env it set; precedence is explicit env > this clone's `.env` > these defaults. `.claude/launch.json` now runs `dev:preview`, keeping the config NAME `betsheet` so existing agent habit (`preview_start({name:'betsheet'})`) lands on the isolated ports automatically - the whole point being that it cannot be forgotten. Port choice is not arbitrary: it dodges life-swipe (8787/5173/5174), the human's pair, **the human's vite fallback range 5176-5180** (`dev-clean.js` sweeps those when the tree is provably this checkout's, and an agent stack IS this checkout, so a person's `dev:clean` would otherwise kill an agent mid-verify - exactly the bug D83 fixed for betsheet-alt), betsheet-alt (8798/5178), the 8790/5177 escape hatch the preflight prints, and the 8899-8920 check band. Second half of the user's ask: `npm run dev:restart` (= `dev:clean --yes && dev`) collapses the two-step remedy that was itself part of why a stale stack stayed in the way long enough to be reported twice; the preflight and dev-watch now print it FIRST, above the look-only `dev:clean` and the env-var escape hatch (now labelled POSIX-shell syntax, since D83 already recorded that shell mismatch biting a user following this repo's own README). The preflight also carries a comment saying an agent stack should no longer appear in its holder list at all, so a future `dev:preview` process named there reads as bypassed isolation rather than shared-by-design. **Third cause found while verifying, and it is not a dev stack at all**: the process actually holding 8788 at report time was `npm start` (`npm run build && node server/index.js`, PID 29728), started minutes AFTER the preflight failure - the user reaching for a served build once dev was refused. `dev:clean` already catches it (it sweeps whoever holds a configured port, since you cannot start without those), but "port in use" meaning a served build rather than an orphaned `npm run dev` is now in the Gotchas. VERIFIED live: `dev:preview` starts on api 8795 / vite 5185 and serves 200 there; with it running, a human `npm run dev` is refused **only** by that `npm start` process and the agent stack appears nowhere in either the preflight's or `dev:clean`'s holder list, which is the isolation working; 5175 stayed free throughout. A DEP0190 warning from `spawn(cmd, args, {shell:true})` was fixed to the single-command-string form. Dev tooling plus one launch config - no server, shared, parser, schema or API change, and no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D98 | Build a card by hand from the /day view | 3 | [#132](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/132) / `day-ticket-builder` | merged | user request 2026-09-05. The ticket builder (D86) worked but was mounted only inside Replay, and Replay's picker carries `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM race_results ...)` - so on an ACTUAL race day the card you want to build is not in the list. Confirmed live before writing anything: Del Mar 2026-09-05 (day 76, 11 races, no results) was absent from the picker, while `/replay/day/76` typed directly rendered in full and previewed a real ticket. The gap was reachability, not capability, which is what keeps this deliverable small - the writer already handles a resultless day (`persistHumanRace` checks `hasResults` and skips inline grading), the results save already auto-grades whatever is on the day, and the per-race projection already 404s only on a missing day. **`ReplayDayBuilderModal` is renamed `DayTicketBuilderModal`** and gains a `context` prop (`'replay'` | `'live'`) that changes NOTHING but the wording; `CardsPanel` mounts it behind "Build card by hand", resuming the day's own latest human card off the cards it already fetched (D60's resume rule, no extra call), with `bankrollCents` passed down from `RaceDayView`. One component rather than two is the point: a card built live and a card built by replaying a past day are the SAME HUMAN card - same template, engine_version, bucket and derived blindness - and the user decided (2026-09-05) they are deliberately NOT separated in reporting. That is still derivable later without a schema change or a hand-set flag (a card whose first `picks_locked_at` predates the day's first `result_charts` row was built live), so nothing is lost by deferring, and invariant 15 is not bent either way. Kept `GET /replay/days/:id/races` as the data source rather than minting a parallel `/race-days/:id/...` endpoint - it is "per-race human-card state for a day", not a Replay concept, and D87 deliberately made `entriesPayload` the ONE projection two callers share; a second endpoint would re-introduce exactly the drift D87 removed. The route name is a historical artifact and renaming it is a follow-up, flagged rather than smuggled in. **Two consequences recorded rather than left to be rediscovered as bugs**: `race_days.replayed_at` is stamped by a LIVE first lock too (so a day played live is marked "replayed" and `GET /replay/random` will never offer it again - arguably right, since you did commit picks on it), and a live card cannot CLOSE until results exist, so it reaches the standing table only after an evening reveal. VERIFIED: `check-human-picks` +11 - the LIVE path had NO coverage at all before this (every other check in that file saves the results chart first, explicitly "so grading runs on lock"), so the new block locks a race on a day with no `race_results` rows (201, `graded` null rather than a throw), proves estimates still fill because D91's estimator reads the morning line and not the chart, proves the first live lock stamps `replayed_at`, pins that blindness reads UNDETERMINED (null) rather than PRE_COMMIT while nothing is revealed - which is why the modal's copy was rewritten mid-implementation to stop over-promising - and then saves results and proves the already-locked card is graded by the save's own hook. `check-replay`, `check-pl`, `npm run build` pass. Browser-verified on the real day 76 through the new button: 11 race cards, bankroll $500 threaded correctly, race 3 composed `$10 W 1` and previewed "read-only, exactly what Lock will store", the Replay path still renders its own wording, a fresh tab showed a CLEAN console, and per D90 `#root` still had children after close. Lock was deliberately NOT clicked - that writes a real HUMAN card and stamps `replayed_at` on the live corpus. No server, schema or API change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D99 | Fix: grade sets stamped with the wrong engine version | 3 | [#134](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/134) / `fix-grade-set-version` | merged | found by D98's new live-path test, the first thing in the repo to grade a non-engine card through a results save. `server/grading.js`'s `gradeAndPersist` defaulted its `engineVersion` to `ENGINE_VERSION`, so the two call sites that relied on that default - `gradeAllCards` (the results-save hook) and `POST /cards/:id/grade` (the card view's "Grade vs results" button) - stamped `lean-1.1` onto HUMAN / LLM_GENERATED / EQB_OTR cards that no engine produced. Invariant 14 says a grade set records the version it was produced under; those rows recorded a false one, and every future `ENGINE_VERSION` bump would have appended another junk set on each results re-save. `persistHumanRace` / `persistLlmRace` / `confirmEquibaseOtr` all passed a version explicitly, which is exactly why the REPLAY order (results first, lock second) never showed it - and why D98's LIVE order, where the results save is the ONLY thing that ever grades the card, hits it every time. **Fixed at the source, not at the call site**: the default now comes from the CARD via a new exported `gradeVersionFor`, so both broken sites and any future one are covered at once. It keys on the `lean-` PREFIX rather than a list of the non-engine labels, so a future bucket is correct without editing the function, and it deliberately keeps engine cards on the CURRENT `ENGINE_VERSION` - they DO have a version axis, and invariant 14's "a regrade under a newer version appends a set" rule depends on a legacy `lean-0` card regrading to `lean-1.1`. `check-human-picks` +4: the results-save hook stamps `human`, no `lean-*` set exists on that card at all, the manual regrade route stamps `human` too (same default, same bug, one fix), and - the control that matters - an engine card on the same day still grades under `ENGINE_VERSION`. The assertion D98 left PINNED to the buggy behaviour is flipped. **Stored-data repair in the same PR** (`scripts/fix-grade-set-versions.js`, new): per affected card it REGRADES under the card's own version and only then drops the `lean-*` set. That order is load-bearing - 3 of the EQB_OTR cards had NO correct set at all, so deleting first would have left them ungraded and moved P/L, permanently if it failed midway; both steps run in ONE transaction. Grading is deterministic from stored tickets + results, so the fresh set must reproduce the stale one cent for cent: the script checks that per card and REFUSES to touch any card where it does not, because a difference would mean the grader changed since those rows were written - a finding, not something to overwrite. The dry run grades IN MEMORY (`gradeCard` directly, never `gradeAndPersist`, which writes) so it genuinely writes nothing - caught and fixed during implementation, since the first draft would have half-applied the repair on a dry run. REAL-CORPUS RUN 2026-09-05: DB backed up first via `VACUUM INTO` (`data/betsheet.sqlite.pre-d99-backup`, verified complete at 2321 graded / 2496 tickets / 101 cards), dry run proved 0 rows written, then 7 cards and 198 stale rows repaired, second run reports 0. VERIFIED against the backup: `graded_tickets_latest` - what every reporting surface actually reads - is byte-identical at $1,927,185 returned / -$10,015 P/L across the same 2321 rows, **0 cards differ on their latest-set totals**, and provenance is now exactly aligned (equibase-otr/equibase-otr 64, human/human 34, lean-1.1/lean-1.1 1973, llm/llm 250, with no cross-stamping anywhere). The 4 non-engine cards carrying no grade set at all are unchanged and correct - all on today's resultless day. `check-grading`, `check-engine`, `check-pl`, `check-human-picks`, `check-llm-cards`, `check-equibase-otr`, `check-sim`, `check-export`, `check-replay`, `check-distribution`, `check-schema`, `npm run build` all pass. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump - grading BEHAVIOUR is untouched, only the label a set is filed under |
| D100 | Card sheet: each race collapsible | 3 | [#136](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/136) / `card-race-collapsible` | merged | user request 2026-09-05. An 11-race card is a very long scroll and the sheet had no way to put a race away - the nested `Entries` / `Bottom Line` / `Results` panels were already `<details>` (D48-QA, D81), but the race block itself was a plain `<div>`. Each race block is now a `<details>` whose `<summary>` IS the existing `race-sheet-head` line, so a collapsed card reads as a one-line-per-race index - number, classification chip, post/surface/distance/type, allocated/spent - with a ticket count appended, which is the one fact the head did not already carry and the thing you want when deciding whether to open it. **Open is the default**: the sheet is read for its tickets, and a card that opens closed would hide the money. Held in state as the COLLAPSED set rather than the open one for the same reason - a race that appears later (a reload after a regrade) is open without having to be added anywhere. `Expand all races` / `Collapse all races` sit above the list rather than in the pagehead, because the pagehead is dropped by D95's `embedded` prop and the Replay day landing needs them too; they only render on a multi-race card. The toggle handler guards on `e.target !== e.currentTarget` - `toggle` does not bubble natively, but React's delegation makes that worth pinning rather than relying on, and a nested `Entries` panel collapsing its whole race would be a silent, confusing bug. CSS: the head is a flex row, so the `<summary>` has to be one too (a block-level flex child otherwise wraps BELOW the native marker - seen and fixed during verification), which drops the marker, so the caret is drawn with `::before` and flipped on `[open]`. It uses the same U+25B6/U+25BC glyphs already rendering elsewhere in the app rather than U+25B8, which this machine's font renders as tofu - also seen in the browser, not guessed. Presentation-only: no server, schema, API or shared change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump, and no change to what any race renders once open. VERIFIED per the definition of done's presentation-only category: `npm run build` exits 0. Browser-verified on the real card #9 of day 76 (2026-09-05, 11 races): all races open on load with the caret inline, `Collapse all races` yields a clean 11-row index, clicking one race's head reopens just that race, and opening its nested `Entries (6)` leaves the race itself open - the guard working |
| D102 | Day builder: unlocked races are kept as drafts | 3 | [#138](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/138) / `builder-drafts` -> `close-locks-previews` | merged | user request 2026-09-05: "allow the user to save races while building card by hand before closing". Per-race `Lock race N` already saved one race immediately, so the real gap was the OTHER direction - a race BUILT but not LOCKED lived only in `DayTicketBuilderModal`'s React state, and Escape, a backdrop click or Close threw it away with no warning. Asked before building, with the alternatives named: a close-time warning that offers to lock, or persisted drafts. **User chose persisted drafts**, and chose the option whose preview stated the storage explicitly. `client/src/drafts.js` (new) keeps unlocked builder text in `localStorage` keyed `betsheet:ticket-draft:v1:<dayId>:<race>`. **This is card data in localStorage, which `client/src/prefs.js` deliberately does not hold** - the rule is honoured by keeping it in a SEPARATE module and re-pointing the prefs row at it, rather than quietly widening prefs. The rejected alternative was a server-side drafts table: a draft is a scratchpad for one browser, not a record the corpus should carry, and the boundary that actually matters is untouched - a draft is TEXT and still goes through preview -> lock -> the server's own independent re-parse (invariant 9) before a single ticket is stored. Nothing about blindness moves either: a draft has no `picks_locked_at`, so `computeBlindness` cannot see it (invariant 15). **A restored draft comes back in a TEXT box, never the builder.** `TicketBuilder` is a one-way text producer that starts empty and emits `''` from a mount effect, so it cannot be seeded - and opening a drafted race into it would have wiped the draft on the spot. That is why the per-race typing toggle (ReplayRaceView's own, D86, same two-mode shape) had to land with this, and why switching INTO the builder says out loud that it clears the box rather than letting it be discovered by losing work. Other behaviour: a `draft` tag and the draft text on the collapsed race row (visible without opening it), a footer count, an explicit `Discard draft`, drafts cleared on lock and on PASS, and `handleLockAll` now clearing text PER SUCCESSFUL RACE instead of wholesale - the old code wiped every race's text at the end of the batch, which ate exactly the work worth keeping when a race failed. Every storage read and write is wrapped (localStorage throws outright where site data is blocked) and an unreadable entry is dropped rather than surfaced; blank text clears a draft, and a draft otherwise dies only on lock, PASS or Discard. Presentation-side only: no server, schema, API or `shared/` change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump, and the two contexts (`live` from `CardsPanel`, `replay` from `ReplayDayLanding`) stay one component per D98. VERIFIED per the definition of done's presentation-only category: `npm run build` exits 0. Browser-verified against the real day 76 (Del Mar 2026-09-05, races 2 and 3 already locked by the user): built `$12 W 4` on race 1, confirmed the exact stored value (`{"text":"$12 W 4","savedAt":"..."}`) under the day+race key, closed the modal, reopened it and found the race carrying its `draft` tag, its text on the collapsed row and `1 draft kept` in the footer; opened it into the text box, PREVIEWED from the restored draft (1 ticket, $12, "exactly what Lock will store"), then Discarded - storage empty, preview and count cleared, `#root` still populated (D90) and zero console errors. **Lock was deliberately not clicked**: that writes a real HUMAN card to the live corpus, the same line D98 drew |
| D101 | GitHub API access from the local git credential, and a local guard on `main` | 3 | `github-api-access` | merged | user request 2026-09-05, prompted by a failure of mine. Twice in one session I could not answer a question about this repo's own PRs - `gh` is not installed and the repo is private, so an unauthenticated call returns `Not Found` - and I inferred a PR number from ordering instead. **It was wrong**: asked to resolve conflicts on PR #133 I reasoned it must postdate #132, landed on `card-params-ui` (the declined D29 branch), resolved those conflicts, hit the decline contradiction and was told to stop; #133 was actually `backfill-payout-estimates`, D96, my own branch - which is what "now rebase D96" then fixed. One authenticated GET says so in a second, and `npm run gh -- pr 133` now does exactly that. **No install and no visibility change**: pushes already authenticate through Git Credential Manager and that credential carries `repo` scope (5000 req/hr), so `scripts/gh-api.js` asks git for it via `git credential fill`. Two properties do the safety work. (1) **The token never escapes**: never printed, never written to disk, never in argv - it is an in-process `fetch` header, so `ps` cannot see it the way it would a `curl -H "Bearer ..."` - and every byte the script prints passes a redactor covering `gho_`/`ghp_`/`github_pat_`/`sk-ant-` shapes on error paths too. Proven, not asserted: across success, refusal, HTTP-404 and usage output (1593 bytes), the real token appears **0 times**. (2) **The allowlist, not the token, decides what may happen.** GCM issued that credential for git and its scopes (`repo, workflow, gist, user, ...`) are wider than this needs and are not ours to narrow, so `ALLOW` in the script is the real constraint: any GET, POST to open a PR or comment, PUT to merge a PR - and nothing that deletes, force-pushes, touches refs or changes repo settings, refused BEFORE a request is sent (verified: DELETE repo, PATCH repo, POST releases and DELETE a branch ref all exit 1 having sent nothing). Owner/repo come from `git remote get-url origin` so the betsheet-alt clone works unchanged; a Git Bash path-mangling bug found while testing (`/repos/...` arriving as `C:/Program Files/Git/repos/...`) is normalized rather than pushed onto the caller. **Merging is permitted by user decision, which CHANGES A RULE** this file's own workflow section stated: "The user reviews and merges - Claude never merges" becomes "The user reviews; Claude merges only when explicitly told to, per PR", with the guardrails spelled out (never unprompted, never a PR the user has not seen, never to get around a failing check). I flagged the contradiction before it was chosen and it was chosen deliberately; the rule is edited in the same commit rather than left to contradict the tooling. **Making the repo public was rejected** (offered, not chosen): it would republish ~48MB of committed third-party PDFs - 4 Del Mar programs, 16 Equibase OTR sheets, an At The Races racecard, an Equibase chart, dmtc pages, ML sheets - committed as PRIVATE fixtures by deliberate decision, from a source that already blocks scripted access; stripping them means rewriting 41MB of history and breaking every check script that reads them. It would also not help the write path. A history scan found no real secret to worry about either way (only `.env.example` is tracked; its key line is a commented 11-char `sk-ant-...` placeholder; zero token-shaped strings in 200 commits). **The guard on `main` is local by necessity**: GitHub answers 403 "Upgrade to GitHub Pro or make this repository public" for both classic protection AND rulesets on this Free/private combination, so `scripts/git-hooks/pre-push` refuses any push whose target ref is `refs/heads/main`, and `install-hooks.js` sets `core.hooksPath` to the TRACKED hooks directory rather than copying into `.git/hooks`, so the hook that runs is the file under review and cannot drift. Its limits are in the hook's own message, not hidden: local-only, bypassable with `--no-verify` by design, and unable to gate a merge on checks. Dev tooling plus docs - no server, schema, parser or API-contract change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D103 | Day builder: closing locks previewed races; locked tickets are delete-only | 3 | [#138](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/138) / `close-locks-previews` (carries D102) | merged | user request 2026-09-05: "closing the ticket builder should lock any tickets previewed. When the ticket builder is opened again, the user can delete a previewed ticket but not edit it." **Close is now the commit.** A preview already IS "read-only, exactly what Lock will store", so discarding it on close was the wrong default; every close path - the footer button (relabelled `Lock N & close`), the ×, Escape and the backdrop - runs one `handleClose`, held in a ref so the once-registered key and backdrop handlers see the CURRENT `pending` rather than a stale closure. A failing race keeps the dialog open with its results block showing, rather than closing over an error nobody saw. Races with text but no preview, or a preview carrying a blocking warning, are still just drafts (D102): nothing is saved that the server has not already agreed to, so invariant 9 is untouched. **Delete, never edit** - and that asymmetry is the blindness rule, not caution for its own sake. Re-locking a race re-stamps `human_race_state.picks_locked_at`, which is precisely what `computeBlindness` reads, so an in-place edit could silently flip a card from PRE_COMMIT to SEQUENTIAL (invariant 15 says blindness is DERIVED, never hand-set). A delete touches no timestamp at all, so the card's derived blindness is identical before and after; editing proper stays in the race view, which owns that rule. `deleteHumanTicket` + `DELETE /api/cards/:cardId/human-tickets/:ticketId` (server/human-cards.js, the same module that owns every other human write) with two refusals, both about not rewriting a record someone has already read: a REVEALED race (409, naming the D28 new-card remedy) and a card carrying ANY grade set (409 - deleting a graded ticket would move a P/L figure already reported, invariant 14's spirit). The revealed check runs FIRST, so a revealed-and-graded race says "revealed" rather than the less specific thing. The guard is on the CARD, not the ticket, because a card's grade set is what P/L reads. Deleting the LAST ticket of a race retires it - allocation row and `human_race_state` row both go, so it reads "Not played" and can be built again - while `race_days.replayed_at` is deliberately NOT rolled back (the day was played; that stays true). A surviving race's `allocations.amount_cents` is recomputed from what is left rather than kept at the pre-delete total, since every P/L surface reads it. New trace event `human_ticket_deleted`, documented in docs/trace-schema.md with the reason it carries no timestamp change. `server/replay.js`'s two ticket projections gain `id` (additive; every other field byte-identical) - the address the delete needs. VERIFIED: `check-human-picks` +13 covering the ungraded path where it actually lives (its own second human card on the live day, D28 making one free, BEFORE the results save): a ticket id per locked ticket, one-of-two deleted leaves the race locked, the allocation recomputed to exactly what remains, the LAST delete retiring the race, the race lockable again afterwards, `replayed_at` surviving, 404 on an unknown ticket AND on an engine card's ticket, plus the two 409s - revealed (message names the reveal even though that card is also graded, proving the guard order) and graded, with a follow-up asserting the refused delete changed nothing. `check-replay`, `check-pl`, `check-export`, `npm run build` all pass. **Browser-verified against a COPY of the real 108-card corpus** (`VACUUM INTO` a temp DB, an isolated `dev:preview` stack on api 8795 / vite 5185 pointed at it) rather than the live one, because this flow WRITES and the live day's human card is the user's own: deleted a $30 exacta box from a locked race and watched its allocation fall $40 -> $10 while the race stayed locked; built race 1, previewed, and the Close button became `Lock 1 & close` and committed it; reopened to find race 1 Locked, delete-only, no Build; deleted its last ticket and watched it return to "Not played" with Build back. Escape closed cleanly with nothing pending, `#root` still populated (D90), zero console errors, and the LIVE database was byte-identical afterwards (2803 tickets / 108 cards, unchanged). No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump | **ID note**: both rows were written as D101/D102 and renumbered after the fact - PR #135 (`npm run gh` + the pre-push guard) merged into main while this work was in flight and had already taken D101, so the drafts row moved to D102 and this one to D103. D101 stays where it landed; the ledger is append-only in spirit, and moving someone else's merged row to tidy the sequence would break every reference to it |
| D104 | Equibase entries HTML parser (pure) | 3 | `equibase-entries-parser` | merged | first step of the simulator/analyzer pivot, and the piece its own P-2.1 assumes already exists (it did not - only the analysis did, merged as #140). Built FIRST, ahead of the pivot's P-0/P-1, because it is pure and additive and breaks nothing, while P-1 deletes ~3,500 lines from a working system. `shared/parsers/equibase-entries.js` takes the page's HTML as a STRING and never fetches: invariant 6 forbids scraping Equibase and Equibase blocks it, so the file arrives because a person saved the page - the same manual-upload posture as D69 and D71. Browser-safe (no `node:` import) so the ingest preview can run it client-side like `shared/entries-parser.js`. **The rule that matters is that columns are mapped from each race's own header row, never a fixed index**: a claiming race inserts `Claim $`, giving 12 columns where an allowance race has 11, and index-based parsing slides jockey/weight/trainer/M-L/live-odds by one on every claiming race. That is not hypothetical - it is the error made while first analysing this fixture, where a 12-column row's M/L was read as its live odds and produced a false "races 3, 6 and 7 have live odds" claim in the requirements doc. A saved view-source page is unwrapped automatically - its markup is escaped twice, so entities need two passes - and ordinary saved HTML parses directly, so either capture method works and nobody has to remember which was used. `effectiveOdds` is live odds when posted, else the morning line, `null` for a scratch, with both raw fields always kept and a decimal exposed beside it. Reuses `morningLineToDecimal` from `shared/entries-parser.js` rather than duplicating odds math - **a deliberately temporary import**, since the pivot's P-2.5 deletes that file and must relocate the helper (`shared/betmath.js` is the natural home) before it goes. Two bugs found and fixed during implementation, both from ordering: UI cruft was being stripped BEFORE whitespace collapsed, but "See More" and "See Less" are separate elements so the phrase only becomes contiguous after collapsing; and the `Jump to Race:` pattern used `.*$`, which once the block was a single line swallowed the race header behind it. | D107 | P-0.1: freeze the pre-pivot corpus before anything is removed | 3 | `archive-pre-pivot-corpus` | merged | the pivot's first step, and the safety net for every removal after it. `scripts/archive-corpus.js` writes two artifacts to `archive/`: a `VACUUM INTO` snapshot (WAL folded in, which a file copy would miss) and one trace export per card, built through `buildCardExport` directly so no server is needed. **Both are committed, and the `.db` is NOT redundant** - checked rather than assumed: a card export carries recipe/races/entries/consensus/allocations/tickets+grades/results/trace, but NOT `llm_card_requests` (the paid, nondeterministic model prompts and responses), `human_race_state` (the lock/reveal timestamps invariant 15 derives blindness FROM), `simulation_runs` (the 50 runs `docs/findings/lean-1.1-program-only.md` cites by id) or `llm_notes`. Those exist ONLY in the snapshot, so a planned factory reset plus one disk failure would lose them permanently and none can be regenerated at any price. It also refuses to clobber an existing archive for the same date without `--force`, because re-running once removals had begun would freeze the wrong thing. `.gitattributes` pins `archive/** -text`, the same reasoning `tests/fixtures/**` already uses: `core.autocrlf` would otherwise rewrite the JSON on checkout, so a fresh clone's bytes would differ from the frozen ones and the recorded sha256 would stop meaning anything. **Nothing in the app reads from `archive/`** - it is never migrated forward, and fixtures promoted for regression testing are COPIED into `tests/fixtures/` (P-0.2) rather than referenced from it. | D115 | P-2.2: schema for the Equibase entries ingest | 3 | `equibase-entries-schema` | merged | the first of P-2, and deliberately its own PR: it is a schema change with a table rebuild, and rebuilds are where this project has the most to lose. That is precisely what the `-- betsheet:schema-rebuild` header exists for: db.js runs such a migration outside the wrapping transaction with FKs OFF, then re-enables them and runs `foreign_key_check` before commit. **VERIFIED AGAINST A COPY OF THE REAL CORPUS, not a synthetic fixture** - the same discipline D54's migration 016 used, and for the same reason.
