The new CHECK value is accepted and an unlisted one refused. **NAMING DEVIATION, deliberate**: the value is `equibase_html`, not the `EQB_MANUAL_UPLOAD` `docs/requirements/equibase-entries-ingest.md` proposed. That column's existing vocabulary is lowercase and names the DOCUMENT the entries came from - `program`, `ml_sheet`, `both` - and `program` was a manual upload too, so "manual upload" does not distinguish anything about this source. `consensus_completeness` is UPPERCASE, but that is a different column with a different convention and copying it here would have left this one inconsistent with itself. NEW COLUMNS, all nullable, none read by any money calculation: `entries.live_odds` / `live_odds_decimal` (the tote price when the capture was late enough to have one - stored BESIDE `morning_line`, never over it, both raw values always kept; **storing it is inert, FEEDING it to generation would not be** - `estimateTicketPayouts` reads `morning_line_decimal`, and repointing it would change what every stored estimate means, an `ENGINE_VERSION`-class change under invariant 14), `entries.medication` (its own column, NOT merged into `equipment` - Lasix and blinkers are different facts and merging loses both), `entries.age_sex` ("5/G", as the page prints it, because splitting it invents a parse the source does not support), `entries.claim_price`, `entries.also_eligible` (kept and flagged rather than dropped, the treatment scratches already get), and `race_days.odds_captured_at` (ONE timestamp for the whole card, which means later races are staler than earlier ones **by construction** - that is the design, and the per-race staleness indicator is what surfaces it, not a defect to be fixed with per-race timestamps the source cannot supply). `claim_price` was NOT in the requirements doc's list; it is added anyway because the parser already extracts it, it is real handicapping information, and a field dropped at ingest cannot be recovered without re-saving the page. ALSO: `server/ingest.js`'s allowlist gains the value, with a comment saying why it is load-bearing - the column's CHECK refuses a bad value loudly, but a value missing from the ARRAY is silently coerced to `program`, so the two must be edited together. `shared/track-codes.js` registers **Kentucky Downs** (`KD`), the first non-Del-Mar entry and the first target for this ingest; an unrecognized track was never an error and still is not. VERIFIED: `npm run check-schema` with nine new assertions covering every property the rebuild could silently have dropped, plus all 17 suites and `npm run build` as regression. No `ENGINE_VERSION` bump - nothing about generation, allocation, ticket construction or grading changed |
| D116 | P-2.1 + P-2.3: wire the Equibase entries ingest - route, writes and UI | 3 | `equibase-entries-ingest` | merged | a race day can now be created from a manually saved Equibase entries page. This is the ingest half of the pivot actually working, and it is what makes a track with no automated feed - Kentucky Downs first - reachable at all now that Del Mar program ingestion is gone (D113). **WIRING THE PARSER FOUND TWO REAL DEFECTS IN IT, which is the argument for wiring before declaring D104 done.** (1) `conditions` was the last 1400 characters of the header block, verbatim. On race 1 that is the page's own "Jump to Race" navigation strip followed by a block of inline JavaScript - `var httpHost`, a purchase-link URL and the rest - and it would have been written to `races.conditions` and read back into every LLM prompt built for that day. (2) Neither `raceType` nor `wagerMenu` was extracted at all, though both are printed on the page. **The wager menu is load-bearing, not decoration**: `TicketBuilder` and `shared/parsers/human-picks.js` both read `races.wager_menu` for minimums and combo costing, and a null there does not fail visibly - it silently falls back to `BET.minimums`, so every Kentucky Downs ticket would have been costed against Del Mar's defaults. THE FIX anchors on the block's actual printed grammar rather than a character count: `Free Tools:` -> wager menu -> `<Track> <RACE TYPE>` -> `Purse $` -> distance -> `(Surface)` -> conditions. The TRACK NAME is what separates the menu from the race type, which is why the page's own track is threaded into `parseHeaderBlock` rather than re-derived there; no track marker inside the span yields both as null plus a non-blocking warning, never a guessed split. **One bug found while writing it**, worth recording because the symptom was subtle: computing the span in a re-sliced copy and subtracting the literal length of `'Free Tools:'` is wrong because that is a REGEX match whose printed length varies with the page's whitespace - the race type came back as `STARTER OPTIONAL CLAIMING $50,000 P`, carrying the `P` off `Purse`. Offsets are computed in the source string now. THE ROUTE: `POST /api/parse/equibase-entries`, preview only (invariant 9) - it never writes, and the client confirms through `POST /api/race-days`, which re-reads what was confirmed rather than trusting the preview. The body is JSON `{html, oddsCapturedAt}` rather than a raw `text/html` body so ONE endpoint serves both capture routes the parser accepts - a saved file read client-side and markup pasted into a textarea - without the client having to know which it is holding. **It is not a fetcher and invariant 6 is untouched**: it takes a string a person saved and chose to upload, and after D113 there is no HTTP client left in this codebase to fetch with. A 4.3MB view-source capture is 4.28MB as a JSON body, inside the existing 10MB limit. THE WRITES: `insertRaceDay` widened to store the six Equibase-only entry columns D115 added plus `race_days.odds_captured_at`. Every other ingest path leaves them null, which reads as "staleness unknown" rather than "fresh". `liveOddsDecimal` is now emitted by the parser beside `liveOdds` - the same text/decimal pairing `morning_line` has had since D04 - so `live_odds_decimal` has a real source instead of being derived at the call site. THE UI: "Upload Equibase entries page" is the primary control in `NewRaceDay.jsx`, with the pasted-text path demoted to the labelled fallback for a page this parser cannot read. The file is read IN THE BROWSER and its markup posted as text, so the server never sees a path and never opens a file; the file's own `lastModified` travels with it as `oddsCapturedAt`, because the page prints no capture time and when it was saved is the only honest answer available. GOLDEN DISCIPLINE, exercised twice: the hand-written assertions were added FIRST and passed against the OLD golden, so the only failure was the golden diff itself - which is the whole point of `check-parsers`' rule that a golden diff alone can bless a regression. The diff was read before being accepted both times: all 113 entries byte-identical, post times / purse / distance / surface identical, nothing removed, `raceType` + `wagerMenu` added and conditions changed on all 11 races the first time; only `liveOddsDecimal` added the second. Eight new hand assertions cover exactly what would have caught the original bugs, including that the menu parses to REAL minimums ($1 exacta = 100c, 50c trifecta = 50c, 10c superfecta = 10c) rather than fallbacks, and that no race's conditions carry `Jump to Race` or `var httpHost`. VERIFIED end to end rather than by unit alone: the real committed capture through the real route into the real database and back out - 11 races, 113 entries, 0 warnings, `entries_source = equibase_html`, `odds_captured_at` stored, track canonicalized to DMR, wager menu and race type on every race, clean conditions, 65 medications, 110 age/sex, 13 claim prices on the 12-column race, 6 also-eligibles, 3 scratches retained, 0 live odds (correct - this capture predates wagering). Then in the browser: the upload control auto-filled Track and Date off the page's own header and rendered the read-only preview. Eleven of those assertions are now permanent in `check-ingest`, placed LAST in that file because the block soft-deletes a day and a tombstone is exactly what the deleted-list and factory-reset assertions above it count. All 17 suites and `npm run build` green. No `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D117 | P-2.4: entries staleness indicator | 3 | `entries-staleness` | merged | the last of the four deliverables `docs/requirements/equibase-entries-ingest.md` laid out, and the one that makes the single-timestamp design honest instead of merely defensible. A day ingested from a saved Equibase page carries ONE `odds_captured_at` for the whole card, because the page prints no per-race capture time, so later races are staler than earlier ones **by construction**. The requirements doc calls that "the design, not a defect to fix later" - which is only true if it is VISIBLE, and this is what makes it visible. **WHAT THE MODULE REFUSES TO CLAIM IS THE DESIGN, not a gap in it.** The capture age is exact: `odds_captured_at` and `now` are both absolute instants, so "saved 43 minutes ago" is a fact. Whether a race has ALREADY RUN is not exact, and `shared/staleness.js` will not pretend otherwise - `races.post_time` is a printed local string ("2:00PM") with no zone. The D104 parser does read one off the page ("PT") but nothing stores it, so comparing that string to a viewer's clock silently assumes the viewer sits in the track's timezone: wrong for a Californian card read from the east coast, and wrong by an hour twice a year even locally. So the comparison is made ONLY where it needs no zone at all - the race day is before the viewer's today (every race has run, true in any timezone on earth) or after it (none has) - and on the day itself it IS made but carries `assumesViewerClock: true` so the caller can label it. A race with no post time, or an unparseable one, returns `ran: null`. **Three separate assertions cover the refusals**, because each has a wrong answer that looks helpful. Likewise a day with NO capture time reads `state: 'unknown'`, never `'fresh'` - every ingest path but the Equibase one leaves the column null, and treating null as fresh would be the worst possible default on a page about freshness. A capture stamped in the FUTURE is a clock disagreement, not freshness: also `unknown`, and it never prints a negative age. PURE, and `now` is always PASSED IN rather than read from the clock - a freshness module that cannot be tested at a fixed instant is one nobody can trust, which is also why `check-staleness` runs with no server and no database. `STALE_AFTER_MINUTES` (75 - roughly two race intervals, so a page saved before the card began is flagged by the time you are betting the third race) is exported and overridable per call, so the UI and the check assert the same number rather than two copies of it. UI on `RaceDayView`: a day-level banner ("Entries saved 3h 0m ago (9:31 PM)", amber past the threshold, with what to do about it) and a per-race tag - `past post` where that can be said, `entries Nm old` where the capture is stale, nothing where neither is true. ONE `now` for the whole render so the banner and every tag agree; re-read on each render rather than held in state, because a stale clock reporting stale entries as fresh is the single thing it must not do. A day with no capture time renders NO banner rather than an "unknown" one - every pre-Equibase day has none, and a permanent notice would be noise. **One presentation bug found in the browser and fixed**: the separating space sat INSIDE the tag element, where its padding swallowed it, and the summary read "post 1:30 PMentries 180m old". VERIFIED: `npm run check-staleness` (new, 38 assertions - the threshold boundary in both directions, the parameter override, every refusal, and the whole-card property that two races share one capture age while differing on whether they have run) plus browser verification against a REAL day ingested from the committed capture with a deliberately 3-hour-old timestamp, which rendered the stale banner and the per-race tag correctly, under the Kentucky Downs track registered in D115. All 18 suites and `npm run build` green. HOUSEKEEPING: the verification days created here and in D113/D116 were removed from the live corpus afterwards, including a soft-deleted Santa Anita tombstone D113 had left behind - invariant 12 already excluded it from every aggregate, but test data does not belong in the real corpus. 75 real race days, 6,784 entries, all 108 cards untouched. No schema change and no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D118 | The legacy corpus soft-deleted, and the empty states that exposed | 3 | `empty-state-honesty` | merged | **User decision 2026-09-06, taken INSTEAD of a factory reset.** The pivot's own plan (P-6.3) ends in a reset; the analysis that preceded this asked what one actually costs and found the answer is more than the plan assumes - the archive is a FILE, while P/L, Distributions and Replay Standing read the live database, so after a reset the running app can answer nothing about the 108 cards, and the 12 LLM cards represent 130 paid, nondeterministic calls that cannot be reproduced at any price. Soft delete gets the same clean surfaces and keeps every row queryable one click away. **The set was corrected before acting**: the recommendation had said "the 75 legacy race days", but only 69 days are lean-only - the other 6 carry all 23 live-bucket cards - so the choice between 67 and 73 days was put to the user rather than guessed, and the user chose all 73 remaining (2 were already deleted), a coherent position given that D112's prompt change makes the 12 stored LLM cards non-comparable with anything generated from here on. **Executed through the REAL `DELETE /api/race-days/:id` route**, never a direct UPDATE, so all 73 landed in the decision-trace as `race_day_deleted` under each day's own correlation id (invariant 12 requires the deletion itself be logged) - 73 events on file, 0 failures, 677 races / 6,510 entries / 106 cards / 2,767 tickets hidden. **Nothing was hard-deleted, and that is asserted rather than claimed**: a `VACUUM INTO` backup was taken first and verified table-by-table BEFORE the operation, and re-compared after - all 24 tables row-for-row identical, 108 cards and 2,811 tickets still on disk. Reversibility was PROVEN, not assumed: day 76 was restored (12 cards back, P/L repopulated, all four engine versions listed) and then re-deleted. **What the operation exposed is the rest of this deliverable.** Hiding the corpus rendered every empty state for the first time, and three of them still advertised deleted features: the home screen offered "upload the program PDF" (D113 deleted it), P/L said "Generate a card" (D111 deleted the button), and Replay offered to "compare against lean" (D111 deleted the engine). Distributions printed the literal `Engine null only.` because its version line never distinguished "no version selected" from a version. **And one real bug**: the deleted-days list rendered "No deleted race days." over a 200 response carrying all 75. Not the API - an unguarded async effect, where two rapid toggles leave two fetches in flight and the superseded one's answer can land last. It had been latent for as long as the active list was never empty, because a stale active response still rendered a plausible table; it became reachable the moment the corpus was hidden. Fixed with a `cancelled` flag whose cleanup returns a FUNCTION, which is the other half of the D90 useEffect rule, and recorded in Gotchas beside it. **This one is load-bearing for the change above** - restore is the entire safety net of a soft delete, and a restore list that reads as empty defeats it. VERIFIED: browser-verified against the real (now hidden) corpus - the exact rapid-toggle sequence that produced the empty list now renders all 75 rows with their Restore buttons, and all four empty states re-read correctly; API-level checks that the day list, P/L, Distributions, Replay days and Replay standing are all empty while `?deleted=1` returns 75. All 18 check suites and `npm run build` green. No schema change and no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump; the database change is data, not migration, and is reversible from the UI |
| D119 | Factory reset: wipe every table, and say so | 3 | `reset-wipe-completeness` | merged | `server/reset.js`'s `WIPE_ORDER` is a hardcoded list of tables, written before migrations 015, 018 and 023 existed. `human_race_state`, `llm_card_requests` and `llm_notes` were therefore never named in it. They WERE cleared - every one of them cascades from `cards` or `race_days`, verified - so the reset was correct; what it was not was **auditable**. The `app_reset` event is the first entry of the new era and is the only record of what the previous one contained, and it under-reported by **169 rows**: 28 blind-play timestamps, 130 paid LLM requests and 11 analyst notes, destroyed and unmentioned. The file's own comment calls that event "auditable, never silent". **Fixed on both sides of the problem.** The three tables are added in FK order - `human_race_state` and `llm_card_requests` before `cards`, `llm_notes` before `race_days` - and the ORDER is documented as load-bearing rather than incidental, because it cannot be derived: most FKs here cascade, but `cards.strategy_template_id`, `simulation_runs.strategy_template_id`, `fetch_attempts.source_id` and `consensus_picks.source_id` are ON DELETE NO ACTION, so the referencing rows must go first. More importantly the list is **no longer trusted to be complete**: any table it has never heard of is deleted FIRST, in the leaf-most position (a new table is far likelier to be a child than a parent), and the wipe then ASSERTS every table is empty **inside the transaction**, so a survivor rolls the whole wipe back and throws - the caller keeps its data and hears about it, instead of being told the app is factory-fresh when it is not. Asserting inside the transaction is deliberate: the log files are still untouched at that point, and a reset that has destroyed the logs but kept rows is the worst possible half-state. `strategy_templates` is the one table legitimately non-empty afterwards - the built-in templates are code, not user data, and are reseeded - and the check now pins that to exactly the three surviving producers, which also proves the twelve retired lean rows are gone. **Also documents the trap that surfaced this.** `resetApp` takes a `db`, but `resetLogs()` does not - it operates on the CONFIGURED log directory, so a reset run against a copy of the database still deletes the real logs. That is how 14 live log files were lost on 2026-09-06 while probing what a reset costs; the trace CONTENT survived in the committed `archive/exports/` (which is precisely what D107 froze it for), but every live export has read `traceStatus: missing` since. Recorded as a warning at the top of the file and in CLAUDE.md's Gotchas, with the remedy `check-ingest.js` has always used and which is why it was never affected: point `BETSHEET_LOG_DIR` at a throwaway directory in the same process. VERIFIED: `check-ingest` gains four assertions that **read the table list out of the schema rather than naming tables**, so they cannot go stale the way `WIPE_ORDER` did - every table empty after a reset, `rowsRemoved` naming every table it wiped, the three post-015 tables reported explicitly, and `strategy_templates` reseeded to exactly `equibase-otr,human,llm`. Plus two probes against a copy of the REAL populated corpus, both with `BETSHEET_LOG_DIR` redirected: the report now names 23 tables instead of 20 and accounts for 29,727 rows instead of 29,558, and a synthetic `future_thing` table absent from `WIPE_ORDER` was wiped AND reported with its true count of 40, which is the self-healing property the fix is actually for. All 18 suites and `npm run build` green. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D120 | P-6.1: the grader still reproduces every archived summary | 3 | `grader-regression` | merged | The assertion the pivot's sequencing puts in front of the factory reset - "prove the grader still reproduces archived summaries BEFORE the reset; a mismatch means grading behaviour changed, which is a finding, not a rounding error" - and the one `scripts/check-graded-card-fixtures.js` had been an explicit PLACEHOLDER for since D108. **RESULT: no drift. 9 of 9 summaries and 386 of 386 per-ticket outcomes reproduce exactly**, across all four completeness buckets, all four engine versions, three LLM models and five dates. The gate is green, which is the outcome that lets a reset proceed - had it not been, this row would be reporting a finding instead. **Per-ticket is asserted, not just the summary**, because a summary can match while two errors cancel; a mismatch prints the offending tickets with their teller calls, archived grade and regraded grade, so it is investigable rather than merely red. The grader is fed ONLY what it is allowed to see - bet type, races, legs, stake, cost - never the stored grade, which would make the comparison circular. **Carries a negative control, and the control earned its place immediately.** A regression test that cannot fail is worse than no test, so the check perturbs one payoff by a single cent and requires the regrade to disagree. The first draft aimed it at "the first finisher with a win price", which sat in a race card 139 never had a win bet in - the total did not move and the control reported a FALSE PASS. It now derives its target from the card's own biggest winning win ticket, so it is aimed at something the grader demonstrably reads. Two further guards assert the harness is feeding real tickets rather than an empty list, which would otherwise sail through every equality on a losing card. Deliberately reuses `shared/grading.js`'s `buildDayResults` while regrouping the fixture's own results block rather than importing `server/grading.js`'s `loadDayResultsFor`: that one reads a live database, and this check must need neither a server nor a corpus - the fixtures ARE the corpus, which is why D107 froze them before any removal began. VERIFIED: `npm run check-graded-card-fixtures`, 53 assertions, all green; the negative control demonstrated failing before it was re-aimed. All 18 suites and `npm run build` green. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump, no production code touched at all - this deliverable is a test |
| D121 | Batch regression harness for the entries parser, and the wrong page diagnosed | 3 | `equibase-batch-regression` | merged | **User request 2026-09-06, with 93 downloaded Equibase pages and a first-draft script.** The goal is the one D104's ledger row flagged and every deliverable since has repeated: the parser's "track-agnostic" claim is UNPROVEN because the only entries fixture is Del Mar. **THE 93 FILES ARE THE WRONG PAGE, and that is the headline finding.** Every one of them is Equibase's race-card INDEX page - `id="entryRaces"`, one row per race carrying purse, race type, distance, surface, starter count and post time, and **not a single horse**. 93 of 93, across roughly 40 tracks including the Kentucky Downs page the pivot names as its first target. The parser returned 0 races for all of them and was CORRECT to; what it could not do was say why. The right artifact is the per-track entries page at `equibase.com/static/entry/<TRACK><MMDDYY><COUNTRY>-EQB.html` - confirmed from the committed Del Mar fixture, which names that path in its own markup - with `CAN` for Canadian tracks and `PUR` for Puerto Rico. 91 exact URLs were derived from the saved files' own links and handed back; 2 Camarero files carry no race links at all. **So the parser gained the diagnosis rather than the corpus gaining a fixture.** `index_page_not_entries` is a blocking warning that names the page to save instead, replacing a generic `no_races` that was true, unhelpful, and read like a parser bug. The generic warning is kept for genuinely unrecognizable input and asserted separately, so the specific one demonstrably refines rather than replaces it. The real Kentucky Downs index page is committed as a fixture, so the assertion runs against the actual artifact a person mis-saves rather than a synthetic stand-in. **The harness is built and proven anyway**, because it is the thing that will settle the claim the moment the right files exist. Corrections to the supplied draft, each of which would have produced a wrong or misleading run: `entriesSource: 'program'` would have filed Equibase pages under the DELETED program parser's provenance - it is `equibase_html` (D115); `raceType` and `wagerMenu` were not passed through at all, and **the wager menu is load-bearing** (`TicketBuilder` and `human-picks.js` read it for minimums, and a null is a silent fallback to Del Mar's numbers, so every ticket at a new track would be costed wrong); the six D115 entry columns were counted as "dropped" when they have had homes since D115, so the run would have under-reported what it stored while actually storing nothing in them; `latin1` reads would mojibake accented horse, jockey and trainer names on a UTF-8 page - stored, and looking like a parser bug; and an argument-filter bug meant any run WITHOUT `--write-report` dropped the directory argument and died on the usage message. `oddsCapturedAt` now travels from the file mtime, matching what D116's UI sends. Read-back verification was widened past row counts to `entries_source`, `odds_captured_at` and the presence of a wager menu, since counts alone pass on a day whose every Equibase-only column is NULL. VERIFIED: against the committed Del Mar fixture the harness imports 11 races / 113 entries with both column counts (11 and 12 - the claiming-race case) and a clean read-back; against all 93 supplied files, **0 crashes and 93 correct diagnoses**, which is itself a real result for the "never throws" contract - 93 real-world pages of an entirely unexpected shape. The Del Mar golden is byte-identical, so the parser change is additive. 5 new assertions in `check-equibase-entries`. All 18 suites and `npm run build` green. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump. **The track-agnostic claim is still UNPROVEN** and this row should not be read as closing it |
| D122 | The entries parser proved across 91 tracks, and the three defects that found | 3 | `equibase-multitrack-fixes` | merged | **D104's "track-agnostic" claim, flagged as UNPROVEN in every ledger row since, is now measured.** 91 real Equibase entries pages supplied by the user, ~40 tracks across the US, Canada and Puerto Rico, 803 races, 6,683 entries, four distinct column layouts (8, 9, 11, 12). Run through D121's harness - the real parser and the real `insertRaceDay`, into a throwaway database. **The first run imported 63 of 91.** Three defects accounted for every failure, all three now fixed, and the run ends **91 of 91, zero crashes, zero read-back mismatches**. **(1) Two scratches in one race killed the whole day - 10 files.** A scratch row prints no program number, so the parser correctly returns null, and `insertRaceDay` coerced null to the literal `'SCR'`. A second scratch in the same race produced a second `'SCR'` and violated `UNIQUE(race_id, program_number)`, failing the entire day's insert. The comment on that line was written for the program parser, where one numberless horse per race was the most that ever appeared. **Fixed as uniqueness, not renaming**: the first numberless entry in a race is still plain `'SCR'`, so every row already stored and `check-ingest`'s existing assertion are untouched, and only the second onward is suffixed. **(2) A card saved days ahead has no program numbers or morning lines - 18 files.** Equibase serves a narrower entries table (`PP, Horse, VS, A/S, Med, [Claim $,] Jockey, Wgt, Trainer`) with no `P#`, no `M/L` and no live odds. **It is a function of LEAD TIME, not of track** - measured: 0-2 days out was the full table on all 53 such files, 3+ days out was reduced on 18 of 38, at tracks as ordinary as Churchill Downs, Gulfstream, Woodbine and Parx. Those columns are missing because the values DO NOT EXIST YET. Without a program number a day cannot be stored at all (NOT NULL, UNIQUE per race), so the post position is used - it is the betting number on a table that has no coupled-entry column for a `1A` to live in - and it is announced with `program_number_from_post_position` rather than substituted silently, **saying the numbers are PROVISIONAL until the field is drawn**. Morning lines stay genuinely empty rather than invented, so `estimateTicketPayouts` declines by identity and no "If it hits" figure is guessed. The operational conclusion belongs with the operator: **save the page 0-2 days out**. **(3) A track spelled two ways on its own page - 12 races.** Lethbridge's header prints `Lethbridge Rmtc`; every race block prints `Lethbridge - Rmtc`. D116's wager-menu split searches for the track name between `Free Tools:` and `Purse`, so the exact `lastIndexOf` missed and BOTH the menu and the race type came back null on every race of both cards. That is the failure mode that matters most quietly: `TicketBuilder` and `human-picks.js` read `races.wager_menu` for minimums and a null falls back to `BET.minimums` - Del Mar's numbers - so every ticket at Lethbridge would have been costed wrong with nothing visibly broken. `looseIndexOf` compares letters and digits only and maps offsets back to the original string, so spacing, hyphens and punctuation cannot break the split without loosening into a fuzzy match. **Recorded, not fixed**: 3 races carry a `Rating` column the parser ignores (non-blocking, and the header cell's own markup leaks an attribute fragment into its text); 6 cards print no wager menu at all, which is the source's limitation and is now reported on its own line rather than counted as a read-back mismatch - the harness was wrong to treat it as one; and 37 track names are absent from `shared/track-codes.js`'s registry, which blocks nothing (each gets a derived code) but means no canonical display spelling. VERIFIED: 91/91 imported, 0 crashes, 0 read-back mismatches, 803 races, 6,683 entries. **The Del Mar golden is byte-identical**, so both parser changes are additive to the path that already worked. Two new fixtures cover the new layouts - Thistledown (reduced table, and one of the six with no wager menu) and Lethbridge (hyphenated track) - with 12 new assertions. All 18 suites and `npm run build` green. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D123 | The track registry filled out: 2 tracks to 39 | 3 | `track-registry` | merged | **User request 2026-09-06**, closing the gap D122's run reported: 37 of the 39 tracks in the 91-page capture were absent from `shared/track-codes.js`. Done in ONE pass rather than a track at a time, because the corpus that names them is a set of pages Equibase replaces daily - the spellings are readable today and not re-downloadable later. **The code is Equibase's own track id**, read out of each page's own `EntriesPlus2` links rather than invented. That is the substance of the change, not bookkeeping: `canonicalizeTrack`'s fallback derives a code from the first three letters of whatever the page printed, which gave Canterbury Park `CAN`, Charles Town `HOL`, Horseshoe Indianapolis `HOR` and Mountaineer `RES`. Those collide with other tracks' real codes and MOVE whenever a page changes its wording - and the code is what the one-day-per-track+date rule and the results-chart mismatch refusal compare on, so a moving code is a silently duplicated race day. Three entries deliberately store a shorter display than the page prints - Charles Town (page: "Hollywood Casino At Charles Town Races"), Lethbridge ("Lethbridge Rmtc", and "Lethbridge - Rmtc" in its own race blocks) and Mountaineer ("Mountaineer Casino Racetrack & Resort") - with every observed long form kept as an alias, so a save spelled either way lands on one row. An unlisted track is still never blocked: it gets a derived code and saves. **A fourth parser defect surfaced while collecting the names.** The entries header regex's character class `[A-Za-z .'-]` excludes `&`, so "Mountaineer Casino Racetrack & Resort" matched only the trailing word **"Resort"** - and that is what would have been stored as `race_days.track` and keyed `UNIQUE(track, date)` on. Fixed by widening the class rather than re-anchoring on the breadcrumb, deliberately: the Del Mar view-source fixture's breadcrumb is mangled by the double-escaped markup, so anchoring there would have broken the one capture that already worked. `/` stays out of the class, which is what keeps the lazy match from crossing the separator and swallowing "Home / Entries /". The Del Mar golden is byte-identical. **New `scripts/check-track-codes.js` (36 assertions), pure**, pinning the properties that would fail SILENTLY and LATE rather than at the time: no two entries sharing a code (which would merge two real tracks' race days into one identity), no spelling resolving to two different tracks (which would make the answer depend on registry order), no alias containing a character the lookup strips before comparing (such an alias can never match and simply reads as unrecognized), every display and every alias round-tripping to its own entry, the derived codes these tracks used to get pinned so a regression is loud, an unrecognized track still never blocked, and meets still a Del Mar-only fact - a newly registered track has none, never a guessed label. The check found a bug in ITSELF on the first run: an assertion called `meetFor(code, date)` when the function takes a date alone. VERIFIED: the 91-file batch re-run reports **zero unrecognized tracks** (was 37) and still imports 91/91 with 0 crashes and 0 read-back mismatches. Registry audited for duplicate codes and cross-track spelling collisions: none. All 19 suites and `npm run build` green. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump. **Existing stored rows are not rewritten** - canonicalization happens at save, so this affects days saved from here on; the corpus is soft-deleted anyway (D118) |
| D124 | Every surface the entries page prints | 3 | `surface-variants` | merged | **Found by doing what the user asked - importing the 91 days into the real app and looking at them.** `races.surface` was NULL on **637 of 803 races** while distance, post time and conditions were populated on all 803. A field that empty is either a source that does not carry it or a parser that cannot read it, and the difference matters: P-7.1's dimension table names surface as one of the columns every later analysis slices on. **It was the parser.** The page prints FOUR surface parentheticals and the regex knew one: `Turf` (166 races), `All Weather Track` (41), `Inner turf` (21), `Outer turf` (8). Reading all four takes 236 of 803 races from 166 - no inference anywhere, the page states every one of them. Stored **verbatim** rather than folded into a Turf/Dirt/All-Weather vocabulary: inner and outer turf are different courses at the same track and a handicapper reads them differently, and a distinction dropped at ingest cannot be recovered without re-saving the page. Normalising for grouping is a read-side question for whatever reports on surface. **Dirt is never printed at all, and is deliberately NOT inferred from the absence.** That was checked rather than assumed: the race-card INDEX page (the artifact D121 diagnosed as the wrong page to ingest) DOES carry an explicit surface column, so joining it per race against the entries pages gives ground truth. Absence of a parenthetical meant Dirt in **567 of 567** joined races - the inference would almost certainly be right. It is still not made: "the page did not say" and "the page said dirt" are different facts, this parser reports the first, and the measurement is recorded here so the decision can be taken deliberately later with the number in front of it. The same join is what proved the other three variants are real and not noise: `All Weather Track` -> index says All Weather (41/41), `Inner turf` and `Outer turf` -> index says Turf (29/29). VERIFIED: new fixture WO090726CAN (Woodbine, three of the four variants on one card) plus a synthetic for `Outer turf` rather than committing a second 1MB page for one assertion; 7 new assertions including that an all-dirt card reports null throughout and never a guessed "Dirt". The Del Mar golden is byte-identical - the change is purely additive. All 19 suites and `npm run build` green. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D125 | Horse names compare and prompt with a parenthetical suffix stripped | 3 | `strip-horse-name-suffix` | merged | **User request**: an Equibase entries page prints a bred-country or state suffix on some names (`Eternal Reign (IRE)`, and the 91-track corpus is full of them - R2 of the check-equibase-otr fixture alone has `Certitude (FR)`), which other sources describing the same horse often omit. `entries.horse_name` correctly keeps the suffix verbatim (invariant 9 - the stored/displayed name never changes), but **five independent, near-identical copies of the same `nameKey` normalizer existed across the codebase, none of them stripping it** - so a results chart's ALSO RAN line, a chart scratch, an Equibase OTR sheet's picks, and (with no normalization at all) the pasted-entries scratch footer would all fail to resolve "Eternal Reign" against "Eternal Reign (IRE)", either producing a spurious non-blocking `name_mismatch` warning or leaving a finisher unresolved. **Consolidated to one canonical implementation** (`shared/parsers/human-picks.js`'s `nameKey`/`stripParens`, now exported) that every other file imports instead of keeping its own copy - the same "one place" discipline D123 applied to track codes, now applied to horse names. `shared/dmtc-results-parser.js`'s own `nameKey` (a fixed `GB\|IRE\|FR\|...` country-code allowlist, already dead code outside a dev script) now delegates to the shared version, generalizing coverage from a fixed list to any parenthetical. `shared/entries-parser.js`'s scratch-footer resolution went from raw `===` (no normalization at all) to the shared `nameKey`, closing a second, worse instance of the same bug class in the same ingest path. **The LLM prompt half**: `server/llm-prompt.js`'s ENTRIES block now prints the bare name (`stripParens`, case preserved) instead of the raw suffixed one - the model no longer has to look past country-code noise on every entry. This is a real, un-versioned prompt-text change (LLM cards have no version axis, same category D112 already documents for the CONSENSUS-section removal), recorded as such in `docs/prompts/llm-card-v1.md`. Storage, extraction and every UI view are untouched - only the comparison layer and the LLM prompt-formatting layer change. VERIFIED end to end on a REAL fixture, not just a synthetic one: `check-equibase-otr`'s existing Del Mar 2026-09-03 day genuinely contains `Certitude (FR)`, and a new assertion proves the `name_mismatch` warning it used to produce is gone, while a real, unrelated mismatch on the same fixture (`Dats Ms. Blame` vs `Dats Ms Blame` - a punctuation difference, not a suffix) still fires, proving the fix is narrowly targeted rather than silencing `name_mismatch` wholesale. New cases in `check-human-picks` (positive, a name-only-token positive, and a negative control confirming a genuinely wrong name still warns) and `check-llm-cards` (the built prompt string). All 19 suites and `npm run build` green. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D127 | Bulk entries ingest: a day's board as one zip | 3 | PR [#167](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/167), branch `zip-reader` | merged | **User request 2026-09-06**, implementing `docs/requirements/zip-entries-upload.md`: an agent collects every track's Equibase entries page on race day and zips them, and one upload creates every race day for that date. A full board is 8-23 tracks, so the single-file path (D116) means 23 previews and 23 saves. **The single-track path is unchanged** - it already existed and already did what a per-track manual upload needs; what it lacked was a name. `/new` now offers two labelled modes, One track and A day's board (zip), rather than a second implementation of the first. **Z-0 was dropped.** The requirements doc opened by calling re-upload-destroys-cards a prerequisite blocker, and a guard was built for it - refusing a replace when cards exist, on both the live and tombstone paths, with counts and an explicit `discardCards` opt-in, browser-verified. The user then clarified that wiping cards on re-upload IS the intended behaviour: entries are the record, and a card built on entries that have since changed is not worth carrying. The guard was removed before commit and the doc corrected in this PR - the MECHANICS it measured are kept there, because anyone re-uploading should know what it costs, but as a documented consequence rather than a defect. Bulk replacement is opt-in per upload (`?replace=1`) instead. **`server/zip-read.js` takes no dependency.** Node has `zlib` but no zip CONTAINER reader, and seven runtime dependencies is a number this project defends. It reads the central directory and inflates entries, and the design is about what it REFUSES: encrypted, zip64, unknown compression method, truncated, bad CRC - each named, never a partial archive a caller would take for the whole. **Bounded because the input is untrusted and decompression amplifies** - the real capture is 10:1, a hostile one trivially 1000:1 - with entry-count, per-entry and total caps, the per-entry one enforced by `inflateRawSync`'s `maxOutputLength` DURING inflation rather than measured after, when the memory would already be allocated. The declared uncompressed size is a cheap reject only and is never believed on its own; a fixture that LIES about its size is asserted to be caught by the real cap. **Zip slip is structurally impossible rather than guarded**: nothing is written to disk and no entry name is resolved as a path, and the check asserts the module imports no `fs` at all. **`server/entries-zip.js`**: preview writes nothing (invariant 9); the save re-reads and re-parses the archive from its own body rather than trusting a client payload (D54/D69/D71). The client posts ~2MB twice, cheaper than a staging area and free of the between-preview-and-confirm drift OTR needed a hash for. **The server unzips, reversing D116's client-side read, on sizing**: a day's raw HTML is 15-17MB against `express.json`'s 10mb limit while the zip is ~2MB - and the property that mattered survives, since the server still never sees a path or opens a file. Policy A (D43) for the batch, ONE TRANSACTION PER DAY so a single bad file cannot roll back the 22 good ones. Each day keeps its own `odds_captured_at` from its own zip entry, the field D117's staleness indicator reads. VERIFIED: `check-zip-read` (hermetic - every archive built byte by byte by a WRITER deliberately independent of the reader, since the cases that matter cannot be produced by zipping a directory) plus `check-entries-zip` (a real server on a temp DB). The reader was separately checked against Python's `zipfile`: all 91 entries of the real capture, sha256 for sha256, identical. Browser-verified through the actual UI on a zip built in-page - a non-html entry excluded, both days recognised as already stored, Save disabled at 0 until Replace is ticked, per-file capture times shown - and deliberately NOT saved, since those synthetic pages would have overwritten real days. All 21 suites and `npm run build` green. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |

| D133 | ReplayRaceView checked for the same backdrop issue; the fix made a house rule | 3 | `modal-backdrop-house-rule` | merged | **User follow-up to D131/D132**: check `ReplayRaceView.jsx` for the same backdrop-click-closes defect, and write the fix down as a standing rule rather than something re-derived per modal. **`ReplayRaceView.jsx` renders no modal at all** - grep for `modal-backdrop` across the whole component turns up nothing, and it is mounted inline in `ReplayDayLanding.jsx`'s page body, same URL, no dialog chrome - so there was nothing to fix there. Copying the LLM modal's component verbatim would have been the fifth near-identical-normalizer bug D125 already named and fixed once; instead `EntriesTable.jsx` is a new shared component with one `normalizeEntry` accepting either casing (`e.programNumber ?? e.program_number`, etc.), same pattern `LlmCardModal`'s own `normalizeTicket` already used for tickets. `LlmCardModal.jsx`'s local `EntriesTable` function is deleted and replaced with the import - net code reduction, not a second copy. `DayTicketBuilderModal.jsx` renders `<EntriesTable entries={r.entries ?? []} />` right after the race-card header, same position the LLM modal uses, for every race regardless of locked/unlocked/build state. ''`, i.e. a blank cell) become `—`, matching what the rest of the entries table and the rest of the app already use for a missing value.
