| D149 | LLM cards: capture generation INPUTS (prompt, response, notes, model, template version), not just outputs, in the trace export | 3 | [#191](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/191) `llm-input-capture` | merged | **User request 2026-09-07**: `betsheet.card-trace-export` recorded everything a generator PRODUCED (tickets, allocations, grades, decision trace) but nothing it CONSUMED - cards 9/10 on race day 268 (Saratoga 2026-09-06, card ids 180/181) were generated from different analyst-notes payloads with no way to reproduce or diff them, and card 180's grade moved from +$91.08 to -$52.90 through six mid-session race regenerations reconstructable only by diffing `ticket_added` timestamps against the final ticket set (98 events for 69 final tickets). **Code archaeology first, per the ticket's own instruction, changed the design**: `server/llm-cards.js`'s `llm_card_requests` (D63/D92) already stores the user prompt, raw response, model and a notes snapshot for every call, append-only, success or failure (invariant 11) - exactly the store the ticket's own "reuse it, don't add a parallel store" line asked for. **A wholly new `card_llm_inputs` table keyed by (card_id, correlation_id) was therefore rejected** in favor of widening the existing row (migration 026, ordinary ALTERs, no rebuild): `correlation_id` (the request never carried its OWN session id before - only cards/trace events did, so there was no way to group "everything one Generate/Regenerate-All session touched"), `system_prompt_text`/`system_prompt_hash` (the system prompt was never stored at all, only reconstructable from the current `buildSystemPrompt`/`SYSTEM_PROMPT` constants - retroactive the moment either next changes), `user_prompt_hash`, `notes_rendered_text` (the composed/sanitized/truncated text the model actually saw, vs the existing `notes_race_text`/`notes_card_text`'s raw human input), `prompt_template_id`/`prompt_template_version`, `request_params` (the resolved `{maxTokens, temperature}`, `server/anthropic-client.js`'s `complete()` now echoes them back rather than a caller re-deriving its defaults). **`PROMPT_TEMPLATE_VERSION` is DERIVED** - a 12-char hash of `SYSTEM_PROMPT` computed at module load, not a number a prompt-fix PR has to remember to bump - because this exact file's own D64/D112/D125/D136/D138/D145/D146/D148 history is eight PRs that edited the prompt and none that carried a version marker; a self-deriving one cannot silently go stale the way a manual counter already has, twice, elsewhere in this codebase (invariant 14's `ENGINE_VERSION`, `saw_classification`). **Three new decision-trace events** (`server/llm-cards.js`, documented in `docs/trace-schema.md`'s new "LLM cards" section): `llm_request_sent` (before the model answers - one event earlier than the `llm_card_requests` row, which needs the outcome too), `llm_response_received` (success, hard failure, or an unparseable response all get one), and `race_regenerated` (`cardId`, `race`, `previousCorrelationId`, `newCorrelationId`, `ticketsRemoved` - emitted whenever `persistLlmRace` replaces tickets already on file for a race; `previousCorrelationId` is the most recent earlier successful request logged for that exact card+race, an approximation since no ticket row links back to the request that produced it, but the append-only log makes it a close one). **Export bumped to schemaVersion 3** (`server/trace-export.js`): a top-level `llmInputs` array, one entry per correlation id that landed at least one ticket on the card - `{correlationId, races, model, requestParams, promptTemplate, requests: [{race, promptHashes, notes, systemPromptRendered, promptRendered, responseRaw}]}`. **Deviates from the ticket's literal JSON example on purpose**: the spec's example collapsed one `promptRendered`/`responseRaw` per correlation id even though `races` could list several - but this generator calls the model once PER RACE, never once per day (D63's own v1 deviation), so a shared correlation id (a "Regenerate All" session, or the same session reused across a day) can cover several genuinely distinct prompts/responses; collapsing them into one shared string would misrepresent calls that never shared one. The per-race detail lives in `requests[]` instead, with `model`/`requestParams`/`promptTemplate` hoisted to the entry level since they are uniform in practice (D76 already fixes a card to one model). **`null` means unknown, never `[]` or "no notes"**: gated on `correlation_id IS NOT NULL`, which no row written before this migration (or by a pre-D149 code path) can ever carry - a non-LLM card, an LLM card that predates this capture, or one with zero matching rows all read `llmInputs: null`. `?omitLlmInputs=1` (`--omit-llm-inputs` on the CLI twin) strips `systemPromptRendered`/`promptRendered`/`responseRaw` and `notes.raw`/`notes.rendered` per request, keeping `notes.hash`/`notes.chars` alongside the always-kept hashes/template/race/model - the shareable form for a card whose notes may carry someone else's copyrighted prose. **Out of scope, per the ticket**: backfilling inputs for existing cards (impossible, never captured), analysis tooling consuming the block, per-race source tagging on tickets, and any change to prompt CONTENT itself (none of the eight prior prompt-fix PRs' text was touched). **Open decision, answered by Kevin before merge (2026-09-07)**: keep the current mutate-in-place behavior on a regenerated race, even on an already-GRADED card - no new card id, no follow-up deliverable scheduled. This PR's `race_regenerated` event plus the original request row surviving untouched is the full remedy asked for; the mutation itself is an accepted, now-fully-traceable behavior rather than a defect to design around. VERIFIED: `npm run check-schema` (migration 026 applies cleanly, no rebuild), new `npm run check-llm-input-capture` (temp-DB server round trip: correlation id / prompt hashes / template id-version / request params / notes snapshot all recomputable independently and matching; `llm_request_sent`/`llm_response_received` bracket a call; export at schemaVersion 3 with a populated `llmInputs` entry, the full per-race prompt/response/notes verbatim; `--omit-llm-inputs`/`?omitLlmInputs=1` strips the right fields and nothing else; a HUMAN card and a simulated pre-migration LLM row both export `llmInputs: null`; regenerating a race writes a NEW request row byte-identical-original-untouched, a `race_regenerated` event naming both correlation ids and the ticket count removed, and the export then carries BOTH correlation ids for that race rather than erasing the superseded one) - all green; `npm run check-llm-cards` (70+ pre-existing assertions, unaffected) and `npm run check-export`/`check-pl`/`check-schema` all still green; `npm run build` green. No `ENGINE_VERSION` bump (LLM cards carry no version axis - this widens what is LOGGED about a call, not how a card is generated or graded). |
| D150 | Static payload builder: one race day as a self-contained JSON file for the Pages target | 4 | `static-pages-target` | merged | **User request 2026-09-07** (`D-scope: Static Pages Target`), which asked for archaeology BEFORE implementation. That archaeology corrected three of the spec's assumptions and is recorded here because two of them changed the design. **(1) The named source does not exist.** The spec asked how a HUMAN card's named source (`emubets`, `drf`, `keeneland-tipsheet`) is recorded; it is not. Those strings appear only in `docs/requirements/card-source-model.md` P-3.2, marked SPECIFIED-NOT-SCHEDULED with no deliverable ID claimed. The only label on a card is `cards.name` (D137). **Kevin's call: drop the requirement from this scope**, so D151 has no source field. **(2) Card source type has no single field** - it is `card.template` / `consensusCompleteness` / `engineVersion` agreeing three ways, which is why D153 checks all three and refuses on disagreement. **(3) `llmInputs` is `null`, never `[]`** - D149 made that distinction load-bearing (null = unknown), so the spec's "llmInputs empty" is implemented as null. Also confirmed: Vite 5.4 single-entry with no second config; `TicketBuilder.jsx` and `EntriesTable.jsx` are ALREADY server-free (so D151 reuses rather than mirrors them, which the spec had assumed would be a re-implementation); and the check scripts are dry-run-by-default with `--yes`, so D153's `--dry-run` inverts the house default and is implemented as an accepted alias instead. THE DELIVERABLE: `npm run build-static-payload -- <raceDayId> [--out <path>] [--reference-cards]`, defaulting to `static/public/payload.json` (the file the deploy publishes, so building a payload and deploying a race day are one act). Read-only - it never inserts, updates or deletes, and a payload build is not an event in a card's history. Entries are carried in **snake_case** deliberately: `shared/parsers/human-picks.js` reads `program_number`/`horse_name` off these rows directly, so the browser needs no adapter that could drift from the server's. `wagerMenu` rides along because a null one silently falls back to Del Mar's minimums. **`payloadHash` covers the race day and NOTHING else**: `generatedAt` is excluded (hashing the clock would make every rebuild a different payload) and `referenceCards` is excluded (an optional build flag the importer cannot know was used - contamination is recorded per card by `sawReferenceCards` instead). The hash therefore answers exactly one question, which is the one D153 asks: are the entries this ticket was built against still the entries on file at home? VERIFIED: new `npm run check-static-payload` (canonicalization is key-order-independent; the hashed region carries exactly four keys; 13 named validation refusals including markup-in-a-field; a real fixture day through the REAL `insertRaceDay`, 10 races / 98 entries; two builds produce an identical hash and `--reference-cards` does not move it, with a negative control proving that renaming one horse DOES; a soft-deleted day refused per invariant 12; the CLI run as a real subprocess) - all green. |
| D151 | The at-the-track ticket builder: a server-free HUMAN card surface in the browser | 4 | `static-pages-target` | merged | A second Vite entry (`vite.static.config.js` -> `static/`) building a card-construction surface with no corpus, no grading, no generation and no database. **Exclusion of LLM/OTR is structural, not a runtime flag**: the static entry has no import path to `LlmCardModal.jsx`, `EquibaseOtrPanel.jsx` or `client/src/api.js`, so nothing needs pruning because nothing was ever referenced - and `npm run check-static-app` proves it against the BUILT bundle with a positive control, so a scan of the wrong file (or of nothing at all) cannot pass silently. **It REUSES `TicketBuilder.jsx` and `EntriesTable.jsx` rather than forking them** (D150's archaeology found both already server-free), and runs `parseHumanPicksText` + `estimateTicketPayouts` in the browser - the identical modules `server/human-cards.js` runs - so the phone validates a ticket with the same code the home server re-parses it with. That does NOT excuse the second parse: invariant 9 is still enforced at import (D153). Storage: IndexedDB, not localStorage (`client/src/drafts.js`'s reasoning does not transfer - there is no server here, so a card exists NOWHERE else until it is exported). Written on every mutation, no save button, because a phone backgrounded and killed at a racetrack runs no unload handler. Drafts key on `raceDayId`, not `payloadHash`, so a mid-day redeploy cannot orphan work in progress. `probeStorage` proves a write survives a read before anything buildable renders and REFUSES TO START otherwise - an afternoon of cards built into a private window that evaporates at tab close is strictly worse than an app that would not open - and a refused `navigator.storage.persist()` is reported in the header rather than hidden, because the remedy (export more often) is the user's to choose. The header carries the `unexported: N` counter and a `beforeunload` guard. Hash routing throughout, so a deep link survives a reload with no SPA fallback to configure. **Browser verification found a real gap**: the active card lived only in React state, so a refresh on `#/race/1` came back with no card selected and the race reading "not played" - the draft was safe in IndexedDB but indistinguishable from lost. It is now remembered in IndexedDB and restored on load. VERIFIED after the fix: a hard refresh mid-build restores the card, its saved text and its re-parsed ticket (`$10 W 1`, cost $10, if-it-hits $210), and lock still works; new `npm run check-static-app` (payload rows and DATABASE rows produce identical tickets cent for cent, with a camelCase negative control; scratches reach the parser; the bundle carries no Anthropic client, LLM prompt template, OTR parser, SQLite/WASM, Equibase fetch, API client or grading). |
| D152 | Export and restore, as a pair, with a rolling backup | 4 | `static-pages-target` | merged | Export alone is a dead end - a wiped browser would leave files useful only at home and no way to keep building the day that produced them - so restore is here too, and it reads exactly what export writes. `shared/static-export.js` owns the document shape AND its validation, so the browser writer and D153's home reader cannot drift. **The document is schemaVersion 3 and uses the card-trace-export vocabulary but carries its OWN schema name** (`betsheet.static-card-export`), because three sections of a real card export cannot honestly exist on a phone: `trace`/`traceStatus` are read back from decision-trace LOG FILES, and emitting `traceStatus: 'missing'` would claim a trace was LOST rather than never written (the import writes the real events at home, where the log lives); `gradeSummary`/`results` need the day's chart, and the static app never reads or writes a grade; and `id` is a device-namespaced STRING rather than the integer AUTOINCREMENT row id, which is minted on import. `llmInputs` IS carried and is always `null` - the same value a desktop HUMAN card's export carries, and never `[]`. Card ids are `<deviceId>-<base36 stamp><salt>` so two phones never collide. **The rolling backup fires a download after every lock or PASS**, which looks profligate and is not: D153's import dedupes by card id, so a second file naming the same card is a no-op, extra files are free, and a download survives storage eviction where IndexedDB explicitly does not. Restore refuses a file whose `raceDayId` does not match the deployed payload, naming both days. Newest-wins on restore, by the card's own `updatedAt`, so restoring an older file cannot overwrite newer work. VERIFIED in the browser: a full `indexedDB.deleteDatabase` followed by restore-from-file recovered the card, its locked race, its ticket and its `sawReferenceCards` stamp; four refusal paths each named their own reason (wrong race day / a card claiming `engineVersion: 'llm'` / malformed JSON / already current); re-importing the same file reported `1 already current` rather than duplicating. **That last test caught a real bug**: a restored card read as `unexported` the instant it arrived, because `putCard` stamped `updatedAt` on the way in - and a counter that cries wolf is a counter nobody reads. Restore now preserves it (`preserveUpdatedAt`, for restore and only for restore). |
| D153 | Import static-web cards into the home database, idempotently | 4 | `static-pages-target` | merged | `npm run import-static-cards -- <path> [--yes]`, taking one export file or a DIRECTORY of them (a phone's downloads folder, which D152's rolling backup fills with ten files describing one afternoon; read oldest-first by `exportedAt`). Dry run by default per the house convention - the spec's `--dry-run` therefore inverts that default, so it is accepted as an explicit no-op alias rather than implemented as written. **Migration 027** adds the identity that makes any of this safe: `cards.id` is an integer AUTOINCREMENT minted locally, so a card built on a phone has no key this database recognizes on a second look, and files built at a racetrack get copied, re-sent and re-imported by hand. `cards.external_id` - a unique INDEX rather than a column constraint, since SQLite's ALTER TABLE cannot carry UNIQUE and a unique index permits the many NULLs every pre-existing card needs - is what turns a second import into `already present` instead of a doubled ticket in the HUMAN bucket. Also `built_on` and `saw_reference_cards` (the same one-way shape as `cards.saw_classification`, D55). No table rebuild: `cards` has ten cascading children and is not rebuilt for three additive columns. **Imported cards merge into the ordinary HUMAN bucket, not a bucket of their own** - invariant 13 buckets on what SIGNAL a card had, and a phone at the track had exactly the signal a laptop at home had. `built_on` is recorded as fact so "did at-track cards do worse?" stays answerable later without splitting the corpus now. **Invariant 9 is not waived by the file**: the import re-parses the ticket TEXT through `persistHumanRace` - the same writer the desktop uses - refuses on any blocking warning, and only REPORTS a disagreement with the device's own parse rather than resolving it in either direction. **Invariant 15 is served rather than bypassed**: the device's real lock times are re-stamped onto `human_race_state.picks_locked_at`, and `race_days.replayed_at` corrected to the earliest real lock. `persistHumanRace` stamps write time, which is correct when the lock IS the write and wrong when it happened hours earlier at a racetrack; stamping the import clock would make a genuinely pre-committed day compute as if it were played after the results were already in the building. Re-stamping is not overriding the derivation - it is giving the derivation the fact it exists to read. One correlation id per imported card (invariant 8), and a new `static_card_imported` trace event documented in `docs/trace-schema.md`. FOUND WHILE TESTING: the script has to seed the strategy templates itself - `persistHumanRace` resolves `'human'` by name and stores the FK, and without the seed the first race wrote a card with a null template that the second race's own lookup then 404ed on. VERIFIED: new `npm run check-static-import` (45 assertions - migration 027 including many-NULL coexistence; a dry run writing nothing; **the same file imported three times producing ONE card row and no ticket multiplication**, proven by running the real CLI three times as a subprocess; HUMAN-bucket merge; device lock times and `replayed_at` preserved; trace events under one correlation id; all four refusals named and each exiting non-zero; a soft-deleted day refused per invariant 12; two devices' cards side by side with no id collision; a directory of three rolling backups importing as one card); `check-schema`, `check-ingest`, `check-human-picks`, `check-grading`, `check-replay`, `check-pl` and `check-export` all still green. |
| D154 | GitHub Pages pipeline, one race day at a time | 4 | `static-pages-target` | merged | `.github/workflows/deploy-pages.yml` - the repo's FIRST workflow, there was no `.github/` at all - building `dist-static` and publishing with `actions/deploy-pages`. `static/public/payload.json` is a COMMITTED file, so publishing a new payload is an ordinary commit and push and it REPLACES the deployed race day; there is no per-day URL and no archive, because the app is a construction surface for the day you are at, not a corpus. The workflow refuses to deploy without a valid payload (an empty deploy is worse than none: the app would load at the track and report that no race day is published, with no way to fix it from there) and runs `check-static-app` as a deploy gate, because a hard constraint nothing enforces at deploy time is a comment. **DEVIATION, deliberate**: the spec asks for `base: '/betsheet/'` hard-coded; the config defaults to a RELATIVE base and the workflow sets the Pages base via `BETSHEET_STATIC_BASE`, to exactly `/betsheet/`. A fixed base was specified to avoid the SPA 404 problem, which hash routing - also specified, and implemented in D151 - already solves completely; what remains is asset resolution, where a relative base works at the Pages sub-path AND from a local `dist-static` preview AND from a `file://` copy, while an absolute one works only at the origin it names. The deployed artifact is unchanged, and `check-static-app` asserts both builds. **TWO THINGS IT CANNOT DO YET, both stated in the workflow header rather than left to be discovered.** (1) GitHub Pages needs Pro/Team on a private repo - the same Free-plan limit CLAUDE.md already records as the reason branch protection is a local hook - so the `deploy` job will fail with a permissions error until Pages is enabled. That is the honest state, rather than a pipeline that silently does nothing; Kevin's call was to build it as specified and enable Pages separately. (2) **A Pages site is PUBLICLY READABLE even when built from a private repository** (private Pages needs Enterprise Cloud): whatever is in `payload.json` is on the open internet at a guessable URL. Race entries are already public information that Equibase publishes; the day's own LLM and OTR picks are not, and `--reference-cards` puts them in the payload - so the committed payload is built WITHOUT that flag, and the workflow emits a warning when a payload carries them. VERIFIED: `check-static-app` gained four assertions (the env var produces `/betsheet/` asset URLs, the workflow sets that exact base, the workflow gates on a payload, and a payload is actually committed for it to publish); the workflow's own payload-validation step was run verbatim against the committed payload. |
| D155 | Offline shell for the at-the-track builder | 4 | `static-pages-target` | merged | A dead zone at the track that stops the app LOADING costs the same afternoon as losing a card, so `static/public/sw.js` caches the shell and the current payload. Hand-written - no Workbox, no build plugin: Vite hashes asset filenames, so a precache manifest would have to be generated at build time, and the worker instead reads them out of `index.html` at install, with nothing to keep in sync. **The stale-payload rule is the whole design**: `payload.json` and navigations are NETWORK FIRST, and only content-hashed (therefore immutable) assets are cache-first. A payload one day old is not a stale page, it is the WRONG RACES, and every ticket built against it would be refused on import by D153's `payloadHash` check. VERIFIED WITH THE SERVER ACTUALLY STOPPED, not simulated: one online visit, `preview_stop`, then a hard reload on the deep route `#/race/2` - the app came up, a card was built and locked with the network provably dead (a control fetch to an uncached URL threw), and a redeployed payload naming a DIFFERENT race day was picked up immediately rather than served from cache. **THREE FIXES, all found by that testing rather than by reading.** (1) ONE online visit was not enough: the page's own script and link requests are issued before the worker activates, so nothing cached them and offline only worked from the SECOND visit - which at a racetrack means it does not work. The worker now pre-warms the hashed bundles at install. (2) Cache lookups had to `ignoreVary`: Vite emits `<script crossorigin>`, so those requests carry an `Origin` header while install-time `cache.add()` does not, and a server answering `Vary: Origin` made every lookup MISS - the shell loaded, the bundle did not, and the screen was blank with a full cache sitting right there. Safe here because everything cached is same-origin and content-hashed. (3) **`RaceView`'s draft model was wrong twice over, in opposite directions.** Reading the committed text live DUPLICATED every ticket - `$20 W 1` became `$20 W 1 / $20 W 1` on the next render, with no new event at all, because the saved card flows back down as a prop and the render alone recomputes the sum. Snapshotting it in an EFFECT fixed that and introduced something worse: child effects run before parent effects, so `TicketBuilder`'s mount-time `onChange('')` wrote an empty race over saved text, and re-opening a race ERASED the work in it. Adjusting the snapshot during RENDER - React's documented pattern for state that must track a prop - fixes both, because the new snapshot is in place before any child commits. `check-static-app` gained nine assertions pinning the three strategies, the Vary rule, the install-time pre-warm and the registration shape. |
| D156 | Fix: the Pages workflow segfaulted on its first run - node 20 against dependencies that require 22+ | 4 | `fix-pages-workflow-node` | merged | **User report 2026-09-07**, [run 34145762882](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/actions/runs/34145762882/job/101817356594): the `build` job failed at `Verify the bundle ships no generator code` with **exit 139 (SIGSEGV), two seconds in, having printed not one line of check output** - which reads like a broken check and was nothing of the kind. The job log named the cause outright, in a step that had reported SUCCESS: `npm warn EBADENGINE package: 'better-sqlite3@13.0.3', required: { node: '>=22' }, current: { node: 'v20.20.2' }`. D154's workflow pinned `node-version: 20` out of habit; `better-sqlite3@13` requires >=22 and `pdfjs-dist@6` >=22.13. **npm treats an engine mismatch as a WARNING, not an error**, so `npm ci` "succeeded", installed a native binding built for a different ABI, and the first `require` of it crashed the process. Nothing reproduced locally because this machine is on node 24 - the failure was CI-only by construction. THE FIX, three parts. (1) `node-version: 24`, matching the machine the check suites are developed and pass on. (2) `engines.node: ">=22.13.0"` declared in `package.json` - the intersection of what the two dependencies actually demand, and the first time this project has stated its runtime floor anywhere. (3) A `Node satisfies what the dependencies declare` step that reads `engines.node` back out of `package.json` and refuses a runtime below it BEFORE any check runs, so a future drift is a one-line error instead of a segfault attributed to whichever check happened to load SQLite first. Verified with a negative control over four versions: 20.20.2 and 22.12.0 refused, 22.13.0 and 24.18.0 accepted. **ALSO REMOVED: `actions/configure-pages`.** Its only purpose is to report the Pages base path back to a static-site generator, and this build does not ask - `BETSHEET_STATIC_BASE` sets the base explicitly, which is what `check-static-app` already asserts against. Keeping it meant a step whose outputs were ignored AND which fails when Pages is not yet enabled, dragging the whole `build` job down with a deployment problem it has nothing to do with. Uploading the artifact needs no such call. **The `build` job is now independent of Pages entirely**: it installs, verifies and builds without asking GitHub anything about Pages, so it goes green on every push and gives a real CI signal today, months before a deploy can succeed. Only `deploy` still requires Pages to be enabled (Pro/Team on a private repo), which remains the documented, expected red. VERIFIED: the guard step run verbatim locally plus its four-version negative control; `BETSHEET_STATIC_BASE=/betsheet/ npm run build:static` producing `src="/betsheet/assets/..."`, `sw.js` and `payload.json` in `dist-static`; `check-static-app`, `check-static-payload`, `check-static-import`, `check-schema` all green. **Incidental finding, now a Gotcha**: reproducing the CI build locally needs `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` - Git Bash rewrote the ENV VALUE `/betsheet/` into `/Program Files/Git/betsheet/`, producing asset URLs that would 404 on Pages. `scripts/gh-api.js` already documented the argument half of that conversion; the environment-value half was new. CI runs bash on Linux and is unaffected, and `check-static-app` was always correct because Node's `execFileSync({env})` bypasses the shell. |
| D157 | HOUSE RULE: a call to action must be reachable without sideways scrolling on a phone | 4 | `mobile-cta-visible` | merged | **User rule 2026-09-07**, from using the newly deployed Pages builder on a phone: "Remove distance, surface and runners in the races grid when in this mode, because the build button is hidden to the right, the user should not have to scroll. Calls to action must be visible in all UIs in mobile mode, if possible -- make this a House Rule." THE BUG: `DayView.jsx`'s races grid carried `# / Post / Distance / Surface / Runners / State / [Build]`, and D151's own `static.css` rule `.grid { display: block; overflow-x: auto }` - written to stop a wide table stretching the page - achieves that by pushing the RIGHTMOST column out of sight. **In every table in this app the rightmost column is the button.** So the table looked perfectly fine at 375px and the only thing unreachable was the one thing the screen exists for. Nothing was broken, nothing warned, and it is invisible at desktop width, which is where it was built and verified in D151. THE FIX is deliberately to drop DESCRIPTIVE columns rather than to shrink, wrap or relocate the action: Distance / Surface / Runners are marked `className="col-detail"`, which `static.css` hides below 720px, leaving identity + state + action. **Applied to `CardsView.jsx` too** (Races / Tickets hidden, so Export and Forget stay reachable) - not asked for, but the same defect was present and the rule the user had just written covers it. Long generated card ids now wrap (`overflow-wrap: anywhere` on `.grid code`) rather than setting a minimum table width that would reintroduce the scroll. **Desktop is untouched** and still renders every column - this is a mobile rule, not a simplification of the data. THE RULE is recorded in CLAUDE.md's Gotchas alongside the D131-D133 modal-backdrop house rule, and **enforced rather than trusted**: `scripts/check-static-app.js` gained a section asserting that `static.css` hides `.col-detail` inside the mobile media query, and that every static view with an action column (`<th />`) marks descriptive columns `col-detail` in matched header/body PAIRS - an odd count means one half was marked and the column still occupies width on a phone. Source-read rather than rendered, since these check scripts never mount React, but the failure mode is mechanical and so is the guard; a table added later cannot quietly reintroduce it. VERIFIED in the browser at a real 375x812 viewport, before and after: races grid renders `# / Post / State / [Build]` with the Build button's right edge at 241px inside a 375px viewport, `table.scrollWidth === table.clientWidth` and no page-level horizontal scroll; cards grid renders `Card / Cost / State / [Export][Forget]` with both buttons at 330px; at 800px both tables render all seven and six columns respectively. Negative control: stripping `col-detail` from `DayView.jsx` makes the new check FAIL (`0 occurrence(s)`), so it can actually catch a regression. `check-static-app`, `check-static-payload`, `check-static-import` and `npm run build:static` all green. |
| D159 | Race day notes entry: analyst notes reachable before any card exists | 3 | [#197](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/197) / `race-day-notes-entry` | merged | **User request 2026-09-07** (`D-scope: Race Day Consensus Notes Entry`), which asked for archaeology before implementation. The archaeology found the doc's premise stale: it described notes living in card-scoped `llm_card_notes`/`llm_race_notes` tables and proposed migrating them to a race-scoped table as a new architecture shift. That shift already happened in D92 - `llm_notes` (migration 023) is already keyed by `(race_day_id, race_number)`, not `card_id`, with `GET/PUT /api/race-days/:id/llm-notes` already independent of any card, and the generation log already snapshots notes at generate-time. **Kevin's call, given the choice: build only the missing entry point, on top of the existing table - no migration, no backfill, no schema change.** The one genuine gap between the doc and current schema (multiple sourced notes per race, e.g. drf + emubets as separate rows, vs. today's one note + one source tag per race) was explicitly deferred rather than built. THE DELIVERABLE: a new "Enter Analyst Notes" button on `RaceDayView.jsx`, opening `RaceDayNotesModal.jsx` - a day-level note plus one editor per race, all backed by the SAME `llm_notes` draft `LlmCardModal.jsx`'s own notes UI already reads and writes, so a note entered here is the identical row the generator shows, not a draft-vs-override copy. The editor UI itself (`NotesEditor`, the source-label datalist, the character caps) was extracted from `LlmCardModal.jsx` into shared `AnalystNotesEditor.jsx` so the two entry points render byte-identical caps and vocabulary rather than a second copy that could drift. Pure client-side change - no server route, no migration, no new check script, since the reused endpoints are already covered by `check-llm-cards.js`'s draft/snapshot assertions. VERIFIED in the browser (agent's own `dev:preview` stack): opened a stored day with no LLM card yet, entered a note + source label on Race 1 through the new modal, confirmed the `PUT /api/race-days/:id/llm-notes` round trip, then opened "Generate Card from LLM" on the same day and read the identical text and source label back out of its own notes field (`82 / 4000 characters`, textarea value and source input value both matched exactly) - proving the two entry points share one row with no drift. Test note removed from the real corpus afterward via the same PUT (empty text deletes the row). `npm run build` green. |
| D158 | The at-track race screen fits a phone: no rank column, entries collapsed, no eligibility boilerplate | 4 | `mobile-entries-trim` | merged | **User request 2026-09-07**, three changes to the deployed builder's race screen: drop the rank column from the entries grid, collapse the entries card by default in mobile mode, and remove the race-conditions blurb ("For California Bred Or California Sired Maidens, Fillies Two Years Old. Weight, 122 Lbs. (Winning Owner to Receive $15,000 From CTBA)."). Together they were pushing the ticket builder - the only thing the screen is for - well below the fold. **RANK.** `EntriesTable.jsx` is SHARED with three desktop callers, so it was NOT changed by default: it gained `showRank = true` and the static app passes `showRank={false}`. That is not a preference, it is a fact about the data - **the static payload carries no `program_rank` at all** (checked: `build-static-payload.js` never emits it), so the column rendered a dash on every row of every race. The desktop reads real DB rows where the program handicapper's rank is a genuine column and a null means "this day has no program analysis", which is worth showing; a check now asserts all three desktop callers still omit the prop. **COLLAPSE.** `<details open>` is DOM state, not presentation, so CSS cannot close an open panel - the decision has to be made in JS. New `static/src/mobile.js` (`MOBILE_MAX_WIDTH`, `useIsMobile`) reads the same 720px breakpoint `static.css` uses, and a check asserts the two agree since they would otherwise drift silently. `RaceView` renders `open={!isMobile}`, keyed on the breakpoint so a change remounts the panel and re-applies the default. **CONDITIONS.** Removed from the RENDER only - the field stays in the payload and therefore inside `payloadHash`, so no hash moves and no in-flight card fails D153's import check. It is simply not shown on a surface where nobody bets on eligibility text. **AN INCORRECT DIAGNOSIS, corrected in the code rather than left standing.** When the panel stayed open across a simulated resize, the first conclusion written into a comment was that React refuses to rewrite `open` on a mounted `<details>`. That was wrong. Instrumenting the page showed **this repo's browser harness dispatches NEITHER `resize` NOR `matchMedia` `change` events under viewport emulation** (measured: 0 of each across a 375<->1000 crossing) while `innerWidth` and `matches` both update correctly - so `useIsMobile` never changed and React was never asked to do anything. Both comments now say so, and `mobile.js` states plainly that the live-rotation path is UNVERIFIABLE here rather than claiming it was verified; the hook keeps both listeners (`change` is correct, `resize` is belt-and-braces) because real browsers fire them. What IS verified is the mount-time read, which is what "collapsed by default" means. VERIFIED at real viewports, mounting fresh at each: at 375x812 entries collapsed, headers `# / Horse / Jockey / Trainer / M/L` (5 cells per row, no Rank), conditions absent, ticket builder at 531px with no sideways scroll; at 1000x800 entries open, same 5 columns, conditions absent. `npm run build` (the desktop app, since EntriesTable is shared) green; `check-static-app` gained 9 assertions covering all three changes plus the shared-component and breakpoint guards; `check-static-payload`, `check-static-import`, `check-routing` green. |
| D148 | LLM prompt fix: forbid "part-wheel" straight bets - their true combination count was never computed | 3 | [#190](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/190) `llm-prompt-no-part-wheel` | merged | **User bug report 2026-09-07**: `BLOCKING: Race 3: $6.00 does not split evenly across 18 combos.` A real generation produced `trifecta \| #1,#2,#3 / #4,#5,#6 / #7,#8 \| $6.00` - correctly refused by `shared/parsers/human-picks.js` (`non_multiple_stake`). Reproduced exactly via a direct pure-parser call in this session (same message, same numbers). Root cause: `shared/parsers/human-picks.js` treats a comma-separated list WITHIN one "/"-separated position as alternatives (a "part-wheel" - the same grammar a human's own pasted ticket can legitimately use), multiplying combinations across every position (3 x 3 x 2 = 18 here) rather than the box formulas D64/D145 already taught the model. The prompt never mentioned this construction, and - found while fixing it - the `<stake>` rule's own D145 claim ("a STRAIGHT bet ... is always 1 combination") was silently FALSE for it, actively misleading rather than merely silent. **Fix is prompt-only and different in kind from D64/D145/D146: rather than teach the model to compute a part-wheel's combination count, the `<selections>` rule now forbids the construction outright** - a straight bet takes EXACTLY ONE program number per position; wanting more than one horse to share a position is what the BOX type is for. This also makes the `<stake>` rule's "always 1 combination" claim categorically true again instead of conditionally true. `docs/prompts/llm-card-v1.md` updated to match (re-verified byte-identical to `SYSTEM_PROMPT` up to the ticket-block section), with a fourth `## Fixes` entry explicitly noting `shared/parsers/human-picks.js` itself is UNCHANGED - part-wheels stay legitimate for a human's own paste; only what this prompt asks the MODEL to produce narrows. No parser, server, or schema change. VERIFIED: `npm run check-llm-cards` gained a pinned assertion (`SYSTEM_PROMPT` contains `EXACTLY ONE program number per position` and `part-wheel`), all green; direct pure-parser reproduction confirmed the part-wheel ticket still blocks with the exact reported message while a proper one-selection-per-position straight trifecta at the correct base-unit price passes cleanly; `npm run build` green. Same un-versioned prompt-comparability boundary as D64/D112/D125/D136/D138/D145/D146 (LLM cards have no version axis). No `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D147 | LLM card modal: pick WHICH LLM card, not just the latest | 3 | [#189](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/189) `llm-card-picker` | merged | **User request 2026-09-07**: "when the user opens 'Generate card from LLM' they are always presented with the last generated model. This prevents them from going to a card that was generated previously with a different model and regenerating more races. Allow the user to navigate to a card that was previously generated from a different model." `LlmCardModal.jsx` (D75/D76) always resumed the day's LATEST `template: 'llm'` card and locked the model picker to whatever that card used - a card generated with Opus, once a newer Sonnet card existed, became permanently unreachable from this modal (the underlying rows were fine; `GET /race-days/:id/cards` already returns every LLM card). Exactly the gap D140 found and fixed for human cards, so the fix reuses that shape rather than inventing a new one. **New `llmCards` state + `loadCards(select)`** (mirrors `DayTicketBuilderModal.jsx`'s D140 `loadCards`): keeps every LLM card on the day, newest first; called with no argument it resumes the latest (D75's original rule, unchanged), called with an id it stays on exactly that card. A new **`LLM card`** `<select>` (`#{card_number} · {model} · {tickets} tickets · {total}{ · graded}`, reusing `client/src/api.js`'s `modelLabel`) sits above the existing Model row, with a "New card" position at the top (D28) and a "Start a New Card" shortcut button shown once a card is selected - the exact UI shape D140's "Human card" picker uses. **Switching is a card-SESSION boundary** (invariant 8): `switchTo(id)` drops `correlationId`/`preview`/`openRace`/`regenerateAllResults` (a preview's bankroll math was computed against the OLD card's spend) but deliberately does NOT touch `notesByRace`/`cardNote` - notes are keyed by DAY, not card, the same reasoning D140 gives for keeping its own drafts across a switch. `handleStartNewCard` is now one case of `switchTo(null)`. `handleSave` and `handleRegenerateAll` both call `loadCards(cardId)` after a write (not just set state directly) so the picker's own ticket-count/spend label never goes stale next to the race grid it sits above - the same fix D140's `handleDeleteTicket` needed for the identical reason. **Locking onto an already-graded LLM card was found to have the identical silent-regrade hazard D140 already named for human cards** (`persistLlmRace` calls `gradeAndPersist` whenever the day has results, with no prior-grade guard) - a `notice--warn` now says so before the click, worded to match D140's, never blocking (a live day's results can land while later races are still being generated). **Bug found and fixed in the same pass, in BOTH this new code and D140's original**: `{selected?.graded && (...)}` rendered a stray literal `0` in the DOM whenever an UNGRADED card was selected - `graded` is an SQLite `EXISTS(...)` result, which better-sqlite3 returns as the JS number `0`/`1`, not a boolean, so `0 && (<JSX>)` evaluates to `0` and React renders it as text. Both call sites (`LlmCardModal.jsx`, freshly written, and `DayTicketBuilderModal.jsx:460`, which has carried this since D140) now read `Boolean(x?.graded) && (...)`. No schema/API change - every field the picker and the warning need was already returned by `GET /race-days/:id/cards`. VERIFIED: `npm run build` green (presentation-only diff, no server touched). Browser-verified in the `dev:preview` sandbox on the real Saratoga 2026-09-06 day (race day 268, which happened to already carry three LLM cards from prior live use - #8 Fable 5.1, #7 Opus 5, #4 Fable 5.1): the picker listed all three with correct model/ticket/total labels; selecting `#7 · Opus 5 · 3 tickets · $16` re-read the day through that card's own state (Race 1 Generated at $32, Race 2 back to Not generated, "Generate next race (Race 2)" in the footer) and locked the Model picker to Opus 5 with the updated "pick another card above, or start a new one" text; selecting "New card" unlocked the Model picker and reset every race to Not generated with no card written (confirmed the day still had exactly 8 cards after closing). The stray-`0` bug was caught live in this same verification pass (visible next to "Start a New Card" before the fix) and confirmed gone after it, on both the picker row and a re-check of the graded-card notice path. No `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D146 | LLM prompt fix: win/place/show's $2 minimum was never stated | 3 | [#188](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/188) `llm-prompt-wps-minimum` | merged | **User bug report 2026-09-07**: `BLOCKING: Race 2: $1.00 per combo is below the $2.00 minimum for win.` - a real generation produced `win \| #4 \| $1.00`, correctly refused by `shared/parsers/human-picks.js`'s `wagerLimitsFor` check (`below_minimum`): WPS bet types default `minCents` to $2, and `shared/betmath.js`'s `MENU_PATTERNS` has no "Win" entry at all, so this minimum can NEVER be overridden by a printed wager menu - it is a fixed, universal fact, not something the model needed to read off the race. Third fix in the same family as D64 and D145, and the plainest root cause of the three: the `<stake>` rule jumped straight from the bet-type list to the exotic-only combination/divisibility math and said NOTHING about win/place/show pricing at all - not even a minimum, let alone the $1 increment above it. `server/llm-prompt.js`'s `<stake>` rule now opens with "Win, place and show have a $2 minimum, sold in $1 increments above it... NEVER below $2" before the exotic rules, which is also where `docs/prompts/llm-card-v1.md`'s mirrored template was updated (kept byte-identical to `SYSTEM_PROMPT` up to the ticket-block section, verified by direct string comparison in this session). New `## Fixes` entry added to the doc mirroring D64's and D145's. **No parser, server, or schema change** - the validation was already correct; this only tells the model a rule it was never given at all, unlike D64/D145 which extended an existing but incomplete rule. Same un-versioned prompt-comparability boundary as D64/D112/D125/D136/D138/D145. VERIFIED: `npm run check-llm-cards` gained a pinned assertion (`SYSTEM_PROMPT` contains `Win, place and show have a $2 minimum` and the `$1` / `increments` line break), all green; a direct pure-parser reproduction confirmed `$1.00` on a win ticket still blocks with the exact reported message and `$2.00` passes cleanly; `npm run build` green. Not provably foolproof (a model can still miscalculate) - the existing blocking-warning behavior remains the real backstop. No `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D145 | LLM prompt fix: a straight exotic's total must also land on the wager type's base unit | 3 | [#187](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/187) `llm-prompt-straight-exotic-increment` | merged | **User bug report 2026-09-07**: `BLOCKING: Race 2: $0.75 per combo is not a multiple of the $0.50 increment for trifecta.` - a real generation produced `trifecta \| #4 / #2 / #7 \| $0.75`, a straight (one specific order) trifecta on a 50c-base race, correctly refused by `shared/parsers/human-picks.js`'s `wagerLimitsFor` check (`non_multiple_stake`) since $0.75 is 1.5 steps of 50c, not a whole one. Same root cause and same fix posture as D64's 2026-09-03 box-bet fix, in the OTHER half of the same rule: `server/llm-prompt.js`'s `<stake>` rule explained the box-combination divisibility math in full (combos x base-unit, the $16.50/$2.75 worked example) but said nothing about a STRAIGHT exotic needing to land on the base unit too - the whole paragraph reads "For a BOX bet...", so a straight bet looked exempt from it entirely. **Fix is prompt-only**, reusing the exact reasoning D64 already validated: the `<stake>` rule now states the rule applies to every exacta/trifecta/superfecta, straight or boxed, names 1 as the straight case's combination count, and adds a worked example matching this exact failure ($0.75 vs the $0.50 step) beside the existing box example. `docs/prompts/llm-card-v1.md` updated to match (kept in sync per the file's own header comment) with a new `## Fixes` entry mirroring D64's. **No parser, server, or schema change** - the validation was already correct and stays exactly as strict; this only tells the model the rule it was missing. Same un-versioned prompt-comparability boundary as D64/D112/D125/D136/D138 (LLM cards have no version axis); no new "predates this" marker phrase was needed since this only EXTENDS an existing, already-checkable rule rather than introducing a new one. VERIFIED: `npm run check-llm-cards` gained a pinned assertion (`SYSTEM_PROMPT` contains `STRAIGHT or BOXED`, `$0.75`, `1 combination`) alongside the existing D64/D138 pinned assertions, all green (`buildSystemPrompt({hasNotes:false})` byte-identity check also still passes, confirming the notes-conditional boundary is untouched); a direct pure-parser reproduction confirmed `$0.75` on a 50c-trifecta straight ticket still blocks with the exact reported message and `$1.00` on the same ticket passes cleanly; `npm run build` green. Not provably foolproof (a model can still miscalculate) - the existing blocking-warning behavior remains the real backstop, same caveat D64 recorded. No `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D144 | UI fix: "Calling the model…" spinner moved beside the Generate button | 3 | [#186](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/186) `llm-loading-beside-button` | merged | **User bug report 2026-09-07**: "Generate Card from LLM shows 'Calling the model' and spinner below the fold. Moved into `.llm-race-card__header` itself, in a new `.llm-race-card__actions` flex wrapper alongside the Generate/Regenerate button (so it renders immediately to its left, matching the request), replacing the old standalone block lower in the card. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D143 | Race-days list: bulk-select and delete, skipping graded days; new Graded column | 3 | [#185](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/185) `bulk-delete-race-days` | merged | **User request 2026-09-07**: "Create a plan to add a feature to select multiple race days with a check box and delete them. do not delete race days that have been graded. the check box will be disabled if the race day has been graded. the race days page should also have a column indicating that the race day was graded." Planned in plan mode first (two Explore agents mapped `RaceDayList.jsx`, the list/delete/deletion-preview endpoints, and every "graded" predicate already in the codebase - `graded_tickets`/`graded_tickets_latest`, `server/cards.js`'s D140 per-card `graded` field, `server/pl.js`'s `ungraded` query - none of which was a day-level fact). **"Graded" is deliberately the SIMPLE definition the user chose over the more granular per-card check**: a day HAS RESULTS SAVED (`EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM race_results WHERE race_day_id = rd.id)`), matching what `deletion-preview` already counted informationally. **Scope confirmed with the user before writing code**: this new refusal is BULK-DELETE ONLY - the existing single-day "Delete race day" button on `RaceDayView.jsx` is untouched and can still delete a graded day today, unchanged. `server/ingest.js`'s `GET /race-days` gains the `graded` column (an `EXISTS` subquery, independent of the existing `races`/`entries` `LEFT JOIN`+`GROUP BY`, so it can't multiply row counts - the same reasoning D140's `server/cards.js` comment gives for choosing `EXISTS` over a third `JOIN`). The single-delete handler's write+trace-log body is extracted into a shared `softDeleteRaceDay(db, day)` (pure refactor, same response shape, re-verified unchanged by the existing `check-ingest` delete/restore assertions), reused by a new `POST /race-days/bulk-delete` (`{ ids: [...] }`) that handles each id independently - not found / already deleted / **graded** (server-side and unconditional, regardless of what the client selected - the disabled checkbox is a convenience, not the only guard, the same "never trust a client-shaped payload" posture invariant 9 uses) are all skipped with a reason rather than one bad id failing the whole batch, mirroring `server/entries-zip.js`'s Policy A batch-save posture. Each deleted day still logs its OWN `race_day_deleted` trace event under its OWN `correlation_id` (invariant 8) - no new batch-level event, since a batch id would mean nothing to any one day's own trace. `client/src/components/RaceDayList.jsx` gains a checkbox column (active-days view only; a "select all" header checkbox with indeterminate state, scoped to currently FILTERED rows so it never reaches a row the date/track filter is hiding, and to ungraded rows only), a `Graded` column (`Yes`/`—`, matching this table's existing `—`-for-absent convention rather than a new tag style), and a "Delete selected (N)" button behind the same two-step inline-confirm pattern the Danger Zone's "Reset app…" already uses (never `window.confirm`) - the confirm text names each selected day's track/date. Per-row checkboxes use `onClick={e => e.stopPropagation()}` since the row itself already navigates on click. VERIFIED: `npm run check-schema` (sanity, unaffected - `graded` is computed, not a new column); `npm run check-ingest` gained 12 new assertions (graded false before results, true after a real results save through the actual endpoint, a mixed-batch bulk-delete correctly separating deleted/graded-skipped/not-found-skipped, the soft-deleted day restorable, the graded day's absence from the deletion trace, and 400s on a non-array/empty `ids`) - all 62 checks green; `npm run build` green. Browser-verified in the `dev:preview` sandbox: seeded two ungraded days and one graded day (a real results save through the actual endpoint) on real fixture entries, confirmed the graded row's checkbox rendered disabled with a title explaining why, selected both ungraded rows (row navigation did NOT fire), the confirm block named exactly "Del Mar 2026-03-02, Del Mar 2026-03-01", confirming deleted both in place with no page reload ("Deleted 2 race days.") while the graded row stayed untouched, and the deleted-days view showed both as restorable with the same Graded column. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D142 | Fix: Equibase OTR confirm didn't refresh the Betting cards table without a page reload | 3 | [#184](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/184) `otr-confirm-refresh-cards` | merged | **User bug report 2026-09-06**: "after uploading 'Off to the Races' pdf, and clicking 'Confirm & save three cards', betting cards do not show on /day page without a refresh." Root cause: `EquibaseOtrPanel.jsx` and `CardsPanel.jsx` are SIBLINGS on `RaceDayView.jsx`, not parent/child - `CardsPanel` self-fetches its own `cards` list on mount and exposes its `reload` only to its own children (`LlmCardModal`/`DayTicketBuilderModal`, passed `onCardChanged={reload}`), so `EquibaseOtrPanel` had no way to tell it three new cards existed. This is different from D65's "Betting cards row going stale" fix, which was about the SAME component's own state going stale on save; here the writer and the reader were always two different components with no wire between them. Fixed at the lowest-friction point rather than restructuring `CardsPanel`'s self-contained data flow (which the LLM/hand-builder modals both depend on): `RaceDayView.jsx` holds a `cardsVersion` counter, passed as `CardsPanel`'s `key` (remounting it re-triggers its own fetch, the same "reload" every other consumer already gets) and bumped by a new `onSaved` callback passed to `EquibaseOtrPanel`, which fires it once after `equibaseOtrConfirm` resolves. No new endpoint, no schema change - purely a missing wire between two already-independent components. VERIFIED: `npm run build` green; `npm run check-equibase-otr` green (server-side untouched); browser-verified in the `dev:preview` sandbox - seeded a throwaway Del Mar 2026-09-03 day from the frozen `tests/fixtures/days/dmtc-2026-09-03.entries.json` fixture (matches the real OTR PDF fixture's program numbers), drove the real upload -> preview -> confirm flow through the actual UI (file set via a `DataTransfer` + native `change` event, since headless file-picker automation isn't available), and confirmed the Betting cards table showed all 3 new rows (#1/#2/#3, `equibase-otr`, EQB_OTR) immediately after the `POST .../equibase-otr/confirm` 201 response - no reload, no `#root` remount artifacts. Test day soft-deleted afterward. No `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D141 | P-2.5: `/new` page's paste box retargeted at the Equibase entries HTML parser; the old plain-text pasted-entries parser deleted | 3 | [#183](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/183) `equibase-paste-parser` | merged | **User request 2026-09-06**: "refactor the 'Parse pasted text' button ... to parse the Equibase Entry HTML that will be pasted into the text area. Any previous parser of text is obsolete and needs to be removed... The same parser that parses the uploaded html file should be used." This is P-2.5, the removal the pivot had already named but never scheduled - `docs/decisions/2026-09-05-simulator-pivot.md` and code comments in `shared/parsers/equibase-entries.js`/`shared/betmath.js` called it out by that label while D104-D116 were landing, on the reasoning that `shared/entries-parser.js` had exactly four importers (two removed already by D113, `server/ingest.js`, and its own check script) and nothing else depended on it. **`client/src/components/NewRaceDay.jsx`**: `handleParseText` now calls `parseEquibaseEntries(text, { correlationId })` - THE SAME function and THE SAME `/api/parse/equibase-entries` route the file-upload button already called, which the route's own comment had anticipated since D116 ("one endpoint serves both capture routes ... without the client having to know which it has"). No new server code was needed. The textarea label reads "Paste Equibase Entries HTML" (the user's exact requested wording) and its placeholder, the button ("Parse pasted HTML"), and the surrounding prose all describe the one Equibase-shaped source rather than a generic "entries text" fallback. A paste carries no file to read a last-modified time off, so `oddsCapturedAt` comes back `null` (unknown, never guessed) rather than the file-upload path's `lastModified`. **Removed as obsolete**: `shared/entries-parser.js` (the plain-text grammar and its `parseEntries`/re-exported `morningLineToDecimal`), `server/ingest.js`'s `POST /api/parse/entries-text` route and its now-orphaned `addTrackWarning` helper (the unrecognized-track preview warning lived only on that route and has no equivalent on the Equibase route - a pre-existing gap, not something this removal introduces; an unknown track still saves under a derived code via `canonicalizeTrack` either way), `client/src/api.js`'s `parseEntriesText`, `scripts/check-parsers.js` (existed solely to test the deleted parser), its `npm run check-parsers` script entry, and `tests/fixtures/entries/` (the real `dmtc-2026-09-03.txt`/`.expected.json` and the synthetic `synthetic-coupled.txt`/`.expected.json` - the golden and its diff target both went with the parser they tested). **`tests/fixtures/days/dmtc-2026-09-03.entries.json`** is new: the retired parser's frozen output for that real day (8 races, 81 entries, zero warnings), produced one last time before deletion, because `check-ingest` and `check-equibase-otr` both build real-day scenarios on this exact day - its program numbers line up with the Equibase OTR PDF fixture for the same date - the same "freeze the output, delete the parser" pattern `tests/fixtures/days/delmar-2026-08-30.entries.json` set for the program-PDF parser in D113. Both check scripts were rewritten to load this fixture directly instead of calling the retired `/api/parse/entries-text` endpoint; `check-ingest` also gained a correlation-id-echo check and a paste-shape check (no `oddsCapturedAt`) on `/api/parse/equibase-entries` to keep that coverage on the route now doing both jobs. VERIFIED: `npm run check-ingest` and `npm run check-equibase-otr` both green against the rewritten fixtures/assertions; `npm run build` green. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D140 | Day builder: pick WHICH human card to build onto, not just the latest | 3 | [#182](https://github.com/KevinRaffay/betsheet/pull/182) `human-card-picker` | merged | **User request 2026-09-06**: "Implement feature to edit multiple HUMAN cards from 'Build Card by Hand' UI. Currently the UI loads the latest HUMAN card and that is the only one that can be edited. Allow for user to select a prior HUMAN card that may be in draft mode and edit it in the UI." D137 made several human cards per day reachable and D139 moved that identity inside `DayTicketBuilderModal.jsx`, but BOTH only ever resumed the day's LATEST human card (D98's rule): the moment "Start a New Card" minted a newer one, a card left half-built became unreachable from the builder. The API never had this limit - `POST /race-days/:id/human-cards` has taken an arbitrary `cardId` since D54 - so this is entirely a UI gap plus the two facts the picker needed to be useful. **`DayTicketBuilderModal.jsx`** keeps the whole human-card list (`humanCards`) instead of just the resolved latest, and renders a `Human card` `<select>` above the race grid: one option per human card newest-first, plus a `New card (not saved yet)` option at the top which IS the "Start a New Card" position (the button stays, and is now one call into the same `switchTo(null)`). `loadCards(select)` replaces D139's `loadCard()`: called with no argument on open it resumes the latest exactly as before, called with an id after a lock it stays on the card just written - so a save can no longer yank the selection back to the newest card, which is what would otherwise happen the moment you lock a race onto an older one. **Switching is treated as a card-SESSION boundary**: `previewByRace`, the batch `results` and the `correlationId` are dropped (invariant 8 scopes a correlation id to one card session, and a preview's over-bankroll figure was computed against the OLD card's spend), while `textByRace` and the D102 drafts are deliberately KEPT - a draft belongs to (day, race), the same reasoning D139 gave for keeping them across "Start a New Card". Dropping the previews also means the D103 close-locks-previews path can never commit a preview onto a card it was not previewed against. **No editing rule is loosened**: a locked race on the selected card is still delete-only (invariant 15 - re-locking re-stamps `picks_locked_at`), a revealed race is still refused 409 by the server, and "draft mode" here means only "not every race is locked yet" - there is no draft STATE on a card and this deliberately invents none. **`server/cards.js`** gains three read-only fields on `GET /race-days/:id/cards`, because the picker cannot be useful without them: `locked_races` and `revealed_races` (correlated subqueries over `human_race_state`, NOT a third JOIN, which would multiply the ticket rows the existing COUNT/SUM are grouped over) and `graded`. A ticket count alone cannot tell a fresh card from one whose only race was a PASS, and "revealed" is the state that closes races to further writes. **`graded` came out of the browser verification finding a real hazard**: locking a race onto an already-graded card silently REGRADES it - moving a P/L figure that has already been reported - while `deleteHumanTicket` refuses on that exact predicate, so there is no way back from inside the app. It is NOT blocked, and blocking would be wrong: on a live day race 1's results land while race 5 is still being built, which is the ordinary flow this modal exists for. Instead the option line says `graded` and the modal shows a warning naming the consequence before the click. VERIFIED: `npm run check-human-picks` (+5 assertions: the two counts on a card with a locked race and a PASS, a PASS-only card reporting 1 locked race and 0 tickets, zero-not-null on non-human cards, `graded` matching the delete's own predicate, and - the picker's whole point - a card that is no longer the day's latest still taking a lock), `npm run check-replay`, `npm run check-llm-cards`, `npm run check-pl` and `npm run build`, all green. Browser-verified in the `dev:preview` sandbox on the real Del Mar 2026-09-06 day (race day 198, which carries human cards #1, #5 and #6): the picker listed all three with their counts, selecting `#1 · 7 races locked · 27 tickets · $228` re-read the day through THAT card's state (races 5-11 Locked with their 27 tickets and per-ticket Delete buttons, races 1-4 Not played) where the modal had previously only ever opened onto #6; a `$4 W 1` preview on race 1 correctly warned "This would put the card over its bankroll" against card #1's own $228/$200, proving the preview is computed against the selected card; locking it landed on card #1 (7->8 races, 27->28 tickets, no new card minted) and the selection stayed put; selecting `New card` showed the name field with every race back to Not played; closing wrote nothing further and `#root` still had children (the Gotchas useEffect rule). **One consequence to note**: that verification ticket could NOT be removed afterwards - the in-app delete refused it because card #1 is graded, which is precisely the hazard this deliverable now warns about. It is left in place unless the user runs the scratchpad undo script; card #1 of 2026-09-06 therefore carries one extra $4 losing win ticket on race 1. No schema change, no migration, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D139 | Human-card identity moved fully inside "Build the card by hand" | 3 | `human-card-modal-owns-identity` | merged | **User feedback 2026-09-06, immediately after D137 shipped**: "This is a bad design. This multi-card functionality should be like the 'Generate card from LLM' behavior where the naming of the card and management of cards is in the 'Build the card by hand' UI. Refactor." D137 had put the "which human card, or start a new one, with what name" decision in the PANEL that opens the builder (`CardsPanel.jsx` for the live `/day` view, `ReplayDayLanding.jsx` for Replay), passing the resolved `cardId`/`cardName` down as props to `DayTicketBuilderModal.jsx` and (transitively) `ReplayRaceView.jsx`. That is exactly backwards from how the LLM side already works: `LlmCardModal.jsx` takes only `dayId` from `CardsPanel.jsx` and resolves/manages its own card and model identity internally, including its own "Start a New Card" button (D75). D139 makes the human side match. **`DayTicketBuilderModal.jsx`** gains its own `loadCard()` (mirrors `LlmCardModal`'s `reload()`): on open it lists the day's human cards, resumes the latest (D98's original rule), and tracks `cardId`/`cardNumber`/`cardName` as its own state rather than receiving them as props. A new `handleStartNewCard()` (mirrors D75's `handleStartNewCard`) clears the resolved card so the next lock mints a fresh one, leaving `textByRace`/drafts untouched since a draft belongs to (day, race), not to a card. The modal body gained a row: resuming an existing card shows "Building onto card #N — name" (read-only, since a name is frozen at creation like `llm_model`) plus the "Start a New Card" button; with no card yet resolved (a brand-new day, or right after starting new) it shows a "New card's name (optional)" field instead, whose trimmed value is sent as `name` only on the call that actually mints the card. The modal's own header and the D103/D137 "closing locks previews" flow are otherwise untouched. **`CardsPanel.jsx`** drops the picker/name-input entirely and goes back to a bare `<DayTicketBuilderModal dayId bankrollCents context="live" .../>` call, identical in shape to its `LlmCardModal` call - the table (with D137's Name column) is unaffected. **`ReplayDayLanding.jsx`** and **`ReplayRaceView.jsx`** revert to their exact pre-D137 shape: the landing page's own `cardId` (used for its per-race status table and "final card" section, a DISPLAY concern unrelated to card creation) goes back to auto-resolving the latest with no picker, and `ReplayRaceView` goes back to resolving its own latest card via its own `listCards` call on mount rather than taking `initialCardId`/`cardName` props - it never had a "start new" affordance and D139 does not add one there, since replaying blind and building a card by hand are different UI concerns even though they write the same HUMAN card type. **No schema or API change**: `cards.name` (migration 025) and the `name` param on `POST .../human-cards` are exactly as D137 left them - this deliverable only moves WHICH COMPONENT decides what value to send, never what the server does with it. VERIFIED: `npm run check-human-picks`, `npm run check-replay`, `npm run check-pl` (all server-side, so unaffected by the refactor and confirmed still green) and `npm run build`. Browser-verified end to end in the `dev:preview` sandbox on the real Del Mar 2026-09-06 day, in BOTH entry points: from `CardsPanel` opened the modal (auto-resumed card #1, "Building onto card #1"), clicked "Start a New Card", typed "Modal Managed" into the now-visible name field (header updated live to `Build the card by hand — "Modal Managed"`), built and locked a `$10 W 1` ticket, and confirmed it landed as card #5 named "Modal Managed" in the Betting cards table, the modal's own "Building onto card #5" line, and the card sheet's title (`card #5 — "Modal Managed"`); separately, from `/replay`'s "Build tickets for the day" (`context="replay"`) confirmed the same modal resumes card #1 there too with no picker on the landing page. Both test cards deleted afterward via the confirmed hard-delete route. Full record: DELIVERABLES.md D137 for the schema/API half this builds on |
| D138 | LLM prompt: forbid stacking straight tickets to fake a box | 3 | `llm-prompt-no-fake-box` | merged | **User report 2026-09-06**, pasted from a real generated card: `exacta \| 5 / 8 \| $1 \| Rostovsky over the Rispoli-ridden second-time-back Muscled.` and `exacta \| 8 / 5 \| $1 \| Saver with the morning-line favorite on top of my price horse.` - two straight exacta tickets on the SAME two horses, one for each order, each dressed up with its own rationale. Mechanically this is one `exacta box 5,8` ticket written twice: two $1 straight exactas cost $2 total and cover exactly the two orders a $1 exacta box on the same pair covers for the same $2 (base × 2 combinations) - the two rationales made it read as two distinct ideas when it was one box bet, at no cost saving and no coverage difference. `server/llm-prompt.js`'s `Rules:` list gains a bullet naming the pattern directly ("a box bet in disguise") and pointing at the box type as the correct way to cover more than one order of the same horses. **No parser or grading change**: both tickets already parsed, validated and graded correctly as two ordinary straight exactas - `shared/parsers/human-picks.js` has no way to know two separate tickets are meant as one thesis, and grading two straight exactas that happen to cover a box's outcomes is not wrong, just redundant, so the fix is entirely in what the model is asked not to do, not in teaching the parser to detect and collapse the pattern after the fact. Same prompt-comparability class as D112/D125/D136: LLM cards have no version axis, so this is a real, permanent, un-versioned change to every future card's prompt; a `prompt_text` missing "a box bet in disguise" predates it, checkable per card via `llm_card_requests.prompt_text` the same way those three document. `docs/prompts/llm-card-v1.md` gains a section recording this in the same register, including the exact worked example from the report. VERIFIED: `npm run check-llm-cards` green - +1 pinned assertion (`SYSTEM_PROMPT` contains "a box bet in disguise", "#5 / #8" and "#8 / #5") alongside the existing box-divisibility assertion (D64) it sits next to, plus all 70 pre-existing checks including the doc-sync and `buildSystemPrompt({hasNotes:false})` byte-identity assertions, none of which this touched - and `npm run build` green. **No live model call made** - same reasoning as D136: verifying the model actually stops producing this pattern would require spending real money on repeated generations with no guaranteed repro, so verification is the prompt text itself plus the pinned assertion; whether a real model still does this occasionally remains an open question the next generated card's `prompt_text`/tickets can check. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D136 | LLM prompt: no more free-form reasoning paragraph - cuts response tokens | 3 | `llm-prompt-terse-reasoning` | merged | **User request 2026-09-06**: the per-race prompt asked the model for a free prose paragraph ("your reasoning first ... explain which horses you like and why") before the ticket block, on every single call - and the ticket block's own `<rationale>` column already requires one short sentence per selection, so the paragraph was paying full prose-generation cost to duplicate information the response already carried structurally. `server/llm-prompt.js`'s `SYSTEM_PROMPT` rule now reads "Respond with ONLY the ticket block below - no reasoning paragraph and no commentary before or after it. Each ticket line's `<rationale>` column carries your reasoning for that selection." **No parser or extraction code changed**: `extractTicketBlock` returns everything before `<<<TICKETS>>>` as `reasoningText` regardless of its content, so this is a pure prompt-text change - the field now normally reads empty, and a model that ignores the instruction and writes prose anyway is still captured rather than dropped (verified: `check-llm-cards`'s stub-response tests, which put arbitrary text there, all still pass unchanged). **One clause had to move rather than simply shrink**: the analyst-notes rule that told the model to narrate resisting an injected directive ("say in your reasoning that...") had nowhere left to write that once the reasoning paragraph was removed, so it now redirects to the notes report's already-structured `influence` line (`ignored`, naming what was attempted) - a field the ticket-block-only response still emits. **Same prompt-comparability class as D112 and D125**: LLM cards have no version axis, so this is a real, permanent, un-versioned change to every future card's prompt; a card whose `prompt_text` contains "plain prose" predates it, the same per-card checkable-boundary convention those two use. `docs/prompts/llm-card-v1.md` gains a section recording this in the same register. **`docs/findings/llm-analyst-notes-v1.md` gains its own amendment**, not a silent edit, because this directly invalidates H2's pre-registered "directive rule" criterion (it explicitly read "does the reasoning acknowledge ignoring it") - the amendment restates the criterion against the `influence` line instead and lands before any notes-augmented card exists, unlike D112's amendment which found existing cards already on the wrong side of its boundary. VERIFIED: `npm run check-llm-cards` green (68 checks unchanged, including the doc-sync assertion and the five pinned `ANALYST_NOTES_CLAUSES` rule phrases, none of which this touched) and `npm run build` green. **No live model call made** - verifying the token reduction against the real API would spend real money per generation, so verification is the check suite (which already exercises `extractTicketBlock`/`buildSystemPrompt` against synthetic responses) plus the prompt/doc text itself; the actual per-call token saving will show up in the next `llm_card_requests` rows once a real card is generated. No schema change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump |
| D137 | Multiple HUMAN cards per race day, with an optional user-entered card name | 3 | `human-card-names` | merged | **User request 2026-09-06**: "add support for multiple HUMAN cards per race day. Allow user to enter optional card name." Investigated before writing anything: D28's append-only rule already lets `persistHumanRace` mint a second human card on the same day (omit `cardId`) - `check-human-picks.js`'s own D103 section already builds a second human card on the live day this way - so the gap was never backend capability, it was that every UI surface (`CardsPanel.jsx`, `ReplayRaceView.jsx`, `ReplayDayLanding.jsx`) silently auto-resumed "the day's own latest human card" with no way to deliberately reach for a second one, unlike `LlmCardModal.jsx`'s existing "Start a New Card" button (D75). **Schema**: migration 025 adds `cards.name TEXT`, nullable, no CHECK - same ordinary-ALTER shape as D76's `cards.llm_model`, and deliberately generic on `cards` rather than human-only (no reason a future LLM/OTR card couldn't carry one, and one column beats three near-identical ones). **Backend**: `persistHumanRace` (`server/human-cards.js`) takes an optional `name`, used ONLY on the call that mints a brand-new card (trimmed, blank/whitespace-only stored as NULL) and silently ignored on every later call to that same card - the exact frozen-at-creation convention `llm_model` already established (invariant 14's spirit); the `card_generated` trace event and `docs/trace-schema.md` both gained the field. `server/cards.js`'s `listCards` query gained `c.name` (the single-card `GET /api/cards/:id` already used `c.*`). **Client**: `CardsPanel.jsx` (the live `/day` view) and `ReplayDayLanding.jsx` (Replay) each gained a "Human card" picker - a `<select>` of the day's existing human cards by `#N — name`, plus a `+ Start a new card…` sentinel that reveals a "Name (optional)" text input - defaulting to "follow the latest" (unchanged behavior) until the user deliberately picks otherwise, and resetting to that default once the builder modal or race view closes so a freshly-minted card is what's shown next. `DayTicketBuilderModal.jsx` and `ReplayRaceView.jsx` both take the resolved `cardId`/`cardName` as explicit props now rather than each re-resolving "the latest" independently via its own `listCards` call - `ReplayRaceView` in particular had done this internally on every mount; lifting it to `ReplayDayLanding` (which already fetched the day's human cards for its own "final card" section) removes a second, potentially-disagreeing resolution rather than adding one. The card's name is threaded into every `lockHumanCard` call unconditionally (harmless once a card exists, since the server ignores it past creation) and displayed in the builder modal's header, the race view's card-info line, `CardsPanel`'s table (new Name column), and `CardView.jsx`'s page title. VERIFIED: `npm run check-schema`, `npm run check-human-picks` (+8: optional name accepted and trimmed, returned by both the single-card and list endpoints, frozen after creation even when a later call passes a different name, whitespace-only and omitted both store NULL rather than `''`, and a day ends up with three independently-addressable human cards - one named, two not), `npm run check-replay`, `npm run check-pl`, `npm run check-export` (the `card_generated` schema-doc sync assertion), `npm run check-graded-card-fixtures`, `npm run check-llm-cards`, and `npm run build` all green. Browser-verified end to end on the real Del Mar 2026-09-06 day in the `dev:preview` sandbox (D97 isolation - never the human's ports): selected "+ Start a new card…", typed "Longshot Special", built and locked a `$10 W 1` ticket, and confirmed the new card appeared as `#4 — Longshot Special` in the table, the picker, and the card sheet's own title (`card #4 — "Longshot Special"`) alongside the pre-existing unnamed `#1` card on the same day - then deleted the test card via the confirmed hard-delete route to leave the sandbox as found. No `ENGINE_VERSION` bump - this is a human-card UI/schema addition, not a grading or allocation change |
